# AP-07 — Bladder Meridian (BL) — Foot Taiyang (足太阳膀胱经)

> **Status:** Phase 2 — ALL 67 ENTRIES COMPLETE (Review pending).
> Phase 1: 6 Tier A entries (BL67 + Back-Shu ×5). Phase 2: 61
> in 12 batches — ALL COMPLETE. Batches 1-12 (BL1–BL66) done.
>
> **Meridian overview:** The Bladder channel (Foot Taiyang) is the
> LONGEST meridian with the MOST points — 67 standard points. Runs from
> inner canthus of eye (BL1) → over vertex → down the back in TWO
> parallel lines (medial 1.5 cun and lateral 3 cun from midline) → down
> posterior leg → lateral foot → ends at lateral nail of 5th toe (BL67).
> Contains ALL 12 Back-Shu points (organ-specific paravertebral points).
> Paired with Kidney (Foot Shaoyin). Peak time: 15:00-17:00.
>
> **Total points:** 67 standard | 6 Phase 1 done | Batches 1-12: 61 done | **67/67 COMPLETE**

---

## BL1 — Tinh Minh (睛明, Jīng Míng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Bright Eyes" (睛 = eye/pupil, 明 = bright/clear) — at the inner
canthus of the eye, in the depression slightly above the medial
angle. The STARTING POINT of the Bladder channel and the MOST
multi-channel meeting point in the entire Atlas: 6 channels
converge here (BL, ST, SI, GB, GV, Yin Qiao Mai — Jiayi Jing).
The SI→BL transition occurs here: SI19 (Tinggong, pre-auricular)
→ BL1 (inner canthus), establishing Hand Taiyang → Foot Taiyang
same-name continuity. Parallel to LI20→ST1 at the nose (Yangming)
and TE23→GB1 at the lateral canthus (Shaoyang). BL1 is the
PRIMARY acupoint for all ophthalmic conditions in TCM.
SAFETY-CRITICAL: orbital contents lie directly deep — retrobulbar
hemorrhage is the most serious non-thoracic acupuncture
complication.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the depression superior to
the inner canthus of the eye, close to the medial orbital wall.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient's eyes closed, the point
is in the small depression between the medial palpebral ligament
(above) and the medial angle of the eye (below), against the
medial orbital wall (lacrimal bone / frontal process of maxilla).

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun (~1.2–2.5 cm) perpendicular,
inserted slowly along the medial orbital wall, pushing the
eyeball LATERALLY. Patient must close eyes. DO NOT lift-thrust,
rotate, or apply any manipulation after insertion — risk of
retrobulbar hemorrhage.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Infratrochlear nerve** — terminal branch of the nasociliary
  nerve (from V1, ophthalmic division of trigeminal). Primary
  sensory innervation of the medial canthus, medial eyelids, and
  bridge of the nose. The nasociliary nerve also gives off the
  long ciliary nerves to the globe.
- Medial palpebral nerve — branch of infratrochlear, to the
  medial palpebral conjunctiva
- Deep: short and long ciliary nerves (sensory to the globe),
  oculomotor nerve (CN III) branches to medial rectus and
  inferior oblique muscles
- Cranial nerve territory: V1 (ophthalmic division). NO spinal
  segment involvement — entirely cranial nerve mechanism.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3

Fascial plane: orbital septum (thin fibrous membrane separating
preseptal from postseptal orbital contents). Superficial:
orbicularis oculi muscle (palpebral portion). Deep: periorbita
(orbital periosteum) lines the medial orbital wall. Minimal
fascial convergence — PROKR2 mechanism unlikely applicable.

Vascular:
- **Angular artery** — terminal branch of the facial artery,
  ascending along the side of the nose to the medial canthus.
  Anastomoses with dorsal nasal artery (ophthalmic).
- **Dorsal nasal artery** — terminal branch of ophthalmic artery,
  emerging above the medial palpebral ligament
- **Superior and inferior medial palpebral arteries** — from
  ophthalmic artery
- Deep: **ophthalmic artery** (branch of internal carotid) within
  the orbit — HIGH RISK if needle enters deep orbital space
- Angular vein → facial vein. Cavernous sinus drainage pathway
  via ophthalmic veins (route for orbital infection → cavernous
  sinus thrombosis).
→ Cross-ref: A04

Muscle layers: skin → orbicularis oculi (palpebral portion) →
medial palpebral ligament → periorbita (orbital periosteum).
Medial rectus muscle lies deep within the orbit, attached to the
medial wall.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

**Meeting point of 6 channels (交会穴):**
BL (Foot Taiyang), ST (Foot Yangming), SI (Hand Taiyang),
GB (Foot Shaoyang), GV (Du Mai), and Yin Qiao Mai.
Source: Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~259 CE).

This is the MOST multi-channel convergence at any single point in
the Atlas. Previous maximum: 3–4 channels at one point (common
for meeting points). 6 channels at BL1 is unprecedented.

The channel convergence is consistent with the eye's importance
across all meridian systems. Ling Shu Ch. 80 (Da Huo Lun, "Grand
Confusion"): "五臟六腑之精氣，皆上注於目" — the essence of all
five Zang and six Fu organs ascends and pours into the eye. The
6-channel meeting at BL1 is the structural expression of this
principle: the eye is the only organ that receives contributions
from ALL major channel families.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** BL1 (Jingming 睛明). Functions: expels Wind,
clears Heat, brightens the eyes, stops lacrimation. THE primary
point for all eye disorders. Indications span the full spectrum:
redness, swelling, pain, itching, lacrimation, blurred vision,
night blindness, color blindness, myopia, optic atrophy,
glaucoma, early-stage cataract. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** No direct correspondence. Periorbital
muscles (orbicularis oculi, corrugator supercilii) can harbor
trigger points, but their referred pain patterns are local
(periorbital aching) rather than the long-distance referral
typical of classic TrP-acupoint correspondence. ✗

**3. Ashi correspondence:** the medial canthus area becomes
tender or pressure-sensitive in eye diseases (conjunctivitis,
glaucoma), acute sinusitis, and frontal headache. Diagnostic
palpation tenderness at BL1 is recognized in TCM as a sign of
Eye-related pathology. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Apanga Marma is at the LATERAL corner of the
eye, not the medial corner. Sthapani Marma is at the glabella
(between the eyebrows). Neither is a precise match for the medial
canthus. ✗

**5. Head zone:** V1 (ophthalmic) dermatome covers the upper
eyelid and medial canthus region. V1 trigeminal afferents →
trigeminal spinal nucleus → NTS/DMNV → parasympathetic output
(A07 §1.7). The trigeminal-vagal parasympathetic pathway (diver's
reflex: cold on V1 territory → bradycardia) demonstrates that V1
stimulation drives autonomic responses. Meaningful V1 territory
correspondence. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Superficial Back Line (SBL) terminates
at the supraorbital ridge / epicranial fascia, reaching the
forehead but not specifically the medial canthus. No strong
myofascial line convergence at the inner eye corner. ✗

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific Kim 2017 data for periorbital
neurogenic spots at BL1. Trigeminal sensitization in migraine
patients does include the V1 territory, but no point-specific
data. ✗

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable. The periorbital area cannot
receive cupping due to anatomical constraints (concave surface,
proximity to globe). ✗

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡).
Low convergence despite extreme clinical importance. Consistent
with BL67 (2/8) — the two BL endpoint points both show low
cross-tradition convergence, reinforcing the observation that
convergence score tracks mechanism CLARITY, not clinical EFFICACY
(Phase 1 ⚡ note). BL1's clinical importance is mediated through
cranial nerve mechanisms that most cross-tradition systems do not
independently identify as anatomically special.

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Ling Shu Ch.80 Da Huo Lun): eye as convergence of
  all Zang-Fu essence
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: eye pain, redness, swelling, lacrimation,
  visual obstruction, night blindness, early cataract
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: all eye diseases, nasal congestion, headache

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- ALL ophthalmic conditions: conjunctivitis, keratitis, optic
  atrophy, optic neuritis, retinitis, myopia, glaucoma,
  color blindness, night blindness
- Lacrimation disorders (excessive tearing, dry eyes)
- Nasal congestion (proximity to nasal cavity)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- MOST-prescribed single point for eye disorders
- Combined with GB37 (Guangming, "Bright Light" — LR Luo) for
  visual improvement — classical pairing
- Combined with Yintang (EX-HN3) for frontal headache + eye strain
- Used with Taiyang (EX-HN5) and BL2 for acute eye conditions

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Myopia (near-sightedness) — 🟡 Plausible**
BL1 included in acupuncture protocols for myopia in systematic
reviews. Evidence quality: low-moderate. Mechanism: possible ciliary
muscle relaxation via parasympathetic modulation through V1
trigeminal-vagal pathway. Specific contribution of BL1 difficult
to isolate from multi-point protocols.

**2. Dry eye syndrome — 🟡 Plausible**
Acupuncture including BL1 shows benefit for dry eye in several
RCTs and systematic reviews. Lacrimal gland innervation via
parasympathetic fibers (CN VII → pterygopalatine ganglion →
lacrimal nerve) may be modulated through periorbital stimulation.

**3. Glaucoma (intraocular pressure) — 🟡 Plausible**
Some evidence for IOP reduction with acupuncture including BL1.
Ciliary body regulation via parasympathetic modulation is the
proposed mechanism. Evidence quality: limited.

**4. Other eye conditions — 🔴 Unvalidated**
Optic atrophy, color blindness, night blindness, cataract — no
modern controlled evidence specific to BL1 despite extensive
classical indications. These represent the classical-era
observational dataset without modern confirmation.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No specific disease-state sensitization data for BL1. The medial
canthus area may show increased tenderness in eye diseases,
sinusitis, and frontal headache. V1 territory allodynia in migraine
patients (Burstein 2000) is well-documented, and BL1 falls within
this sensitization zone.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon)

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

BL1 operates entirely through cranial nerve mechanisms:

**Primary pathway: V1 trigeminal → parasympathetic.**
Infratrochlear nerve (nasociliary → V1) → trigeminal spinal
nucleus → NTS (nucleus tractus solitarius) → DMNV (dorsal motor
nucleus of vagus) → parasympathetic efferents. This trigeminal-
vagal pathway (A07 §1.7) explains:
- Lacrimal effects: parasympathetic to lacrimal gland
- IOP regulation: ciliary body modulation
- Potential bradycardic response with deep stimulation (diver's
  reflex pathway — cold/pressure on V1 → cardiac vagal output)

**Non-segmental:** 100% cranial nerve mechanism. No spinal segment
involvement. Unlike back points (segmental) or distal limb points
(PROKR2/supraspinal), BL1 works through direct cranial nerve
access to brainstem autonomic centers.

**Gap:** the 6-channel meeting at BL1 is classical theory. No
modern mechanism explains why 6 meridian trajectories converge at
this specific point. The multi-channel designation may reflect the
eye's polyvalent innervation (V1+V2+III+IV+VI+VII) rather than
actual "energy" convergence.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3, A07 S1.7

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular insertion along the medial
orbital wall. Patient MUST close eyes. Insert slowly. NO
manipulation (lift-thrust, rotation) after insertion. Retain 15–30
minutes. Remove slowly, press with cotton ball for 1–2 minutes to
prevent hematoma.
EA: generally NOT used at BL1 due to proximity to the globe and
orbital structures. If used: low frequency (2 Hz), minimal
intensity.

Bấm huyệt (B02): gentle fingertip pressure at the medial canthus
depression. Common self-treatment for eye fatigue (the instinctive
"pinching the bridge of the nose" gesture approximates BL1
acupressure). Safe and widely used.

Cứu (B08): CONTRAINDICATED. Thermal stimulation near the eye is
dangerous (corneal thermal injury risk).

Dry needling (B11): not applicable — BL1 is not associated with
a myofascial trigger point pattern.

### An toàn

**⚠ HIGH RISK — ORBITAL CONTENTS**

**Retrobulbar hemorrhage:** the most serious non-thoracic
acupuncture complication. Orbital veins have NO VALVES — hemorrhage
can track posteriorly into the retrobulbar space. Compressive
hematoma → proptosis → stretching of optic nerve → optic nerve
ischemia → PERMANENT VISION LOSS. Risk increases with:
(a) deep insertion beyond 1.0 cun, (b) any needle manipulation,
(c) anticoagulant therapy, (d) coagulopathy.

**Management of orbital hematoma:** immediate firm pressure on
closed eye × 10 minutes. Monitor for proptosis, visual acuity
loss, pupil dilation. Emergency ophthalmology referral if vision
affected.

**Absolute rules:**
1. NO manipulation after insertion (no lift-thrust, no rotation)
2. Needle direction: along the medial orbital wall, NEVER toward
   the globe or laterally
3. Maximum depth: 1.0 cun (~2.5 cm)
4. Press cotton ball for 1–2 minutes after withdrawal
5. Ask about anticoagulant use before needling

**Relative contraindications:** severe coagulopathy, retrobulbar
tumors, recent orbital surgery, Graves' ophthalmopathy with
proptosis.

**Infection route:** ophthalmic veins communicate with the
cavernous sinus — orbital infection can lead to cavernous sinus
thrombosis. Strict sterile technique is essential.

## BL2 — Toản Trúc (攒竹, Cuán Zhú)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Gathered Bamboo" (攒 = gathered/clustered, 竹 = bamboo) — at
the medial end of the eyebrow, directly above BL1, in the
depression of the supraorbital notch. The name evokes the cluster
of eyebrow hairs gathering at the medial end. BL2 and TE23
(Sizhukong, "Silk Bamboo Hollow") BRACKET the eyebrow: BL2 at the
medial end, TE23 at the lateral end, with Yuyao (EX-HN4) between
them at mid-eyebrow. This medial-lateral eyebrow triad spans two
cranial nerve territories: BL2 = V1 medial branches (supraorbital/
supratrochlear), TE23 = V1 lateral + V2 (zygomaticotemporal).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the depression at the
medial end of the eyebrow, on the supraorbital notch (or foramen).

Anatomical landmarks: palpate the supraorbital margin of the
frontal bone from lateral to medial — the supraorbital notch is a
palpable groove (or foramen in ~25% of individuals) approximately
at the junction of the medial 1/3 and lateral 2/3 of the
supraorbital margin. BL2 is in this notch, at the medial termination
of the eyebrow. Directly above BL1 (Jingming) at the inner canthus.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun (~1.2–2.0 cm) transverse insertion
laterally along the eyebrow, or oblique inferiorly toward the
orbital rim. Subcutaneous needling (horizontal insertion under the
eyebrow skin) is common.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraorbital nerve** — major branch of the frontal nerve
  (from V1, ophthalmic division of trigeminal). Emerges through
  the supraorbital notch/foramen. Provides sensory innervation to
  the upper eyelid, forehead, and anterior scalp to the vertex.
  BL2 is DIRECTLY OVER the supraorbital nerve exit point.
- **Supratrochlear nerve** — medial branch of the frontal nerve,
  running more medially than the supraorbital. Supplies the medial
  forehead and upper eyelid near the midline.
- Motor: temporal branch of facial nerve (CN VII) to frontalis
  and corrugator supercilii muscles
- Cranial nerve territory: V1 (ophthalmic division).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3

Fascial plane: subcutaneous tissue overlying the periosteum of the
frontal bone. Corrugator supercilii and frontalis muscles converge
at this area.

Vascular:
- **Supraorbital artery** — accompanies the supraorbital nerve
  through the notch/foramen. Branch of ophthalmic artery (from
  internal carotid).
- Supratrochlear artery — medial companion
- Frontal branch of superficial temporal artery
→ Cross-ref: A04

Muscle layers: skin → orbicularis oculi → frontalis → corrugator
supercilii → periosteum of frontal bone. At the supraorbital
notch, the nerve and artery emerge between bone and muscle.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category designations. No Five Shu, Yuan-Source,
Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu, Front-Mu, Eight Confluent,
or Hui-Meeting.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** BL2 (Cuanzhu 攒竹). Functions: expels Wind,
clears Heat, brightens the eyes, alleviates pain. Major frontal
headache point. Indications: headache (frontal/supraorbital),
eye pain, lacrimation, blurred vision, facial paralysis
(frontalis weakness), supraorbital neuralgia, sinusitis. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** corrugator supercilii and frontalis muscle
TrPs cause referred pain to the supraorbital area and forehead.
Dorsher mapping supports moderate correspondence between
supraorbital acupoints and frontalis/corrugator TrPs. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** the supraorbital notch area is
routinely tender in frontal headache, eye strain, and acute
sinusitis. Patients often spontaneously report tenderness at this
site. Common clinical finding. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Sthapani Marma is located at the glabella
(between the eyebrows), corresponding more closely to Yintang
(EX-HN3) than to BL2 at the medial eyebrow end. Approximate zone
overlap only. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** V1 dermatome — the supraorbital region is
classic V1 territory. The supraorbital nerve exit at the notch
represents a well-defined V1 landmark. Trigeminal-autonomic
pathway operative. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the epicranial fascia connects frontalis
→ galea aponeurotica → occipitalis → SBL (Superficial Back Line).
BL2 at the eyebrow is at the ANTERIOR terminus of the SBL's
epicranial connection. Weak but present myofascial continuity. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific Kim 2017 data. Trigeminal
sensitization (V1 allodynia) in migraine includes the supraorbital
area, but no point-specific neurogenic spot documentation. ✗

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — facial cupping at the
eyebrow is not a standard practice. ✗

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡,
Head zone 🟡, AT 🟡). The eyebrow supraorbital notch is a
well-recognized site across multiple traditions — a palpable bony
landmark with a predictable nerve exit. Higher convergence than
BL1 (3/8) despite lower clinical prominence.

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: headache, visual dizziness, eye pain,
  tearing in wind, supraorbital swelling
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: frontal headache, eye redness, blurred
  vision, rhinorrhea, facial paralysis, epilepsy
- Nanjing: not specifically named

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Frontal headache (PRIMARY indication)
- Eye pain, redness, lacrimation, blurred vision
- Supraorbital neuralgia
- Facial paralysis (frontalis weakness — "drooping eyebrow")
- Sinusitis, rhinitis

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- MOST-used point for frontal headache (with Yintang, Taiyang)
- Combined with Taiyang (EX-HN5) and BL1 for acute eye conditions
- TE23 + BL2 electroacupuncture for frontal headache and
  eye-related conditions (eyebrow bracket pair)
- Facial paralysis protocol: BL2 for frontalis + ST4/ST6 for
  lower face

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Tension-type headache — 🟡 Plausible**
BL2 frequently included in acupuncture protocols for tension-type
headache. Frontal headache mechanism: supraorbital nerve
decompression effect (analogous to surgical decompression of
supraorbital nerve for migraine, Guyuron 2009). Acupuncture at
the nerve exit point may modulate afferent signaling.

**2. Supraorbital neuralgia — 🟡 Plausible**
Directly targets the supraorbital nerve at its exit point. Local
gate-control and nerve modulation mechanisms are anatomically
straightforward. Limited controlled evidence.

**3. Facial paralysis — 🔴 Unvalidated**
BL2 included in Bell's palsy protocols (targeting frontalis via
CN VII temporal branch). Evidence for acupuncture in Bell's palsy
is mixed; BL2's specific contribution is not isolated.

**4. Eye conditions — 🔴 Unvalidated**
Classical indications for eye disease at BL2 lack specific modern
evidence. BL1 is the primary eye point; BL2's role is typically
as an adjunct.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

The supraorbital notch area may become sensitized in frontal
headache, migraine (V1 allodynia), and sinusitis. Supraorbital
nerve tenderness is a recognized clinical sign in primary headache
disorders. No specific Kim 2017 or Chen Rixin data for BL2.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon)

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Primary pathway: supraorbital nerve → trigeminal nucleus →
brainstem autonomic centers.**
BL2 directly accesses the supraorbital nerve at its exit through
the supraorbital notch — a precise anatomical landmark. This is
one of the most ANATOMICALLY PREDICTABLE acupoints in the Atlas:
known nerve, known exit point, known dermatomal territory.

**Mechanism for frontal headache:** supraorbital nerve modulation
via gate control at the nerve exit point. BL2 is functionally
analogous to supraorbital nerve blocks (0.5–1 mL local anesthetic
at the same notch site) used in emergency medicine for frontal
headache and laceration repair.

**Mechanism for eye effects:** V1 trigeminal-vagal pathway, shared
with BL1 but accessed at a different V1 branch (supraorbital vs
nasociliary). Whether supraorbital stimulation at BL2 produces the
same parasympathetic effects as nasociliary stimulation at BL1 is
untested.

**Non-segmental:** 100% cranial nerve mechanism (V1), no spinal
segment involvement.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3, A07 S1.7

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 0.5–0.8 cun transverse insertion laterally along the
eyebrow (subcutaneous). Alternatively, oblique toward the orbital
rim. De Qi: local distension and soreness at the supraorbital
notch.
EA: TE23–BL2 pair (electroacupuncture across the eyebrow) for
frontal headache and periorbital conditions. 2–15 Hz.

Bấm huyệt (B02): thumb or fingertip pressure into the
supraorbital notch. Common self-treatment for frontal headache and
eye fatigue. Moderate pressure × 30–60 seconds.

Cứu (B08): generally avoided near the eyebrow (proximity to eye,
risk to eyebrow hair). Some sources permit indirect moxa.

Dry needling (B11): applicable if targeting corrugator/frontalis
TrPs. Shallow insertion into the muscle belly.

### An toàn

**Periorbital hematoma:** supraorbital artery runs through the
notch alongside the nerve. Needle insertion may puncture this
artery, causing a periorbital hematoma ("black eye"). Not
dangerous but cosmetically distressing. Prevention: press after
withdrawal × 1–2 minutes.

**Orbital entry risk:** deep inferior-directed needling could
enter the orbit through the superior orbital rim. Keep needle
depth shallow and angle tangential to the bony surface.

**Relative contraindication:** anticoagulant therapy (higher
hematoma risk at this vascular site).

## BL3 — Mi Xung (眉冲, Méi Chōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Eyebrow Ascension" (眉 = eyebrow, 冲 = rush/ascend). Directly
above BL2, 0.5 cun within the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun lateral
to GV24 (Shenting). First of 7 scalp points (BL3–BL9) running
along the BL trajectory 1.5 cun lateral to the GV midline, from
the anterior hairline to the occiput. Headache, nasal obstruction,
dizziness, epilepsy.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard: 0.5 cun directly above the anterior hairline,
directly above BL2, level with GV24.
Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun (~0.7–1.2 cm) transverse insertion
posteriorly along the scalp. Subcutaneous.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: supraorbital nerve (V1, frontal nerve branch) — the same
nerve as BL2 but further along its course into the scalp.
Spinal segment: not applicable (CN V1).
Vascular: supraorbital artery, frontal branch of superficial
temporal artery.
Muscle: frontalis (galea aponeurotica begins nearby).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: BL3 (Meichong). Headache, nasal obstruction, dizziness,
epilepsy. ✓
Trigger point: frontalis TrPs in the anterior scalp. 🟡
Ashi: may be tender in frontal headache. 🟡
Marma: no correspondence. ✗
Head zone: V1 dermatome, anterior scalp. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: galea aponeurotica = SBL epicranial fascia. 🟡
Neurogenic spot: no specific data. ✗
Cupping: not applicable (scalp). ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡,
AT 🟡).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Jiayi Jing, Dacheng: headache, dizziness, nasal obstruction,
epistaxis, epilepsy. Limited classical emphasis — a transitional
point between the important face points (BL1-2) and the vertex
scalp points (BL5-7).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific modern evidence for BL3. Scalp acupuncture in this
region is studied as part of Jiao's scalp acupuncture zones rather
than individual point research. 🔴 for all indications.

### An toàn

Low risk. Subcutaneous scalp needling. Scalp vascularity: scalp
lacerations bleed profusely due to rich arterial anastomoses, but
acupuncture needle gauge causes minimal bleeding. No deep
structures at risk.

---

## BL4 — Khúc Sai (曲差, Qū Chāi)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Deviating Turn" (曲 = bent/curved, 差 = difference/deviation).
0.5 cun within the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun lateral to GV24
(same as BL3 — some sources differentiate: BL3 directly above
BL2, BL4 at the standard 1.5 cun lateral mark). Headache, nasal
congestion, epistaxis.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard: 0.5 cun within the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun
lateral to the midline (GV24). Medial to the junction of the
frontal bone and parietal bone (coronal suture area).
Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun transverse posteriorly.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: supraorbital nerve (V1) lateral branch, transitioning
toward the parietal scalp.
Vascular: frontal branch of superficial temporal artery.
Muscle: frontalis → galea aponeurotica.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: BL4 (Quchai). Headache, nasal obstruction, epistaxis. ✓
Trigger point: scalp fascial TrPs. 🟡
Ashi: may be tender in headache. 🟡
Marma: no correspondence. ✗
Head zone: V1 dermatome, anterior-lateral scalp. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: galea aponeurotica (SBL). 🟡
Neurogenic spot: no data. ✗
Cupping: not applicable. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡,
AT 🟡).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Jiayi Jing: headache, nasal obstruction, epistaxis.
Limited classical emphasis.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific evidence. 🔴 for all indications.

### An toàn

Low risk. Subcutaneous scalp needling. Same safety profile as BL3.

## BL5 — Ngũ Xứ (五处, Wǔ Chù)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Five Places" (五 = five, 处 = places). 1 cun posterior to the
anterior hairline, 1.5 cun lateral to GV23 (Shangxing). The name
may refer to the five sensory organs converging in the head
region. Headache, dizziness, epilepsy.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard: 1 cun posterior to the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun
lateral to GV23. On the parietal scalp.
Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun transverse posteriorly.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: supraorbital nerve V1 (terminal scalp branches) — nearing
the V1/C2 transition zone on the scalp.
Vascular: superficial temporal artery (frontal branch).
Muscle: galea aponeurotica (the epicranial aponeurosis).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: BL5 (Wuchu). Headache, dizziness, epilepsy, rhinitis. ✓
Trigger point: scalp fascial TrPs possible. 🟡
Ashi: may be tender in headache. 🟡
Marma: Adhipati Marma (crown) is at the vertex — nearby but
more posterior/superior. ✗
Head zone: V1/C2 transition zone. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: galea aponeurotica (SBL). 🟡
Neurogenic spot: no data. ✗
Cupping: not applicable. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡,
AT 🟡).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Jiayi Jing: headache, dizziness, blurred vision, epilepsy.
Dacheng: adds rhinitis, epistaxis.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific evidence. 🔴 for all indications.

### An toàn

Low risk. Subcutaneous scalp needling. Standard scalp point safety.

## BL6 — Thừa Quang (承光, Chéng Guāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Receiving Light" (承 = receive/support, 光 = light). 2.5 cun
posterior to the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun lateral to GV21
(Qianding). Near the vertex of the skull on the parietal bone.
Headache, visual disorders, nasal congestion. MOXA PROHIBITED in
some classical sources (Jiayi Jing: 禁灸).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard: 2.5 cun posterior to the anterior hairline, 1.5
cun lateral to GV21.
Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun transverse insertion.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: the V1/C2 transition zone on the parietal scalp. At this
latitude, V1 (supraorbital) territory is giving way to C2
(greater occipital nerve) territory. Both nerves overlap in
the parietal region.
Vascular: parietal branch of superficial temporal artery,
anastomosis with occipital artery branches.
Muscle: galea aponeurotica.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.3

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: BL6 (Chengguang). Headache, blurred vision, nasal
congestion, febrile disease. ✓
Trigger point: scalp TrPs possible. 🟡
Ashi: may be tender in headache. 🟡
Marma: no correspondence. ✗
Head zone: V1/C2 overlap zone. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: galea aponeurotica (SBL). 🟡
Neurogenic spot: no data. ✗
Cupping: not applicable. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡,
AT 🟡).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Jiayi Jing: headache, blurred vision, rhinorrhea. Moxa prohibited
(5th moxa prohibition in the Atlas, after LU8, LR10, LR12, TE23).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific evidence. 🔴 for all indications.

### An toàn

Low risk. Subcutaneous scalp needling. **Moxa prohibition:**
BL6 is one of the points where classical sources prohibit moxibustion
(Jiayi Jing). Reason unclear — possibly because the parietal scalp
hair ignites easily, or a classical prohibition based on empirical
adverse effects. 5th moxa prohibition documented in Atlas
(LU8/LR10/LR12/TE23/BL6).

## BL7 — Thông Thiên (通天, Tōng Tiān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Reaching Heaven" (通 = reach/connect/penetrate, 天 = heaven).
4 cun posterior to the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun lateral to
GV20 (Baihui). Near the vertex, level with GV20. The name
suggests the point's proximity to the vertex — the "highest"
point on the body. Headache, nasal congestion, rhinitis. BL7
is the BL channel's closest approach to GV20 (Baihui), the most
clinically important vertex point.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard: 4 cun posterior to the anterior hairline, 1.5 cun
lateral to GV20 (Baihui).
Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun transverse insertion.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: parietal scalp — primarily greater occipital nerve (C2)
territory at this latitude, with possible V1 overlap. This is
the V1→C2 transition completed.
Vascular: parietal/occipital branches of superficial temporal
artery.
Muscle: galea aponeurotica.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.2

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: BL7 (Tongtian). Headache, nasal congestion, rhinitis,
dizziness, epistaxis. Reportedly one of the more clinically
used BL scalp points for nasal disorders. ✓
Trigger point: scalp TrPs possible. 🟡
Ashi: may be tender in headache. 🟡
Marma: Adhipati Marma at the crown — BL7 is near the vertex
(1.5 cun lateral to GV20). Approximate correspondence. 🟡
Head zone: C2 dermatome, parietal scalp. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: galea aponeurotica (SBL vertex). 🟡
Neurogenic spot: no data. ✗
Cupping: not applicable. ✗

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡,
Head zone 🟡, AT 🟡). Highest of the scalp group — proximity to
vertex (GV20, Adhipati Marma) adds Marma correspondence.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Jiayi Jing: headache, heavy head, dizziness, nasal obstruction.
Dacheng: rhinitis, epistaxis, facial paralysis (upper face).
BL7 is among the more clinically referenced scalp BL points,
primarily for nasal disorders.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific evidence. 🔴 for all indications.

### An toàn

Low risk. Standard scalp point safety.

## BL9 — Ngọc Chẩm (玉枕, Yù Zhěn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Jade Pillow" (玉 = jade, 枕 = pillow). On the occiput, at the
level of the upper border of the external occipital protuberance,
1.3 cun lateral to GV17 (Naohu). Named for the occipital bone,
which rests on the pillow — the "jade pillow" is a classical
metaphor for the occiput. BL9 marks the BL channel's arrival at
the occiput before descending to BL10 at the posterior neck.
Occipital headache, visual disorders, nasal obstruction.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard: level with the superior border of the external
occipital protuberance, 1.3 cun lateral to GV17 (Naohu), in the
depression on the lateral aspect of the occipital protuberance.
Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun transverse insertion.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: greater occipital nerve (C2) — the GON ascends over the
occiput and BL9 is in its territory. Also the occipital artery
accompanies the GON.
Vascular: occipital artery (from external carotid).
Muscle: occipitalis (posterior belly of occipitofrontalis) —
attaching to the superior nuchal line.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.2

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: BL9 (Yuzhen). Occipital headache, visual disorders, nasal
obstruction, dizziness. ✓
Trigger point: occipitalis muscle TrPs — refer pain to the
occiput and posterior orbit. 🟡
Ashi: occipital region may be tender in headache. 🟡
Marma: no precise correspondence (Krikatika is lower, at the
neck-skull junction — closer to BL10). ✗
Head zone: C2 dermatome. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: SBL — occipitalis → galea aponeurotica. The
occipital attachment of the SBL's epicranial fascia. 🟡
Neurogenic spot: no data. ✗
Cupping: not applicable. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡,
AT 🟡).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Jiayi Jing: occipital headache, heaviness of the head, visual
dizziness, nasal obstruction.
Dacheng: adds aversion to wind, pain in the eyes.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific evidence. 🔴 for all indications.

### An toàn

Low risk. Subcutaneous needling over the occipital bone. The
occipital artery runs in this region — slight bleeding possible
if punctured. No deep structures at risk (bone provides a natural
depth limiter at the occiput).

---

### Batch 2 Observations

**Observation 4 — BL SCALP V1→C2 NERVE GRADIENT.**
BL3–BL9 trace a posterior arc from anterior hairline (BL3) to
occiput (BL9), crossing the V1 (supraorbital) → C2 (greater
occipital) nerve transition on the scalp. BL3-BL4 are in V1
territory (supraorbital nerve terminal branches). BL5-BL6 are
in the V1/C2 overlap zone. BL7-BL9 are in C2 territory (greater
occipital nerve). This gradient is CONTINUOUS — no sharp boundary
like the cranial-to-spinal jump at ST8→ST9 (Obs. 9, AP-03).
The V1/C2 overlap is mediated by the cervico-trigeminal
convergence zone (A07 §1.2), explaining why both frontal
(V1) and occipital (C2) headaches respond to vertex acupuncture.

**Observation 5 — GALEA APONEUROTICA AS UNIFORM SUBSTRATE.**
All 7 scalp points (BL3–BL9) share the SAME deep tissue: the
galea aponeurotica (epicranial fascia). This is the longest
run of anatomically IDENTICAL deep structure in the Atlas.
The points are differentiated ONLY by their nerve supply (V1
vs C2) and scalp arterial territory (supraorbital vs
superficial temporal vs occipital). Traditional indications
also show minimal differentiation — headache, nasal disorders,
dizziness appear at nearly every point. The scalp group
suggests that traditional point differentiation may exceed
actual anatomical differentiation on the calvarium.

**Observation 6 — MOXA PROHIBITION BL6 (5th in Atlas).**
BL6 is the 5th moxa prohibition documented: LU8 (radial artery
thermal risk), LR10 (femoral artery), LR12 (femoral artery +
spermatic cord), TE23 (eye proximity), BL6 (scalp — reason
unclear). The first 4 have clear vascular or organ-damage
rationales. BL6's prohibition may reflect empirical observation
of scalp moxa adverse effects (hair ignition, scalp burn) rather
than deep structural risk. This is the FIRST moxa prohibition
without a clear anatomical danger rationale.

---

## BL10 — Thiên Trụ (天柱, Tiān Zhù)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Celestial Pillar" (天 = heaven/celestial, 柱 = pillar/column) —
at the posterior neck, 1.3 cun lateral to GV15 (Yamen), within
the posterior hairline, on the lateral border of the trapezius
muscle. The name evokes the cervical spine as the "pillar
supporting heaven (the head)." A **Window of Heaven (天牖穴)**
point — one of the ORIGINAL 5 Window of Heaven listed in Ling Shu Ch. 2 and
21 (ST9 人迎, LI18 扶突, TE16 天牖, **BL10 天柱**, LU3 天府).
BL10 occupies the POSTERIOR position in the Window of Heaven cervical ring.
This is the 6th Window of Heaven point documented in detail in the Atlas
(after LI18 AP-02, ST9 AP-03, SI16+SI17 AP-06, TE16 AP-10).
BL10 sits at the cervico-trigeminal convergence zone — where C2
dermatome overlaps with trigeminal V1/V3 territory (A07 §1.2) —
giving it dual access to both cervical spinal and trigeminal-
vagal circuits.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.3 cun lateral to GV15
(Yamen), in the depression on the lateral aspect of the trapezius
muscle, at the level of the superior border of the posterior
hairline.

Anatomical landmarks: GV15 (Yamen) is in the midline, in the
depression below the spinous process of C1 (atlas) — between the
occiput and C2. BL10 is 1.3 cun (~3 cm) lateral to this midline
point, in the depression at the lateral border of the upper
trapezius, where the trapezius meets the semispinalis capitis
and splenius capitis muscles. The greater occipital nerve (C2)
emerges in this region.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun (~1.2–2.0 cm) perpendicular or
slightly inferiorly directed. CAUTION: the foramen magnum and
cisterna magna lie deep at the midline; lateral needling at BL10
avoids this risk but the vertebral artery runs through the
suboccipital triangle more medially.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Greater occipital nerve (GON)** — dorsal ramus of C2. The
  largest cutaneous nerve of the posterior scalp. Emerges between
  the obliquus capitis inferior and semispinalis capitis, pierces
  the trapezius aponeurosis, and ascends to innervate the
  posterior scalp to the vertex. BL10 is in the zone where the
  GON emerges through the trapezius.
- **Suboccipital nerve** — dorsal ramus of C1. Motor to
  suboccipital muscles (rectus capitis posterior major/minor,
  obliquus capitis superior/inferior). No cutaneous component.
- **Third occipital nerve** — dorsal ramus of C3. Cutaneous to
  the upper posterior neck. C2-C3 overlap at this level.
- The **cervico-trigeminal convergence zone** — C2-C3 afferents
  converge with trigeminal (V1, V3) afferents in the trigeminal
  spinal nucleus (pars caudalis), extending to C2-C3 spinal cord
  levels. This convergence explains why suboccipital stimulation
  can modulate trigeminal-mediated headache.
- Spinal segments: C1, C2, C3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.2, A07 S1.2

Fascial plane: the nuchal fascia (posterior cervical fascia)
attaches to the external occipital protuberance and nuchal line
superiorly, and to the spinous processes of the cervical
vertebrae. Trapezius, semispinalis capitis, and splenius capitis
converge in this region — a significant fascial convergence zone.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular:
- **Occipital artery** — branch of external carotid artery.
  Crosses the suboccipital triangle, piercing the trapezius
  aponeurosis near the greater occipital nerve.
- **Deep cervical artery** — branch of costocervical trunk
  (subclavian artery). Supplies the deep posterior neck muscles.
- Vertebral artery — runs through the transverse foramina of
  C1-C6 and crosses the posterior arch of C1 (in the suboccipital
  triangle). More MEDIAL than BL10, but deep needling toward the
  midline must avoid this structure.
→ Cross-ref: A04

Muscle layers: skin → trapezius (superficial border) →
semispinalis capitis → splenius capitis. The suboccipital triangle
(rectus capitis posterior major, obliquus capitis superior/
inferior) lies deeper and more medial, surrounding the vertebral
artery and C1-C2 nerve roots.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

**Window of Heaven (天牖穴)**

One of the ORIGINAL 5 Window of Heaven points in Ling Shu Ch. 2 and 21:
ST9 (人迎), LI18 (扶突), TE16 (天牖), **BL10 (天柱)**, LU3
(天府). The expanded group of ~10 points forms a cervical ring
around the neck at the body-head transition zone (AP-02 Obs. 26).

BL10 within the cervical ring: **POSTERIOR position.** Together
with GV16 (Fengfu, posterior midline — not yet documented), BL10
flanks the posterior cervical midline from the posterolateral
aspect. Compare: ST9+LI18 (anterior pair), SI16+SI17 (lateral),
TE16 (posterolateral), CV22 (anterior midline). The ring is now
nearly circumferentially documented:

| Position | Points | Status |
|----------|--------|--------|
| Anterior | ST9, LI18 | DONE (AP-03, AP-02) |
| Lateral | SI16, SI17 | DONE (AP-06) |
| Posterolateral | TE16 | DONE (AP-10) |
| **Posterior** | **BL10** | **THIS ENTRY** |
| Posterior midline | GV16 | Pending (AP-13) |
| Anterior midline | CV22 | Pending (AP-14) |
| Limb exception | LU3 | Noted (AP-01, pre-Window of Heaven architecture) |
| Lateral chest | PC1 | Noted (AP-09, some traditions) |

Window of Heaven Atlas status: **6 of ~10 documented** in full Window of Heaven architecture
(LI18, ST9, SI16, SI17, TE16, BL10). Remaining: GV16, CV22, LU3
(re-document), PC1.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 Obs. 26, AP-01 LU3, AP-06 SI16/SI17,
AP-10 TE16

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** BL10 (Tianzhu 天柱). Functions: expels Wind,
benefits the head and neck, clears the nose, brightens the eyes.
Window of Heaven function: treats conditions where ascending
circulation to the head is disrupted. Indications: occipital
headache, neck stiffness, nasal obstruction, sore throat,
shoulder pain, dizziness, mania. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** **strong correspondence.** Suboccipital
muscles (semispinalis capitis, splenius capitis, upper trapezius)
are among the most extensively documented trigger point locations
in Travell & Simons. Semispinalis capitis TrPs refer pain to the
occiput and temporal region in a band pattern. Upper trapezius TrP1
(at the shoulder-neck junction) refers superiorly to the temple.
The BL10 location overlaps with the superior attachment zone of
these trigger point patterns. 🟢
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** highly reactive in tension-type
headache, cervicogenic headache, and neck stiffness. The
suboccipital region is one of the most consistently tender areas
in headache patients. Spontaneous pressure-seeking behavior
(patients pressing the back of the neck) targets this zone. 🟢

**4. Marma point:** **Krikatika Marma** — located at the junction
of the neck and skull, governing neck mobility. Classified as
Sandhi (joint-type) Marma. The base of skull / upper cervical
junction is a recognized vital point in Ayurvedic martial arts
(trauma to this zone can cause loss of consciousness). BL10 falls
within the Krikatika zone. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** C2 dermatome covers the posterior scalp. BL10
is at C1-C2 level, within the C2 dermatome. The cervico-
trigeminal convergence zone at C2-C3 (where spinal and trigeminal
afferents share second-order neurons) is a well-established
neuroanatomical entity explaining occipital-to-frontal headache
referral. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Superficial Back Line (SBL) passes
through the suboccipital region via epicranial fascia →
occipitalis → galea aponeurotica → SBL continues through erector
spinae. BL10 at the trapezius lateral border is at the SBL's
cervico-cranial transition zone. The upper trapezius attachment
to the nuchal line is an SBL landmark. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** the suboccipital region is a well-known
zone of referred tenderness and sensitization in headache
disorders. While no Kim 2017-specific data exists for BL10,
cervicogenic referred pain patterns consistently involve this
zone, suggesting neurogenic inflammatory mechanisms. 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** posterior neck cupping is practiced in TCM
for Wind-Cold invasion, headache, and neck stiffness. Cupping at
the nape region (GV14 area and lateral) is standard. BL10 at the
hairline is at the upper border of the feasible cupping zone. 🟡

**Convergence score: 8/8** (TCM, TrP 🟢, Ashi 🟢, Marma 🟡,
Head zone 🟡, AT 🟡, Neurogenic 🟡, Cupping 🟡). All 8 systems
show meaningful correspondence — the HIGHEST convergence score
in the entire Atlas. The suboccipital
region is a uniquely multi-tradition convergence zone: recognized
as clinically significant independently by TCM, Western trigger
point therapy, Ayurvedic Marma, Head/dermatome mapping, fascial
anatomy, neurogenic inflammation research, and traditional
cupping practice. See Obs. 3.

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch. 21: listed as Window of Heaven (天牖穴)
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: headache, stiff neck, visual dizziness,
  nasal obstruction, sore throat
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: occipital headache, Wind-Cold, neck pain and
  stiffness, mania, epilepsy

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Occipital headache (PRIMARY indication)
- Neck stiffness and pain
- Nasal obstruction, sinusitis
- Sore throat, voice loss (Window of Heaven indication)
- Dizziness, visual disturbance

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- PRIMARY posterior neck point for occipital headache and neck
  stiffness (with GB20, GV16)
- Combined with GB20 (Fengchi) — the MOST common neck point
  pairing in clinical practice. GB20 is 2 cun lateral to GV16
  (in the depression between upper trapezius and SCM). BL10 is
  1.3 cun lateral to GV15. The two points access different levels
  of the suboccipital musculature.
- Window of Heaven protocols for throat disorders: BL10 combined
  with LI18, CV22

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Tension-type headache — 🟡 Plausible**
BL10 frequently included in acupuncture protocols for tension-type
headache. The suboccipital region is a primary target. Mechanism:
modulation of C2 afferent input via greater occipital nerve →
trigeminal spinal nucleus convergence. Analogous to greater
occipital nerve blocks (local anesthetic at the GON exit point
— same anatomical zone as BL10).

**2. Cervicogenic headache — 🟡 Plausible**
C2 is THE definitive cervical segment for cervicogenic headache
(International Headache Society criteria). BL10 at C1-C2 directly
accesses C2 dorsal ramus territory. Diagnostic anesthetic blockade
of the C2 nerve is the gold standard for diagnosing cervicogenic
headache — BL10 acupuncture targets the same neural structure.

**3. Neck stiffness / cervical pain — 🟡 Plausible**
Local musculoskeletal mechanism via trigger point release in
suboccipital muscles. Anatomically straightforward (local needling
of hypertonic cervical musculature). Not point-specific evidence.

**4. Nasal obstruction / sinusitis — 🔴 Unvalidated**
Classical Window of Heaven indication. Possible mechanism: cervico-trigeminal
convergence → sphenopalatine ganglion modulation → nasal mucosa
vasomotor effects. No specific evidence for BL10 in nasal
conditions.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

The suboccipital region is one of the most consistently sensitized
zones in headache patients. Greater occipital nerve tenderness is a
clinical sign in tension-type headache, migraine, and cervicogenic
headache. BL10 area sensitization may reflect C2 dorsal ramus
neurogenic inflammation. However, no specific Kim 2017 or Chen
Rixin data exists for BL10.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon)

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

BL10 operates through two converging pathways:

**Primary pathway 1: C2 dorsal ramus → spinal cord.**
Greater occipital nerve (C2 dorsal ramus) → spinal dorsal horn
C2-C3 → segmental modulation of cervical musculature. This is a
segmental mechanism explaining the local neck stiffness and
occipital headache effects.

**Primary pathway 2: C2 → trigeminal spinal nucleus.**
The trigeminocervical complex (TCC): trigeminal spinal nucleus pars
caudalis extends from the brainstem to the C2-C3 spinal cord
level. C2-C3 afferents converge with V1/V3 trigeminal afferents
on shared second-order neurons (WDR neurons in laminae I/V). This
convergence explains:
- Occipital → frontal headache referral (C2 input perceived as
  frontal V1 pain)
- BL10 efficacy for frontal headache despite posterior location
- Nasal effects (V1/V2 trigeminal → sphenopalatine ganglion)
- Visual effects (V1 territory includes the orbit)

**Window of Heaven mechanism:** the cervical ring's shared
anatomy — ALL ascending pathways (carotid, vertebral, vagus,
sympathetic, spinal cord) pass through the neck. BL10's posterior
position accesses the vertebral artery corridor and the
suboccipital neural complex. Unlike anterior Window of Heaven points (ST9,
LI18) that access carotid-vagal structures, BL10 accesses
vertebral-cervical structures.

**Partial segmental (C1-C3):** unlike fully non-segmental distal
points (LI4, ST36), BL10 has a strong segmental component at
C1-C3 combined with the cervico-trigeminal cross-modal access.
Approximately 60–70% segmental.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.2, A07 S1.2, S1.7

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 0.5–0.8 cun perpendicular or slightly inferiorly
directed. Avoid directing the needle MEDIALLY toward the midline
(vertebral artery, spinal cord via foramen magnum). De Qi:
local distension, soreness, may radiate to the occiput.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm thumb pressure into the suboccipital
depression lateral to the trapezius. Self-treatment: patients
can apply bilateral pressure at BL10 for occipital headache.
Common clinical instruction.

Cứu (B08): moxa applicable (stick moxa for Wind-Cold headache).
Safe if hair protected.

Dry needling (B11): applicable for suboccipital trigger points.
Semispinalis capitis and splenius capitis are primary targets.
Careful depth control — vertebral artery lies deep.

### An toàn

**Vertebral artery:** runs through the suboccipital triangle
medial to BL10. The vertebral artery crosses the posterior arch of
C1 in a groove (sulcus arteriosus). Deep medial-directed needling
from BL10 could potentially approach this artery. Risk: vertebral
artery dissection → posterior circulation stroke.

**Prevention:** needle directed perpendicular or slightly
inferiorly, NEVER medially. Maximum depth: 0.8 cun at this
location.

**Spinal cord / brainstem:** the foramen magnum is deep at the
midline (GV15/GV16 level). BL10 at 1.3 cun lateral is NOT at
the midline, but awareness of this deep structure is important.

**Caution with bilateral simultaneous stimulation:** bilateral
BL10 EA should use low intensity — the proximity to the brainstem
(C1-C2 level) warrants conservative stimulation parameters.

---

### Batch 1 Observations

**Observation 1 — BL1 MULTI-CHANNEL CONVERGENCE (6 channels).**
BL1 meeting 6 channels (BL, ST, SI, GB, GV, Yin Qiao Mai) is the
highest channel convergence at any single point in the Atlas.
Previous maximum: LR13 meeting 4 channels. 6 channels at one
point is unprecedented and maps to the classical principle "五臟
六腑之精氣，皆上注於目" (essence of all organs ascends to the
eye). Anatomically, the medial canthus is an orbital-nasal-
vascular convergence zone (V1, angular artery/vein, orbital
venous drainage), but the number of MERIDIAN trajectories passing
through this zone (6) far exceeds the number of distinct neural
structures (primarily V1 nasociliary). The 6-channel meeting may
reflect the eye's polyvalent innervation (V1+V2+III+IV+VI+VII)
mapped onto the meridian system, rather than 6 independent neural
pathways converging at one point.

**Observation 2 — BL CHANNEL ANTERIOR-TO-POSTERIOR ARC.**
The BL channel starts ANTERIORLY at BL1 (inner canthus = anterior
face), ascends to BL2 (supraorbital = superior face), traverses
the vertex (BL3-BL8), and arrives POSTERIORLY at BL9-BL10
(occiput/posterior neck). This frontal-to-occipital arc is
UNIQUE — no other meridian makes an anterior-to-posterior
transition over the head. ST stays anterior, GB stays lateral,
GV stays midline posterior. The BL arc aligns with the Superficial
Back Line (SBL) trajectory through the epicranial fascia:
supraorbital ridge → galea aponeurotica → occipitalis → erector
spinae → plantar fascia. BL1-BL10 may represent the cephalic
segment of the SBL, with BL11 onward following the thoracolumbar
segment.

**Observation 3 — BL10 HIGHEST CONVERGENCE SCORE (8/8).**
BL10 achieves 8/8 cross-tradition convergence — the highest in
the Atlas if confirmed. The suboccipital region at C1-C2 is
independently recognized as clinically significant by ALL 8
cross-tradition systems: TCM (Tianzhu), trigger points
(semispinalis/splenius TrPs), Ashi (headache tenderness),
Marma (Krikatika), Head zone (C2 dermatome + cervico-trigeminal
convergence), Anatomy Trains (SBL cervico-cranial transition),
neurogenic spot theory (suboccipital sensitization), and cupping
(nape cupping for Wind-Cold). This aligns with the Phase 1 ⚡
framework note: higher convergence → clearer mechanism
understanding. BL10's mechanism IS the clearest of the three
head/neck points (BL1 3/8, BL2 6/8, BL10 8/8): C2 dorsal ramus →
trigeminocervical complex is a textbook-level pathway. The
gradient BL1(3/8)→BL2(6/8)→BL10(8/8) tracks the shift from
cranial nerve territory (V1, harder for non-TCM traditions to
identify as "acupuncture-relevant") to cervical spinal nerve
territory (C2, where trigger points, dermatomes, and fascial
lines all converge naturally).

---

## BL11 — Đại Trữ (大杼, Dà Zhù)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Great Shuttle" (大 = great, 杼 = shuttle/weaving shuttle) — at the
upper back, 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border of the spinous
process of T1. BL11 is the FIRST POINT on the medial back line
(1.5 cun lateral, T1→S4) — where the BL channel transitions from
the head/neck segment (BL1–BL10) to the paravertebral segment.
A **Hui-Meeting point (八会穴)** — specifically the Hui-Meeting of
Bones (骨会, Nanjing Ch.45), one of 8 tissue Hui-Meeting points.
Also a triple meeting point: BL (Foot Taiyang) + GV (Du Mai) +
SI (Hand Taiyang, same-name pair). The SI–BL meeting here and the
SI19→BL1 transition at the eye form two anatomical anchors of the
Taiyang same-name linkage: one at the face (orbital), one at the
upper back (paravertebral). BL11 is the FIRST of TWO Hui-Meeting
points on the BL channel (BL17 Geshu = Hui-Meeting of Blood,
Batch 4). BL hosts 2/8 Hui-Meeting — more than any other meridian.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T1.

Anatomical landmarks: T1 is identified by counting down from C7
(vertebra prominens — the most prominent spinous process at the
cervicothoracic junction). T1 spinous process is the next one below
C7. BL11 is 1.5 cun (~3–4 cm) lateral to this point, in the
paravertebral musculature. The first rib articulates with T1, and
the lung apex extends ABOVE the first rib posteriorly — relevant
for pneumothorax risk.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun (~1.2–2.0 cm) oblique insertion
toward the spine. PNEUMOTHORAX RISK — the lung apex extends above
the first rib, making BL11 the HIGHEST (most superior) pneumothorax
risk point on the BL medial back line.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T1 spinal nerve — primary
  sensory innervation of paravertebral region at T1 level
- T1 also contributes to the brachial plexus (lower trunk,
  C8–T1), giving BL11 a unique position: the only medial
  back-line point where the corresponding spinal segment
  contributes to BOTH paravertebral and upper limb innervation
- Spinal segment: T1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (upper extent). At T1, the
fascia transitions from cervical architecture (nuchal ligament
territory, where BL10 sits) to thoracolumbar fascia proper. The
cervicothoracic junction represents a significant change in fascial
structure: from the mobile cervical segment to the rib-stabilized
thoracic segment.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of supreme (highest) intercostal artery,
from the costocervical trunk of the subclavian artery. Small
caliber paravertebral vessels.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → trapezius (upper fibers)
→ rhomboid minor → serratus posterior superior → erector spinae
(longissimus dorsi) → 1st rib / transverse process of T1.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — BL11 is on the medial back line but is NOT an
organ Back-Shu. T1 has no assigned organ in the Back-Shu system.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.

**Hui-Meeting: YES — Bones (骨会)**

One of 8 Hui-Meeting points (八会穴, Nanjing Ch.45), each governing
a specific tissue type. "Bone disorders go to Dazhu" (骨病者取大杼).
The 8 Hui-Meeting: Zang=LR13, Fu=CV12, Qi=CV17, Blood=BL17,
Sinew=GB34, Vessel=LU9, **Bone=BL11**, Marrow=GB39. BL channel
hosts 2/8 (BL11+BL17) — the most of any single meridian.

**Meeting of BL + GV + SI**

BL11 is a crossing point (交会穴) of three channels: Bladder (Foot
Taiyang), Governing Vessel (Du Mai), and Small Intestine (Hand
Taiyang). The BL–SI meeting reflects Taiyang same-name pairing;
SI15 (Jianzhongshu) at T1, 2 cun lateral to midline, is only
0.5 cun lateral to BL11. The GV meeting reflects the proximity of
the midline GV channel to the paravertebral BL line at the upper
thoracic level — GV14 (Dazhui) at C7/T1 and GV13 (Taodao) at T1
cluster with BL11–BL12 in a small anatomical zone.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL11 (Dazhu 大杼) — Hui-Meeting of Bones. Traditional
  functions: strengthen bones and sinews, benefit the joints, expel
  Wind-Heat, descend rebellious Qi, open the chest. The Hui-Meeting
  designation assigns BL11 theoretical governance over ALL skeletal
  conditions — a systemic claim that exceeds segmental scope.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T1 paravertebral trigger points
  in upper erector spinae, rhomboid minor, and serratus posterior
  superior. Referred pain: upper back, posterior shoulder,
  interscapular region. The cervicothoracic junction is one of the
  most common trigger point locations for neck-upper back pain.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: cervicothoracic junction tenderness is
  extremely common — tender in cervical spondylosis, upper back
  strain, and postural dysfunction. One of the most frequently
  tender paravertebral locations.

Marma point: **Possible correspondence.** Amsa Marma (Frawley) is
  in the shoulder/upper back region but centered more laterally.
  BL11 at the cervicothoracic junction is at the medial border of
  Amsa territory.

Head zone: T1 dermatome = medial forearm/upper arm. T1 is the FIRST
  sympathetic segment (stellate ganglion). No direct "bone"
  viscerotome — the Head zone framework maps visceral organs, not
  tissue types. **✗ NOT APPLICABLE** — bone governance is a TCM
  tissue-type classification, not a segmental visceral mapping.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line (Myers) along erector spinae.
  BL11 is at the cervicothoracic transition on the SBL — where the
  cervical segment (nuchal ligament → occipitalis) meets the
  thoracic segment (thoracolumbar fascia → erector spinae). A key
  fascial transition node.

Neurogenic spot: Kim 2017 cardiac neurogenic spots at C8–T2. T1 is
  within this range, but relevance is cardiac, not skeletal. The
  overlap is coincidental to BL11's Hui-Meeting designation.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Paravertebral cupping at T1 level is
  practiced as part of upper back cupping protocols including
  BL11–BL13 with GV14. Not a primary named cupping site.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains, cupping). Marma 🟡 → possible 6/8. Head zone ✗ (tissue-type
classification ≠ visceral segmental mapping). Neurogenic spot
overlap coincidental (cardiac, not bone).

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Nanjing Ch.45: "骨会大杼" — bone disorders go to Dazhu. The
  Hui-Meeting designation establishes BL11 as the master point for
  all skeletal conditions.
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): stiffness of spine,
  fever and chills, headache, Wind-Cold, cough with counterflow Qi
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE): neck stiffness, shoulder
  and back pain, bone pain

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Neck and upper back stiffness/pain (primary musculoskeletal)
- Cough, fever, headache (Wind-Heat / exterior condition)
- Bone diseases (Hui-Meeting traditional designation)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with GV14 (Dazhui) and BL12 (Fengmen) for Wind-Heat
  exterior conditions — the upper back "Wind points" triad
- Combined with BL23 (Shenshu) and ST36 (Zusanli) for bone health
  tonification — classical bone-strengthening protocol
- Combined with GB34 (Hui-Meeting of Sinew) and GB39 (Hui-Meeting
  of Marrow) for musculoskeletal conditions — bone-sinew-marrow
  Hui-Meeting combination

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Cervical spondylosis / neck-upper back pain — 🟡 Plausible**

BL11 included in multi-point acupuncture protocols for cervical
spondylosis and upper back pain. Not studied as isolated point.
Mechanism: local trigger point release + gate control in T1
dermatome. The cervicothoracic junction is a common site of
musculoskeletal dysfunction, and local needling has plausible
anatomical rationale. 🟡 — mechanistically plausible but no
BL11-specific evidence.

**2. Bone metabolism / osteoporosis — 🔴 Unvalidated**

The Hui-Meeting of Bones designation suggests systemic skeletal
governance. Some acupuncture protocols for osteoporosis include
BL11, but no study has isolated BL11's contribution to bone
metabolism markers (BMD, osteocalcin, CTX). T1 has no privileged
skeletal access compared to any other spinal level — no segmental
mechanism supports systemic bone governance from T1.

**3. Wind-Heat / common cold — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional use in combination with GV14 and BL12. No specific
evidence for BL11 alone in exterior conditions.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: cervicothoracic junction shows
increased muscle tone and tenderness in patients with cervical
spondylosis and chronic upper back pain. The C7–T1 transition zone
is a biomechanical stress concentration point — sensitization here
likely reflects local musculoskeletal load rather than organ-specific
viscerosomatic reflex.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Local musculoskeletal (primary) + non-segmental
Hui-Meeting claim (unresolved).**

For musculoskeletal indications:
T1 dorsal ramus → T1 dorsal horn → gate control (Aβ) + segmental
inhibition → local pain modulation in cervicothoracic region.
Straightforward gate control / trigger point mechanism.

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL11 at T1 → dorsal horn T1 → local pain modulation
  ✅ MATCH for local musculoskeletal indications
  ✗ NO MATCH for systemic "bone governance" — T1 has no
    privileged access to skeletal metabolism
  Segmental contribution for local effects: ~100%
  Segmental contribution for bone Hui-Meeting claim: ~0%
```

The Hui-Meeting of Bones designation presents a BSA challenge:
the claim is SYSTEMIC (all bones), but the mechanism is LOCAL
(T1 paravertebral). No known neural pathway connects T1 stimulation
to global bone metabolism. Possible interpretations: (a) the
classification is clinically useful as a treatment-selection
heuristic without requiring a systemic mechanism; (b) an
unidentified neuroendocrine pathway exists; or (c) the Hui-Meeting
system reflects theoretical framework rather than empirical
correspondence. The same challenge applies to other Hui-Meeting
points (LR13 for all Zang, GB34 for all sinews).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S5.1, C04 S3.4

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Same safety
  protocol as BL13 (thoracic pneumothorax zone). De Qi: distending
  sensation in upper back, may radiate to shoulder.
  EA: commonly paired with BL12 or GV14 for cervical conditions.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL11: used for chronic bone conditions (osteoporosis,
  chronic arthritis). Part of bone-tonification protocols with
  BL23 and ST36.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Giác hơi (B07):
  Cupping at BL11–BL12 area for Wind-Cold/Wind-Heat. Sliding
  cupping from GV14 down to BL13 is a common clinical protocol.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — lung apex extends above the first rib
  posteriorly. Oblique insertion toward spine is critical. BL11 is
  the MOST SUPERIOR pneumothorax risk point on the BL medial back
  line. Same depth precautions as BL13: max 0.5–0.8 cun oblique.

Vascular risk: Minimal — paravertebral vessels are small caliber.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

## BL12 — Phong Môn (风门, Fēng Mén)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Wind Gate" (风 = wind, 门 = gate/door) — at the upper back, 1.5
cun lateral to the lower border of the spinous process of T2. The
name designates this point as the "gate through which Wind enters
the body" — the primary TCM concept linking BL12 to exterior
pathogenic invasion (Wind-Cold, Wind-Heat). A **Meeting point of
BL + GV** (Du Mai), the second of two GV-meeting points on the
medial back line (BL11 + BL12). Together with BL11, BL12 forms the
transition zone where the BL channel enters the thoracic
paravertebral corridor. BL12 is one of the most frequently cupped
acupoints — cupping at BL12 for common cold is among the most
widely practiced traditional applications of cupping therapy.
Note: BL41 (Fufen), the FIRST point on the outer back line (3 cun
lateral), is also at T2. BL12 and BL41 share the same spinal level
but differ by 1.5 cun lateral distance — the first medial-outer
pair on the BL back lines.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T2.

Anatomical landmarks: T2 is two spinous processes below C7
(vertebra prominens). BL12 is 1.5 cun (~3–4 cm) lateral, in the
paravertebral musculature. The scapular spine is approximately at
T3 level — BL12 is just above the scapular spine.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun (~1.2–2.0 cm) oblique insertion
toward the spine. Pneumothorax risk at T2 level.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T2 spinal nerve — primary
  sensory innervation of paravertebral region at T2 level
- Spinal segment: T2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T2, the needle passes
through the fascial septum between trapezius and erector spinae.
Similar architecture to BL13 (T3) — the upper thoracic paravertebral
zone shares consistent fascia throughout T1–T6.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 2nd posterior intercostal artery (from
supreme intercostal artery or directly from thoracic aorta). Small
caliber paravertebral vessels.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → trapezius (upper fibers)
→ rhomboid minor → erector spinae (longissimus dorsi) → 2nd rib /
transverse process of T2.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — BL12 is NOT an organ Back-Shu despite being on
the medial back line. T2 has no assigned organ. Front-Mu: No.
Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

**Meeting of BL + GV**

BL12 is a crossing point of BL (Foot Taiyang) and GV (Du Mai). The
second of two GV-meeting points on the medial back line (BL11 at T1,
BL12 at T2). Below T2, the medial back-line points are pure BL
channel — the organ Back-Shu (BL13 onward) do not cross GV. The
T1–T2 GV crossing zone coincides with GV14 (Dazhui, C7/T1) and
GV13 (Taodao, T1), clustering multiple GV points with BL11–BL12
in a compact anatomical zone.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL12 (Fengmen 风门) — Wind Gate. Traditional
  functions: expel Wind (Wind-Cold or Wind-Heat), release the
  exterior, benefit the Lung, regulate Wei Qi (defensive Qi).
  THE primary posterior point for early-stage exterior invasion.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T2 paravertebral trigger points
  in upper erector spinae and rhomboid minor. Referred pain:
  interscapular region, posterior shoulder. Upper trapezius TrPs
  also refer to this zone.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: upper back tenderness in patients with common
  cold / upper respiratory infection. The "Wind Gate" concept may
  reflect empirical observation that this area becomes sensitized
  during early respiratory illness — consistent with viscerosomatic
  reflex from upper respiratory tract inflammation.

Marma point: **Possible correspondence.** Bruhati Marma (Frawley)
  covers the interscapular region approximately T3–T6. BL12 at T2
  is slightly above Bruhati but at its superior border.

Head zone: T2 dermatome → medial upper arm. T2 is within the
  cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5) but ABOVE the primary respiratory
  viscerotome (T3–T5). **🟡 Partial** — T2 overlaps with cardiac
  innervation but has limited Head zone support for BL12's primary
  Wind/respiratory indications.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line along erector spinae. BL12
  is in the upper thoracic segment of the SBL.

Neurogenic spot: Kim 2017 cardiac neurogenic spots at C8–T2. **T2
  is within this range** — the LAST segment of the C8–T2 cardiac
  spot concentration zone. Neurogenic spots at T2 are predicted in
  cardiac disease; BL12's primary indication is respiratory, not
  cardiac, but the location-based convergence counts.

Cupping point: **Primary cupping location.** BL12 is one of the
  MOST frequently cupped acupoints in TCM. Cupping at BL12 for
  Wind-Cold is among the most traditional and widely practiced
  cupping applications. Sliding cupping from BL12 to BL23 is a
  standard technique. Named in B07 cupping protocols.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains, neurogenic spot, cupping). Head zone 🟡 and Marma 🟡 →
possible 7–8/8. Note: neurogenic spot match is for cardiac
territory (C8–T2), not respiratory — counts by location but the
indication mismatch should be noted.

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): common cold with fever
  and chills, headache, cough, nasal congestion, upper back
  stiffness
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE): Wind-Cold with sneezing,
  runny nose, cough; Wind-Heat with fever, sore throat

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Common cold (Wind-Cold or Wind-Heat) — primary indication
- Cough, nasal congestion, sneezing
- Upper back stiffness, neck pain
- Urticaria (Wind-related skin condition)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- THE primary posterior point for early-stage common cold — "Wind
  Gate" is the most-used name for posterior cold treatment
- Combined with GV14 + BL13 for the upper back "Wind triad"
- Cupping at BL12 area = the single most common clinical cupping
  application worldwide
- Combined with LI4 (distal Wind-expelling) for comprehensive Wind
  treatment: LI4 (anterior/distal) + BL12 (posterior/proximal)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Common cold / upper respiratory infection — 🟡 Plausible**

BL12 is included in virtually all acupuncture protocols for common
cold in clinical trials. Systematic reviews of acupuncture for URI
show modest benefits for symptom reduction and duration. BL12 is
always part of multi-point protocols with GV14, BL13, LI4, LI11 —
never studied in isolation. Mechanism may involve: (a) upper
thoracic sympathetic modulation of respiratory mucosa (T1–T4
sympathetic → superior cervical ganglion → nasal/pharyngeal
vasculature), (b) local cupping-induced immune mediator release, or
(c) somatosympathetic reflex reducing bronchial tone. 🟡 — plausible
mechanism, moderate clinical evidence, not point-specific.

**2. Cupping for respiratory conditions — 🟡 Plausible**

Cupping at the BL12–BL13 area supported by systematic reviews for
respiratory conditions. Dry cupping at the upper back (including
BL12 zone) shows benefits for chronic cough and asthma symptom
relief. The cupping mechanism includes local hyperemia, neurogenic
inflammation (CGRP/SP release), and possible reflex effects on
bronchial tone via T2–T3 sympathetic. 🟡 — cupping evidence is
stronger than needle evidence at this location.

**3. Upper back stiffness / local pain — 🟡 Plausible**

Local musculoskeletal effect via trigger point release and gate
control at T2 paravertebral muscles. Not point-specific but
anatomically straightforward.

**4. Urticaria / Wind-skin conditions — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional Wind-related indication. No BL12-specific evidence.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: the upper back / BL12 area may show
increased sensitivity during early-stage upper respiratory
infection. TCM practitioners report "cold back" or tender upper
back in patients with developing colds. If confirmed by formal
study, this would support the "Wind Gate" concept — T2 area
sensitization via viscerosomatic reflex from upper respiratory
tract inflammation. No formal quantitative study exists.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Upper thoracic sympathetic modulation (primary for
respiratory) + local musculoskeletal (primary for pain).**

For respiratory/exterior conditions:
T2 dorsal ramus afferents → T2 dorsal horn → somatosympathetic
reflex → upper thoracic sympathetic chain → sympathetic innervation
of upper respiratory mucosa (T1–T4 sympathetic → superior cervical
ganglion → nasal/pharyngeal vasculature and glands).

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL12 at T2 → dorsal horn T2 → T2 sympathetic → upper respiratory
  T2 is at UPPER EDGE of lung viscerotome (T3–T5) but within
  the broader upper respiratory sympathetic zone (T1–T4)
  🟡 PARTIAL MATCH — not direct lung segmental, but upper
  respiratory sympathetic pathway plausible
  Segmental contribution: ~50–70%
```

Cupping mechanism adds:
- Mechanical tissue disruption → local neurogenic inflammation
- CGRP/SP release from C-fibers → vasodilation, immune cell
  recruitment in paravertebral tissue
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

For local musculoskeletal:
Gate control via Aβ mechanoreceptor activation. Standard trigger
point mechanism. Segmental contribution: ~100%.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.1

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Same safety
  protocol as BL13. De Qi: distending sensation in upper back.
  Manual needle preferred over EA for exterior conditions.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL12: used for Wind-Cold conditions (warming the back to
  expel cold). Direct or indirect moxa, often with ginger slice for
  "warming and dispersing cold."
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Giác hơi (B07):
  **PRIMARY MODALITY for BL12.** Cupping at BL12 is one of the most
  practiced cupping applications globally. Dry cupping (留罐), flash
  cupping (闪罐), or sliding cupping (走罐) from BL12 down to BL23.
  Cups left for 5–15 minutes. Cupping marks at BL12 are often among
  the most vivid (darkest petechiae), possibly reflecting high local
  vascular response at this level.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T2 thoracic level. Same precautions as
  BL13: oblique insertion toward spine, max 0.5–0.8 cun. Increased
  risk in thin patients and emphysema/COPD.

Vascular risk: Minimal at standard needle depth.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### Batch 3 Observations

**Observation 7 — BL MEDIAL BACK LINE TRANSITION (BL10→BL11).**
BL11 marks the first point on the BL medial back line (1.5 cun
lateral, T1→S4). The transition from BL10 (suboccipital, C2 dorsal
ramus) to BL11 (cervicothoracic, T1 dorsal ramus) spans a C2→T1
gap — skipping C3–C8 entirely. The BL channel has NO points in the
cervical spine between BL10 (posterior neck) and BL11 (upper back).
Six cervical levels (C3–C8) are NOT represented on the medial back
line, despite being the most mobile spinal segment. This gap likely
reflects the medial back line's functional bias: VISCERAL access
(Back-Shu) and thoracolumbar coverage, while the cervical spine is
served by posterior neck points (BL10, GV14–GV16) and lateral
cervical points (GB20, SI15, TE16).

**Observation 8 — T3–T5 CARDIOPULMONARY BACK-SHU CLUSTER.**
With BL14 (PC Shu, T4) inserted between BL13 (Lung Shu, T3) and
BL15 (Heart Shu, T5), the Atlas now documents a continuous
3-segment cardiopulmonary cluster: Lung(T3)→PC(T4)→Heart(T5).
These 3 adjacent points access overlapping autonomic territory:
cardiac sympathetic (T1–T5) and pulmonary sympathetic (T3–T5)
viscerotomes overlap precisely at T3–T5. All 3 MATCH their organ
viscerotome (Dorsher). The TCM distinction between Lung-Shu,
Pericardium-Shu, and Heart-Shu maps to genuine but overlapping
neural territories — T3 is more "pulmonary-weighted," T5 is more
"cardiac-weighted," and T4 (PC) sits at the overlap. This supports
the concept that TCM organ differentiation at Back-Shu points
reflects a GRADIENT rather than discrete neural boundaries.

**Observation 9 — HUI-MEETING: SYSTEMIC CLAIM FROM LOCAL POINT.**
BL11 (Hui-Meeting of Bones) claims governance over ALL skeletal
conditions from a single T1 location. This is structurally parallel
to other Hui-Meeting points: LR13 for all Zang, GB34 for all
sinews, CV17 for all Qi. No segmental mechanism supports systemic
bone governance from T1 — T1 dorsal ramus accesses only local
paravertebral structures. The Hui-Meeting system may represent:
(a) a clinically useful treatment-selection heuristic (practical
classification rather than mechanism-based), (b) neuroendocrine
pathways not yet mapped, or (c) a theoretical framework assigning
master-point status based on clinical tradition. This challenge
applies to the entire Hui-Meeting system. A fuller analysis will
follow when BL17 (Hui-Meeting of Blood) is documented in Batch 4.

---

## BL67 — Chí Âm (至阴, Zhì Yīn)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis) — THE PARADOX POINT**
The strongest clinical evidence for a non-pain application in the entire
BSA framework (breech presentation — Cochrane 2023), combined with the
WEAKEST mechanism explanation. S1 dermatome has NO direct connection to
the uterus (T10-L1, S2-S4). The most important unsolved question in
acupuncture science. The ONLY Tier 1 point where the primary modality
is moxibustion, not needle.

Name 至阴 (Zhì Yīn) = "Reaching Yin" or "Utmost Yin" — last point on
the longest Yang meridian (Taiyang), crossing to the Yin side of the
body. Classical understanding: transition from maximum Yang to Yin.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of the 5th toe,
0.1 cun posterior to the corner of the nail.

Anatomical landmarks: at the junction of lines drawn along the lateral
border and the base of the 5th toenail. Minimal subcutaneous tissue —
