the point is essentially skin over periosteum of the distal phalanx of
the 5th toe.

Needle depth range: 1-2 mm superficial oblique insertion (Jing-Well
point — minimal depth). More commonly stimulated with MOXA than needle
(see Kỹ thuật section).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Lateral dorsal digital nerve of 5th toe (from sural nerve) — primary
  sensory innervation of the dorsolateral aspect of the 5th toe
- Plantar proper digital nerve (from lateral plantar nerve) — plantar
  aspect contribution
- Spinal segments: S1 (sural nerve), S1-S2 (lateral plantar nerve)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: minimal — skin directly over periosteum at this location.
No significant fascial layer at the nail corner of the 5th toe. This is
notable because it means the PROKR2 fascial mechanism (A02, A03) likely
does NOT apply here — no connective tissue plane for PROKR2+ neurons to
reside in.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal digital artery of 5th toe (from 4th dorsal metatarsal
artery, branch of dorsalis pedis). Small caliber vessel — minimal
vascular risk.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers: NONE at this location. The point is skin → minimal
subcutaneous tissue → periosteum. No muscle belly, no intermuscular
septum. This distinguishes BL67 from virtually all other high-evidence
acupoints (ST36, LI4, PC6, SP6 — all located in or between muscles).

Receptor density: no specific data for BL67. However, nail folds
generally have high C-fiber density (nociceptive) and TRPV1 receptor
density (thermosensitive). This is relevant because BL67's primary
clinical application uses HEAT (moxibustion).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: **Jing-Well (Metal)** — the last/most distal point on the
  Bladder channel. In the Mother-Son (Five Element) system, Metal is the
  "son" of Earth (the Bladder's element = Water; Jing-Well element =
  Metal for Yang channels). Jing-Well points are traditionally used
  for: mental restlessness, loss of consciousness, fullness below the
  heart, heat conditions.
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: traditionally listed as pregnancy contraindication alongside
  LI4 and SP6 — BUT is specifically USED during pregnancy (for breech
  version at 32-36 weeks). This creates a unique paradox: forbidden
  DURING pregnancy, yet its most-studied application IS a pregnancy
  application. In practice: contraindicated in early/mid pregnancy,
  specifically indicated at 32-36 weeks for breech.

### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL67 (Zhiyin 至阴) — Jing-Well (Metal) of Foot Taiyang.
  Traditional functions: expels Wind, clears Heat, opens orifices
  (head), promotes labor, corrects fetal malposition.

Trigger point: **No correspondence.** Dorsher's mapping (2006-2022)
  covers 93.3% of acupoints, but Jing-Well points at the nail corners
  of the toes have no anatomically corresponding trigger point — there
  is no muscle tissue at this location, so no myofascial trigger point
  can form. This is an important NEGATIVE finding: BL67 works by a
  mechanism that CANNOT be explained by the trigger point model.

Ashi correspondence: Not a typical tender point in clinical practice.
  The 5th toe nail corner is not a site of spontaneous tenderness or
  palpation-based identification.

Marma point: **No direct correspondence.** The nearest Marma is Kshipra
  (between 1st-2nd toes, corresponding more to LR3/KI1-2 area), which
  is ~4-5 cm medial. No Marma point is described at the lateral 5th
  toe in Frawley's 107-point system.

Head zone: S1 dermatome. The S1 viscerotome corresponds to RECTUM and
  partial BLADDER innervation (via pudendal nerve S2-S4 overlap region),
  but NOT to the uterus (T10-L1 sympathetic, S2-S4 parasympathetic).
  **This is THE core of the paradox:** the dermatome-viscerotome model
  (A07 §3.1) does NOT predict BL67's primary clinical effect (breech
  version → uterine action).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S3.5, S4.2

Anatomy Trains: the Superficial Back Line (Myers) extends from the
  plantar fascia → gastrocnemius/soleus → hamstrings → sacrotuberous
  ligament → erector spinae → galea aponeurotica. The 5th toe is at the
  LATERAL edge of the plantar fascia insertion, peripheral to the main
  Superficial Back Line. The Lateral Line passes through peroneus longus
  and brevis to the lateral foot — BL67 is near the distal terminus.
  Neither line directly connects to the uterus. Convergence: marginal.

Neurogenic spot: **No data.** Kim 2017 focused on cardiac dermatome
  (C8-T2) neurogenic spots. No study has examined whether neurogenic
  inflammatory spots appear near BL67 during pregnancy with breech
  presentation — this would be a highly informative study.

Cupping point: **Not applicable.** The 5th toe is not a cupping
  location in any tradition.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM + Head zone S1). The LOWEST convergence
score among all Phase 1 Tier A points. This is itself significant: BL67
has the strongest clinical evidence AND the lowest cross-tradition
convergence. The multi-system convergence zone model (A09 §2.2) predicts
that points with MORE system overlap should have STRONGER effects. BL67
violates this prediction.

⚡ **Framework note:** BL67's convergence score (2/8) is the lowest of
any Tier A point, yet it has one of the strongest single-indication
evidence bases (Cochrane 2023). This challenges A09 §2.2's convergence
zone model, which predicts stronger effects at higher-convergence
locations. Either: (a) the convergence model applies to mechanism-
dependent effects but BL67 works via a convergence-independent pathway,
or (b) we are missing a system-level overlap at BL67 that current
mapping does not detect.
→ Extends: A09 S2.2, C04 S3.2

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.1 Ben Shu): listed as Jing-Well point of Bladder
  channel. Used for headache, vertex pain, eye disorders.
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): headache, eye pain, nasal
  obstruction, difficult labor (难产, nán chǎn).
- Bei Ji Qian Jin Yao Fang (Sun Simiao, 652 CE): moxibustion at BL67
  for correcting fetal malposition (胎位不正, tāi wèi bù zhèng). This
  is the EARLIEST known documentation of the breech application.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE): difficult labor, retained
  placenta, fetal malposition, headache.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Difficult labor and malposition of fetus (primary modern indication)
- Headache, nasal obstruction, eye pain
- Retention of urine (classical, related to Bladder channel)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- The obstetric application (breech version) is the OVERWHELMINGLY
  dominant clinical use, documented consistently across 1,400+ years
  (Sun Simiao 652 CE → present). No other point has such a narrow yet
  well-documented primary application with moxibustion as the
  specifically indicated modality.
- Traditional MOXA method, not needle — this modality specificity is
  rare. Most acupoints are primarily needled; BL67 is one of the few
  where tradition specifically prescribes heat over insertion.
- The "eye disorders" indication likely relates to BL1 (inner canthus)
  being on the same channel — classical "treat the channel" logic, not
  point-specific effect.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Breech presentation (cephalic version) — 🟡 Plausible**

The most-studied single-point clinical application involving moxibustion.
Extensive evidence base but mechanism UNKNOWN, which prevents 🟢.

Cochrane Systematic Review (Coyle et al. 2023, Cochrane Database Syst
Rev Issue 5 — updated from 2012):
- 13 trials, 2,181 women
- Moxa + usual care vs usual care alone: probably reduces breech
  presentation at birth (moderate certainty evidence)
- Moxa + usual care vs sham moxa + usual care: probably reduces breech
  at birth (moderate certainty)
- Does NOT reduce overall cesarean section rate
- Probably reduces oxytocin use during labor
- Quality: moderate certainty — limited by impossibility of blinding
  for moxibustion, mostly Chinese studies, high heterogeneity

Individual key RCTs:
- Cardini & Weixin 1998 (JAMA 280(18):1580-1584): n=260, 33 weeks,
  moxa BL67 30 min/day × 1-2 weeks. Cephalic 75.4% vs 47.7%
  (P<0.001, RR 1.58, 95% CI 1.29-1.94). Highest-impact journal for
  any moxa study.
- Neri et al. 2004 (J Matern Fetal Neonat Med 15(4):247-252): n=226,
  33-35 weeks, acupuncture + moxa BL67. Cephalic 53.6% vs 36.7%.
  Cesarean for breech 52.3% vs 66.7% (P<0.05).
- Acupressure RCT 2021 (Explore 18(4):442-447): n=138, 32-35 weeks,
  acupressure BL67 bilateral 10 min/day × 2 weeks. Cephalic rotation
  82.6% vs 17.4%. Notable: ACUPRESSURE (not moxa) also effective at
  BL67 for breech.

Additional SR/MA:
- Zhang et al. 2021 (Healthcare 9(6):619): 16 RCTs, 2,555 women.
  Moxa significantly increased cephalic presentation (RR 1.39, 95% CI
  1.21-1.58).

Population difference: moxibustion appears MORE effective in Asian
populations than non-Asian populations (Coyle 2023, Zhang 2021).

Sham comparison: sham moxa (non-burning moxa stick at BL67) vs verum:
  Cochrane 2023 found moxa probably MORE effective than sham (moderate
  certainty). This suggests point-specific AND heat-specific effects
  beyond placebo/sham.

**2. Increased fetal activity — 🟡 Plausible**

Neri et al. 2002 (Reprod Sci 9(3)):
- NST (non-stress test) during acupuncture + moxa at BL67
- Significant reduction in fetal baseline heart rate
- More fetal heart rate accelerations and movements during treatment
- Fetal movements: 48.45/hour (moxa) vs 35.35/hour (control)
- 🟡 Single study design, but consistent with mechanism hypotheses

Neri et al. 2007 (J Soc Gynecol Investig 14(suppl)):
- Compared 3 stimulation methods at BL67: acupuncture alone, moxa alone,
  acupuncture + moxa
- All 3 increased fetal activity, with acupuncture + moxa showing
  the strongest effect
- 🟡 Supports point-specificity (BL67 as site) over modality-specificity

**3. Difficult labor / retained placenta — 🔴 Unvalidated**
Traditional indication from Jiayi Jing (259 CE) and Dacheng (1601 CE).
No modern clinical study specifically examining BL67 for these
indications. Classical use.

**4. Headache / vertex pain — 🔴 Unvalidated**
Neijing indication. Jing-Well points are traditionally used for head
conditions ("lower points treat upper"). No BL67-specific headache
evidence exists separately from general acupuncture headache studies.

**5. Eye disorders — 🔴 Unvalidated (previously claimed, retracted)**
The Cho et al. 1998 PNAS study claimed BL67 stimulation activated the
visual cortex on fMRI, appearing to validate the traditional "brightens
the eyes" indication via a neural pathway. This study was RETRACTED in
2006 (5/8 authors withdrew) due to methodological artifacts (Beissner
2011 identified specific flaws: eyes-closed baseline confound). A09
§3.2 documents this in detail. The retraction eliminates the only
proposed modern mechanism for BL67's eye indication. Traditional
indication = DATA, but no modern support.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S3.2

### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: **No data.** No study has examined whether
BL67 shows altered sensitivity during breech presentation vs cephalic
presentation. This would be a critical test of the dynamic acupoint
hypothesis (C04 §4): if BL67 shows neurogenic inflammation or threshold
change specifically during breech pregnancy, it would support a
disease-state-dependent mechanism.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: UNKNOWN — the most honest answer.**

Track A systems activated at BL67:
- A01 (Peripheral nervous system): sural nerve (S1) → dorsal horn →
  ascending pathways to brainstem/supraspinal centers. This is confirmed
  anatomically but does NOT explain why S1 input would affect the uterus.
- A06 (Chemical): TRPV1 thermoreceptors activated by moxibustion heat.
  C-fiber activation at nail fold (high C-fiber density). This explains
  HOW the thermal signal enters the nervous system but not WHERE it goes.
- Other Track A systems: no PROKR2 mechanism (no fascial plane), no
  significant mast cell or vascular convergence at this location.

**Why the segmental model FAILS at BL67:**
```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  Skin stimulus at segment X → dorsal horn → organ in segment X

BL67 CASE:
  S1 (sural nerve) → dorsal horn S1 → ???
  Uterus = T10-L1 (sympathetic) + S2-S4 (parasympathetic)
  S1 has NO overlap with either innervation territory

  GAP: 1 full segment (S1 vs S2) at minimum
  The segmental model DOES NOT predict this effect
```

**Five proposed mechanism hypotheses (all 🔴 Hypothesis):**

1. **Neuroendocrine cascade** — S1 thermal stimulus → ascending
   spinothalamic/spinoreticular → hypothalamus → ACTH/cortisol release
   → placental estrogen conversion → prostaglandin synthesis →
   myometrial sensitivity → increased fetal activity → version
   facilitation.
   Evidence: some studies show moxibustion affects cortisol, estradiol,
   ACTH levels. But this is a NON-SPECIFIC pathway — any strong
   peripheral thermal stimulus should trigger it. Does not explain
   point specificity. 🔴

2. **Adjacent segment spillover** — S1 afferents partially overlap with
   S2 territory → partial activation of S2-S4 pelvic parasympathetic
   via spinal interneuron spread.
   Problem: segmental reflexes are typically PRECISE, not diffuse.
   S1 → S2 spillover is possible but weak. The large effect size
   (RR 1.39-1.58) is hard to explain by a weak spillover. 🔴

3. **Supraspinal PAG mediation** — S1 thermal nociception → ascending
   → PAG → descending modulation of T10-L1 and S2-S4 spinal segments
   → uterine tone change. Similar to LI4's PAG descending pathway but
   with a different output target.
   Problem: why BL67 specifically and not any other S1 point? 🔴

4. **Fetal activity facilitation** — The effect may not be "causing
   version" but "increasing the probability of spontaneous version" by
   increasing fetal movement. At 33 weeks, ~50% of breech babies
   spontaneously turn anyway (Cardini control group = 47.7%). If
   moxibustion increases fetal activity (documented: 48.45 vs 35.35
   movements/hour), more movement = more opportunities for the fetus
   to find the cephalic position. This is the MOST conservative
   hypothesis — it requires explaining fetal activity increase (still
   hard) but not a specific S1 → uterine mechanism.
   Strongest support: control group version rates of 36-48% suggest
   the natural process is already active; moxa may FACILITATE, not
   CAUSE, version. 🟡 (upgraded from 🔴 due to observational support)

5. **Heat-specific pathway** — Moxibustion activates TRPV1+ C-fibers
   specifically. The thermal signal may activate a pathway that needle
   insertion does NOT. However, Neri 2007 found that acupuncture ALONE
   at BL67 also increased fetal activity (though less than moxa or
   combination), weakening a pure heat-specificity explanation. 🔴

**Gap:** The gap between observation and mechanism is the LARGEST of any
acupoint in the entire Atlas. Unlike ST36 (PROKR2 molecular mechanism),
PC6 (NTS convergence), LI4 (PAG descending), or SP6 (dual S2-S4 +
non-segmental), BL67 has NO validated mechanism for its primary
clinical effect. This is not a failure of the framework — it is a
mapped research gap. The honest answer is: "we don't know how it works."

⚡ **Framework note:** BL67 breech evidence directly challenges C04
S3.4's "60-70% segmental" estimate for acupoint-organ effects. BL67 has
ZERO percent segmental basis for its primary indication — S1 has no
uterine connection. This is the MOST EXTREME violation of the segmental
model among all Phase 1 points. Pattern across 5 completed Tier 1
points: ST36 = ~0% segmental (PROKR2-molecular), PC6 = ~30-40%
(NTS convergence), SP6 = ~50% (dual pathway), LI4 = ~0% segmental
(PAG descending), BL67 = 0% segmental AND no alternative mechanism
identified. C04's "60-70% segmental" was always an average; the Atlas
now shows it varies from 0% to ~100% (Back-Shu points, to be
documented). The average may be approximately correct but is
misleading as a per-point prediction.
→ Extends: C04 S3.4, A07 S5.1
→ Challenges: C04 S3.4 (as per-point prediction)

⚡ **Framework note:** BL67 is the ONLY Phase 1 point where the
primary modality is moxibustion, not needle. All other Phase 1 points
(ST36, ST25, PC6, LI4, SP6) are primarily needled. BL67's mechanism
may involve a HEAT-SPECIFIC pathway (TRPV1 C-fiber activation →
supraspinal thermal nociceptive processing → neuroendocrine cascade)
that is fundamentally different from needle mechanisms (PROKR2,
mechanical fascial signaling). B08 S5.3 proposed a "thermal-point
convergence model" but BL67 VIOLATES this model too — the model
requires dermatome-viscerotome overlap, which BL67 does not have.
The honest conclusion: B08's thermal-point model is incomplete and
may require a pathway not captured in the current 5-level framework.
→ Extends: B08 S5.3
→ Challenges: B08 S5.3 (thermal-point convergence model itself)

⚡ **Framework note:** The Cho et al. 1998 BL67 → visual cortex PNAS
study (RETRACTED 2006) is a critical methodological lesson already
documented in A09 S3.2. From the Atlas perspective: this retraction
eliminated the ONLY proposed neural pathway for BL67's traditional
"eye disorders" indication. It also demonstrates the danger of
confirming traditional claims via neuroimaging without adequate controls.
The Atlas records the traditional indication as DATA while noting the
modern evidence was retracted.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S3.2

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 1-2 mm, superficial oblique insertion toward the
  toenail root. Jing-Well point technique — prick to bleed is a
  classical variation for acute conditions.
  De Qi: minimal — Jing-Well points produce sharp, localized sensation
  rather than the spreading/distending De Qi of deeper points.
  EA: not typically used at BL67 due to minimal tissue depth. When
  studied (Neri 2007), needle stimulation alone DID increase fetal
  activity but less effectively than moxa or combination.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Bấm huyệt (B02):
  Acupressure at BL67 for breech: effective in RCT (2021, n=138).
  Bilateral BL67 acupressure, 10 minutes/day × 2 weeks, showed 82.6%
  vs 17.4% cephalic rotation. This is significant because it
  demonstrates the point's effect is NOT moxa-exclusive — pressure
  also works at this location.
  PROKR2 depth question: NOT relevant — no fascial plane at BL67.
→ Cross-ref: B02 S8

Cứu (B08) — **PRIMARY MODALITY for this point:**
  Method: indirect moxibustion with moxa stick (most studied).
  Bilateral BL67, moxa held ~2-3 cm from skin, 15-30 minutes per
  session (Cardini protocol: 30 min/day). Patient can self-administer
  or partner-administer at home.
  Thermal parameters: moderate heat, patient reports warmth without
  burning. TRPV1 activation threshold: 42-43°C.
  Treatment window: 32-36 weeks gestational age (optimal: 34 weeks).
  Duration: daily for 1-2 weeks, or until version confirmed by
  ultrasound.
  The moxa stick (not direct cone) is standard for BL67 to avoid
  burns at the delicate nail fold.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2, S4.2

Dry needling (B11):
  Not applicable — no muscle tissue at BL67, no myofascial trigger
  point, no local twitch response possible.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.2

So sánh kỹ thuật: Neri 2007 compared acupuncture vs moxibustion vs
  combination at BL67. All three increased fetal activity, but
  combination was most effective. The 2021 acupressure RCT suggests
  pressure is also effective. Ranking (by available evidence for
  breech): moxa (most studied, Cochrane) > moxa + acupuncture > moxa
  + acupressure > acupressure alone > acupuncture alone.

### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: None — distal toe location.

Vascular risk: Minimal — dorsal digital artery of 5th toe is small
  caliber. Standard precaution against excessive depth (periosteum is
  immediately beneath skin).

Pregnancy:
  Traditionally contraindicated during general pregnancy alongside LI4
  and SP6 — but SPECIFICALLY INDICATED at 32-36 weeks for breech
  version. This creates a unique safety profile:
  - Early/mid pregnancy: AVOID (precautionary, traditional prohibition)
  - 32-36 weeks with confirmed breech: INDICATED (primary application)
  - Active labor: classical use for difficult labor (traditional)
  
  Safety data: Cochrane 2023 found no significant adverse maternal or
  fetal effects from BL67 moxibustion in the included trials. NST
  monitoring (Neri 2002) showed increased fetal activity but no fetal
  distress patterns. The intervention is considered SAFE when properly
  timed and administered.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S8.3, B08 S8.1

Special restrictions: Diabetic neuropathy patients may have impaired
  thermal sensation at the toes — increased burn risk with moxibustion.
  Peripheral vascular disease — impaired healing at extremities.

---

---

## Back-Shu Points — Segmental Proof Group (背俞穴)

> **Group rationale:** The 5 Zang Back-Shu points below are Phase 1
> Group D — "Segmental Proof." They demonstrate the clearest segmental
> convergence in the Atlas. Unlike BL67 (0% segmental, mechanism
> unknown), Back-Shu points sit AT the vertebral level of their paired
> organ's autonomic innervation, making the somatovisceral reflex
> mechanism directly predictable from anatomy.
>
> **The 5 Zang organs:** Lung, Heart, Liver, Spleen, Kidney — the most
> important organs in TCM. Their Back-Shu points are the most clinically
> used of the 12 Back-Shu. The other 7 (BL14, BL19, BL21, BL22, BL25,
> BL27, BL28) are deferred to Phase 2.
>
> **Shared evidence base:**
> - Dorsher 2009-2022: 9/12 Back-Shu match organ autonomic innervation
>   (odds ~1 in 5 billion). ALL 5 points below are in the matching group.
>   The 3 non-matching are BL21 (Stomach), BL25 (Large Intestine),
>   BL27 (Small Intestine) — none included here.
> - Beissner & Henke 2011: Back-Shu = Head's maximum points
>   (independent East-West discovery of viscero-cutaneous reflexes)
> - Kim 2017: neurogenic inflammatory spots appear at acupoint
>   locations in the dermatome of diseased organs (confirmed for cardiac
>   C8-T2 and colitis T10-L1 dermatomes)
> - Sato 1997: somatovisceral reflexes demonstrated in spinal
>   preparations (no brain required for segmental organ modulation)
>
> **Shared anatomy:** All Back-Shu points are 1.5 cun (~3-4 cm) lateral
> to the lower border of the spinous process. They lie in paravertebral
> musculature (multifidus, longissimus, iliocostalis), innervated by the
> medial branch of the dorsal ramus of the corresponding spinal nerve.
> The thoracolumbar fascia provides a common fascial convergence plane
> (A03). PROKR2 mechanism (A02) is potentially applicable — fascial
> plane + dense innervation + connective tissue convergence.
>
> **Shared mechanism:** Somatovisceral reflex (A01 §3.4, A07 §1.6).
> Skin/muscle stimulus at segment X → dorsal ramus afferents → dorsal
> horn → convergent WDR neurons (lamina V) → preganglionic sympathetic
> neurons → organ in segment X. Preserved in spinal-level preparations
> (Sato 1997). For Back-Shu, segmental contribution ≈ 100%.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.4, A07 S1.6, S4.2, S4.4, B01 S3.1, C04 S3.2

---

### BL13 — Phế Du (肺俞, Fèi Shū)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis) — Back-Shu of Lung**

Name 肺俞 (Fèi Shū) = "Lung Transporting Point." The most direct
posterior access to the lung's segmental innervation.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of T3.

Needle depth range: 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.2-2.0 cm) oblique insertion toward
the spine. NEVER perpendicular — pneumothorax risk (pleura ~4.3 cm
beneath skin at this level).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T3 spinal nerve — primary sensory
  and motor innervation of paravertebral region at this level
- Spinal segment: T3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (posterior layer). At T3 level, the
needle passes through the fascial septum between trapezius and erector
spinae (longissimus dorsi). Significant connective tissue convergence —
PROKR2 mechanism potentially applicable (A02, A03).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 3rd posterior intercostal artery (from
thoracic aorta). Small caliber paravertebral vessels.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue →
trapezius → rhomboid minor → erector spinae (longissimus dorsi /
iliocostalis) → 3rd rib / transverse process of T3. Multifidus lies
medial, closer to the spinous process.

Receptor density: no BL13-specific data. Paravertebral muscles are
richly innervated by proprioceptive and nociceptive fibers. Muscle
spindle density in multifidus is among the highest in the body.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No (Back-Shu is a separate category from Five Shu)
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Lung (Feishu) at T3**
Front-Mu: No (LU1 Zhongfu is Front-Mu of Lung)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: commonly combined with LU1 (Front-Mu) in Shu-Mu pairing
  for comprehensive lung treatment (chronic = BL13, acute = LU1)

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL13 (Feishu 肺俞) — Back-Shu of Lung. Traditional
  functions: tonify Lung Qi, nourish Lung Yin, clear Lung Heat,
  release exterior, stop cough and wheezing.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Dorsher mapping: paravertebral
  trigger points in erector spinae / multifidus at T3 level. Referred
  pain pattern: upper back, interscapular region. Upper trapezius
  trigger points also refer to this area.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: commonly tender in patients with chronic
  respiratory conditions. Diagnostic palpation tenderness at BL13
  considered a sign of Lung dysfunction in TCM.

Marma point: **Possible correspondence.** Bruhati Marma (Frawley) is
  located in the upper back / interscapular region (approximately T3-T6
  level). BL13 falls within this zone but is not a precise 1:1 match.

Head zone: T3 dermatome. Lung viscerotome = T3-T5 (A07 §1.6). T3 is
  within the lung's innervation range. **✅ MATCH — the segmental model
  directly predicts BL13's effect on pulmonary function.**
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, S4.2

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line (Myers) runs along the erector
  spinae. BL13 is directly on this line. Deep Front Line also passes
  through the posterior mediastinum at this level.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** Kim 2017 demonstrated neurogenic spots
  at cardiac dermatome (C8-T2). The model predicts similar phenomena at
  T3-T5 during pulmonary disease, though no lung-specific study exists.

Cupping point: **Yes.** BL13 specifically named as a common TCM cupping
  location (B07 §3.2) for respiratory conditions. Cupping at BL13 is
  one of the most traditional cupping applications.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, cupping). Possible Marma correspondence adds to 6-7/8.
Among the HIGHEST convergence scores in Phase 1. Contrasts sharply
with BL67 (2/8) on the same meridian.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51 Bei Shu): listed as Back-Shu of Lung
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): cough, dyspnea, hemoptysis,
  consumption, afternoon fever, night sweats
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic/deficiency conditions of Lung
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): cough, asthma, blood-streaked
  phlegm, nasal congestion, spontaneous sweating

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Cough, wheezing, asthma (primary indication)
- Night sweats, afternoon fever (Yin deficiency heat)
- Upper back pain, stiffness

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- THE primary posterior point for all respiratory conditions
- Combined with LU1 (Shu-Mu pairing) for comprehensive lung treatment
- Combined with BL43 (Gaohuangshu, outer Back-Shu line) for chronic
  respiratory conditions and tuberculosis
- Moxa at BL13 for chronic deficiency; needle for acute excess

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. COPD (respiratory function) — 🟡 Plausible**

BL13 (Feishu) is the most frequently selected acupoint for COPD in
clinical trials, along with BL23 and EX-B1 (Dingchuan). Overview of
19 systematic reviews/meta-analyses (AMSTAR 2: critically low quality):
acupuncture benefits lung function (FEV1, FVC), acute exacerbation
frequency, 6-minute walk distance, mMRC dyspnea, and CAT scores. 🟡
because evidence quality is limited — most individual SRs are critically
low by AMSTAR 2, and methodological heterogeneity is high.
(Multiple SRs, see Efficacy and Safety overview 2024)

**2. Asthma — 🟡 Plausible**

BL13 among most-used acupoints for asthma. Animal model evidence:
acupuncture at BL13 improved pulmonary function and tissue inflammation
in asthma model rats (reduced inflammatory cell infiltration). fMRI
studies examined brain activity changes with BL13 stimulation in asthma
patients. 🟡 — limited human evidence, mechanism via segmental reflex
plausible.

**3. Upper back pain / interscapular stiffness — 🟡 Plausible**

Local musculoskeletal effect via trigger point / gate control mechanism.
Not point-specific evidence but anatomically straightforward (local
needling of paravertebral muscles).

**4. Night sweats / tuberculosis — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Classical indication. No modern evidence specific to BL13 for these
conditions. Historical significance (tuberculosis was a major
traditional application).

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: BL13 commonly shows palpation tenderness
in patients with chronic lung conditions (asthma, COPD). This is
consistent with the Kim 2017 neurogenic inflammation model — organ
disease → antidromic C-fiber activation in same dermatome → local
inflammatory changes → altered sensitivity. No formal study quantifying
BL13 threshold changes in lung disease.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — TEXTBOOK segmental pathway.**

T3 dorsal ramus afferents → T3 dorsal horn → convergent WDR neurons
→ T3 preganglionic sympathetic neurons → sympathetic chain → pulmonary
plexus → bronchomotor tone, bronchial vasculature, mucus secretion.

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL13 at T3 → dorsal horn T3 → sympathetic T3 → lungs (T3-T5)
  ✅ MATCH — T3 is within lung viscerotome
  Segmental contribution: ~100%
```

Additional pathways:
- Gate control (A01 §3.1): Aβ from needle grasp → local pain modulation
  in upper back / interscapular region
- Fascial signaling (A03): thoracolumbar fascia mechanotransduction
- Axon reflex (A01 §3.3): local neurogenic inflammation → CGRP/SP
  → vasodilation, immune modulation in paravertebral region
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.1-S3.4, A07 S1.6, S4.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.2-2.0 cm) OBLIQUE toward spine.
  NEVER perpendicular. Direction: medial-inferior at 45-60° angle.
  De Qi: distending, heavy sensation in paravertebral muscles.
  EA: 2 Hz for chronic (tonification), higher frequency for acute
  bronchospasm. Commonly paired with LU1 or EX-B1 (Dingchuan).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Bấm huyệt (B02):
  Sustained pressure on paravertebral muscles at T3. Less studied
  than needle for respiratory effects. PROKR2 depth: fascial plane
  accessible at moderate pressure.
→ Cross-ref: B02 S8

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL13: traditional for chronic Lung deficiency (chronic cough,
  chronic asthma, tuberculosis recovery). Indirect moxa with moxa stick
  or ginger slice. CV4 + BL13 + ST36 = classical tonification protocol.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Giác hơi (B07):
  Cupping at BL13: one of the most traditional cupping applications for
  respiratory conditions. Dry cupping or sliding cupping along Bladder
  channel at upper back. Creates localized hyperemia and neurogenic
  inflammation in T3 dermatome.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

Dry needling (B11):
  Trigger point in erector spinae / multifidus at T3. Local twitch
  response possible in longissimus. Primary target: musculoskeletal
  upper back pain, not organ-specific effect.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES — thoracic Back-Shu risk.** Pleura lies
  approximately 4.3 cm (~1.4 cun) beneath the skin at T3. Oblique
  angle toward spine is critical. Safe depth limit: 0.5-0.8 cun
  oblique. Increased risk in thin patients, emphysema/COPD patients
  (hyperinflated lungs closer to chest wall).
  → Pneumothorax is the most reported serious acupuncture adverse event.

Vascular risk: Minimal — paravertebral arteries are small caliber.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication for BL13.

---

### BL14 — Quyết Âm Du (厥阴俞, Jué Yīn Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Pericardium**

Name 厥阴俞 (Jué Yīn Shū) = "Jueyin Transporting Point." Jueyin
(厥阴) is the channel name for Pericardium — "Terminal Yin" or
"Absolute Yin," the deepest Yin level in the six-channel system.
BL14 is the Back-Shu of the Pericardium organ (心包), at T4, 1.5
cun lateral to the spine. The 6th organ Back-Shu documented in the
Atlas (after BL13/Lung, BL15/Heart, BL18/Liver, BL20/Spleen,
BL23/Kidney). BL14 sits between BL13 (Lung, T3) and BL15 (Heart,
T5), creating a dense cardiopulmonary Back-Shu cluster at T3–T5.
The T4 segmental level aligns with the cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5)
— both BL14 and BL15 access cardiac autonomic territory, reflecting
the functional overlap between Pericardium ("Heart Protector") and
Heart in TCM. PC1 (Tianchi) on the anterior Pericardium channel is
ALSO at T4 segmental level (AP-09), creating a T4 anterior-
posterior pair: PC1 (front) ↔ BL14 (back), analogous to the
Front-Mu / Back-Shu pairing concept but connecting two separate
channels at the same spinal level.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T4.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun (~1.2–2.0 cm) oblique insertion
toward the spine. Pneumothorax risk — same precautions as BL13
and BL15.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T4 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: T4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T4, the needle passes
through the fascial septum between trapezius (mid-fibers) and
erector spinae. Same fascial architecture as BL13 (T3) and BL15
(T5) — the T3–T5 cluster shares nearly identical tissue layers.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 4th posterior intercostal artery (from
thoracic aorta). Small caliber paravertebral vessels.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → trapezius (mid-fibers)
→ rhomboid major → erector spinae (longissimus dorsi) → 4th rib /
transverse process of T4.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Pericardium (Jueyinshu) at T4**
Front-Mu: No (CV17 Shanzhong is Front-Mu of Pericardium)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with CV17 for comprehensive Pericardium
  treatment. CV17 is ALSO the Hui-Meeting of Qi — the Pericardium
  Front-Mu carries dual designation (Front-Mu + Hui-Meeting).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL14 (Jueyinshu 厥阴俞) — Back-Shu of Pericardium.
  Traditional functions: regulate Heart Qi, unbind the chest, calm
  the Shen (spirit), harmonize the Stomach. Despite Pericardium
  designation, traditional indications overlap substantially with
  BL15 (Heart Shu) — both address cardiac and chest conditions.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T4 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae / multifidus. Referred pain: interscapular
  region, precordium (chest). Very similar to BL13 (T3) and BL15
  (T5) trigger point patterns — the T3–T5 zone shows nearly
  continuous trigger point territory.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: interscapular tenderness at T4 in cardiac
  patients. The cardiac referred pain pattern extends across the
  left T3–T5 paravertebral region as a gradient rather than a
  single discrete point.

Marma point: **Correspondence.** Bruhati Marma (Frawley) covers
  the interscapular region T3–T6. BL14 at T4 is squarely within
  Bruhati territory — same zone as BL13 and BL15.

Head zone: T4 dermatome. Cardiac viscerotome = T1–T5. T4 is in the
  MIDDLE of the cardiac innervation range — arguably better centered
  than BL15 (T5, lower edge). Pericardium is anatomically part of
  the cardiac complex, sharing innervation via cardiac plexus and
  phrenic nerve. **✅ MATCH — T4 within cardiac viscerotome. Both
  BL14 (T4) and BL15 (T5) appropriately access cardiac territory.**
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, S4.2

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line along erector spinae at T4.

Neurogenic spot: Kim 2017 cardiac neurogenic spots concentrated at
  C8–T2. T4 is 2 segments below the primary concentration zone but
  within the full cardiac autonomic range (T1–T5). **🟡 Partial** —
  within cardiac territory but below the highest-density C8–T2
  neurogenic spot zone.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Paravertebral cupping at T4 practiced
  as part of upper-to-mid back protocols. Not a primary named
  cupping site.

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Marma, Head
zone, Anatomy Trains). Neurogenic spot 🟡 and cupping 🟡 → possible
7–8/8. Comparable to BL13 (6/8), above BL15 (5/8).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): heart pain, chest
  oppression, vomiting, cough
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE): palpitation, chest pain,
  sighing, stuffy chest, cough with counterflow Qi, vomiting

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Heart pain, palpitation, chest oppression
- Cough, vomiting, nausea
- Upset stomach (via Jueyin's stomach-calming function)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with BL15 (Heart Shu) for comprehensive cardiac Back-Shu
  treatment: BL14 (T4) + BL15 (T5) = adjacent Back-Shu pair
  covering the cardiac viscerotome from two segmental levels
- Combined with CV17 (Front-Mu of PC) for Shu-Mu pairing
- Combined with PC6 (Neiguan) for cardiac conditions: PC6 provides
  vagal-NTS pathway, BL14 provides segmental Back-Shu pathway —
  dual-mechanism approach similar to BL15+PC6

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Cardiac conditions (palpitation, chest pain) — 🟡 Plausible**

BL14 included in multi-point cardiac acupuncture protocols alongside
BL15 and PC6. Not studied as isolated point. Mechanism via T4
somatovisceral reflex to cardiac autonomic territory is anatomically
well-supported. T4 is within cardiac innervation range (T1–T5),
confirming Dorsher matching. 🟡 — mechanistically plausible
(segmental cardiac), no isolated evidence.

**Dorsher matching: YES.** Pericardium viscerotome corresponds to
cardiac innervation (T1–T5). BL14 at T4 is within this range.
6/6 Zang organ Back-Shu documented so far (BL13/BL14/BL15/BL18/
BL20/BL23) ALL match their organ viscerotome. The 3 non-matching
(BL21/BL25/BL27) are all Fu organ Shu — pattern holds.

**2. Chest oppression / thoracic discomfort — 🟡 Plausible**

Local T4 musculoskeletal effect combined with cardiac autonomic
modulation. Part of thoracic Back-Shu protocols.

**3. Nausea / vomiting — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication attributed to Jueyin channel's stomach-
calming function. No specific evidence for BL14 for nausea
(contrast PC6 with Cochrane-level PONV evidence via NTS-vagal).
BL14's T4 segment does not directly access gastric innervation
(T6–T10).

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: BL14 at T4 is expected to show
tenderness in cardiac patients, similar to BL15 at T5. The cardiac
viscerosomatic reflex creates a paravertebral sensitization gradient
across T1–T5, not a single discrete point. BL14 and BL15 likely
show overlapping sensitization. No BL14-specific quantitative data.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, A07 S4.3

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — segmental cardiac pathway.**

T4 dorsal ramus afferents → T4 dorsal horn → convergent WDR neurons
→ T4 preganglionic sympathetic neurons → sympathetic chain →
cardiac plexus → heart rate, contractility, coronary tone.

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL14 at T4 → dorsal horn T4 → sympathetic T4 → heart (T1–T5)
  ✅ MATCH — T4 is within cardiac viscerotome (middle of range)
  Segmental contribution: ~100%
```

BL14 vs BL15: both access the cardiac viscerotome at different
segmental levels (T4 vs T5). The clinical distinction between
Pericardium-Shu and Heart-Shu does NOT map to distinct anatomical
targets — both converge on the same cardiac plexus. From BSA
perspective, the T4–T5 pair represents REDUNDANT segmental access
to the heart, potentially increasing Back-Shu treatment robustness
(2 adjacent segments > 1 for somatovisceral cardiac reflex).

The PC channel's cardiac effects operate through TWO distinct
pathways depending on point location:
- PC1 (T4, anterior chest): T4 segmental → same as BL14
- PC3–PC9 (forearm/wrist): median nerve → NTS → vagal (supraspinal)
BL14 and PC1 share the SAME T4 segmental mechanism despite being on
different channels (BL vs PC) — convergent anatomy from opposite
body surfaces.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.4, A07 S1.6, AP-09 PC1

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Same safety
  protocol as BL13 and BL15. De Qi: distending sensation in
  interscapular region. EA: can pair with BL15 for bilateral
  cardiac Back-Shu stimulation at T4+T5.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL14: for Pericardium/Heart deficiency (cold chest,
  chronic palpitation with cold sensation). Similar protocol to
  BL15 moxa.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T4 thoracic level. Same precautions as
  BL13 and BL15: oblique insertion toward spine, max 0.5–0.8 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal at standard depth.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL15 — Tâm Du (心俞, Xīn Shū)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis) — Back-Shu of Heart**

Name 心俞 (Xīn Shū) = "Heart Transporting Point." Posterior segmental
access to cardiac autonomic innervation.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of T5.

Needle depth range: 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.2-2.0 cm) oblique insertion toward
the spine. Pneumothorax risk — same precautions as BL13.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T5 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: T5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T5 level, needle passes through
fascial septum between trapezius (lower fibers) and erector spinae.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 5th posterior intercostal artery.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue →
trapezius (lower fibers) / rhomboid major → erector spinae (longissimus
dorsi) → 5th rib / transverse process of T5.

Receptor density: no BL15-specific data. Muscle tone at BL15 reported
as highest among 5 Zang Back-Shu points (clinical measurement studies).

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Heart (Xinshu) at T5**
Front-Mu: No (CV14 Juque is Front-Mu of Heart)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with CV14 for comprehensive heart treatment.
  Combined with BL44 (Shentang, outer Back-Shu line) for Shen
  (spirit/mind) disturbances.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL15 (Xinshu 心俞) — Back-Shu of Heart. Traditional
  functions: regulate Heart Qi, nourish Heart Blood, calm the Shen,
  open chest, clear Heart Fire.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Paravertebral trigger points in
  erector spinae / multifidus at T5 level. Interscapular trigger
  points commonly refer pain to the precordium (chest), creating a
  viscerosomatic / somatovisceral feedback loop.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: cardiac-related back tenderness. Patients with
  heart disease commonly report tenderness at left T3-T5 paravertebral
  region (referred pain pattern consistent with Head zone mapping).

Marma point: **Possible correspondence.** Bruhati Marma covers the
  interscapular region approximately T3-T6. BL15 falls within this zone.

Head zone: T5 dermatome. Heart viscerotome = T1-T5 (A07 §1.6). T5 is
  at the LOWER edge of the cardiac innervation range. Kim 2017 found
  neurogenic spots concentrated at C8-T2 (upper range) in hypertensive
  rats — 67% matching acupoint locations including PC6, HT7. BL15 at T5
  is BELOW Kim's primary cardiac spot concentration but within the full
  T1-T5 cardiac range. **✅ MATCH — segmental model predicts cardiac
  effects, though primary cardiac sensory convergence is at T1-T4.**
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, S4.2, S4.3

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line.

Neurogenic spot: **Partially overlapping.** Kim 2017 cardiac spots at
  C8-T2. BL15 at T5 is 3 segments below the primary concentration.
  The cardiac innervation extends to T5, so BL15 is at the periphery
  of the predicted neurogenic spot zone.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Not specifically named in B07 §3.2 list
  (BL13, BL17, BL20, BL23, BL25 named), but paravertebral cupping at
  T5 is clinically practiced.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Possible Marma and cupping add to 5-7/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Heart
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: heart palpitation, chest pain, anxiety, poor
  memory, epilepsy, mania, vomiting blood
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic Heart conditions
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: insomnia, night sweats, dream-disturbed sleep,
  absent-mindedness, chest oppression

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Heart palpitation, chest pain (cardiac)
- Anxiety, insomnia, poor memory (Shen disturbance)
- Epilepsy, mania (classical neuropsychiatric indication)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- The PRIMARY posterior point for all cardiac conditions in TCM
- Shu-Mu pairing with CV14 (Juque) for comprehensive heart treatment
- Combined with PC6 (Inner Pass) for palpitation / chest pain —
  PC6 provides vagal-NTS pathway, BL15 provides segmental Back-Shu
  pathway = DUAL mechanism approach
- Combined with HT7 (Shenmen) for insomnia/anxiety — shared Shen-
  calming function via different pathways

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Arrhythmia / cardiac function — 🟡 Plausible**

EA at BL15 improved arrhythmia-induced cardiomyopathy in animal models:
restored disordered conduction, calcium handling, reduced ventricular
dilation, decreased atrial fibrillation onset. EA at BL15 modulated PVN
(paraventricular nucleus) neuron activity, preventing AF-induced
circulation dysfunction. Clinical: acupuncture (including BL15 in point
prescription) showed 35% AF recurrence at 12 months vs 69% sham
(comparable to 27% amiodarone). 🟡 — promising preclinical, limited
clinical; mechanism via T5 sympathetic modulation of cardiac function.
(EA BL15 cardiomyopathy study 2025; PVN-AF study 2023)

**Important caveat:** One comparative study found BL15 stimulation
improved systolic function but INCREASED arrhythmia risk during acute
myocardial ischemia. Context-dependent: BL15 may be beneficial for
chronic arrhythmia but potentially hazardous during acute ischemia.

**2. Depression / bipolar disorder — 🟡 Plausible**

BL15 included as part of 5-Zang Back-Shu protocol (BL13+BL15+BL17+BL18+
BL20+BL23) for bipolar depression (RCT protocol). BL15's role is
specific to the Heart-Shen (spirit/mind) system. Not isolated as single
point in depression studies.

**3. Insomnia / anxiety — 🟡 Plausible**

Part of multi-point protocols combining BL15 with HT7 and PC6. No
BL15-specific isolated evidence for insomnia. Mechanism: T5 sympathetic
modulation may reduce cardiac sympathetic hyperactivity associated with
anxiety-related palpitation/insomnia.

**4. Chest pain — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication. Modern cardiology has largely supplanted
acupuncture for acute chest pain. No specific evidence for BL15 as
sole intervention for angina.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: Kim 2017 demonstrated neurogenic spots at
C8-T2 dermatome in hypertensive rats, 67% matching cardiac acupoints.
BL15 at T5 is at the lower edge of the cardiac innervation range but
tenderness at BL15 is clinically reported in cardiac patients (left-
sided interscapular pain is a recognized cardiac referred pain pattern).
Muscle tone at BL15 is the highest among the 5 Zang Back-Shu points
(clinical measurement), possibly reflecting cardiac-related
paravertebral sensitization.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, A07 S4.3

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — segmental cardiac pathway.**

T5 dorsal ramus afferents → T5 dorsal horn → convergent WDR neurons
→ T5 preganglionic sympathetic neurons → sympathetic chain → cardiac
plexus → heart rate, contractility, coronary tone.

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL15 at T5 → dorsal horn T5 → sympathetic T5 → heart (T1-T5)
  ✅ MATCH — T5 is at lower edge of cardiac viscerotome
  Segmental contribution: ~100%
```

The BL15 + PC6 combination represents a DUAL-PATHWAY cardiac approach:
- BL15: segmental T5 → sympathetic → cardiac (somatovisceral reflex)
- PC6: median nerve → NTS → vagal → cardiac (vagal-brainstem pathway)
Two independent mechanisms converging on the same target organ.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.4, A07 S1.6

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5-0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Same safety protocol
  as BL13. De Qi: distending sensation in interscapular region.
  EA: studied at BL15 for arrhythmia. Frequency-specific cardiac
  effects warrant further investigation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL15: traditional for Heart Yang / Heart Qi deficiency
  (chronic palpitation with cold extremities, fatigue).
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Dry needling (B11):
  Trigger point therapy at T5 erector spinae. For interscapular pain,
  not cardiac effects per se.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — same thoracic precautions as BL13. Oblique
  insertion toward spine, max depth 0.5-0.8 cun.

Cardiac context: EA at BL15 may INCREASE arrhythmia risk during ACUTE
  myocardial ischemia. Use with caution in acute cardiac settings.
  Appropriate for chronic conditions with stable hemodynamics.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL16 — Đốc Du (督俞, Dū Shū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Governor Vessel Shu" (督 = governor [vessel], 俞 = transporting
point) — at the mid-thoracic back, 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T6. Despite the name referencing
the Governing Vessel (Du Mai), BL16 is NOT a GV meeting point
(unlike BL11 and BL12 which are actual BL–GV crossing points) and
NOT an organ Back-Shu (no Zang-Fu organ is assigned to T6). The
name may reflect a classical positional association with the GV
midline rather than a functional channel crossing. BL16 sits in the
T6–T8 diaphragm transition zone — between the cardiopulmonary
cluster (T3–T5) and the hepatobiliary cluster (T9–T10).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T6.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Pneumothorax
risk persists at T6 (lower thorax, still within rib cage).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T6 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: T6
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T6, the superficial muscle
layer transitions from trapezius (above) to latissimus dorsi
(below) — BL16 is at this fascial overlap zone.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 6th posterior intercostal artery.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → trapezius (lower fibers)
or latissimus dorsi (upper fibers) → erector spinae → 6th rib /
transverse process of T6.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu despite being named "Shu."
The 督 in the name does not confer GV meeting point status.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL16 (Dushu 督俞). Traditional functions: regulate
  Heart Qi, strengthen the back, harmonize the diaphragm. Minor
  point rarely used as a primary.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T6 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae. Mid-thoracic TrPs with interscapular referred
  pain.

Ashi correspondence: mid-thoracic tenderness — nonspecific, may
  relate to postural strain or upper abdominal visceral referral.

Marma point: **Possible.** T6 is at the lower border of Bruhati
  Marma territory (approximately T3–T6).

Head zone: T6 → peripheral diaphragm innervation, at the lower edge
  of cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5) and upper edge of gastric
  viscerotome (T6–T10). Transition zone. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data at T6.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable, not a primary site.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains,
cupping). Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡 → possible 5–6/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: cardiac pain, abdominal pain, borborygmus
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: chest/diaphragm fullness, back stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Cardiac pain, abdominal pain
- Borborygmus (intestinal sounds)
- Back pain (local)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL16 has no individual evidence
base. A minor point rarely included in clinical protocols as primary
selection. Local musculoskeletal effect is the only plausible modern
application.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T6, within rib cage. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.5–0.8 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL17 — Cách Du (膈俞, Gé Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Diaphragm Shu" (膈 = diaphragm, 俞 = transporting point) — at the
mid-thoracic back, 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border of the
spinous process of T7. The **Hui-Meeting of Blood (血会)** — one of
8 tissue Hui-Meeting points (Nanjing Ch.45), governing ALL blood
disorders. This is the 2nd Hui-Meeting on the BL channel (after
BL11 Hui-Meeting of Bones), confirming BL as the only meridian
hosting 2/8 Hui-Meeting. BL17 is NOT an organ Back-Shu: the
diaphragm is not a Zang-Fu organ, so "Diaphragm Shu" describes
anatomical location (T7 = diaphragm level), not organ assignment.
T7 marks the anatomical boundary between thoracic organs above
(heart, lungs) and abdominal organs below (liver, spleen, stomach)
— the diaphragm is the physical separator. In the Back-Shu
sequence, BL17 sits between BL15/Heart (T5) and BL18/Liver (T9),
at the thoraco-abdominal transition. The T8 gap (no named BL point
at T8) extends this transition zone: BL17(T7)→gap(T8)→BL18(T9).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T7.

Anatomical landmarks: T7 is the vertebra at the level of the
inferior angle of the scapula (with arms at sides) — a reliable
surface landmark. BL17 is 1.5 cun lateral to T7 spinous process,
at the diaphragm level.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Pneumothorax
risk at T7 (within thoracic cage, diaphragm approaching).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T7 spinal nerve — primary
  sensory innervation at this level
- The GREATER SPLANCHNIC NERVE (T5–T9) traverses this segmental
  zone. At T7, preganglionic sympathetic fibers exit via the
  sympathetic chain → celiac ganglion → abdominal viscera including
  the SPLEEN (blood reservoir organ). Potentially relevant to
  BL17's "blood governance" claim
- Peripheral diaphragm receives intercostal innervation T7–T12
  (sensory). Central diaphragm = phrenic nerve (C3–C5, motor +
  sensory)
- Spinal segment: T7
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T7, the superficial muscle
layer transitions from trapezius (above T6) to latissimus dorsi
(T7 and below). This fascial transition corresponds to the
diaphragm anatomical level.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 7th posterior intercostal artery. Small
caliber paravertebral vessels.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → latissimus dorsi
(or its aponeurosis) → erector spinae (longissimus dorsi /
iliocostalis) → 7th rib / transverse process of T7.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu (diaphragm ≠ Zang-Fu
organ). BL17 is a positional "Shu" named for the diaphragm
structure, like BL16 (named after GV).
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.

**Hui-Meeting: YES — Blood (血会)**

One of 8 Hui-Meeting points (八会穴, Nanjing Ch.45). "Blood disorders
go to Geshu" (血病者取膈俞). The 8 Hui-Meeting: Zang=LR13, Fu=CV12,
Qi=CV17, **Blood=BL17**, Sinew=GB34, Vessel=LU9, Bone=BL11,
Marrow=GB39. BL channel hosts 2/8 (BL11 Bone + BL17 Blood).

The Hui-Meeting of Blood is arguably the MOST clinically utilized
Hui-Meeting designation after LR13 (Zang) — BL17 appears in
virtually every TCM protocol for anemia, bleeding, and blood stasis.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL17 (Geshu 膈俞) — Hui-Meeting of Blood. Traditional
  functions: nourish Blood (补血), cool Blood (凉血), invigorate
  Blood (活血), harmonize the diaphragm, descend rebellious Qi.
  The triple blood function (nourish, cool, invigorate) makes BL17
  the universal blood point — applicable to deficiency, Heat, and
  stasis equally.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T7 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae at the inferior scapular angle level. Referred
  pain: interscapular and lower thoracic back.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: mid-back tenderness at T7 in patients with
  diaphragm-related conditions (persistent hiccup, reflux) and
  generalized visceral discomfort. Less consistently tender than
  organ-specific Back-Shu points.

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** T7 is below Bruhati
  (T3–T6) and above lower back Marma points. No standard Marma at
  T7 in Frawley's system.

Head zone: T7 dermatome → diaphragm peripheral innervation
  (intercostal T7–T12). T7 is also the upper boundary of the
  gastric/hepatic viscerotome (T6–T10). **🟡 Partial** — segmental
  match for peripheral diaphragm only (central diaphragm = phrenic
  C3–C5) and for upper abdominal viscera.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae. T7 is at the transition
  from upper (trapezius-influenced) to lower (latissimus-influenced)
  posterior trunk.

Neurogenic spot: no data specific to T7 for blood disorders. Kim
  2017 cardiac range (C8–T2) does not extend to T7. The "blood
  governance" claim has no neurogenic-spot analogue — neurogenic
  spots are organ-specific, not tissue-type-specific.

Cupping point: **Named.** BL17 specifically listed in B07 §3.2 as
  a common cupping location. Bloodletting + cupping (刺络拔罐) at
  BL17 is practiced for Blood-Heat patterns.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains, cupping). Head zone 🟡 → possible 6/8. Marma ✗.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Nanjing Ch.45: "血会膈俞" — blood disorders go to Geshu
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): vomiting blood,
  hemoptysis, hiccup, poor appetite, borborygmus, night sweats,
  bone-steaming fever (tuberculosis)
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE): all blood disorders,
  vomiting, hiccup, diaphragm fullness, asthma

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Blood disorders: anemia, bleeding (hematemesis, hemoptysis),
  blood stasis (primary Hui-Meeting indication)
- Vomiting, hiccup (diaphragm function)
- Skin rashes, urticaria (Blood-Heat)
- Night sweats (Yin deficiency)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with SP10 (Xuehai, "Sea of Blood") for comprehensive
  blood treatment — the 2 most important blood points in TCM
- Combined with BL20 (Spleen Shu) for blood production deficiency
  (Spleen governs Blood production in TCM)
- ST36 + BL20 + BL17 = classical Qi-Blood tonification formula
  for chronic anemia
- Wind-Bi formula: GB30 + GB31 + BL17 + SP10 for chronic
  arthralgia (documented in AP-11 GB30)
- Bloodletting at BL17 (superficial pricking + cupping) for
  Blood-Heat patterns — acute bleeding, skin eruptions

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Anemia / blood production — 🟡 Plausible**

BL17 included in acupuncture protocols for anemia and
chemotherapy-induced myelosuppression. Part of multi-point
protocols (BL17+BL20+ST36) for blood cell production support.
Animal data: EA at the BL17 zone improved hematopoietic parameters
in anemic models. Mechanism may involve: T7 sympathetic → greater
splanchnic nerve (T5–T9) → celiac ganglion → splenic vascular
modulation → splenic blood reservoir function. 🟡 — plausible
pathway, limited clinical evidence as isolated point.

**2. Hiccup / diaphragm dysfunction — 🟡 Plausible**

BL17 "Diaphragm Shu" at T7 = diaphragm level. Acupuncture for
persistent hiccup has moderate evidence from systematic reviews
(BL17 among most-selected points along with PC6 and ST36). The
segmental mechanism is plausible: T7 intercostal → peripheral
diaphragm. However, CENTRAL diaphragm motor innervation = phrenic
(C3–C5), not T7 intercostal. 🟡 — segmental pathway exists but
targets the peripheral component.

**3. Blood stasis disorders — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional Hui-Meeting indication. No modern evidence linking BL17
needling to blood rheology parameters (viscosity, platelet
aggregation, fibrinolysis). The "invigorate Blood" claim lacks a
specific modern mechanism at T7.

**4. Vomiting / gastric disorders — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication. No BL17-specific evidence for nausea
(contrast PC6 with Cochrane PONV evidence).

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: BL17 area may show increased tenderness
in diaphragmatic dysfunction (persistent hiccup, GERD). The T7
paravertebral region receives viscerosomatic afferents from both
diaphragm and upper abdominal organs, potentially sensitizing the
area in multiple disease states. No formal quantitative study.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex at T7 (diaphragm indications) +
splenic pathway for blood governance (hypothesis).**

For diaphragm indications:
T7 dorsal ramus afferents → T7 dorsal horn → intercostal nerve T7
→ peripheral diaphragm sensory modulation.

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL17 at T7 → dorsal horn T7 → T7 intercostal → peripheral
  diaphragm
  🟡 PARTIAL MATCH — peripheral diaphragm only
  (central diaphragm motor = C3-C5 phrenic)
  Segmental contribution for diaphragm: ~30-50%
```

For blood governance (Hui-Meeting claim):
T7 somatovisceral reflex → greater splanchnic nerve (T5–T9) →
celiac ganglion → splenic artery → spleen (blood reservoir and
recycling organ). This provides a PLAUSIBLE segmental link between
T7 and the blood system — unlike BL11 (Bone Hui-Meeting) which has
NO segmental pathway to the skeletal system. The 2 BL Hui-Meeting
points illustrate a dichotomy: BL17 (Blood) = potentially
segmentally explicable via splenic pathway; BL11 (Bone) = no
segmental mechanism identified. However, this pathway remains 🔴
Hypothesis — no study has demonstrated BL17 specifically modulates
splenic vascular tone or hematopoietic function.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S5.1, C04 S3.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. De Qi: distending
  at mid-back. EA: used in hematological protocols, combined with
  BL20 bilaterally for blood tonification.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL17: for Blood deficiency (chronic anemia, pallor).
  Combined with ST36+BL20 moxa for Qi-Blood tonification.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Giác hơi (B07):
  Cupping at BL17: named in B07 cupping protocols. Bloodletting +
  cupping (刺络拔罐) at BL17 for acute Blood-Heat — pricking then
  vacuum extraction induces local bleeding.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T7, lower thorax. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.5–0.8 cun. Risk similar to BL18.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL18 — Can Du (肝俞, Gān Shū)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis) — Back-Shu of Liver**

Name 肝俞 (Gān Shū) = "Liver Transporting Point." Segmental access to
hepatic autonomic innervation at T9.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of T9.

Needle depth range: 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.2-2.0 cm) oblique insertion.
Pneumothorax risk persists at this level (lower thorax).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T9 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: T9
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T9, needle passes through
latissimus dorsi (or its aponeurosis) → erector spinae.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 9th posterior intercostal artery.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue →
latissimus dorsi (aponeurosis at this level) → erector spinae
(longissimus dorsi / iliocostalis) → 9th rib / transverse process T9.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Liver (Ganshu) at T9**
Front-Mu: No (LR14 Qimen is Front-Mu of Liver)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with LR14 for comprehensive liver treatment.
  Combined with BL19 (Danshu, Back-Shu of Gallbladder at T10) for
  hepatobiliary conditions — adjacent Back-Shu points reflecting the
  intimate Liver-Gallbladder relationship in both TCM and anatomy.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL18 (Ganshu 肝俞) — Back-Shu of Liver. Traditional
  functions: spread Liver Qi, nourish Liver Blood, benefit the eyes,
  extinguish Liver Wind.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Erector spinae trigger points at T9.
  Right-sided T9 paravertebral tenderness is recognized in conventional
  medicine as hepatobiliary referred pain pattern.

Ashi correspondence: hepatobiliary disease commonly produces right-
  sided upper back tenderness at T7-T9 level. Right BL18 may be more
  clinically significant than left BL18 for liver conditions (laterality
  reflects anatomical liver position).

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** No standard Marma point at
  T9 paravertebral location in Frawley's 107-point system.

Head zone: T9 dermatome. Liver viscerotome = T7-T9 (A07 §1.6). T9 is
  at the lower edge of the hepatic range. **✅ MATCH.**
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** Model extrapolation from Kim 2017. No
  liver-specific study.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Not in B07 §3.2 named list but
  paravertebral cupping at mid-thoracic level is practiced.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Liver
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: hypochondriac pain, jaundice, bitter taste,
  dizziness, eye disorders, epistaxis
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: convulsions, opisthotonos, blurred vision,
  lateral costal pain, sighing

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Hypochondriac pain, jaundice, hepatitis
- Eye disorders (Liver opens into eyes — TCM theory)
- Dizziness, convulsions (Liver Wind)
- Depression, irritability (Liver Qi stagnation)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- Combined with BL20 (Pishu) for Liver-Spleen disharmony — one of
  the most common TCM pattern combinations
- Combined with LR3 (Taichong, Yuan-Source of Liver) for Liver
  Qi stagnation — Back-Shu + distal Yuan-Source = comprehensive
- Eye disorder indications relate to TCM "Liver opens into the eyes"
  doctrine — contrast with BL67's retracted eye claim (Cho 1998)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Gastrointestinal function — 🟡 Plausible**

BL18 included in PRISMA-compliant systematic review of Back-Shu points
for GI organs (115 articles). Acupuncture at Back-Shu (BL18, BL20,
BL22) effective for GI diseases through spinal nerve compression,
sympathetic nerve regulation, and diaphragmatic connection. BL18 among
top 10 most-selected points for diabetic gastroparesis.

**2. Depression (Liver Qi stagnation pattern) — 🟡 Plausible**

BL18 used as supplementary point in liver-soothing acupuncture for
subthreshold depression. Part of multi-point protocols, not isolated.
TCM rationale: Liver governs emotional flow (Qi circulation). Modern
interpretation: T9 sympathetic modulation may affect hepatic metabolism
and related neuroendocrine pathways.

**3. Hepatitis / liver function — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication supported by segmental logic (T9 → liver) but
no quality systematic review of BL18 specifically for hepatic disease.

**4. Eye disorders — 🔴 Unvalidated**

TCM indication based on "Liver opens into the eyes." No modern evidence
linking BL18 to visual function. Unlike BL67 (retracted Cho study),
BL18 eye claims have not been tested with modern methods.

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — segmental hepatic pathway.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL18 at T9 → dorsal horn T9 → sympathetic T9 → hepatic plexus
  (via greater splanchnic nerve T5-T9 → celiac ganglion → liver)
  ✅ MATCH — T9 is within liver viscerotome (T7-T9)
  Segmental contribution: ~100%
```

Hepatic sympathetic effects: modulation of hepatic blood flow, bile
secretion, hepatic glucose metabolism, Kupffer cell activity.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, S4.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 0.5-0.8 cun oblique toward spine. EA commonly used in
  GI protocols combining BL18 with BL20 and BL22.

Cứu (B08): Moxa for Liver Blood / Liver Yin deficiency (chronic).

Dry needling (B11): trigger point at T9 erector spinae for local
  musculoskeletal pain.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — lower thorax. Still within rib cage at T9.
  Oblique insertion, max 0.5-0.8 cun. Risk lower than upper thoracic
  Back-Shu but still present.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL19 — Đảm Du (胆俞, Dǎn Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Gallbladder**

Name 胆俞 (Dǎn Shū) = "Gallbladder Transporting Point." BL19 is
the Back-Shu of the Gallbladder organ, at T10, 1.5 cun lateral to
the spine. The 7th organ Back-Shu documented in the Atlas (after
BL13/Lung, BL14/PC, BL15/Heart, BL18/Liver, BL20/Spleen,
BL23/Kidney). BL19 sits adjacent to BL18 (Liver Shu, T9), creating
a hepatobiliary Back-Shu pair at T9–T10 — mirroring the BL14–BL15
(PC–Heart at T4–T5) cardio-pericardial pair. Liver and Gallbladder
are anatomically and functionally inseparable (bile production →
storage → secretion), and their adjacent Back-Shu placement reflects
this relationship. Shu-Mu pairing: BL19 (T10) + GB24 (Front-Mu of
GB at 7th intercostal space, T7 level, AP-11). The 3-segment gap
(T10 vs T7) is identical to the BL18+LR14 pair (T9 vs T6) — a
consistent hepatobiliary Shu-Mu pattern.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T10.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. Pneumothorax
risk reduced at T10 (lower thorax, diaphragm attachment beginning)
but still present.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T10 spinal nerve
- Part of the LESSER SPLANCHNIC NERVE territory (T10–T11), but T10
  also overlaps with the greater splanchnic (T5–T9) range — the
  T9–T10 zone shares afferent pathways to the celiac ganglion via
  both splanchnic systems
- Spinal segment: T10
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T10, the needle passes
through latissimus dorsi aponeurosis → erector spinae. Same
architecture as BL18 (T9).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 10th posterior intercostal artery.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → latissimus dorsi
(aponeurosis) → erector spinae (longissimus dorsi / iliocostalis)
→ 10th rib / transverse process of T10.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Gallbladder (Danshu) at T10**
Front-Mu: No (GB24 Riyue is Front-Mu of Gallbladder — AP-11)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with GB24 (T10 + T7 = 3-segment gap).
  Combined with BL18 (Liver Shu, T9) for hepatobiliary conditions
  — the most natural Back-Shu pair on the medial line.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL19 (Danshu 胆俞) — Back-Shu of Gallbladder.
  Traditional functions: clear Damp-Heat from Liver and Gallbladder,
  benefit the Gallbladder, harmonize Shaoyang. Combined with GB34
  (Yanglingquan, He-Sea + Hui-Meeting of Sinew) for comprehensive
  hepatobiliary treatment.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T10 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae. Right-sided T10 tenderness recognized in
  biliary referred pain (cholecystitis). Continuous with BL18 (T9)
  hepatobiliary trigger point zone.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: right-sided T10 paravertebral tenderness in
  cholecystitis and biliary colic. Posterior equivalent of Murphy's
  sign — right BL19 tenderness in gallbladder disease. Laterality
  significant: right BL19 > left for biliary conditions (anatomical
  GB position = right upper quadrant).

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** No standard Marma at T10
  paravertebral in Frawley's system.

Head zone: T10 dermatome. Gallbladder viscerotome = T6–T10 (via
  greater/lesser splanchnic nerves). T10 is at the LOWER EDGE of GB
  innervation range. **✅ MATCH — Dorsher matching confirmed.**
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** Kim 2017 model predicts neurogenic
  spots at T6–T10 during biliary disease. Right-sided T9–T10
  tenderness in cholecystitis is consistent with viscerosomatic
  neurogenic sensitization. No gallbladder-specific formal study.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Paravertebral cupping at T10
  practiced in lower thoracic protocols. Not a primary named site.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Neurogenic spot 🟡 predicted → possible 6/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Gallbladder
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: jaundice, bitter taste, lateral costal pain,
  vomiting yellow-green bile
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic Gallbladder conditions
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: jaundice, hypochondriac pain, insomnia
  (Gallbladder governs decision/courage)

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Jaundice, bitter taste, hepatobiliary disorders
- Costal/hypochondriac pain
- Vomiting, poor appetite
- Insomnia, frequent sighing (Shaoyang/GB spirit function)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with BL18 (Liver Shu, T9) for hepatobiliary conditions
  — the Back-Shu pair reflecting TCM Liver-Gallbladder partnership
- Combined with GB24 (Front-Mu) for Shu-Mu GB treatment
- Combined with GB34 (Yanglingquan) for comprehensive biliary:
  GB34 = distal He-Sea + Hui-Meeting Sinew, BL19 = proximal
  Back-Shu — dual access (distal + proximal)
- Insomnia indication: GB's TCM role in courage/decision-making —
  "gallbladder deficiency" (胆虚) = timidity, insomnia

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Hepatobiliary / GI function — 🟡 Plausible**

BL19 included in PRISMA-compliant systematic review of Back-Shu
for GI organs (same review cited in BL18). Part of hepatobiliary
protocols. T10 somatovisceral reflex to biliary system via greater/
lesser splanchnic nerves is anatomically well-supported. Dorsher
matching confirmed (T10 within GB viscerotome T6–T10). 🟡 —
segmental mechanism plausible, no BL19-specific isolated evidence.

**Dorsher matching: YES.** GB viscerotome = T6–T10. BL19 at T10 is
within range. 7/7 organ Back-Shu documented so far ALL match
(BL13/BL14/BL15/BL18/BL19/BL20/BL23). The 3 non-matching
(BL21/BL25/BL27) are all Fu organ Shu — pattern holds.

**2. Jaundice / cholecystitis — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication with strong segmental logic. No modern trial
testing BL19 specifically for biliary disease.

**3. Insomnia / emotional — 🔴 Unvalidated**

TCM indication based on GB's decision/courage function. No evidence
linking T10 paravertebral stimulation to sleep regulation.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: right-sided BL19 (T10) tenderness in
cholecystitis is well-documented clinically. Consistent with
gallbladder viscerosomatic reflex: biliary inflammation → splanchnic
afferents → T9–T10 dorsal horn → increased paravertebral muscle
tone and cutaneous sensitivity. Right > left laterality reflects
anatomical GB position.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, A07 S4.3

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — segmental biliary pathway.**

T10 dorsal ramus afferents → T10 dorsal horn → convergent WDR
neurons → T10 preganglionic sympathetic → lesser splanchnic nerve
→ aorticorenal ganglion → biliary system. T10 also overlaps with
greater splanchnic (T5–T9 extending to T10) → celiac ganglion →
hepatic/biliary plexus.

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL19 at T10 → dorsal horn T10 → sympathetic T10 → biliary system
  ✅ MATCH — T10 at lower edge of GB viscerotome (T6-T10)
  Segmental contribution: ~100%
```

BL18–BL19 hepatobiliary pair: both access overlapping visceral
territory via splanchnic nerves. T9 (Liver) and T10 (GB) are
adjacent segments within the greater splanchnic territory overlap.
The clinical distinction between "Liver Shu" and "GB Shu" maps to
slightly different segmental weights within a continuous innervation
zone — same gradient pattern as BL14–BL15 (PC–Heart at T4–T5).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.4, A07 S1.6

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. De Qi: distending
  in lower interscapular region. EA: paired with BL18 bilaterally
  for hepatobiliary protocols. Combined with GB34 distally.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL19: for Gallbladder deficiency (timidity, insomnia,
  chronic indigestion). Less commonly moxaed than BL20 or BL23.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES but reduced** — T10 at lower rib cage.
  Pleural reflection approaches costodiaphragmatic recess. Risk
  lower than upper thoracic but still present. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.5–0.8 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### Batch 4 Observations

**Observation 10 — BL17 HUI-MEETING OF BLOOD: SPLENIC PATHWAY.**
Unlike BL11 (Hui-Meeting of Bones with NO segmental mechanism for
systemic governance), BL17 (Hui-Meeting of Blood at T7) has a
PLAUSIBLE segmental pathway to the blood system: T7 somatovisceral
reflex → greater splanchnic nerve (T5–T9) → celiac ganglion →
splenic artery → spleen (primary blood reservoir and recycling
organ). T7 is centrally positioned within the greater splanchnic
range. If BL17's "blood governance" is mediated through splenic
vascular modulation, the Hui-Meeting claim gains anatomical support.
