The 2 BL Hui-Meeting points thus illustrate a dichotomy: BL17
(Blood) = potentially segmentally explicable; BL11 (Bone) = no
segmental mechanism identified. This dichotomy may extend to the
entire 8-point Hui-Meeting system — some Hui-Meeting designations
may have segmental basis (BL17 Blood via spleen, GB34 Sinew via
local musculotendinous anatomy) while others represent theoretical
classification without segmental substrate.

**Observation 11 — T6–T8 DIAPHRAGM TRANSITION ZONE.**
BL16 (T6), BL17 (T7), and the T8 gap (no BL point) form a
3-level transition zone between the cardiopulmonary cluster
(T3–T5) and the hepatobiliary cluster (T9–T10). BL17 at T7 =
diaphragm level = anatomical boundary between thoracic and
abdominal organs. T8 is the ONLY thoracic level without a named BL
point between T1 and T12 (plan §6 Q7). This gap may reflect a
genuine transition in visceral innervation density — the T8
dermatomal zone falls between cardiac (T1–T5) and hepatogastric
(T6–T10) viscerotomes, with no dominant organ claiming the segment.

**Observation 12 — BL18–BL19 HEPATOBILIARY ADJACENT PAIR.**
BL18 (Liver Shu, T9) and BL19 (Gallbladder Shu, T10) are adjacent
Back-Shu for anatomically paired organs. This mirrors the BL14–BL15
(PC–Heart at T4–T5) pattern: "protector-protectee" relationships
(PC protects Heart; GB assists Liver via bile) produce adjacent
Back-Shu. Both hepatobiliary Shu-Mu pairs show identical 3-segment
gaps: BL19+GB24 (T10 vs T7) = 3 segments, BL18+LR14 (T9 vs T6) =
3 segments. The consistent gap may reflect a structural principle:
Front-Mu points (anterior) sit 3 segments ABOVE their corresponding
Back-Shu (posterior) in the upper-abdominal organ zone.

### BL20 — Tỳ Du (脾俞, Pí Shū)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis) — Back-Shu of Spleen**

Name 脾俞 (Pí Shū) = "Spleen Transporting Point." T11 paravertebral —
the segmental match here requires nuanced innervation analysis.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of T11.

Needle depth range: 0.5-0.8 cun oblique. T11 is at the lower thorax
— pneumothorax risk decreased (floating ribs territory) but not absent.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T11 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: T11
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (transition zone — posterior layer
becomes more prominent at thoracolumbar junction). Latissimus dorsi
aponeurosis overlies this level.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 11th posterior intercostal artery (subcostal).

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue →
latissimus dorsi (aponeurosis) → erector spinae → 11th rib (floating
rib) / transverse process T11.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Spleen (Pishu) at T11**
Front-Mu: No (LR13 Zhangmen is Front-Mu of Spleen — also Hui-Meeting
  of Zang organs)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with LR13 for comprehensive Spleen treatment.
  BL20 is arguably the single most important point for digestive
  tonification in TCM clinical practice.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL20 (Pishu 脾俞) — Back-Shu of Spleen. Traditional
  functions: tonify Spleen Qi, resolve Dampness, nourish Blood, raise
  Yang (for prolapse), regulate transportation and transformation.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Erector spinae / multifidus trigger
  points at T11. Referred pain to lower back / flank region.

Ashi correspondence: tender with chronic digestive dysfunction. TCM
  diagnostic palpation of BL20 for Spleen deficiency assessment.

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.**

Head zone: T11 dermatome. Spleen viscerotome per A07 §1.6 = T6-T8.
  **T11 is OUTSIDE the primary range (T6-T8).** However, Dorsher counts
  BL20 as a MATCH. Resolution: the spleen receives sympathetic
  innervation via the celiac plexus, which receives preganglionic fibers
  from the greater splanchnic (T5-T9) AND lesser splanchnic (T10-T11)
  nerves. T11 falls within the lesser splanchnic nerve range. The A07
  viscerotome table uses the PRIMARY innervation range (T6-T8 via
  greater splanchnic → celiac ganglion) but does not capture the FULL
  preganglionic contribution including lesser splanchnic.
  **✅ MATCH via lesser splanchnic nerve (T10-T11).** Requires nuanced
  understanding of multi-level sympathetic contributions.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** No spleen-specific study.

Cupping point: **Yes.** BL20 specifically named as common TCM cupping
  location (B07 §3.2) for digestive / Spleen conditions.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone
[via lesser splanchnic], Anatomy Trains). Cupping adds to 6/8.

⚡ **Framework note:** BL20's segmental match depends on including
the lesser splanchnic nerve (T10-T11) contribution to the celiac
plexus. The A07 §1.6 viscerotome table lists Spleen at T6-T8, which
is the PRIMARY innervation range via greater splanchnic nerve only.
This table entry should be annotated to show the EXTENDED range (T6-T11
via both greater and lesser splanchnic nerves) — the current simplified
listing could mislead readers into thinking BL20 is a non-matching
Back-Shu point, when it demonstrably is.
→ Extends: A07 S1.6 (viscerotome table needs annotation)

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Spleen
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: abdominal distension, diarrhea, vomiting, edema,
  poor appetite, fatigue, blood in stool
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: chronic diarrhea, jaundice, weakness of four limbs,
  inability to digest food

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Abdominal distension, diarrhea, dysentery (digestive)
- Edema, poor appetite, fatigue (Spleen Qi deficiency)
- Uterine bleeding, blood in stool (Spleen controls Blood)
- Prolapse of organs (Spleen raises Yang)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- THE central tonification point for digestion in TCM
- Combined with ST36 (He-Sea of Stomach) for Spleen-Stomach
  tonification — the most fundamental digestive point combination
- Combined with BL18 for Liver-Spleen disharmony (emotional stress →
  digestive dysfunction — one of the most common clinical patterns)
- B01 example: BL20 (T11) → sympathetic → Spleen = textbook
  somatovisceral reflex demonstration

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Gastrointestinal disorders — 🟡 Plausible**

PRISMA systematic review (115 articles) on BL18, BL20, BL22: effective
for GI diseases through segmental nerve and sympathetic modulation.
BL20 among top 10 most-selected acupoints for diabetic gastroparesis
(combined with CV12, ST36, PC6, ST25, BL21).

**2. Bipolar depression (5-Zang protocol) — 🟡 Plausible**

BL20 included in 5-Zang Back-Shu protocol (BL13+BL15+BL17+BL18+BL20+
BL23) for bipolar depression. RCT protocol exists; part of multi-point
approach, not isolated.

**3. Chronic fatigue / Spleen Qi deficiency — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Major traditional indication with strong clinical tradition consensus
but no BL20-specific systematic review. BL20 is prescribed in most
fatigue/digestive-deficiency protocols but always as part of
multi-point combinations.

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — segmental via lesser splanchnic.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL20 at T11 → dorsal horn T11 → preganglionic sympathetic T11
  → lesser splanchnic nerve → celiac ganglion → splenic plexus
  → spleen (vasomotor, immune modulation)
  ✅ MATCH via lesser splanchnic (T10-T11)
  Segmental contribution: ~100%
```

B01 explicitly cites BL20 as the example of somatovisceral reflex:
"BL20 (T11) → sympathetic → Spleen = neuroanatomical basis for
Back-Shu → organ effects." Preserved in spinal preparations.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S7 (L3 spinal cord mechanisms)

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 0.5-0.8 cun oblique. EA frequently combined with BL18,
  BL22 for digestive protocols. 2 Hz for tonification (chronic
  deficiency), higher frequency for diarrhea (sympathetic activation).

Cứu (B08): Moxa at BL20 is classical for Spleen Yang deficiency.
  Combined with CV4, ST36, BL23 for comprehensive Yang tonification.

Giác hơi (B07): BL20 cupping specifically indicated. Back cupping
  at T11 region for digestive complaints.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **Lower** than upper thoracic. T11 is at floating
  rib level — pleural reflection is higher (less lung tissue at this
  level). Still requires oblique insertion.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL21 — Vị Du (胃俞, Wèi Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Stomach**

Name 胃俞 (Wèi Shū) = "Stomach Transporting Point." BL21 is the
Back-Shu of the Stomach organ, at T12, 1.5 cun lateral to the
spine. The 8th organ Back-Shu documented in the Atlas (after
BL13/Lung, BL14/PC, BL15/Heart, BL18/Liver, BL19/GB, BL20/Spleen,
BL23/Kidney). BL21 is the FIRST Dorsher non-matching Back-Shu
point: T12 is 3 segments BELOW the Stomach's primary viscerotome
(T6–T9 per A07 §1.6). The other 7 documented organ Shu ALL match
their organ's viscerotome (with BL20 and BL23 matching via extended
innervation ranges). BL21 breaks this pattern decisively — no
extended sympathetic pathway connects T12 to the stomach.
Shu-Mu pairing: BL21 (T12) + CV12 (Front-Mu of Stomach, at
epigastric midline ~T8 level). Notably, the Front-Mu CV12 at T8
IS within the Stomach viscerotome (T6–T9) — the Front-Mu matches
where the Back-Shu does not.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T12.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. T12 is at
the thoracolumbar junction — pneumothorax risk minimal (pleural
reflection above T12 at costodiaphragmatic recess, 12th rib is
floating rib). Deeper needling approaches kidney territory at
oblique angle, but standard depth is safe.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of T12 spinal nerve
- Subcostal nerve (ventral ramus of T12) provides somatic
  innervation to anterolateral abdominal wall — overlaps
  epigastric dermatome but this is SOMATIC, not visceral
- T12 = least splanchnic nerve territory → aorticorenal ganglion
  → renal/adrenal, NOT gastric. The stomach receives sympathetic
  innervation via greater splanchnic (T5–T9, via celiac ganglion).
  T12 does NOT contribute to the celiac ganglion pathway.
  Interganglion fibers between aorticorenal and celiac ganglia
  exist anatomically but represent a minor, indirect connection —
  NOT comparable to BL20's direct lesser splanchnic → celiac match.
- Spinal segment: T12
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia at the thoracolumbar junction
— the transition where the posterior layer becomes a dominant
aponeurotic structure. Latissimus dorsi origin. Same fascial
architecture as BL20 (T11).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of subcostal artery (12th thoracic
intercostal equivalent, last branch before lumbar arteries).
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → latissimus dorsi
(aponeurosis) → erector spinae (iliocostalis lumborum) → 12th
rib (floating rib) / transverse process of T12.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Stomach (Weishu) at T12**
Front-Mu: No (CV12 Zhongwan is Front-Mu of Stomach)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with CV12 for comprehensive Stomach
  treatment. BL21+CV12+ST36 = the classical three-point Stomach
  tonification protocol. BL21 commonly combined with BL20
  (Spleen Shu, T11) for the Spleen-Stomach pair (Pi-Wei 脾胃) —
  adjacent Back-Shu reflecting the Earth pair relationship.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL21 (Weishu 胃俞) — Back-Shu of Stomach.
  Traditional functions: harmonize the Stomach, regulate Stomach
  Qi (counterflow/hiccup/nausea), promote digestion. Combined
  with BL20 (Spleen Shu) for Pi-Wei treatment — the most
  fundamental organ pair in TCM internal medicine.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T12 paravertebral trigger
  points in erector spinae / quadratus lumborum (QL attaches to
  12th rib and L1–L4 transverse processes). Thoracolumbar junction
  TrPs with referred pain to epigastric and lumbar regions.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: T12 paravertebral tenderness may occur in
  gastric disorders. However, the primary Head zone for Stomach
  pain is T7–T9 epigastric — NOT T12. BL21 tenderness in Stomach
  disease is clinically reported but less consistent than upper
  Back-Shu tenderness in their respective organ disease (e.g.,
  BL18/T9 in liver disease, BL19/T10 in biliary disease).

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** No standard Marma at
  T12 paravertebral in Frawley's system.

Head zone: T12 dermatome. Stomach viscerotome = T6–T9 (A07 §1.6).
  T12 is 3 segments BELOW the primary range. Even extending to
  the lesser splanchnic (T10–T11) and least splanchnic (T12), the
  least splanchnic projects to the aorticorenal ganglion (renal),
  NOT the celiac ganglion (gastric). **❌ NON-MATCHING.** This is
  1 of 3 Back-Shu points that do not match organ viscerotome
  (BL21/Stomach, BL25/LI, BL27/SI — plan §6 Q2). A07 §4.2
  lists BL21 in the Beissner example as "same segmental territory"
  — this characterization is imprecise for strict viscerotome
  matching.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, A07 S4.2

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae at thoracolumbar
  junction — a key mechanical transition point.

Neurogenic spot: **Not predicted for Stomach.** Kim 2017 predicts
  neurogenic spots at T6–T9 for gastric pathology. T12 is outside
  this range, consistent with the viscerotome mismatch.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Thoracolumbar cupping practiced but
  BL21 not a primary named site.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains,
cupping). Head zone ✗ (non-matching). Neurogenic ✗ (not predicted).
Ashi 🟡 inconsistent.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Stomach
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: epigastric pain, abdominal distension, poor
  appetite, vomiting, borborygmus
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic Stomach conditions
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: nausea, vomiting, gastric pain, emaciation

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Epigastric pain, nausea, vomiting
- Abdominal distension, poor appetite
- Borborygmus, diarrhea
- Back pain (local)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with BL20 (T11) for Pi-Wei (Spleen-Stomach) pair
  — adjacent Back-Shu at T11–T12, the Earth pair
- Combined with CV12 (Front-Mu) for Shu-Mu Stomach treatment
- Included in diabetic gastroparesis protocols (with CV12, ST36,
  PC6, ST25 — PRISMA review cited in BL20)
- Less commonly used as sole primary point than BL20 — BL20
  (Spleen Shu) is THE central digestive tonification point while
  BL21 plays a supporting role

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Gastrointestinal disorders — 🟡 Plausible**

BL21 included in the PRISMA systematic review of Back-Shu for GI
diseases (same review cited in BL20, BL22). Among top acupoints
selected for diabetic gastroparesis protocols. 🟡 because BL21
appears in multi-point protocols, not isolated.

**Dorsher matching: NO.** Stomach viscerotome = T6–T9. BL21 at T12
is 3 segments below the primary sympathetic innervation range. No
extended pathway rescue (unlike BL20 at T11 via lesser splanchnic).
The least splanchnic nerve (T12) projects to the aorticorenal
ganglion for renal innervation, NOT to the celiac ganglion for
gastric innervation. BL21 is the 1st of 3 non-matching Back-Shu
points; all 3 are Fu (Yang) organ Shu, and all 3 are displaced
CAUDALLY from their expected viscerotome.

**2. Chronic gastritis / dyspepsia — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication with plausible clinical utility. No
BL21-specific trial. Most protocols use BL21 as part of multi-point
approaches (BL20+BL21+CV12+ST36).

**3. Local back pain — 🟡 Plausible**

Thoracolumbar junction (T12) is a common site for mechanical back
pain. Local musculoskeletal effect at T12 paravertebral is the most
straightforward modern application, independent of organ-directed
claims.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

BL20–BL21 adjacent pair (T11–T12): the Spleen-Stomach Earth pair
produces adjacent Back-Shu, paralleling BL14–BL15 (PC–Heart at
T4–T5) and BL18–BL19 (Liver–GB at T9–T10). Unlike the other two
pairs where both points match their organ viscerotome, BL20–BL21
has SPLIT matching: BL20 matches (via lesser splanchnic) but BL21
does not. The Zang member matches; the Fu member does not.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: NON-SEGMENTAL for gastric effects. UNKNOWN pathway.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL21 at T12 → dorsal horn T12 → sympathetic T12 →
  least splanchnic nerve → aorticorenal ganglion → KIDNEY/ADRENAL
  ❌ NON-MATCHING for Stomach (viscerotome T6-T9)
  T12 sympathetic does NOT project to celiac ganglion (gastric)

POSSIBLE MECHANISMS:
  (a) Abdominal wall somatic reflex: subcostal nerve (T12)
      innervates epigastric abdominal wall → possible indirect
      viscerosomatic interaction via convergent dorsal horn neurons
  (b) Non-segmental: T12 stimulation → ascending pathways →
      brainstem NTS → vagal modulation of gastric function
  (c) Local effect only: T12 paravertebral → gate control for
      thoracolumbar back pain, with gastric effects being
      coincidental or secondary to pain relief
```

BL21 is the FIRST Back-Shu to demonstrate that the segmental model
(A07) does NOT universally explain Back-Shu → organ effects. For
7/7 previously documented organ Shu, the segmental pathway was
clear (~100% segmental contribution). BL21 breaks this pattern.
If BL21 genuinely influences gastric function (not just local back
pain), the mechanism is non-segmental.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, C04 S3.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique toward spine. De Qi: deep
  aching at thoracolumbar junction. EA: commonly paired with BL20
  bilaterally for digestive protocols. Combined with CV12 and ST36
  distally for comprehensive gastric treatment.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL21: for Stomach deficiency (chronic nausea, poor
  appetite). Often moxaed bilaterally with BL20 for Pi-Wei
  tonification. Less commonly used alone than BL20 or BL23.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **Minimal** — T12 at thoracolumbar junction,
  12th rib is floating rib. Pleural reflection is above T12.
  Oblique insertion remains standard practice.

Kidney proximity: similar to BL23 — kidneys lie anterolateral
  but at standard depth (0.5–0.8 cun), needle remains within
  muscle layers.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL22 — Tam Tiêu Du (三焦俞, Sānjiāo Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Triple Burner**

Name 三焦俞 (Sānjiāo Shū) = "Triple Burner Transporting Point."
BL22 is the Back-Shu of the Triple Burner (San Jiao, TE), at L1,
1.5 cun lateral to the spine. The 9th organ Back-Shu documented.
BL22 presents a unique philosophical problem: the Triple Burner
is described in classical texts as 有名無形 "having name but no
form" (Nanjing Ch.38) — no discrete anatomical organ corresponds
to the Triple Burner in Western medicine. Unlike the other 11 organ Back-Shu
where a specific target organ can be identified, BL22 is the
Back-Shu of a FUNCTIONAL CONCEPT rather than a discrete structure.
From BSA perspective, BL22 at L1 accesses whatever anatomical
structures happen to be innervated at L1 level — primarily renal
fascia, adrenal gland (via least splanchnic T12 + lumbar splanchnic
L1–L2), and the superior mesenteric ganglion territory. The Triple Burner's
traditional "water metabolism" function (水道) maps most closely
to renal/adrenal physiology.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of L1.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.0 cun perpendicular or slightly oblique.
L1 is below thoracic cage — no pneumothorax risk. Similar depth
and safety profile to BL23 (L2).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of L1 spinal nerve
- L1 contributes to lumbar splanchnic nerves → renal plexus /
  superior hypogastric plexus. L1 sympathetic fibers reach the
  kidney, adrenal, and upper urinary tract.
- T12–L1 = renal hilum level (kidneys lie anterolateral to L1–L2
  vertebral bodies)
- Spinal segment: L1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia — three-layer structure well
developed at L1 (posterior, middle, anterior layers). Similar to
BL23 (L2) architecture.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 1st lumbar artery (from abdominal
aorta).
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → thoracolumbar fascia
(posterior layer) → erector spinae (iliocostalis lumborum,
longissimus thoracis) → quadratus lumborum → transverse process
of L1.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Triple Burner (Sanjiaoshu) at L1**
Front-Mu: No (CV5 Shimen is Front-Mu of the Triple Burner)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with CV5 (Shimen). BL22 sits between
  BL21 (Stomach Shu, T12) and BL23 (Kidney Shu, L2) — the Triple Burner
  Shu occupies the exact interspace between the last thoracic
  and first major lumbar organ Shu.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL22 (Sanjiaoshu 三焦俞) — Back-Shu of the Triple Burner.
  Traditional functions: regulate water passages (利水道), promote
  Qi transformation (氣化), resolve edema and dampness. The Triple Burner governs
  the "waterways" of the body — fluid metabolism across Upper
  (thorax), Middle (abdomen), and Lower (pelvis) Burners.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** L1 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae. Thoracolumbar junction TrPs with referred
  pain to lumbar and flank regions. Non-organ-specific.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: L1 tenderness in urinary/renal disorders
  (overlapping with BL23 territory). Not specific to any
  Triple Burner-defined condition.

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** No Marma at L1 in
  Frawley's system.

Head zone: L1 dermatome. The Triple Burner has NO viscerotome (no organ). If
  the Triple Burner is interpreted as "water metabolism" → closest anatomical
  correlate is the kidney/adrenal system (viscerotome T10–L1).
  L1 is at the lower edge of renal viscerotome. **🟡
  INDETERMINATE — no organ to match against.** Dorsher's 9/12
  count implies the Triple Burner is included (as matching or excluded); if
  mapped to kidney/adrenal, L1 would be a borderline match.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: **Not applicable.** No Triple Burner organ → no predicted
  neurogenic spot pattern.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Lumbar cupping practiced. BL22 not
  a primary named site (BL23 more commonly targeted).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains).
Head zone 🟡 indeterminate. Cupping 🟡 applicable. The lowest
convergence among organ Back-Shu, reflecting the fundamental
problem: no discrete target organ to converge upon.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Triple Burner
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: edema, borborygmus, abdominal distension,
  lumbar/spinal stiffness, urinary difficulty
- Nanjing Ch.38: San Jiao (三焦) 有名無形 "name without form"
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: edema, vomiting, diarrhea, lumbar stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Edema, water retention (Triple Burner water metabolism)
- Borborygmus, abdominal distension
- Lumbar stiffness, back pain
- Urinary difficulty
- Vomiting, diarrhea

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with BL20 (T11) + BL23 (L2) for fluid metabolism
  disorders — BL22 bridges Spleen (T11) and Kidney (L2), the
  two organs TCM considers central to water metabolism
- Included in GI protocols: PRISMA review of BL18/BL20/BL22 for
  GI diseases through sympathetic modulation (cited in BL20)
- Less commonly used as sole primary than BL20 or BL23

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Gastrointestinal disorders — 🟡 Plausible**

BL22 included in the PRISMA systematic review of Back-Shu (BL18,
BL20, BL22) for GI diseases. Effective through segmental nerve
and sympathetic modulation. 🟡 — part of multi-point protocols.

**Dorsher matching: INDETERMINATE.** The Triple Burner has no discrete organ → no
viscerotome to compare against. BL22 at L1 is within the renal
viscerotome (T10–L1) but whether Triple Burner→kidney mapping is valid is a
philosophical question, not an anatomical one. Excluded from the
matching/non-matching analysis of specific organ Shu.

**2. Edema / water metabolism — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Core Triple Burner indication. No modern trial. Closest evidence targets
BL23 (L2, Kidney Shu), not BL22 (L1).

**3. Local back pain — 🟡 Plausible**

Lumbar paravertebral point with musculoskeletal application.

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: L1 somatovisceral reflex — renal/adrenal territory.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL22 at L1 → dorsal horn L1 → sympathetic L1 →
  lumbar splanchnic nerves → renal plexus → kidney/adrenal
  ✅ MATCH for renal/adrenal (viscerotome T10-L1)
  ❓ INDETERMINATE for "Triple Burner" (no organ)
```

BL22 accesses the SAME segmental territory as BL23 (L2, Kidney
Shu). The 1-segment separation (L1 vs L2) is within the
continuous range of renal innervation. From pure neuroanatomy,
BL22 and BL23 stimulate overlapping spinal segments and reach
similar visceral targets. The TCM distinction between the Triple Burner (water
metabolism) and Kidney (water organ) maps onto the SAME anatomical
substrate — conceptual partitioning of a continuous gradient.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.8–1.0 cun perpendicular or slightly oblique.
  No pneumothorax risk at L1. De Qi: deep aching in lumbar region.
  EA: paired with BL20 and BL23 for digestive/urinary protocols.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL22: for Triple Burner deficiency / water metabolism dysfunction.
  Often included as part of bilateral lumbar moxa protocols.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L1 is below the lung fields.

Kidney proximity: L1 = renal hilum level. At standard depth
  (0.8–1.0 cun at 1.5 cun lateral to midline), needle remains
  within muscle layers. Same safety profile as BL23.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL23 — Thận Du (腎俞, Shèn Shū)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis) — Back-Shu of Kidney**
The MOST CLINICALLY USED Back-Shu point. Primary point for chronic low
back pain — the most common pain condition worldwide.

Name 腎俞 (Shèn Shū) = "Kidney Transporting Point." Lumbar
paravertebral access to renal and lower back territory.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of L2.

Needle depth range: 0.8-1.2 cun (~2-3 cm) perpendicular or slightly
oblique. SAFER than thoracic Back-Shu — below T12, no lung tissue.
Deeper needling possible due to thicker lumbar musculature.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of L2 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: L2
- Note: L2 dorsal ramus crosses the transverse process of L3 —
  medial branch block at L2 is a standard interventional pain
  procedure for lumbar facet joint pain
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia — the THICKEST and most complex
fascial structure at this level. Three layers (posterior, middle,
anterior) enclose the erector spinae and quadratus lumborum. The
posterior layer is richly innervated with nociceptive and proprioceptive
fibers. Thoracolumbar fascia pathology is implicated in chronic LBP
(Langevin 2011: -56% shear strain in chronic LBP vs healthy).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 2nd lumbar artery (from abdominal aorta).

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue →
thoracolumbar fascia (posterior layer) → erector spinae (iliocostalis
lumborum, longissimus thoracis) → quadratus lumborum → transverse
process of L2. Multifidus is prominent at lumbar levels, deep to
erector spinae and close to spinous process.

Receptor density: no BL23-specific data. Pain sensitivity at BL23
reported as HIGHEST among 5 Zang Back-Shu points (clinical
measurements). Muscle tone and stiffness LOWEST — consistent with the
lumbar region having less tonic muscular support than thoracic levels.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Kidney (Shenshu) at L2**
Front-Mu: No (GB25 Jingmen is Front-Mu of Kidney)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with GB25 (Jingmen). Combined with GV4
  (Mingmen, "Gate of Vitality" between L2-L3 spinous processes) for
  Kidney Yang tonification — GV4 at midline + bilateral BL23 = the
  most powerful lumbar Yang tonification combination in TCM.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL23 (Shenshu 腎俞) — Back-Shu of Kidney. Traditional
  functions: tonify Kidney Qi/Yang/Yin/Essence, strengthen lumbar
  region, benefit bones and marrow, regulate water metabolism,
  grasp Lung Qi (Kidney receives Qi from Lung for deep respiration).

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Erector spinae, multifidus, and
  quadratus lumborum trigger points at L2. Quadratus lumborum trigger
  points are among the most common causes of low back pain. Referred
  pain: lower back, sacroiliac region, hip, groin.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: L2 paravertebral tenderness is common in both
  renal disease (referred pain) and lumbar musculoskeletal pain. BL23
  is frequently the most tender paravertebral point in patients with
  chronic low back pain.

Marma point: **Possible correspondence.** Katikataruna Marma is in the
  hip/sacroiliac region — approximately at L4-S1 level, below BL23.
  No precise L2 Marma point in Frawley's system.

Head zone: L2 dermatome. Kidney viscerotome per A07 §1.6 = T10-L1.
  L2 is ONE segment below the primary range. However, the renal plexus
  receives contributions from lumbar splanchnic nerves (L1-L2) in
  addition to the lesser splanchnic (T10-T11). Dorsher counts BL23 as
  a MATCH. **✅ MATCH — L2 is at the edge of renal innervation range
  when including lumbar splanchnic contributions.**
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line. The lumbar erector spinae is
  a major component. Thoracolumbar fascia is a key node in the Deep
  Front Line and Lateral Line connections.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** No kidney-specific study from Kim 2017.
  Colitis study showed T10-L1 spots for large intestine pathology —
  adjacent territory.

Cupping point: **Yes.** BL23 specifically named as common TCM cupping
  location (B07 §3.2). Cupping at BL23 for Kidney tonification and
  lumbar pain is widely practiced.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, cupping). Possible Marma adds to 6-7/8. Among the
highest in Phase 1.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Kidney
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lower back pain, tinnitus, deafness, seminal
  emission, urinary disorders, edema, dizziness
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic Kidney deficiency
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: impotence, irregular menstruation, nocturnal
  emission, blurred vision, weakness of knees

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lower back pain (primary modern indication)
- Tinnitus, deafness (Kidney opens into ears — TCM)
- Impotence, seminal emission, infertility
- Urinary disorders, edema
- Chronic asthma (Kidney grasps Lung Qi — TCM)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- THE most-used Back-Shu point in clinical practice
- THE most prescribed acupoint for chronic low back pain
- Combined with GV4 (Mingmen) bilaterally = "Three Needle at Lumbar"
- B08 specifically lists BL23 + GV4 moxa for Yang deficiency
  tonification protocol
- Kidney Yang Deficiency protocol: BL23 + GV4 + KI3 + KI7 + moxa
  (B01 point selection patterns)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Chronic low back pain — 🟡 Plausible**

BL23 is one of the most frequently prescribed acupoints for chronic
LBP in systematic reviews. BL23-BL40, BL23-BL25, BL23-BL60 are the
most common point combinations. BL23-GV3 and BL23-BL25 showed the
LARGEST average effect sizes for pain reduction. Recent 2026 SR/MA:
acupuncture significantly reduced pain in LBP vs control. 🟡 rather
than 🟢 because at BL23, distinguishing local musculoskeletal effect
(trigger point, gate control at L2 paravertebral) from specific
organ-directed Kidney effect is difficult — both mechanisms converge.
(Acupuncture & Acupoints for Low Back Pain SR 2023; Acupuncture vs
usual care for chronic LBP MA 2026)

**2. Chronic kidney disease — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional Kidney tonification indication. No quality systematic
review of BL23 specifically for renal function in CKD patients.
Segmental logic (L2 → renal plexus) is sound but clinical evidence
for organ-specific renal improvement is lacking.

**3. Male infertility / sexual dysfunction — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Major traditional indication. Some RCTs of acupuncture for male
infertility include BL23, but always as part of multi-point protocols.
No BL23-isolated evidence.

**4. Tinnitus / deafness — 🔴 Unvalidated**

TCM "Kidney opens into ears." No evidence linking L2 segmental
stimulation to auditory function. Non-segmental mechanism would be
required.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Pain sensitivity at BL23 is the highest among all 5 Zang Back-Shu
points (clinical measurements). This may reflect: (a) the lumbar
region's role as the most common site of musculoskeletal pain, (b)
thoracolumbar fascia pathology in chronic LBP (Langevin 2011: -56%
shear strain), or (c) renal disease-related sensitization in the L2
dermatome.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: DUAL — somatovisceral reflex + local musculoskeletal.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL23 at L2 → dorsal horn L2 → preganglionic sympathetic L2
  → lumbar splanchnic nerve → renal plexus → kidney
  (vasomotor, renin release, tubular function)
  ✅ MATCH — L2 is at edge of renal innervation (T10-L2 extended)
  Segmental contribution for RENAL effects: ~100%

LOCAL MUSCULOSKELETAL:
  BL23 at L2 → gate control + trigger point therapy in
  erector spinae, multifidus, quadratus lumborum
  → local pain relief in lumbar region
  This is NOT an organ-specific effect but a LOCAL effect
  that coincidentally occurs at the same anatomical location.
```

BL23 uniquely demonstrates the CONVERGENCE of two independent
therapeutic logics at one point: (1) segmental organ therapy (Kidney
via L2 sympathetic) and (2) local musculoskeletal therapy (LBP via
paravertebral trigger points). Both are valid; they are not competing
explanations but SIMULTANEOUS mechanisms at the same location.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.1-S3.4, A07 S1.6

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.8-1.2 cun (~2-3 cm) perpendicular or slightly
  oblique. DEEPER than thoracic Back-Shu due to thicker lumbar
  musculature and absence of pneumothorax risk at L2. De Qi: deep
  distending/aching sensation in lumbar region, may radiate to hip.
  EA: commonly used for LBP. 2 Hz → enkephalin (chronic pain);
  100 Hz → dynorphin (acute pain); alternating 2/100 Hz → maximum
  synergy (Han model).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL23: specific indication in B08. BL23 + GV4 bilateral moxa
  = primary Kidney Yang tonification protocol. Moxa stick or moxa box
  over lumbar region. B08 S3.2 lists BL23 among most common moxa points.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Giác hơi (B07):
  Cupping at BL23: named as common cupping location in B07. Dry or
  flash cupping at lumbar region for LBP and Kidney tonification.
→ Cross-ref: B07 S3.2

Dry needling (B11):
  Quadratus lumborum and multifidus trigger points at L2 are primary
  dry needling targets for LBP. Local twitch response in multifidus
  and QL. This addresses the musculoskeletal component of BL23's
  therapeutic effects.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L2 is below the lung fields. This is the
  SAFEST of the 5 Phase 1 Back-Shu points for deep needling.

Kidney proximity: kidneys lie anterolateral to L1-L2 vertebral bodies,
  protected by the quadratus lumborum and erector spinae. At standard
  needle depth (0.8-1.2 cun at 1.5 cun lateral to midline), the needle
  remains within the muscle layers, well superficial to the kidneys.
  Direct kidney puncture would require 4-6 cun depth at unusual angle.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication for BL23 itself, though lumbar
  region treatment may require positional modification (side-lying) in
  late pregnancy.

---

### BL24 — Khí Hải Du (气海俞, Qìhǎi Shū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Sea of Qi Shu" (气海 = Sea of Qi [= CV6], 俞 = transporting
point) — at the lumbar back, 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of L3. NOT an organ Back-Shu. Named after
CV6 (Qihai, "Sea of Qi" on the anterior midline), BL24 is the
posterior correspondent of the CV6 level. Positioned between BL23
(Kidney Shu, L2) and BL25 (LI Shu, L4), filling the L3 gap in
the Back-Shu distribution.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of L3.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. No pneumothorax
risk. Deep lumbar musculature allows deeper needling.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of L3 spinal nerve
- Spinal segment: L3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (posterior layer — thick
aponeurotic structure at L3, enclosing erector spinae).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 3rd lumbar artery.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → thoracolumbar fascia
→ erector spinae → transverse process of L3.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL24 (Qihaishu 气海俞). Traditional functions:
  strengthen the low back, regulate Qi, benefit menstruation.
  Minor point.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** L3 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae / multifidus. Lumbar TrPs with local referred
  pain.

Ashi correspondence: L3 paravertebral tenderness in lumbar pain.
  Nonspecific.

Marma point: **Possible.** Katikataruna Marma territory (lower
  lumbar / sacroiliac), though typically mapped lower (L4–S1).

Head zone: L3 dermatome. No specific organ viscerotome at L3 — gap
  between renal (T10–L1) and sacral pelvic (S2–S4) innervation.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data at L3.

Cupping point: lumbar cupping applicable, not a primary named site.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains).
Marma 🟡 → possible 4/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, lower abdominal distension
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lumbar stiffness, menstrual disorders, diarrhea

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar pain (primary modern indication)
- Menstrual disorders
- Diarrhea

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL24 has no individual
evidence base. A minor lumbar point. Local musculoskeletal effect
is the only plausible modern application.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L3, well below lung fields.

Kidney proximity: kidneys above L3. No kidney risk at standard
depth. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL25 — Đại Trường Du (大肠俞, Dàcháng Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Large Intestine**

Name 大肠俞 (Dàcháng Shū) = "Large Intestine Transporting Point."
BL25 is the Back-Shu of the Large Intestine organ, at L4, 1.5 cun
lateral to the spine. The 10th organ Back-Shu documented. BL25 is
the SECOND Dorsher non-matching Back-Shu: L4 is 3 segments BELOW
the Large Intestine viscerotome (T10–L1 per A07 §1.6). Like BL21
(Stomach, T12 vs T6–T9), the displacement is exactly 3 segments
caudal. The pattern strengthens: both GI tract Fu organ Shu
documented so far are displaced downward by the same margin.

Shu-Mu pairing: BL25 (L4) + ST25 (Tianshu, Front-Mu of LI, at
umbilical level T10, AP-03). As with BL21/Stomach, the Front-Mu
ST25 at T10 IS within the LI viscerotome (T10–L1) — confirmed by
Liu 2020 (ST25 high-intensity EA activates sympathetic spinal
pathway at T10, PROKR2-independent, AP-03). The Front-Mu matches
segmentally; the Back-Shu does not.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of L4.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. No pneumothorax
risk. Deep lumbar musculature. L4 is at iliac crest level — a
common palpation landmark.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of L4 spinal nerve
- L4 is within the lumbar plexus (L1–L4) → femoral nerve, lateral
  femoral cutaneous nerve territory. These are SOMATIC nerves to
  the lower limb, NOT visceral pathways to the large intestine.
- L4 does NOT contribute to inferior mesenteric ganglion (which
  receives preganglionic fibers from L1–L2 lumbar splanchnic
  nerves for descending/sigmoid colon innervation)
- L4 does NOT contribute to pelvic splanchnic nerves (S2–S4
  parasympathetic to distal colon/rectum)
- Spinal segment: L4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia — well developed at L4,
posterior layer encloses erector spinae. At iliac crest level.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 4th lumbar artery.
→ Cross-ref: A04 S3.2

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → thoracolumbar fascia
→ erector spinae (iliocostalis lumborum) → transverse process L4.
At L4, the quadratus lumborum is less prominent than at L1–L2.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Large Intestine (Dachangshu) at L4**
Front-Mu: No (ST25 Tianshu is Front-Mu of LI — AP-03)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with ST25 for comprehensive LI treatment.
  BL25+ST25 is the classical combination for constipation and
  diarrhea. Combined with ST37 (Lower He-Sea of LI) for three-
  level LI treatment: Back-Shu (posterior) + Front-Mu (anterior)
  + Lower He-Sea (distal).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL25 (Dachangshu 大肠俞) — Back-Shu of Large
  Intestine. Traditional functions: regulate the intestines
  (constipation/diarrhea), strengthen the low back, promote
  excretion. Combined with ST25 (Front-Mu) for Shu-Mu protocol.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** L4 paravertebral trigger points
  in erector spinae / multifidus. Lumbar TrPs with local referred
  pain to lower back and sacroiliac region.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: L4 paravertebral tenderness in chronic low
  back pain. This tenderness correlates with musculoskeletal
  pathology at L4–L5 (the most common site for disc herniation /
  degeneration), NOT with LI disease. Unlike upper Back-Shu where
  tenderness correlates with organ dysfunction (e.g., BL18/T9 in
  liver disease), BL25 tenderness is primarily musculoskeletal.

Marma point: **Possible.** Katikataruna Marma territory (L4–S1,
  sacroiliac region).

Head zone: L4 dermatome. LI viscerotome = T10–L1 (A07 §1.6). L4
  is 3 segments BELOW the LI innervation range. L4 does not
  contribute to either sympathetic (lumbar splanchnic L1–L2 →
  inferior mesenteric ganglion) or parasympathetic (pelvic
  splanchnic S2–S4) innervation of the large intestine.
  **❌ NON-MATCHING.** 2nd of 3 non-matching Back-Shu. Kim 2017
  colitis model: neurogenic spots at T10–L1, NOT L4 — confirming
  mismatch.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: **Not predicted for LI at L4.** Kim 2017: colitis
  rats showed neurogenic spots at T10–L1 dermatome. L4 is outside
  this range. ST25 at T10 IS within Kim's colitis spot range —
  the Front-Mu is segmentally accurate while the Back-Shu is not.

Cupping point: **Applicable.** Lumbar cupping practiced. Not a
  primary named site.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Marma 🟡, Anatomy
Trains). Head zone ✗ (non-matching). Neurogenic ✗ (not predicted).
Ashi 🟡 musculoskeletal.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Large Intestine
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: abdominal distension, borborygmus, diarrhea,
  constipation, lumbar pain
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic LI conditions
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lower abdominal pain, lumbar stiffness,
  diarrhea, dysentery

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Low back pain (primary modern indication)
- Abdominal distension, diarrhea, constipation
- Dysentery
- Muscular atrophy of lower limbs

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with ST25 (Front-Mu) for Shu-Mu LI treatment
- BL23+BL25 is one of the most common point combinations for
  chronic low back pain (systematic reviews), though this likely
  reflects their lumbar musculoskeletal effect rather than
  organ-directed action
- More commonly used for LBP than for intestinal conditions in
  modern practice — the LBP application is local, the intestinal
  application requires an unknown non-segmental pathway

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Chronic low back pain — 🟡 Plausible**

BL25 is among the most frequently prescribed acupoints for chronic
LBP. BL23–BL25 pair commonly used in systematic reviews. BL23–BL25
showed the LARGEST average effect sizes for pain reduction (cited
in BL23 evidence section). Evidence supports L4 paravertebral
acupuncture for LBP via local musculoskeletal mechanisms (gate
control, trigger point release in erector spinae/multifidus). This
does NOT support the organ-directed "Back-Shu of LI" claim — it
supports a local mechanical effect.

**Dorsher matching: NO.** LI viscerotome = T10–L1. BL25 at L4 is
3 segments below. No sympathetic pathway connects L4 to the large
intestine. L4 is the 2nd non-matching Back-Shu. The pattern is
consistent: BL21 (Stomach, T12 vs T6–T9) and BL25 (LI, L4 vs
T10–L1) are both GI tract Fu organ Shu displaced exactly 3
segments caudally.

**2. Intestinal disorders (constipation/diarrhea) — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Core traditional indication. No BL25-specific trial for intestinal
conditions. The segmental mismatch undermines the theoretical basis
for isolated BL25 → LI organ effects.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

BL25 tenderness in chronic LBP patients is well-documented but
correlates with L4 musculoskeletal pathology (disc disease, facet
arthropathy), not LI disease. This contrasts with upper Back-Shu
(BL18 tenderness in liver disease, BL19 tenderness in biliary
disease) where tenderness is viscerally driven. At L4, the
musculoskeletal signal overwhelms any potential visceral signal.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: NON-SEGMENTAL for intestinal effects. LOCAL for LBP.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL25 at L4 → dorsal horn L4 → NO sympathetic pathway to LI
  ❌ NON-MATCHING for Large Intestine (viscerotome T10-L1)
  L4 sympathetic → femoral nerve territory (somatic lower limb)

LOCAL MUSCULOSKELETAL MODEL:
  BL25 at L4 → gate control + trigger point therapy in erector
  spinae, multifidus at L4 → local pain relief
  ✅ EVIDENCE-SUPPORTED for LBP (via BL23-BL25 pair evidence)

ORGAN-DIRECTED (if real):
  BL25 → LI effects would require non-segmental mechanism:
  ascending → brainstem → vagal/parasympathetic → LI modulation
  Or: completely non-neural mechanism (unknown)
```

BL25 demonstrates DUAL IDENTITY in the Back-Shu system: (1) its
modern clinical use for LBP is well-supported by local mechanisms,
(2) its traditional identity as "Back-Shu of LI" has no segmental
basis. The LBP application requires NO reference to the LI organ;
the intestinal application requires an unknown non-segmental
pathway.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, C04 S3.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. De Qi: deep aching in
  lumbar region. EA: commonly paired with BL23 bilaterally for LBP.
  Combined with ST25 (Front-Mu) for intestinal protocols.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL25: for chronic diarrhea (LI deficiency cold). Often
  moxaed as part of lumbar protocol with BL23.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L4, well below lung fields.

Vascular risk: Minimal. At iliac crest level. No major deep
  structures at risk at standard depth.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL26 — Quan Nguyên Du (关元俞, Guānyuán Shū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Gate of Origin Shu" (关元 = Gate of Origin [= CV4], 俞 =
transporting point) — at the lumbar back, 1.5 cun lateral to the
lower border of the spinous process of L5. NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Named after CV4 (Guanyuan, "Gate of Origin" on the anterior
midline). BL26 is the LAST point on the medial BL back line before
the sacral segment begins (BL27 at S1). L5 is the lumbosacral
junction — the transition from lumbar to sacral innervation, and
the most common level for disc herniation.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of L5.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. No pneumothorax
risk. L5 = lumbosacral junction, thick musculature.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Medial branch of dorsal ramus of L5 spinal nerve
- L5 contributes to the lumbosacral trunk → sciatic nerve
  territory (somatic lower limb)
- Spinal segment: L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (posterior layer), transitioning
to sacral fascia.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 5th lumbar artery (variable — may be
small at L5, supplemented by median sacral artery branch).

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → thoracolumbar fascia
→ erector spinae (primarily multifidus at this level) → mammillary
process of L5.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL26 (Guanyuanshu 关元俞). Traditional functions:
  strengthen the low back, warm the Lower Burner, regulate
  urination. Minor point.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** L5 paravertebral trigger points
  in multifidus — L5 is the most common site for deep multifidus
  TrPs in chronic LBP patients.

Ashi correspondence: L5 tenderness in chronic LBP — extremely
  common, reflecting L5–S1 disc and facet pathology (the most
  common level for degenerative lumbar disease).

Marma point: **Possible.** Katikataruna Marma territory (L5–S1).

Head zone: L5 dermatome. No specific organ viscerotome. L5 is
  transitional between lumbar somatic and sacral parasympathetic
  territories.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL along erector spinae / multifidus.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data at L5.

Cupping point: lumbosacral cupping applicable, not a primary site.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Marma 🟡 → possible 5/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, diarrhea, lower abdominal
  distension
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lumbar stiffness, urinary frequency, diarrhea

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar pain (primary modern indication)
- Diarrhea
- Urinary frequency, enuresis
- Abdominal distension

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL26 has no individual
evidence base. L5 paravertebral is clinically important for LBP
(most common disc herniation level), but this is a local
musculoskeletal effect, not organ-directed.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L5, well below lung fields.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Lumbosacral junction. No major structures
  at risk at standard depth.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### Batch 5 Observations

**Observation 13 — DORSHER NON-MATCHING: FU ORGAN CAUDAL DISPLACEMENT.**
BL21 (Stomach, T12) and BL25 (LI, L4) are the 1st and 2nd Dorsher
non-matching Back-Shu points (the 3rd, BL27/SI at S1, is pending
Batch 6). Both share a CONSISTENT PATTERN:
(a) Both are Fu (Yang) organ Shu — not Zang.
(b) Both are displaced CAUDALLY from their organ viscerotome by
    exactly 3 segments (BL21: T12 vs T6–T9; BL25: L4 vs T10–L1).
(c) Neither has an extended sympathetic pathway rescue (unlike BL20
    which matched via lesser splanchnic, or BL23 via lumbar
    splanchnic).
(d) Both Front-Mu points for these organs (CV12/Stomach at ~T8,
    ST25/LI at T10) ARE within the correct viscerotome — the
    Front-Mu system is segmentally accurate where the Back-Shu
    system is not.
(e) Kim 2017 neurogenic spot model predicts spots at the VISCEROTOME
    level (T6–T9 for Stomach, T10–L1 for LI), NOT at the Back-Shu
    level (T12, L4) — further confirming the mismatch.

Hypothesis: the caudal displacement in GI tract Fu organ Shu may
reflect PHYSICAL ORGAN POSITION mapping rather than INNERVATION
mapping. The stomach's pylorus/antrum extends to T12–L1 level
physically. The large intestine's descending colon passes the iliac
crest at L4 level. The classical system may have been derived from
organ physical position on the posterior body wall, which for GI
tract organs (long tubes with mesenteric suspension) diverges from
their sympathetic innervation level. Zang organs (compact, fixed,
innervation and physical location co-localized) show no such
divergence. 🟡 Plausible.

**Observation 14 — BL22: BACK-SHU WITHOUT AN ORGAN.**
BL22 (Triple Burner, L1) is the only Back-Shu for an organ that
has no anatomical equivalent. From BSA perspective, BL22 at L1
accesses renal/adrenal territory (viscerotome T10–L1). BL22 sits
between BL21 (T12) and BL23 (L2) — all three access overlapping
T12–L2 sympathetic territory. The TCM distinction between Stomach
(T12), Triple Burner (L1), and Kidney (L2) maps onto a CONTINUOUS 3-segment
zone, with organ assignments representing conceptual partitioning
of a gradient rather than discrete segmental boundaries. The Triple Burner's
convergence score (3/8 — lowest among organ Back-Shu) reflects this
fundamental problem: without a target organ, cross-tradition systems
have nothing to converge upon.

**Observation 15 — EVEN DISTRIBUTION: BACK-SHU SPACING T3→S2.**
The 12 organ Back-Shu span T3 to S2 (17 spinal segments). Their
distribution is remarkably even: T3(Lung), T4(PC), T5(Heart),
[gap T6–T8], T9(Liver), T10(GB), T11(Spleen), T12(Stomach),
L1(Triple Burner), L2(Kidney), [gap L3], L4(LI), [gap L5], S1(SI), S2(BL).
From T3 to T5: 3 consecutive. From T9 to L2: 6 consecutive. If
the 3 non-matching Fu Shu were placed at their viscerotome-
predicted positions (Stomach ~T7, LI ~T11, SI ~T10), severe
clustering would result: T10 would host BOTH GB and SI, T11 would
host BOTH Spleen and LI, and T7 would conflict with BL17
(Diaphragm). The caudal displacement of Fu Shu may serve a
DISTRIBUTIONAL function: spreading 12 Back-Shu across 17 segments
to avoid clustering, at the cost of segmental accuracy for 3 Fu
organs. The even spacing is verifiable; the claim of intentional
distributional design is interpretive. 🟡 Plausible.

---

### BL27 — Tiểu Trường Du (小肠俞, Xiǎocháng Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Small Intestine**

Name 小肠俞 (Xiǎocháng Shū) = "Small Intestine Transporting Point."
BL27 is the Back-Shu of the Small Intestine organ, at S1, 1.5 cun
lateral to the median sacral crest. The 11th organ Back-Shu
documented. BL27 is the THIRD and FINAL Dorsher non-matching
Back-Shu: S1 is 5–6 segments BELOW the Small Intestine viscerotome
(T9–T10 per A07 §1.6) — the MOST EXTREME mismatch of all 12 organ
Shu. The displacement gradient across the non-matching triad:
BL21/Stomach (3 segments), BL25/LI (3 segments), BL27/SI (5–6
segments).

Uniquely among the 3 non-matching, BL27's traditional indications
are overwhelmingly UROGENITAL (seminal emission, urinary disorders),
NOT enteric (intestinal pain, diarrhea). At S1, BL27 accesses
sacral nerve territory (pelvic plexus, pudendal nerve origin) —
the anatomy of the point matches its CLINICAL USE, not its ORGAN
DESIGNATION. The classical "Small Intestine Shu" label at S1 is
the most extreme example of organ assignment diverging from both
innervation anatomy AND clinical indication.

Shu-Mu pairing: BL27 (S1) + CV4 (Guanyuan, Front-Mu of SI, at
hypogastric level ~L2–L3). Unlike BL21/Stomach and BL25/LI where
the Front-Mu matches the organ viscerotome, CV4 at ~L2–L3 is ALSO
outside SI viscerotome (T9–T10). The SI is unique: BOTH its
Front-Mu AND Back-Shu are displaced from the organ innervation.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the median
sacral crest, at the level of the 1st posterior sacral foramen.
BL27 is at the LEVEL of the 1st posterior sacral foramen but NOT
within it (the Baliao points BL31–34 enter the foramina directly).

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. Sacral bone
prevents deep penetration. No pneumothorax risk.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Lateral branches of dorsal rami of S1 spinal nerve
- S1 = sacral plexus territory → sciatic nerve (L4–S3),
  superior gluteal nerve (L4–S1), inferior gluteal nerve (L5–S2)
- Pelvic splanchnic nerves originate from S2–S4 (not S1), but
  S1 fibers contribute to the sacral plexus surrounding pelvic
  structures
- S1 has NO sympathetic or parasympathetic connection to the
  small intestine. SI sympathetic innervation = T9–T10 (via
  greater splanchnic → superior mesenteric ganglion). SI
  parasympathetic innervation = vagus nerve (brainstem, not
  spinal).
- Spinal segment: S1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: sacral periosteum overlying the sacral bone. Deep
fascia blending with erector spinae aponeurosis and gluteus
maximus fascia.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: lateral sacral artery (from internal iliac artery),
dorsal branches.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue
→ sacral fascia → rudimentary multifidus / erector spinae origin
→ sacral periosteum. Muscle layers thinner than lumbar levels.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Small Intestine (Xiaochangshu) at S1**
Front-Mu: No (CV4 Guanyuan is Front-Mu of SI)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with CV4. Note: CV4 at ~L2–L3 is ALSO
  outside SI viscerotome (T9–T10) — unique among Shu-Mu pairs
  where BOTH reference points miss the organ innervation.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL27 (Xiaochangshu 小肠俞) — Back-Shu of Small
  Intestine. Traditional functions: regulate SI, clear Damp-Heat
  from Lower Burner, benefit urination. Notably: traditional
  indications emphasize UROGENITAL function (seminal emission,
  leukorrhea, urinary difficulty) far more than enteric function
  — inconsistent with SI organ designation but consistent with
  S1 sacral anatomy.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** S1 paravertebral trigger points
  in deep sacral musculature (multifidus, erector spinae origins).
  Sacroiliac TrPs with referred pain to gluteal and posterior thigh.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: S1 sacral tenderness in sacroiliac joint
  dysfunction and pelvic pain. Not associated with SI enteric
  disease.

Marma point: **Possible.** Nitamba Marma (gluteal region) or
  Katikataruna Marma (sacral-hip junction).

Head zone: S1 dermatome. SI viscerotome = T9–T10 (A07 §1.6). S1
  is 5–6 segments BELOW the SI innervation range — the MOST
  EXTREME mismatch of all 12 organ Back-Shu. **❌ NON-MATCHING.**
  3rd of 3 non-matching (completing triad: BL21/Stomach, BL25/LI,
  BL27/SI). All 3 are GI tract Fu organs.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL via sacrotuberous ligament / sacral
  attachments.

Neurogenic spot: **Not predicted for SI at S1.** Kim 2017: SI
  viscerotome T9–T10 predicts periumbilical neurogenic spots,
  not sacral.

Cupping point: sacral cupping less commonly practiced. Not a
  primary site.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains).
Head zone ✗ (non-matching, most extreme). Marma 🟡 → possible 4/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Small Intestine
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lower abdominal distension, seminal emission,
  enuresis, hematuria, dysentery
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic SI conditions
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lumbar/sacral pain, seminal emission, leukorrhea,
  urinary difficulty

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Seminal emission, leukorrhea (urogenital — PRIMARY indications)
- Lower abdominal pain, abdominal distension
- Diarrhea, dysentery
- Lumbar/sacral pain, sciatica (local)
- Urinary disorders (enuresis, hematuria)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Traditional indications overwhelmingly UROGENITAL: seminal
  emission, leukorrhea, enuresis. This matches S1 sacral anatomy,
  NOT SI enteric anatomy.
- Combined with BL28 (Bladder Shu, S2) for pelvic/urogenital
  conditions — BL27–BL28 sacral pair functions as a pelvic
  treatment unit regardless of different organ designations
- Less commonly used for enteric SI conditions (practitioners
  prefer CV4/ST25/ST37 for intestinal complaints)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Urogenital / pelvic — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Primary traditional indication. No BL27-specific trials. S1
sacral stimulation is neuroanatomically plausible for pelvic
visceral effects (sacral nerve contributions to pelvic plexus),
but this is a PELVIC mechanism, not an INTESTINAL one.

**Dorsher matching: NO.** SI viscerotome = T9–T10. BL27 at S1 is
5–6 segments below — the MOST EXTREME mismatch. The displacement
gradient: BL21/Stomach (3), BL25/LI (3), BL27/SI (5–6). Completes
the 3/3 non-matching set. All 3 are GI tract Fu (Yang) organ Shu.

**2. Lower abdominal / enteric — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Classical "SI Back-Shu" indication. S1 has no neural pathway to
the small intestine. If BL27 produces intestinal effects, the
mechanism is entirely non-segmental and unknown.

**3. Local sacral/lumbar pain — 🟡 Plausible**

S1 paravertebral point with local musculoskeletal application.
Sacroiliac, piriformis, and gluteal pain — local mechanisms.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

BL27 indications are almost entirely pelvic/urogenital despite its
SI organ designation. This represents the clearest MISMATCH between
organ name and clinical function in the entire Back-Shu system. The
point behaves clinically as a "Pelvic Shu" (accessing S1 sacral
nerve territory), not as a "Small Intestine Shu."
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: PELVIC somatovisceral at S1, NOT intestinal.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL27 at S1 → dorsal horn S1 → sacral plexus →
  pelvic viscera (bladder, reproductive, lower urinary)
  ❌ NON-MATCHING for Small Intestine (viscerotome T9-T10)
  ✅ MATCH for pelvic viscera (S1 contributes to sacral plexus)

FUNCTIONAL REALITY:
  BL27 functions as a PELVIC point, not an intestinal point.
  Clinical indications (seminal emission, enuresis, leukorrhea)
  match S1 sacral anatomy perfectly.
  "Small Intestine Shu" label is historically assigned
  but anatomically and clinically misleading.
```

BL27 is the strongest evidence that some Back-Shu organ assignments
are NOMINAL rather than FUNCTIONAL. The point works for what S1
anatomy can access (pelvic viscera), not for what the classical
label claims (small intestine). This extends Observation 13's
physical-position hypothesis: BL27 may mark the level where the SI
terminates physically (ileocecal junction projects approximately to
S1 level), even though its innervation is far above.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, C04 S3.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. Sacral bone prevents
  excessive depth. De Qi: dull aching in sacral region, may
  radiate to gluteal area. Combined with BL28 (S2) bilaterally
  for pelvic/urogenital protocols.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL27: for chronic seminal emission / leukorrhea (SI/BL
  deficiency cold). Part of sacral moxa protocols.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — sacral level. No lung tissue.

Vascular risk: Lateral sacral artery branches — small, minimal
  risk. Sacral bone protects pelvic viscera from deep penetration.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication but sacral needling may
  be uncomfortable in late pregnancy.

---

### BL28 — Bàng Quang Du (膀胱俞, Pángguāng Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Back-Shu of Bladder**

Name 膀胱俞 (Pángguāng Shū) = "Bladder Transporting Point." BL28
is the Back-Shu of the Bladder organ, at S2, 1.5 cun lateral to
the median sacral crest. The 12th and FINAL organ Back-Shu — with
BL28, ALL 12 organ Back-Shu points in the TCM system are documented
in the Atlas. BL28 is also SELF-REFERENTIAL: the Bladder channel
(BL) hosts the Back-Shu of its own organ (Bladder) — the only
channel where the Back-Shu's target organ matches the host meridian.

Dorsher matching: YES. Bladder viscerotome = T11–L2 (sympathetic,
storage phase) + S2–S4 (parasympathetic, voiding phase). BL28 at
S2 is within the S2–S4 parasympathetic range. This is the ONLY
Back-Shu that matches via parasympathetic innervation rather than
sympathetic — all other 8 matching Shu use sympathetic pathways.
S2 specifically accesses the pelvic splanchnic nerves → detrusor
muscle → voiding reflex.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the median
sacral crest, at the level of the 2nd posterior sacral foramen.
As with BL27, BL28 is at the LEVEL of the 2nd posterior sacral
foramen but NOT within it (BL32, a Baliao point, enters the 2nd
foramen directly).

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. Sacral bone
prevents deep penetration.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Lateral branches of dorsal rami of S2 spinal nerve
- S2 = CRITICAL level for bladder innervation. Pelvic splanchnic
  nerves (nervi erigentes) originate from S2–S4 → pelvic plexus
  → detrusor muscle (bladder wall). S2 is the primary segment
  for the micturition reflex.
- S2 also contributes to pudendal nerve (S2–S4): external
  urethral sphincter (voluntary continence), pelvic floor muscles
- DUAL BLADDER INNERVATION:
  Sympathetic: T11–L2 → hypogastric nerve → storage/filling
  Parasympathetic: S2–S4 → pelvic splanchnic → voiding/emptying
  BL28 at S2 accesses the PARASYMPATHETIC (voiding) pathway.
- Spinal segment: S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: sacral periosteum. Sacral surface narrowing
caudally. Deep fascia of gluteus maximus overlying.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: lateral sacral artery branches (internal iliac).

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue
→ sacral fascia → vestigial erector spinae / sacral multifidus
→ sacral periosteum. Minimal muscle bulk at S2.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
**Back-Shu: YES — Bladder (Pangguangshu) at S2**
  12th/12th organ Back-Shu = LAST in the Atlas.
  SELF-REFERENTIAL: BL channel hosts BL organ's Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No (CV3 Zhongji is Front-Mu of Bladder)
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: Shu-Mu pairing with CV3 (Zhongji) for comprehensive
  Bladder treatment. BL28 (posterior, S2, parasympathetic access)
  + CV3 (anterior, suprapubic, sympathetic territory) — covering
  BOTH phases of bladder function (storage + voiding).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL28 (Pangguangshu 膀胱俞) — Back-Shu of Bladder.
  Traditional functions: regulate Bladder Qi transformation
  (膀胱氣化), benefit urination, clear Damp-Heat from Lower
  Burner. "The Bladder is the Official of the Capital, storing
  fluids; through Qi transformation, fluids can be excreted"
  (Su Wen Ch.8).

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** S2 paravertebral trigger points
  in sacral musculature. Referred pain to gluteal, sacral, and
  perineal regions. Overlapping with BL27 (S1) territory.
→ Cross-ref: A08 S3.1

Ashi correspondence: S2 tenderness in urinary disorders —
  viscerally driven. Bladder distension or infection can produce
  sacral tenderness at S2–S4 via viscerosomatic reflex (bladder
  afferents → S2–S4 dorsal horn → increased paravertebral muscle
  tone). Genuine viscerosomatic reflex at the correct segmental
  level — paralleling upper Back-Shu tenderness patterns (BL18
  in liver disease, BL19 in biliary disease).

Marma point: **Possible.** Nitamba Marma (gluteal/sacral) or Guda
  Marma (anorectal region, S2–S4 territory).

Head zone: S2 dermatome. Bladder viscerotome = T11–L2
  (sympathetic) + S2–S4 (parasympathetic). BL28 at S2 is within
  the S2–S4 parasympathetic range. **✅ MATCH — via
  parasympathetic (pelvic splanchnic nerves).** The ONLY Back-Shu
  matching through parasympathetic rather than sympathetic.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL via sacral attachments. Deep Front Line
  connections through pelvic floor fascia.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** Kim 2017 model predicts spots at
  viscerotome level during bladder pathology. S2–S4 spots for
  bladder inflammation expected — consistent with BL28 location.

Cupping point: sacral cupping less common. Not a primary site.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Neurogenic 🟡 predicted → possible 6/8. Highest
among sacral Back-Shu points.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.51): Back-Shu of Bladder
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: urinary difficulty, enuresis, diarrhea,
  lumbar/sacral stiffness
- Nanjing Ch.67: Back-Shu for chronic Bladder conditions
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: urinary retention, frequent urination, low
  back pain, constipation

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Urinary disorders: retention, frequency, enuresis, incontinence
  (primary modern indications)
- Lumbar/sacral pain (local)
- Diarrhea, constipation
- Lower abdominal distension

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Combined with BL27 (S1) for pelvic/urogenital unit
- BL28+CV3 Shu-Mu combination for urinary disorders
- Combined with SP6 + KI3 + BL23 for comprehensive urinary
  treatment
- Modern relevance: sacral-level stimulation for bladder control
  parallels modern sacral neuromodulation (SNM) — BL31–34
  (Batch 7) offer direct sacral foramen access

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Urinary disorders (retention / incontinence) — 🟡 Plausible**

S2 sacral stimulation for bladder function has strong anatomical
support: S2 pelvic splanchnic nerves → detrusor muscle →
micturition reflex. Modern sacral neuromodulation (InterStim at
S3) for overactive bladder/incontinence validates the principle
that sacral nerve stimulation modulates bladder function. BL28
at S2 accesses adjacent territory. 🟡 because evidence is for
sacral stimulation in general (BL31–34 foramina), not BL28
specifically.

**Dorsher matching: YES.** Bladder viscerotome includes S2–S4
(parasympathetic voiding pathway). BL28 at S2 is within range.
9th of 9 matching Back-Shu points (of 12 total; 3 do not match).
UNIQUE: the only Back-Shu matching via parasympathetic (pelvic
splanchnic) rather than sympathetic pathway.

**2. Enuresis / nocturnal incontinence — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication with strong segmental logic. S2
parasympathetic → detrusor → voiding reflex is the precise
pathway. No BL28-isolated trial.

**3. Local sacral pain — 🟡 Plausible**

Sacral paravertebral point. Local musculoskeletal application.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

BL28 tenderness may be viscerally driven in bladder disease — S2
viscerosomatic reflex from bladder inflammation → sacral
paravertebral sensitization. This parallels upper Back-Shu
patterns (BL18/liver, BL19/biliary, BL23/renal) where
disease-state sensitization correlates with organ dysfunction.
However, S2 tenderness in sacral pain is common regardless of
bladder status — visceral vs musculoskeletal signal separation
is difficult at this level.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: Somatovisceral reflex — PARASYMPATHETIC sacral.**

```
SEGMENTAL MODEL (A07):
  BL28 at S2 → dorsal horn S2 → pelvic splanchnic nerves (S2-S4)
  → pelvic plexus → detrusor muscle → micturition reflex
  ✅ MATCH via parasympathetic (viscerotome S2-S4)
  Segmental contribution: ~100% (for voiding phase)

NOTE: Bladder has DUAL innervation:
  Sympathetic: T11-L2 → hypogastric nerve → internal sphincter
    → STORAGE (filling, continence)
  Parasympathetic: S2-S4 → pelvic splanchnic → detrusor
    → VOIDING (emptying, micturition)
  BL28 at S2 accesses the VOIDING pathway specifically.
  The STORAGE pathway (T11-L2) is accessed by BL23 (L2,
  Kidney Shu) — coincidentally, since BL23 is designated
  as Kidney Shu, not Bladder Shu.
```

BL28 is the only Back-Shu matching via parasympathetic (pelvic
splanchnic) rather than sympathetic pathway. This reflects the
unique dual innervation of the bladder organ. BL28 at S2 targets
the voiding/emptying function specifically.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6, C04 S3.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular. Sacral bone. De Qi:
  dull aching in sacral region, may refer to perineum. EA:
  combined with BL27 bilaterally for pelvic protocols, or with
  BL32 (2nd sacral foramen) for urinary disorders.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa at BL28: for Bladder deficiency cold (frequent pale urine,
  enuresis). Part of sacral moxa with BL27 and CV3.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — sacral level.

Vascular risk: Lateral sacral artery branches — minimal. Sacral
  bone protects pelvic viscera.

Pregnancy: Sacral points with caution in late pregnancy. S2
  parasympathetic activation could theoretically influence
  micturition reflex.

### BL29 — Trung Lữ Du (中膂俞, Zhōnglǚ Shū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Middle Backbone Shu" (中 = middle, 膂 = backbone/spine, 俞 =
transporting point) — at S3, 1.5 cun lateral to the median sacral
crest. NOT an organ Back-Shu. Positioned between BL28 (Bladder
Shu, S2) and BL30 (S4), in the mid-sacral pelvic splanchnic
territory (S2–S4). S3 is the primary target for modern sacral
neuromodulation (InterStim) — a connection documented in BL33
(Batch 7).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the median
sacral crest, at the level of the 3rd posterior sacral foramen.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular. Sacral bone at S3.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Lateral branches of dorsal rami of S3 spinal nerve
- S3 = pelvic splanchnic nerve territory → parasympathetic to
  bladder, rectum, reproductive organs
- S3 = pudendal nerve contribution (S2–S4): external urethral
  and anal sphincters, perineal sensation
- Spinal segment: S3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: sacral periosteum. Sacral surface narrowing
caudally. Gluteal fascia overlying.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: lateral sacral artery branches.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue
→ sacral fascia → minimal musculature → sacral periosteum.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL29 (Zhonglüshu 中膂俞). Traditional functions:
  strengthen the low back, regulate the Lower Burner, benefit
  urination. Minor sacral point.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** S3 sacral TrPs in deep sacral
  musculature. Referred pain to gluteal and perineal regions.

Ashi correspondence: S3 tenderness in sacral/pelvic pain.

Marma point: **Possible.** Guda Marma territory (anorectal/sacral).

Head zone: S3 dermatome → pelvic floor, perineal. Within
  parasympathetic territory for bladder/rectum (S2–S4).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL via sacral attachments.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data.

Cupping point: not commonly practiced at S3.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains).
Marma 🟡 → possible 4/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, diarrhea, hernia
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: sacral stiffness, dysentery, lower abdominal
  pain

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar/sacral pain (primary)
- Diarrhea, hernia
- Lower abdominal distension

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL29 has no individual
evidence base. S3 sacral territory — pelvic parasympathetic
access is anatomically real (S3 is the primary target for modern
sacral neuromodulation/InterStim), but this evidence is documented
under BL33 (3rd sacral foramen, Batch 7).

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — sacral level.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: Sacral points with caution
  in late pregnancy.
