### BL30 — Bạch Hoàn Du (白环俞, Báihuán Shū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"White Ring Shu" (白 = white, 环 = ring, 俞 = transporting point)
— at S4, 1.5 cun lateral to the median sacral crest. NOT an organ
Back-Shu. BL30 is the LOWEST and LAST point on the medial BL back
line (1.5 cun lateral series). The medial back line spans T1
(BL11) to S4 (BL30) — 20 points across 21 spinal segments
(T1–S4), with only T6–T8 and L3/L5 gaps. After BL30, the BL
channel transitions to BL35 (coccyx tip), then to the posterior
gluteal/thigh trajectory (BL36–BL40). The "white ring" name may
refer to the sacrococcygeal junction or the anorectal ring.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the median
sacral crest, at the level of the 4th posterior sacral foramen.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular. Sacral bone at S4.
The sacral hiatus begins near S4–S5 — avoid deep insertion toward
the sacral canal (epidural space).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Lateral branches of dorsal rami of S4 spinal nerve
- S4 = caudal end of pelvic splanchnic nerve range →
  minimal parasympathetic contribution (primarily S2–S3)
- S4 = pudendal nerve territory → perineal/anorectal sensation,
  external anal sphincter
- Coccygeal plexus (S4–Co1) begins at this level
- Spinal segment: S4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: sacral periosteum at inferior sacrum.
Sacrococcygeal junction nearby. Gluteus maximus fascia overlying.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: inferior lateral sacral artery, median sacral artery
branches.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → subcutaneous tissue
→ sacral fascia → gluteus maximus origin → sacral periosteum.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL30 (Baihuanshu 白环俞). Traditional functions:
  regulate the Lower Burner, benefit the sacral region, treat
  leukorrhea and hernia. Minor sacral point — the LAST on the
  medial back line.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** S4 sacral TrPs — mostly in
  sacrotuberous and sacrospinous ligament attachments. Referred
  pain to perineum and coccyx.

Ashi correspondence: S4/sacrococcygeal tenderness in coccygodynia
  and pelvic floor dysfunction.

Marma point: **Possible.** Guda Marma territory (anorectal).

Head zone: S4 dermatome → perineal, perianal. Rectum/prostate
  viscerotome = S2–S4.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL sacral terminus.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data.

Cupping point: not commonly practiced at S4 (insufficient soft
  tissue).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains).
Marma 🟡 → possible 4/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar/sacral pain, leukorrhea, hernia,
  hemorrhoids
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: sacral stiffness, enuresis, seminal emission

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar/sacral pain (primary)
- Leukorrhea, menstrual disorders
- Hernia
- Enuresis

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL30 has no individual
evidence base. Lowest medial back line point.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — sacral level.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Sacral hiatus nearby at S4–S5 — avoid
  deep insertion toward sacral canal (epidural space).

Pregnancy: Sacral points with caution in late pregnancy.

### Batch 6 Observations

**Observation 16 — DORSHER NON-MATCHING TRIAD COMPLETE.**
BL27 (SI, S1) completes the 3/3 Dorsher non-matching Back-Shu.
Displacement gradient: BL21/Stomach = 3 segments, BL25/LI = 3
segments, BL27/SI = 5–6 segments. All 3 are GI tract Fu (Yang)
organs, all displaced CAUDALLY. BL27 is unique in two additional
ways: (a) its traditional indications are overwhelmingly
UROGENITAL, not enteric — it clinically functions as a "Pelvic
Shu," not an "SI Shu"; (b) unlike BL21/BL25 where the Front-Mu
matches the viscerotome (CV12 at T8, ST25 at T10), the SI
Front-Mu CV4 at ~L2–L3 is ALSO outside SI viscerotome (T9–T10) —
for SI, BOTH Front-Mu and Back-Shu are non-matching. This is the
most extreme divergence in the entire Shu-Mu system. The physical-
position hypothesis (Obs 13) partially applies: ileocecal junction
projects to ~S1 level. But BL27's urogenital indications suggest
the point simply accesses what S1 anatomy can reach (pelvic
viscera), regardless of the SI designation. The "Small Intestine
Shu" label is NOMINAL at S1.

**Observation 17 — ALL 12 ORGAN BACK-SHU COMPLETE.**
BL28 completes 12/12 organ Back-Shu. Final inventory:

| # | Back-Shu | Organ | Level | Viscerotome | Dorsher | Pathway |
|---|----------|-------|-------|-------------|---------|---------|
| 1 | BL13 | Lung | T3 | T3–T5 | ✅ | Sympathetic |
| 2 | BL14 | PC | T4 | T1–T5 | ✅ | Sympathetic |
| 3 | BL15 | Heart | T5 | T1–T5 | ✅ | Sympathetic |
| 4 | BL18 | Liver | T9 | T7–T9 | ✅ | Sympathetic |
| 5 | BL19 | GB | T10 | T6–T10 | ✅ | Sympathetic |
| 6 | BL20 | Spleen | T11 | T6–T8* | ✅* | Sympathetic |
| 7 | BL21 | Stomach | T12 | T6–T9 | ❌ | — |
| 8 | BL22 | Triple Burner | L1 | N/A | 🟡 | Sympathetic |
| 9 | BL23 | Kidney | L2 | T10–L1* | ✅* | Sympathetic |
| 10 | BL25 | LI | L4 | T10–L1 | ❌ | — |
| 11 | BL27 | SI | S1 | T9–T10 | ❌ | — |
| 12 | BL28 | Bladder | S2 | S2–S4 | ✅ | **Parasympath.** |

*BL20 matches via extended lesser splanchnic (T10–T11).
*BL23 matches via extended lumbar splanchnic (T10–L2).

Summary: 9 match, 3 don't (BL22 indeterminate counted as matching
per Dorsher). The 3 non-matching are ALL GI tract Fu organs
displaced caudally. The 9 matching include 8 via sympathetic + 1
via parasympathetic (BL28). The segmental model explains 75%
(9/12) of Back-Shu; the 3 exceptions are the most analytically
valuable output of BL Phase 2.

**Observation 18 — BL28: ONLY PARASYMPATHETIC BACK-SHU.**
Among 9 matching Back-Shu, 8 match through sympathetic pathways
(greater/lesser/least splanchnic → prevertebral ganglia) and 1
through parasympathetic (pelvic splanchnic S2–S4 → detrusor). BL28
is unique because the bladder has DUAL innervation governing two
phases: sympathetic T11–L2 (storage) and parasympathetic S2–S4
(voiding). BL28 at S2 targets the voiding/emptying function
specifically. The storage pathway (T11–L2) is coincidentally
accessed by BL23 (L2, Kidney Shu). This anatomical overlap may
explain the TCM concept that "Kidney governs water" and "Kidney
and Bladder are Interior-Exterior paired" — both organ functions
converge at overlapping spinal segments.

---

## Back-Shu Group — Framework Notes

⚡ **Framework note — Segmental confirmation:** The Back-Shu group
confirms the predicted ~100% segmental contribution, completing the
range observed across Phase 1 Tier A points:
```
0% segmental:     BL67 (S1, unknown), ST36 (PROKR2, molecular),
                  LI4 (PAG descending, supraspinal)
~30-40% segmental: PC6 (NTS-vagal, brainstem convergence)
~50% segmental:    SP6 (dual S2-S4 segmental + PAG non-segmental)
~100% segmental:   Back-Shu ×5 (somatovisceral reflex, textbook)
```
C04's "60-70% segmental" estimate is now empirically bracketed.
The average across these 11 points may approximate 60-70%, but
individual variance spans the full 0-100% range. The segmental
model is not a GENERAL predictor — it is a SPECIFIC predictor for
points where dermatome-viscerotome overlap exists (primarily truncal
and paravertebral points). For distal limb points (ST36, LI4, BL67),
non-segmental mechanisms dominate entirely.
→ Extends: C04 S3.4

⚡ **Framework note — Convergence-mechanism correlation:** The Back-Shu
group has convergence scores of 5-6/8 — the HIGHEST among Phase 1
groups — AND their mechanism is the MOST clearly understood
(somatovisceral reflex, confirmed in spinal preparations). Combined
with BL67 (2/8, mechanism UNKNOWN), this supports a correlation:
higher convergence → clearer mechanism understanding. A09 §2.2's
convergence zone model may primarily predict mechanism CLARITY (how
well we understand WHY a point works) rather than clinical EFFICACY
(whether the point produces clinical outcomes). BL67 proves that
high efficacy can exist with low convergence and unknown mechanism.
→ Extends: A09 S2.2 (convergence predicts mechanism clarity, not
necessarily clinical efficacy)

---

## Sacral Foramina — Baliao Group (八髎) + Coccyx

> The Baliao (八髎, "Eight Bone-Holes") = BL31–BL34. Four bilateral
> points, each entering a posterior sacral foramen to access sacral
> nerve roots S1–S4 directly. The 八 = 4 × 2 sides = 8. The 髎
> character (bone-hole) uniquely describes the anatomical landmark —
> no other standard acupoint naming encodes a bony passage.
>
> **Architecturally unique:** The Baliao are the ONLY standard
> acupoints in the 361-point system that enter anatomical bone
> openings to directly contact nerve roots. All other acupoints
> stimulate at the body surface or within muscle/fascial layers,
> accessing nerves indirectly via dorsal rami or superficial
> branches. The Baliao enter the SACRAL FORAMINA → direct nerve
> root contact → pelvic parasympathetic plexus. This makes them
> the MOST mechanistically transparent points in the Atlas.
>
> **Distinction from BL27–BL30:** The medial back line points
> (BL27–BL30, 1.5 cun lateral to median sacral crest) are at the
> LEVEL of sacral foramina but NOT within them. They stimulate
> dorsal rami on the sacral surface. The Baliao points enter the
> foramina and access the VENTRAL rami / nerve roots directly.

### BL31 — Thượng Liêu (上髎, Shàng Liáo)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Baliao #1**
"Upper Bone-Hole" (上 = upper, 髎 = bone-hole) — at the 1st
posterior sacral foramen. BL31 accesses the S1 nerve root
directly. First of the four Baliao. S1 contributes to the
superior gluteal nerve (L4–S1) and sciatic nerve (L4–S3).
Less commonly used clinically than BL32 (S2) for pelvic
conditions because S1 is at the upper edge of the pelvic
parasympathetic range (S2–S4).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the 1st posterior sacral
foramen. Located midway between the posterior superior iliac
spine (PSIS) and the median sacral crest, at the level of the
1st sacral segment.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular into the foramen.
The posterior sacral foramen is approximately 1.5 cm deep → the
needle enters the bone opening and contacts the S1 nerve root
or its branches within the sacral canal.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Direct S1 nerve root access** via the posterior sacral foramen
- S1 ventral ramus → sciatic nerve (L4–S3), superior gluteal
  (L4–S1), inferior gluteal (L5–S2) contributions
- S1 dorsal ramus → posterior sacral skin and muscle
- S1 is at the UPPER EDGE of pelvic parasympathetic range
  (primarily S2–S4)
- Spinal segment: S1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: the needle passes through gluteal fascia and
periosteum into the sacral foramen. Within the foramen: epidural
fat, nerve root, and vascular plexus.

Vascular: lateral sacral artery branches within the foramen.
Risk of venous plexus puncture within the sacral canal.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → gluteal fascia →
sacral periosteum → foramen → sacral canal contents.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Special: **Baliao group** (八髎, 1st of 4).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL31 (Shangliao 上髎). Traditional functions:
  regulate menstruation, benefit urination, strengthen the lumbar
  region. Part of the Baliao group for pelvic/gynecological
  conditions.

Trigger point: No standard trigger point — foramen points access
  nerve roots, not muscle tissue.

Ashi correspondence: S1 foramen tenderness on deep palpation in
  sacral/pelvic pain.

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** No foramen-entry Marma.

Head zone: S1 dermatome → gluteal/posterior thigh. Within sacral
  nerve territory for pelvic structures.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: not applicable (intra-foraminal, not fascial).

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not applicable (bony foramen).

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡). Lowest of the Baliao
group — S1 is at the edge of pelvic parasympathetic territory.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, menstrual disorders, urinary
  difficulty, leukorrhea
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: sacral pain, irregular menstruation, seminal
  emission

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar/sacral pain
- Menstrual disorders, dysmenorrhea
- Urogenital: urinary difficulty, leukorrhea

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated** as isolated point. BL31
appears in Baliao group protocols but not individually tested.
S1 is less critical for pelvic parasympathetic access than S2–S3.

#### An toàn

**Foramen-specific risks:** needle enters the sacral canal via
  the posterior foramen. Risk of epidural puncture if inserted
  too deeply. Vascular plexus within the sacral canal — risk of
  hematoma. Nerve root damage possible with aggressive technique.
  Standard practice: gentle perpendicular insertion, stop at
  first De Qi (nerve root contact).

Pregnancy: **Caution.** Baliao stimulation may influence pelvic
  autonomic function. Avoid in early/mid pregnancy. May be used
  during labor (BL32 specifically — see below).

---

### BL32 — Thứ Liêu (次髎, Cì Liáo)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Baliao #2**

Name 次髎 (Cì Liáo) = "Second Bone-Hole" (次 = second/next, 髎 =
bone-hole) — at the 2nd posterior sacral foramen. BL32 accesses
the S2 nerve root directly. THE MOST CLINICALLY USED Baliao point
and the MOST IMPORTANT sacral acupoint for pelvic/gynecological
conditions. S2 is the PRIMARY segment for the pelvic splanchnic
nerves (nervi erigentes, S2–S4) and the pudendal nerve (S2–S4).
BL32 at the S2 foramen provides the most direct access to the
parasympathetic innervation of the bladder, uterus, and pelvic
floor in the entire Atlas.

BL32 = the traditional equivalent of modern sacral nerve
stimulation (SNS). Modern InterStim devices (Medtronic, FDA
approved 1997 for overactive bladder/incontinence) typically
target the S3 foramen, but the principle is identical: electrode
or needle enters a posterior sacral foramen → stimulates the
sacral nerve root → modulates pelvic visceral function. BL32 at
S2 has the LONGEST clinical history — at least 1,700 years of
documented use for pelvic conditions.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the 2nd posterior sacral
foramen. Located between the PSIS and the median sacral crest,
at the level of the 2nd sacral segment.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.5 cun perpendicular into the foramen.
The 2nd foramen is typically the largest of the four posterior
sacral foramina, allowing slightly deeper insertion. The needle
contacts the S2 nerve root or its branches within the sacral
canal. De Qi at BL32: characteristic radiating sensation to the
perineum, genitalia, or lower abdomen — reflecting S2 pudendal/
pelvic splanchnic territory.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Direct S2 nerve root access** via the 2nd posterior sacral
  foramen — the PRIMARY segment for pelvic parasympathetic
  innervation
- S2 ventral ramus → pelvic splanchnic nerves (S2–S4, but S2
  provides the LARGEST contribution to the pelvic plexus)
- S2 → pudendal nerve (S2–S4): external urethral sphincter,
  external anal sphincter, perineal sensation, clitoral/penile
  dorsal nerve
- Pelvic plexus targets: detrusor muscle (bladder voiding),
  uterine smooth muscle, rectal smooth muscle, erectile tissue
  vascular smooth muscle
- BL32 at S2 foramen = MOST direct access to all of these
  targets in the entire Atlas
- Spinal segment: S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: needle passes through gluteal fascia → sacral
periosteum → into foramen → sacral canal contents. Within the
foramen: epidural fat, nerve root sheath, vascular plexus.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: lateral sacral artery and venous plexus within the
sacral canal. Batson's venous plexus communication.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → gluteal fascia →
sacral periosteum → foramen → sacral canal.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
Back-Shu: **No** — NOT an organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No
Special: **Baliao group** (八髎, 2nd of 4). MOST clinically used
  Baliao. Combined in labor pain protocol: LI4+SP6+BL32+GB21
  (Larki 2025 MA, AP-04). Combined with BL28 (Bladder Shu, S2)
  for urinary disorders — BL28 stimulates S2 dorsal ramus on the
  sacral surface while BL32 enters the S2 foramen directly.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL32 (Ciliao 次髎) — Baliao #2. Traditional
  functions: regulate menstruation (primary), benefit urination,
  strengthen the lumbar region, promote labor. The MOST
  frequently prescribed Baliao for gynecological conditions.

Trigger point: Not applicable — foramen point, not muscular.
  However, the S2 nerve root accessed by BL32 innervates the
  pelvic floor muscles (levator ani, coccygeus) → pelvic floor
  trigger point treatment may use S2 as the access level.

Ashi correspondence: deep S2 foramen tenderness on palpation in
  dysmenorrhea and pelvic pain. The tenderness is likely nerve
  root sensitization rather than muscle tenderness.

Marma point: **No precise correspondence.** No foramen-entry Marma
  in standard Ayurvedic systems.

Head zone: S2 dermatome → perineal, genital. Bladder viscerotome
  S2–S4. Uterus viscerotome includes S2–S4. BL32 at S2 is
  WITHIN the pelvic parasympathetic viscerotome for bladder,
  uterus, and rectum. **✅ MATCH** for pelvic viscera.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: not directly applicable (intra-foraminal). The
  pelvic floor forms a fascial diaphragm connected to the Deep
  Front Line — S2 nerve root innervates this structure.

Neurogenic spot: **Predicted.** Kim 2017 model predicts S2–S4
  neurogenic spots in bladder/pelvic inflammation. S2 foramen
  area would be within the predicted zone.

Cupping point: not applicable (foramen entry).

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, Ashi, Head zone, neurogenic 🟡
predicted). Higher than other Baliao because S2 is centrally
positioned in the pelvic parasympathetic range.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: dysmenorrhea, irregular menstruation,
  leukorrhea, urinary difficulty, lumbar/sacral pain
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: difficult labor, lower abdominal pain,
  seminal emission, constipation
- Classical emphasis on gynecological conditions — BL32 is the
  single most frequently cited Baliao for menstrual disorders

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Dysmenorrhea (primary modern indication)
- Irregular menstruation, leukorrhea
- Urinary disorders: frequency, retention, incontinence
- Lumbar/sacral pain
- Labor pain management

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- BL32 is the MOST prescribed Baliao — dominant in protocols
  for dysmenorrhea, labor pain, and urogenital disorders
- Labor pain protocol: LI4+SP6+BL32+GB21 (Larki 2025 MA:
  pain reduction MD -1.19 to -2.32, GRADE low-moderate, AP-04)
- Combined with SP6 for dysmenorrhea — dual S2–S4 access:
  BL32 (direct nerve root via foramen) + SP6 (segmental at
  posterior tibial nerve → S2–S4 spinal level)
- Combined with BL28 for urinary disorders: BL28 (dorsal ramus
  on sacral surface) + BL32 (ventral ramus via foramen) =
  complementary access to S2

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Labor pain — 🟡 Plausible**

Larki et al. 2025 (meta-analysis, 37 RCTs, ~3,900 participants,
B02): LI4+SP6+BL32+GB21 acupressure protocol for labor pain.
Pain reduction: MD -1.19 to -2.32 vs touch/sham/no-intervention
(GRADE: low to moderate certainty). BL32 is included in virtually
all labor acupressure protocols. 🟡 because multi-point protocol
— cannot isolate BL32's contribution. Mechanism: S2 pelvic
splanchnic modulation of uterine smooth muscle + S2 pudendal
nerve gate control for perineal pain.
→ Cross-ref: B02 S4, AP-04 (SP6 evidence)

**2. Dysmenorrhea — 🟡 Plausible**

BL32 is among the most frequently prescribed acupoints for
primary dysmenorrhea. S2 pelvic splanchnic nerves innervate the
uterus — stimulation may modulate uterine contraction amplitude.
Multiple RCTs include BL32 in protocols for dysmenorrhea, but
usually combined with SP6, CV4, or other points. 🟡 — plausible
anatomical mechanism, no BL32-isolated trial.

**3. Urinary disorders — 🟡 Plausible**

S2 nerve root → pelvic splanchnic → detrusor (bladder voiding)
and pudendal → external urethral sphincter (continence). Modern
sacral neuromodulation (InterStim, FDA 1997) validates that sacral
nerve stimulation at S2–S3 effectively treats overactive bladder
and urinary incontinence. BL32 at S2 accesses the same neural
substrate. 🟡 because evidence is for modern SNM (typically S3),
not specifically BL32 acupuncture.

**4. Modern sacral neuromodulation comparison:**
InterStim typically targets S3 foramen (BL33 territory). BL32
targets S2. Both access pelvic parasympathetic nerve roots. The
choice of S3 over S2 in modern SNM was determined by clinical
trials showing optimal efficacy/safety ratio at S3, not because
S2 is ineffective. BL32 at S2 has ~1,700 years of documented
clinical use for pelvic conditions — it is the TRADITIONAL
precursor to modern SNM.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

BL32 De Qi is distinctive: patients report radiating sensation to
perineum, genitalia, or lower abdomen — directly reflecting S2
pudendal/pelvic splanchnic territory. This is NOT local muscle
aching (as with Back-Shu points) but NERVE ROOT stimulation
response. The quality of De Qi at foramen points differs from
surface points and can serve as a confirmation that the needle
has entered the foramen and contacted the nerve root.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Mechanism: DIRECT sacral nerve root stimulation at S2 foramen.**

```
DIRECT ACCESS MODEL:
  BL32 needle enters 2nd posterior sacral foramen →
  contacts S2 nerve root / branches →
  S2 pelvic splanchnic nerves → pelvic plexus →
  bladder detrusor, uterine smooth muscle, rectal smooth muscle
  + S2 pudendal nerve → pelvic floor, sphincters, perineum
  ✅ DIRECT nerve root stimulation (NOT somatovisceral reflex)
  
  DISTINCTION FROM BACK-SHU:
  Back-Shu (BL13-BL28): needle in muscle → dorsal ramus →
    dorsal horn → convergent sympathetic outflow → organ
    = INDIRECT (somatovisceral reflex, ~2 synapses)
  Baliao (BL31-34): needle in foramen → nerve root → target
    = DIRECT (nerve root contact, ~1 synapse)
```

BL32 demonstrates the clearest mechanism in the Atlas: the needle
physically contacts the nerve root that innervates the target
organ. No convergence theory or reflex arc required. This is
the traditional equivalent of percutaneous nerve root stimulation
— a standard procedure in modern interventional neurology.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3, A07 S1.6

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.8–1.5 cun perpendicular INTO the foramen. The
  2nd posterior sacral foramen is palpable as a depression medial
  to the PSIS. De Qi: characteristic radiation to perineum/
  genitalia (S2 territory) confirms nerve root contact. EA:
  commonly used at BL32 bilaterally for dysmenorrhea and labor
  pain. 2 Hz EA at BL32 for uterine contraction modulation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Chỉ áp (B02):
  Acupressure at BL32: the labor pain protocol
  (LI4+SP6+BL32+GB21) uses firm pressure over the 2nd sacral
  foramen during contractions. Partner-administrable, no equipment
  needed.
→ Cross-ref: B02 S4

Cứu (B08):
  Moxa over the sacral foramina region: indirect moxa (moxa box
  or moxa stick held above the sacral surface) covering BL31–34
  bilaterally for chronic pelvic conditions. Direct foramen moxa
  less common.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

#### An toàn

**Foramen-specific risks:** same as BL31 — epidural puncture,
  venous plexus hematoma, nerve root injury with aggressive
  technique. The 2nd foramen is typically the largest, reducing
  the risk of periosteal contact during entry.

Pregnancy: **CONTRAINDICATED in early/mid pregnancy** (sacral
  parasympathetic stimulation may promote uterine contractions).
  **Used therapeutically during active labor** — LI4+SP6+BL32+GB21
  protocol specifically for labor pain management. The same
  mechanism (uterine smooth muscle modulation) that makes BL32
  contraindicated before term makes it therapeutic during labor.

---

### BL33 — Trung Liêu (中髎, Zhōng Liáo)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Baliao #3**
"Middle Bone-Hole" (中 = middle, 髎 = bone-hole) — at the 3rd
posterior sacral foramen. BL33 accesses the S3 nerve root
directly. S3 is the PRIMARY target for modern sacral
neuromodulation (InterStim, Medtronic) — the S3 foramen was
selected in clinical trials as the optimal level for overactive
bladder/urinary incontinence treatment. BL33 is thus the closest
traditional equivalent to the modern InterStim electrode position.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the 3rd posterior sacral
foramen.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.2 cun perpendicular into the foramen.
The 3rd foramen is smaller than the 2nd.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Direct S3 nerve root access** via the 3rd posterior sacral
  foramen
- S3 = central pelvic splanchnic contribution → parasympathetic
  to bladder, rectum, uterus
- S3 = pudendal nerve contribution → sphincter control, perineal
  sensation
- S3 is the target of InterStim (FDA-approved sacral
  neuromodulation): electrodes placed at S3 foramen for
  overactive bladder, urinary incontinence, fecal incontinence
- Spinal segment: S3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: gluteal fascia → sacral periosteum → foramen →
sacral canal.

Vascular: lateral sacral artery branches within foramen.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → gluteal fascia → foramen.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No. Special: **Baliao group** (八髎, 3rd of 4).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL33 (Zhongliao 中髎). Traditional functions:
  regulate the Lower Burner, benefit urination, treat constipation.

Trigger point: not applicable (foramen point).

Ashi correspondence: S3 foramen tenderness in pelvic/urogenital
  conditions.

Marma point: **No correspondence.** No foramen-entry Marma.

Head zone: S3 dermatome → perineal. Within pelvic parasympathetic
  viscerotome (S2–S4). Bladder, rectum viscerotome.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: not directly applicable.

Neurogenic spot: predicted at S2–S4 for pelvic pathology.

Cupping point: not applicable.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Head zone 🟡).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: urinary difficulty, constipation,
  leukorrhea, lumbar pain
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lumbar/sacral pain, irregular menstruation

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Urogenital: urinary difficulty, frequency, constipation
- Lumbar/sacral pain
- Menstrual disorders

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Overactive bladder / incontinence — 🟢 Confirmed (for S3
sacral nerve stimulation, not BL33 acupuncture specifically)**

Modern sacral neuromodulation (InterStim) at S3 has Level 1
evidence for overactive bladder and urinary/fecal incontinence
(multiple RCTs, FDA approved 1997). This validates that S3 nerve
root stimulation modulates pelvic visceral function. BL33
acupuncture at the same foramen accesses the same nerve root but
with different stimulation parameters. The evidence supports the
PRINCIPLE (S3 stimulation works) but not the specific MODALITY
(acupuncture vs implanted electrode). 🟢 for the nerve target;
the traditional point predates the modern validation by ~1,700
years.

**2. Other pelvic indications — 🔴 Unvalidated** as acupuncture.

#### An toàn

**Foramen-specific risks:** same as BL31/BL32.

Pregnancy: **Caution.** Same pelvic parasympathetic concerns.

---

### BL34 — Hạ Liêu (下髎, Xià Liáo)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Baliao #4**
"Lower Bone-Hole" (下 = lower, 髎 = bone-hole) — at the 4th
posterior sacral foramen. BL34 accesses the S4 nerve root. The
LOWEST sacral foramen point. S4 is at the caudal end of the
pelvic splanchnic range — minimal parasympathetic contribution
compared to S2–S3. S4 primarily contributes to the pudendal
nerve (perineal sensation, anal sphincter) and coccygeal plexus
(S4–Co1).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the 4th posterior sacral
foramen.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular into the foramen.
The 4th foramen is the smallest. Sacral hiatus lies just below
(S4–S5).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Direct S4 nerve root access** via the 4th posterior sacral
  foramen
- S4 = caudal pelvic splanchnic contribution (minimal vs S2–S3)
- S4 = pudendal nerve contribution → perineal/anorectal
  sensation, external anal sphincter
- S4–Co1 = coccygeal plexus → anococcygeal nerve
- Spinal segment: S4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: gluteal fascia → sacral periosteum → foramen.

Vascular: inferior lateral sacral artery branches.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → gluteal fascia → foramen.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No. Special: **Baliao group** (八髎, 4th of 4).
BL34 = last Baliao (lowest foramen).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL34 (Xialiao 下髎). Traditional functions:
  regulate the Lower Burner, benefit urination, treat lumbar pain.
  Least used Baliao.

Trigger point: not applicable (foramen point).

Ashi correspondence: S4 foramen tenderness — less commonly
  assessed than S2–S3.

Marma point: **No correspondence.**

Head zone: S4 dermatome → perianal. Rectum/prostate viscerotome
  S2–S4. Within range.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: not applicable.

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not applicable.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Head zone 🟡).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lower abdominal pain, urinary difficulty,
  lumbar/sacral pain
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: constipation, leukorrhea

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar/sacral pain
- Urinary difficulty
- Lower abdominal pain

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL34 has no individual
evidence. Least used Baliao. S4 contributes minimally to pelvic
parasympathetic — S2–S3 are the primary pelvic nerve root targets.

#### An toàn

**Foramen-specific risks:** same as other Baliao. The 4th foramen
  is adjacent to the sacral hiatus — avoid directing the needle
  inferiorly into the sacral canal.

Pregnancy: **Caution.**

---

### BL35 — Hội Dương (会阳, Huì Yáng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Meeting of Yang" (会 = meeting/gathering, 阳 = Yang) — lateral
to the tip of the coccyx, 0.5 cun lateral. NOT a Baliao point
(not in a foramen). NOT an organ Back-Shu. BL35 is at the
terminal end of the BL sacral trajectory, between the medial back
line (BL30, S4) and the posterior gluteal trajectory (BL36,
gluteal fold). The coccyx tip is innervated by the coccygeal nerve
(S4–Co1) — the lowest named spinal nerve.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 0.5 cun lateral to the tip
of the coccyx.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular or oblique
superiorly. Coccygeal periosteum is very superficial.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Anococcygeal nerve (from coccygeal plexus, S4–Co1)
- Inferior cluneal nerves (S1–S3) contribute to the region
- Pudendal nerve terminal branches nearby (inferior rectal nerve)
- Spinal segment: S4–Co1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: gluteal fascia at coccyx insertion. Anococcygeal
ligament (raphé between coccyx and anus).

Vascular: median sacral artery terminal branch. Inferior rectal
artery branches.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → gluteus maximus
insertion → coccygeal periosteum. Thin tissue at the coccyx tip.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL35 (Huiyang 会阳). Traditional functions: treat
  hemorrhoids, diarrhea, impotence. "Meeting of Yang" — the BL
  (most Yang channel, Taiyang) converges at the lowest point
  before transitioning to the posterior limb trajectory.

Trigger point: **Possible.** Coccygeal attachments of gluteus
  maximus and coccygeus — tender in coccygodynia.

Ashi correspondence: coccygeal tenderness in coccygodynia and
  pelvic floor dysfunction. Direct correlation with coccyx pain.

Marma point: **Possible.** Guda Marma (anorectal junction) is
  in this territory.

Head zone: S4–Co1 → perianal/coccygeal. Rectum viscerotome
  S2–S4.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL sacral terminus — coccyx is the inferior
  attachment of the dural tube / spinal meninges.

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not applicable (insufficient tissue).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi, Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡
→ possible 4/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: hemorrhoids, diarrhea, bloody stool
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: impotence, leukorrhea, hemorrhoids

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Hemorrhoids (primary traditional indication)
- Diarrhea
- Impotence
- Leukorrhea

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL35 has no individual
evidence base. Hemorrhoid treatment at the coccyx level has
anatomical plausibility (inferior rectal nerve/artery territory)
but no trial data.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — coccygeal level.

Vascular risk: inferior rectal artery branches. Minimal at
  standard depth.

Contamination risk: proximity to the anus requires strict
  antiseptic technique.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication but coccygeal area may
  be uncomfortable in late pregnancy (lithotomy position).

---

### Batch 7 Observations

**Observation 19 — BALIAO = DIRECT NEURAL ACCESS.**
The Baliao (BL31–BL34) are architecturally unique in the Atlas:
the ONLY standard acupoints that enter anatomical bone openings
(posterior sacral foramina) to directly contact nerve roots. All
other acupoints stimulate at the body surface or within muscle/
fascial layers, accessing nerves indirectly. Mechanism comparison:

| Feature | Back-Shu (BL13–BL28) | Baliao (BL31–BL34) |
|---------|---------------------|-------------------|
| Access | Dorsal ramus (muscle) | Nerve root (foramen) |
| Synapses | ~2 (dorsal horn → ANS) | ~1 (root → target) |
| Mechanism | Somatovisceral reflex | Direct stimulation |
| Ambiguity | Which pathway? | Minimal |
| De Qi | Local aching | Radiating to perineum |

The Baliao are the MOST mechanistically transparent points in the
Atlas: the needle physically contacts the nerve root that
innervates the target organ. No convergence theory needed.

**Observation 20 — BL32 = TRADITIONAL SACRAL NEUROMODULATION.**
Modern sacral neuromodulation (InterStim, FDA 1997) uses an
implanted electrode at S3 foramen (BL33 territory) for overactive
bladder/incontinence. Traditional BL32 uses a needle at S2 foramen
for pelvic/gynecological conditions. Same principle: device enters
posterior sacral foramen → stimulates sacral nerve root →
modulates pelvic visceral function. The traditional approach
predates the modern by ~1,700 years. The choice of S3 (modern) vs
S2 (traditional primary) may reflect different optimization
targets: S3 optimal for bladder continence (modern trials), S2
optimal for dysmenorrhea/labor (traditional experience). Both work
because S2–S3–S4 form a CONTINUOUS parasympathetic nerve root
group to the pelvic plexus.

---

## Posterior Gluteal / Thigh / Popliteal (BL36–BL40)

Transition from sacral/coccygeal territory to the posterior limb.
The sciatic nerve (L4–S3) — the largest nerve in the body — is
the dominant neural substrate for this entire region. The BL
channel trajectory follows the sciatic nerve from the gluteal
fold (BL36) through the posterior thigh (BL37) to the popliteal
fossa (BL38–BL40), where the sciatic bifurcates: BL39 tracks the
common peroneal nerve laterally, BL40 tracks the tibial nerve
centrally. This region contains BL40 (Weizhong), one of the most
clinically important acupoints in all of TCM — a Four Command
Point, He-Sea, Lower He-Sea of Bladder, and classical
bloodletting site.

---

### BL36 — Thừa Phù (承扶, Chéng Fú)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Support" (承 = receive/support, 扶 = hold up) — at the center
of the gluteal fold (infragluteal crease). The sciatic nerve is
at its most superficial at this level, emerging from beneath the
inferior border of the gluteus maximus. BL36 is the first BL
point on the posterior limb after the sacral/coccygeal territory
(BL35).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): at the midpoint of the
gluteal fold (inferior gluteal crease), with the patient prone.

Needle depth range: 1.0–2.0 cun (~2.5–5 cm) perpendicular.
Deep needling may contact the sciatic nerve — patient reports
electric sensation radiating down the posterior thigh.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Posterior femoral cutaneous nerve** (S1–S3) — inferior
  cluneal branches at the gluteal fold. Primary cutaneous
  innervation.
- **Sciatic nerve** (L4–S3) — runs deep to the gluteus maximus,
  emerging at the inferior border. At BL36, the sciatic nerve is
  at its most superficial — the gluteus maximus inferior border
  is the anatomical landmark where the nerve becomes accessible.
- Inferior gluteal nerve (L5–S2) — motor to gluteus maximus
- Spinal segment: S1–S3 (cutaneous), L4–S3 (sciatic deep)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: gluteal fascia continuous with deep fascia of the
thigh. Transition between gluteal compartment and posterior thigh
compartment.

Vascular: inferior gluteal artery branches, first perforating
artery (from profunda femoris).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue (often substantial at
gluteal fold) → gluteus maximus inferior border → hamstring
origins (long head of biceps femoris + semitendinosus +
semimembranosus from ischial tuberosity).

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL36 (Chengfu 承扶). Traditional functions: treat
  hemorrhoids, lumbar/gluteal pain, sciatic pain, leg weakness.
  "Support" — the gluteal fold supports body weight in sitting.

Trigger point: **Yes.** Gluteus maximus inferior border trigger
  points. Hamstring origin (ischial tuberosity) trigger points
  (Travell & Simons). Referred pain: posterior thigh, gluteal
  region.

Ashi correspondence: gluteal fold tenderness in sciatica,
  hamstring strains, piriformis syndrome. Commonly tender with
  prolonged sitting.

Marma point: **Possible.** Nitamba Marma (buttock region) in the
  gluteal territory. Not precise correspondence.

Head zone: S1–S3 dermatome. Bladder viscerotome S2–S4 partially
  overlaps.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line (SBL) — gluteal fold is
  the transition from sacrolumbar fascia to the hamstring
  compartment.

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not standard.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, Trigger Point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Marma 🟡 → possible 5/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: hemorrhoids, sciatic pain, gluteal pain,
  difficulty walking
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: leg weakness, numbness of lower limb

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Sciatic pain (primary modern indication)
- Hemorrhoids
- Gluteal pain
- Low back pain (radiating)
- Lower limb weakness/paralysis

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL36 has no individual
evidence base. Appears in multi-point sciatica protocols (GB30 +
BL36 + BL40 variation) but is never isolated.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: inferior gluteal artery branches. Minimal at
  standard depth.

Nerve risk: **Sciatic nerve.** Deep needling may contact the
  sciatic nerve trunk — produces strong electric radiating
  sensation. Avoid repeated aggressive stimulation when the
  patient reports radiating sensation down the leg.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL37 — Ân Môn (殷门, Yīn Mén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Gate of Abundance" (殷 = abundant/rich, 门 = gate) — on the
posterior midline of the thigh, 6 cun below BL36. The sciatic
nerve runs deep within the hamstring compartment at this level,
between biceps femoris and adductor magnus. BL37 is the only
standard acupoint in the mid-posterior thigh.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 6 cun below BL36, on the
line connecting BL36 and BL40, on the posterior thigh.

Needle depth range: 1.0–2.0 cun (~2.5–5 cm) perpendicular.
The sciatic nerve is deep (3–5 cm depending on body habitus).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Posterior femoral cutaneous nerve** (S1–S3) — continues
  distally along the posterior thigh midline. Primary cutaneous
  innervation.
- **Sciatic nerve** (L4–S3) — within the posterior thigh
  compartment, deep to the long head of biceps femoris.
  Muscular branches to the hamstrings arise from the sciatic
  trunk at this level.
- Spinal segment: S1–S2 (cutaneous), L4–S3 (sciatic deep)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: fascia lata (deep fascia of the thigh),
continuous with the gluteal fascia above.

Vascular: perforating arteries (from profunda femoris artery,
2nd and 3rd perforators cross this region).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → fascia lata → hamstrings:
semitendinosus (medial), biceps femoris long head (lateral),
semimembranosus (deep medial). Sciatic nerve runs between biceps
femoris and adductor magnus.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL37 (Yinmen 殷门). Traditional functions: treat
  lumbar/posterior thigh pain, channel obstruction.

Trigger point: **Yes.** Hamstring trigger points — semitendinosus
  and biceps femoris long head (Travell & Simons). Referred pain:
  posterior thigh, popliteal region, sometimes gluteal.

Ashi correspondence: posterior thigh tenderness in hamstring
  injuries, sciatic pain, lumbar disc referred pain.

Marma point: **Possible.** Urvi Marma (thigh Marma) is in the
  mid-thigh region. Classified as Mamsa (muscle) Marma.

Head zone: S1–S2 dermatome at posterior mid-thigh.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line (SBL) — hamstring segment
  between ischial tuberosity and popliteal fossa. BL37 is at
  the midpoint of this continuous myofascial track.

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not standard.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, Trigger Point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Marma 🟡 → possible 5/8.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain radiating to thigh, difficulty
  bending forward, posterior thigh pain
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: sciatic pain, posterior thigh numbness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Low back pain (radiating to posterior thigh)
- Posterior thigh pain
- Lower limb weakness

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL37 has no individual
evidence. Appears occasionally in sciatica protocols as a
trajectory point between BL36 and BL40.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: perforating arteries from profunda femoris cross
  this region. Standard depth is safe.

Nerve risk: deep needling may approach the sciatic nerve trunk,
  though at mid-thigh the nerve is deeper than at BL36.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL38 — Phù Khích (浮郄, Fú Xì)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Superficial Cleft" (浮 = floating/superficial, 郄 = cleft/
crevice) — 1 cun above BL39, on the medial side of the tendon
of biceps femoris at the popliteal fossa border. The name
describes a superficial depression (cleft) at the superolateral
popliteal fossa. Transition point from posterior thigh (sciatic
trunk) to popliteal fossa (bifurcation zone). One of the least-
used BL points in clinical practice.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1 cun above BL39, on the
medial side of the tendon of biceps femoris.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.5 cun (~1.2–3.8 cm) perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Common peroneal nerve** (L4–S2) — after the sciatic
  bifurcation, courses along the medial border of the biceps
  femoris tendon. BL38 is directly over this nerve course.
- Posterior femoral cutaneous nerve terminal branches
- Lateral sural cutaneous nerve (from common peroneal)
- Spinal segment: L4–S2 (deep), S1–S2 (cutaneous)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: deep fascia at the superolateral popliteal fossa.
Biceps femoris tendon is the lateral boundary of the popliteal
diamond.

Vascular: superior lateral genicular artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → biceps femoris
tendon (medial border) → popliteal fat → common peroneal nerve.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL38 (Fuxi 浮郄). Traditional functions: treat
  knee numbness, popliteal sinew stiffness, constipation
  (channel-level BL intestinal function).

Trigger point: **Possible.** Distal biceps femoris trigger points
  near the fibular head insertion. Referred pain: lateral knee,
  popliteal region.

Ashi correspondence: tenderness at the lateral popliteal border
  in biceps femoris tendinopathy. Not well-documented.

Marma point: **Possible.** Janu Marma (knee joint) broadly
  covers the popliteal region.

Head zone: S1–S2 dermatome at lateral popliteal.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line (SBL) — biceps femoris
  tendon is the lateral SBL path that continues to the fibular
  head.

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not standard.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Anatomy Trains, Head zone 🟡).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: knee numbness, popliteal sinew stiffness,
  inability to flex/extend
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: constipation, lower limb heaviness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Knee numbness/stiffness
- Popliteal pain
- Constipation (channel-level indication)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL38 has no individual
evidence. One of the least-used BL points clinically.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: superior lateral genicular artery. Minimal.

Nerve risk: common peroneal nerve runs adjacent — deep needling
  medially could contact this nerve (produces foot dorsiflexion
  sensation). Avoid repeated stimulation.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL39 — Ủy Dương (委阳, Wěi Yáng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Entrusted Yang" (委 = entrust/delegate, 阳 = Yang) — at the
lateral end of the popliteal crease, medial to the tendon of
biceps femoris. BL39 is the **Lower He-Sea of Triple Burner
(TE)** — the 5th of 6 Lower He-Sea points documented in the
Atlas (ST36/Stomach, ST37/LI, ST39/SI, GB34/GB already done;
BL40/BL remaining). The "entrusted" name may reflect the Lower
He-Sea concept: the Triple Burner organ "entrusts" (delegates) its Lower
He-Sea function to the BL channel because TE is an arm channel
with no He-Sea on the lower limb.

The Triple Burner "organ" (三焦) is 有名無形 ("name without form") — the
same philosophical problem documented at BL22 (Back-Shu of the Triple Burner at
L1). BL22 and BL39 are the two BL channel points assigned to an
organ that has no discrete anatomy. BL22 accesses the Triple Burner via
paravertebral dorsal ramus at L1 (renal/adrenal territory).
BL39 accesses the Triple Burner from the popliteal fossa — the mechanism must
be entirely non-segmental.

Naming pair: BL39 委阳 (Weiyang) and BL40 委中 (Weizhong) share
the character 委 (bend/crook of the knee). 阳 = lateral side;
中 = center. Anatomically: BL39 lateral (common peroneal nerve),
BL40 central (tibial nerve).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): at the lateral end of the
popliteal crease, on the medial border of the tendon of biceps
femoris.

Anatomical landmarks: with the knee slightly flexed, palpate the
biceps femoris tendon at its insertion on the fibular head. BL39
is on the medial side of this tendon, at the level of the
popliteal crease. BL38 is 1 cun above; BL40 is medial at the
crease center.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.5 cun (~1.2–3.8 cm) perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Common peroneal nerve** (L4–S2) — at the lateral popliteal
  fossa, the common peroneal nerve courses along the medial
  border of the biceps femoris tendon before wrapping around
  the fibular neck. BL39 is directly over this nerve.
- Lateral sural cutaneous nerve (branch of common peroneal) —
  sensory to lateral calf
- Posterior femoral cutaneous nerve terminal branches
- Spinal segment: L4–S2 (common peroneal), S1–S2 (cutaneous)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: deep fascia at the lateral popliteal fossa.
Biceps femoris tendon is the lateral boundary of the popliteal
diamond.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: superior lateral genicular artery (branch of popliteal
artery). Small saphenous vein lateral tributaries.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → biceps femoris
tendon (medial border) → popliteal fat → common peroneal nerve
→ lateral head of gastrocnemius origin → knee joint capsule
(posterolateral).

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: **No** — BL39 is NOT a Five Shu point of the BL
channel.
Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

**Lower He-Sea of Triple Burner (三焦之下合穴):**
Lingshu Ch.4 (邪氣臟腑病形): "三焦者...下合於委陽" — "Triple
Burner's Lower He-Sea is at Weiyang." This is the 5th of 6
Lower He-Sea points in the Atlas.

The Lingshu principle (六腑皆出足三阳): all six Fu organs have
their Lower He-Sea on the three Yang foot channels (ST, GB, BL),
ensuring every Fu organ has visceral treatment access via the
lower limbs. BL39 is the only Lower He-Sea hosted on the BL
channel for a non-BL organ.

Lower He-Sea distribution:

| Fu organ | Channel | Lower He-Sea | Host | Same? |
|----------|---------|-------------|------|-------|
| Stomach | ST (leg) | ST36 | ST | Yes |
| LI | LI (arm) | ST37 | ST | No |
| SI | SI (arm) | ST39 | ST | No |
| GB | GB (leg) | GB34 | GB | Yes |
| **TE** | **TE (arm)** | **BL39** | **BL** | **No** |
| Bladder | BL (leg) | BL40 | BL | Yes |

Pattern: all 3 arm Yang channels (LI, SI, TE) have their Lower
He-Sea on a different leg channel. All 3 leg Yang channels have
their Lower He-Sea on their OWN channel (He-Sea = Lower He-Sea
overlap). ST hosts 3 (1 own + 2 arm organs), BL hosts 2 (1 own
+ 1 arm organ), GB hosts 1 (own only).
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST36/ST37/ST39, AP-11 GB34 Observation 19

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL39 (Weiyang 委阳). Traditional functions: treat
  urinary disorders (Triple Burner water metabolism), lumbar stiffness,
  axillary swelling. Lower He-Sea function: treats Triple Burner-related
  conditions — water metabolism, fluid distribution, and the
  "waterway" (水道) function of the Triple Burner.

Trigger point: **Yes.** Biceps femoris tendon trigger points at
  the distal insertion (Travell & Simons). Referred pain: lateral
  and posterior knee, lateral popliteal fossa.

Ashi correspondence: lateral popliteal tenderness in knee
  conditions, especially with biceps femoris tendinopathy or
  lateral knee instability.

Marma point: **Yes.** Janu Marma (knee joint Marma). The
  posterior/lateral knee falls within this Sandhi (joint) Marma
  territory.

Head zone: L5–S2 at lateral popliteal. The Triple Burner has no clear
  viscerotome (no discrete organ). The Head zone at BL39 does NOT
  correspond to any Triple Burner-specific visceral territory — consistent
  with the Triple Burner's 有名無形 status. The Lower He-Sea mechanism for the Triple Burner
  must be entirely non-segmental.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Superficial Back Line (SBL) — the biceps femoris
  tendon at the lateral popliteal fossa is the lateral SBL track
  toward the fibular head.

Neurogenic spot: no specific data.

Cupping point: not standard.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, Trigger Point, Ashi, Marma,
Anatomy Trains).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Lingshu Ch.4: Lower He-Sea of the Triple Burner — for Triple Burner Fu organ disorders
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: urinary difficulty, distension of lower
  abdomen, lumbar/back stiffness, axillary swelling
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: enuresis, edema, abdominal fullness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Urinary disorders (dysuria, urinary retention) — Lower He-Sea
  of the Triple Burner function
- Lumbar stiffness
- Lower abdominal pain/distension
- Edema (Triple Burner water metabolism function)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- Combined with BL28 (Back-Shu of BL at S2) and CV3 (Front-Mu
  of BL) for urinary disorders — three-level approach: Back-Shu
  (segmental) + Front-Mu (segmental) + Lower He-Sea
  (non-segmental)
- Combined with TE5 (Luo-Connecting of the Triple Burner at forearm) for
  TE-channel protocol: TE5 (arm) + BL39 (leg Lower He-Sea) for
  fluid metabolism disorders
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Urinary disorders — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional Lower He-Sea indication for Triple Burner-related fluid
conditions. No RCTs testing BL39 specifically for urinary
disorders. Modern urinary protocols prefer BL28 (segmental
S2 access), CV3 (Front-Mu), and SP6 (three Yin meeting) over
BL39. The Triple Burner's role in "water metabolism" is a TCM functional
concept without direct anatomical correlate.

**2. Lumbar stiffness — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Local popliteal indication with no individual evidence. BL40
(adjacent, centrally located) dominates lumbar treatment at the
popliteal fossa.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

No documented disease-specific sensitization. BL39 does not show
the dramatic LBP-associated tenderness seen at BL40 — the
lateral popliteal fossa is less reactive than the central fossa
in lumbar conditions. May show sensitization in lateral knee
conditions (biceps femoris tendinopathy).

#### Cơ chế giải thích (Proposed Mechanistic Explanations)

**Lower He-Sea of the Triple Burner — mechanistic problem:**
BL39 (common peroneal nerve, L4–S2) is assigned to treat
conditions of the Triple Burner, an organ with no discrete anatomy and
therefore no viscerotome. The Back-Shu of the Triple Burner (BL22 at L1)
accesses the renal/adrenal territory — a plausible autonomic
target for "water metabolism." But BL39 at the popliteal fossa
has no segmental or anatomic pathway to any Triple Burner-like structure.

Possible mechanism: if BL39 modulates pelvic/abdominal fluid
metabolism, it must work via supraspinal pathways (common
peroneal → L4–S2 dorsal horn → ascending → brainstem autonomic
centers → descending modulation of renal/bladder function). This
is the same supraspinal mechanism proposed for all non-segmental
Lower He-Sea points (see Observation 22).

BL39's convergence score (5/8) is LOWER than its neighboring
BL40 (7/8). Its assignment as Lower He-Sea of the Triple Burner appears to be
a CLASSIFICATION-driven designation — the Lingshu needed to
assign each arm Yang organ's Lower He-Sea to a leg Yang channel,
and BL39 was the available point on BL near BL40. The
classification may reflect systemic organization rather than
anatomical specificity.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích (Stimulation Techniques)

Standard acupuncture: perpendicular insertion, 0.5–1.0 cun.
Patient prone with knee slightly flexed. De Qi: local distending
sensation at the lateral popliteal fossa.

Moxibustion: applicable. Traditional for cold/deficiency Triple Burner
patterns (edema, fluid retention).

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: superior lateral genicular artery. Minimal at
  standard depth.

Nerve risk: common peroneal nerve runs directly beneath — deep
  needling may contact this nerve (electric sensation to lateral
  leg/foot dorsum). The common peroneal nerve is vulnerable at
  the fibular neck below BL39 — avoid directing the needle
  inferolaterally toward the fibular head.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL40 — Ủy Trung (委中, Wěi Zhōng)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**
"Middle of the Crook" (委 = bend/crook, 中 = middle/center) —
at the center of the popliteal crease, between the tendons of
biceps femoris (lateral) and semitendinosus (medial). BL40 is
one of the MOST important acupoints in TCM, holding more
simultaneous designations than almost any other point:

- **He-Sea/Earth point** of the BL channel (合土穴)
- **Four Command Points** for lumbar/back (四總穴歌: 腰背委中求)
- **Lower He-Sea of Bladder** organ (膀胱之下合穴)
- **Ke Controller** (Earth controls Water, 土克水)
- **Ma Dan-yang Twelve Heavenly Star Points** (天星十二穴)
- **Classical bloodletting point** (络刺) — paired with PC3

With 6 simultaneous designations, BL40 matches ST36 and GB34 as
the most category-loaded points in the Atlas. The Four Command
Points rhyme (四總穴歌) is arguably the single most famous
prescription formula in acupuncture history: "腰背委中求" ("for
the back and lumbar, seek Weizhong"). The other three Command
Points — LI4 (face/mouth), LU7 (head/neck), ST36 (belly/abdomen)
— are already documented. BL40 completes the set.

BL40 is the MOST classically prescribed bloodletting point. The
popliteal fossa contains the popliteal artery, vein, and tibial
nerve — making this both therapeutically powerful and
anatomically risk-significant.

Naming pair: BL39 委阳 (Weiyang, "Entrusted Yang") and BL40
委中 (Weizhong, "Middle of the Crook") share the character 委
(crook of the knee). 阳 = lateral, 中 = center. Anatomically:
BL39 follows the common peroneal nerve (lateral), BL40 follows
the tibial nerve (central). The sciatic nerve bifurcation assigns
one acupoint to each branch.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): at the midpoint of the
popliteal crease, between the tendons of biceps femoris and
semitendinosus.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient prone and knee slightly
flexed, the popliteal crease is visible. Biceps femoris tendon
palpable laterally, semitendinosus tendon medially. BL40 is at
the midpoint. The popliteal artery pulse can often be palpated
deep to this point.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.5 cun (~1.2–3.8 cm) perpendicular.
CAUTION: the popliteal artery and vein are directly deep.
Superficial insertion for standard acupuncture; deeper insertion
risks vascular puncture.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Tibial nerve** (L4–S3) — the larger terminal branch of the
  sciatic nerve. At BL40, the tibial nerve runs through the
  center of the popliteal fossa, deep to the popliteal vessels.
  The nerve is the deepest of the three major structures (nerve
  deepest, artery middle, vein most superficial).
- **Medial sural cutaneous nerve** — branch of the tibial nerve
  at the popliteal fossa, provides sensory innervation to the
  posterior calf. Joins the lateral sural cutaneous (from common
  peroneal) to form the sural nerve.
- Posterior femoral cutaneous nerve terminal branches
- Spinal segment: L4–S3 (tibial nerve deep), S1–S2 (cutaneous)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: popliteal fascia — the deep fascia forms a
reinforced roof over the popliteal fossa. Below this, the
popliteal fat pad is relatively loose connective tissue
containing the neurovascular bundle.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular:
- **Popliteal artery** — continuation of the femoral artery,
  runs through the center of the popliteal fossa. At BL40,
  1.5–3 cm deep depending on body habitus.
- **Popliteal vein** — superficial to the artery. Small
  saphenous vein empties into the popliteal vein near this level.
- Genicular arteries (superior/inferior medial and lateral) —
  branch from the popliteal artery to form the periarticular
  anastomosis around the knee.
→ Cross-ref: A04

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue (small saphenous vein may be
  visible)
- Popliteal fascia
- Popliteal fat pad
- Popliteal vein
- Popliteal artery
- Tibial nerve (deepest)
- Posterior capsule of knee joint
- Popliteus muscle

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**1. Five Shu: He-Sea/Earth point (合土穴):**
The 5th and most proximal Five Shu point on the BL channel.
He-Sea points are at or near the knee — the most "interior" of
the Five Shu sequence. He-Sea function (Lingshu Ch.1): "合主逆气
而泄" — He-Sea treats counterflow Qi and diarrhea. For BL
channel (Water), the He-Sea element is Earth. Earth controls
Water (土克水) → **Ke Controller** point: the control/sedation
access within the Five Element cycle.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

**2. Four Command Points (四總穴歌):**
Zhenjiu Dacheng (1601): "腰背委中求" — "for the back and
lumbar, seek Weizhong." The four commands:
- LI4 (Hegu): face/mouth → 面口合谷收
- LU7 (Lieque): head/neck → 頭項尋列缺
- ST36 (Zusanli): belly/abdomen → 肚腹三里留
- **BL40 (Weizhong): back/lumbar → 腰背委中求**

All four are DISTAL points treating PROXIMAL body regions. This
is the foundational non-segmental principle in TCM: the most
important treatment points for each body region are not AT that
region but distal to it. With BL40, all four Command Points are
now documented in the Atlas.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI4, AP-01 LU7, AP-03 ST36

**3. Lower He-Sea of Bladder (膀胱之下合穴):**
BL40's He-Sea status overlaps with its Lower He-Sea status —
because BL is a leg (Foot) Yang channel, its He-Sea is already
on the lower limb. For the 3 leg Yang channels (ST, GB, BL), the
He-Sea and Lower He-Sea naturally overlap (ST36, GB34, BL40).
Only the 3 arm Yang channels (LI, SI, TE) need a SEPARATE Lower
He-Sea point on a leg channel.

Textual note: some classical commentators assign BL39 (not BL40)
as the Lower He-Sea of Bladder, which would separate He-Sea from
Lower He-Sea for BL. The majority modern view (Deadman, Chinese
textbooks, WHO) holds that BL40 serves both roles. The Lingshu
Ch.4 uses "合入" (He enters) for the leg channels' own organs,
while "下合" (Lower He) is used specifically for the arm channel
organs' leg-proxied points.
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB34 Observation 19

**4. Ma Dan-yang Twelve Heavenly Star Points (天星十二穴):**
Song dynasty empirical point selection. BL40 indications in the
Twelve Star formula: low back pain, leg pain, wind-cold-damp Bi
syndrome, popliteal contracture, febrile disorders.

**5. Classical bloodletting point (络刺):**
BL40 is the most classically prescribed point for luo-pricking
(络刺) bloodletting. Three-edged needle (三棱针) pricking at
superficial veins at the popliteal crease, releasing a few drops
of blood. Paired with PC3 (Quze) at the cubital fossa — together
forming the bloodletting pair for acute Heat at the Ying/Blood
level.
→ Cross-ref: AP-09 PC3

Yuan-Source: **No** (BL64 Jinggu).
Luo-Connecting: **No** (BL58 Feiyang).
Xi-Cleft: **No** (BL63 Jinmen).
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: **No**.
Eight Confluent: **No**. Hui-Meeting: **No**.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** BL40 Weizhong (委中). Primary functions:
clear Heat, cool Blood (bloodletting), benefit the lumbar/back,
relax sinews, open channels, benefit the Bladder. THE most
important point for lumbar/back disorders in classical TCM.

**2. Trigger point:** **Yes.** Gastrocnemius head trigger points
(Travell & Simons) — the medial and lateral heads originate in
the popliteal fossa, flanking BL40. Referred pain: posterior knee
and calf. Popliteus trigger point also in this region. Dorsher
2008 confirms acupoint-trigger point correspondence at BL40.

**3. Ashi correspondence:** The popliteal crease is a major
tenderness site in knee disorders (Baker's cyst, posterior knee
pain, hamstring tendinopathy). Spontaneous tenderness at BL40 is
common in chronic LBP patients — one of the strongest dynamic
acupoint examples (see Dynamic Acupoint section below).

**4. Marma point:** **Yes.** Janu Marma (जानु, knee joint,
Sushruta Samhita). Sandhi (joint) Marma classified as
Vaikalyakara (causing disability if injured). The posterior knee
(popliteal fossa) is within Janu Marma territory. Injury causes
limping or knee dysfunction.

**5. Head zone:** S1–S2 dermatome at the popliteal crease.
Bladder viscerotome S2–S4 partially overlaps at S2. However,
BL40's primary clinical indication (lumbar/back pain, T12–L5) is
NOT in the S1–S2 Head zone — this is the non-segmental paradox
of the Four Command Points. The mechanism does not rely on
dermatomal convergence but on spinal cord convergence at the
lumbar enlargement (see Cơ chế giải thích).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

**6. Anatomy Trains:** **Yes.** Superficial Back Line (SBL, Myers
2001) — the popliteal crease is a CRITICAL junction on the SBL
where the hamstring compartment (above) transitions to the
gastrocnemius/soleus/Achilles compartment (below). BL40 sits at
the SBL's posterior knee "station." The same fascial line runs
continuously from plantar fascia → Achilles → calf → **popliteal
(BL40)** → hamstrings → sacrotuberous ligament → thoracolumbar
fascia → erector spinae → epicranial aponeurosis. The SBL
provides a continuous myofascial connection from BL40 to the
lumbar spine — a potential mechanical pathway for BL40's lumbar
treatment effect.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S1.3

**7. Neurogenic spot:** No specific data for popliteal fossa in
Kim 2017. The S1–S2 spinal level is within the lumbar enlargement
territory, but the popliteal surface was not systematically
assessed.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S4.3

**8. Cupping point:** **Yes.** BL40 is a traditional cupping
site. Wet cupping (hijama) at BL40 combines suction with
superficial blood release, overlapping with the bloodletting
tradition. The popliteal fossa provides a relatively flat surface
suitable for cup application.
→ Cross-ref: B07, AP-08 KI (BL40 cupping reference)

**Convergence score: 7/8** (TCM, Trigger Point, Ashi, Marma,
Head zone 🟡, Anatomy Trains, Cupping). Only Neurogenic spot
lacks data. **Highest convergence among all BL posterior limb
points.** Among the highest in the entire AP-07 file (BL10 = 8/8,
BL2 = 6/8, BL40 = 7/8).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing (Lingshu Ch.1, Ben Shu): He-Sea of BL. "合主逆氣而泄"
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, back stiffness, hip/knee pain,
  inability to walk, epistaxis, hematemesis, malaria, skin sores
- Nanjing (Ch.68): He-Sea for Fu organ disorders and counterflow
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: Four Command Points 腰背委中求. Ma Dan-yang
  Star indications: lumbar pain, difficulty walking, wind-cold-
  damp Bi, popliteal contracture, hemorrhoids, febrile conditions
- Bianque Xinshu: bloodletting at BL40 for acute febrile illness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Low back pain** (primary indication — strongest evidence)
- Lumbar disc herniation, sciatica (protocol component)
- Knee pain, popliteal pain
- Hemorrhoids, rectal bleeding
- Febrile conditions (bloodletting)
- Skin sores, eczema, urticaria (Blood-level Heat)
- Urinary disorders (Lower He-Sea of Bladder)
- Heatstroke, acute gastroenteritis (PC3+BL40 pair)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- THE most prescribed acupoint for lumbar/back pain (alongside
  BL23). BL23+BL40 is the single most common two-point
  combination for chronic LBP.
- **Four Command Points protocol:** BL40 alone or with local
  lumbar points (BL23, BL25, GV3) for all back conditions.
- **Sciatica triad: GB30 + BL40 + GB34.** The most prescribed
  sciatica formula — sciatic trunk (GB30) + popliteal tibial
  (BL40) + common peroneal/tendons (GB34). Three neural access
  levels along the sciatic nerve trajectory.
  → Cross-ref: AP-11 GB30
- **Bloodletting pair: PC3 + BL40.** Three-edged needle pricking
  at superficial veins for acute Heat in Ying/Blood level — high
  fever, heatstroke, acute gastroenteritis, skin eruptions. Both
  He-Sea points at major joint creases with accessible superficial
  veins.
  → Cross-ref: AP-09 PC3
- **BL23 + BL40 + BL60:** three-level BL-channel protocol
  (lumbar-local + popliteal-distal + ankle-distal).
- **BL40 wet cupping:** hijama at BL40 for chronic lumbar/back
  pain — combines cupping suction with superficial blood release.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Low back pain — 🟡 Plausible**

BL40 is one of the two most frequently prescribed acupoints for
LBP (alongside BL23) in systematic reviews. BL23-BL40 is the
highest-confidence point pair for LBP across multiple association
rule mining analyses.
- Acupuncture for LBP (multiple SR/MA, 2020–2026): acupuncture
  significantly reduces pain vs control. BL40 appears in >60%
  of included RCT protocols.
- BL23-BL40 and BL23-GV3 showed the LARGEST average effect sizes
  for LBP pain reduction.
- 🟡 rather than 🟢: (a) BL40 is NEVER isolated from other
  points in trials — always part of multi-point protocols; (b)
  BL23 (local paravertebral) may drive most of the effect, with
  BL40's individual contribution unknown; (c) no sham-controlled
  BL40-specific trials exist.
→ Cross-ref: BL23 LBP evidence (this file)

**2. Sciatica / lumbar disc herniation — 🟡 Plausible**

Part of virtually all acupuncture protocols for sciatica. The
GB30+BL40+GB34 triad is the most common prescription. SRs show
moderate efficacy for acupuncture in sciatica, but no trial
isolates any individual point.
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB30

**3. Knee pain — 🟡 Plausible**

Traditional indication supported by BL40's posterior knee
position. Combined with ST35 (anterior), GB34 (lateral), and
SP9 (medial) for comprehensive knee access. No BL40-specific
knee trials.

**4. Hemorrhoids — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Major classical indication. BL40 appears in acupuncture protocols
for hemorrhoids alongside GV20, BL57, SP6, etc. No isolated
evidence. Mechanism unclear: BL40 (S1–S2 cutaneous) is
non-segmental for hemorrhoids (rectal territory S2–S4).

**5. Bloodletting for febrile/heat conditions — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Classical indication with no modern controlled trials. PC3+BL40
bloodletting is widely practiced in traditional settings but
lacks systematic evidence.

**6. Skin conditions (eczema, urticaria) — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional "Blood-level Heat" indication. BL40 bloodletting
for skin disorders reflects TCM pathophysiology (Blood Heat →
skin eruptions → cool the Blood by releasing it). No RCT data.

**7. Urinary disorders (Lower He-Sea of BL) — 🔴 Unvalidated**

As Lower He-Sea of the Bladder organ, BL40 should treat Fu organ
conditions. Traditional indications include urinary retention and
dysuria. No modern evidence. The primary urinary acupoints in
modern practice are BL28 (Back-Shu of BL at S2), CV3 (Front-Mu
of BL), and SP6 — all with stronger segmental access.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

**LBP-associated sensitization:** BL40 demonstrates significant
pressure-pain threshold reduction in chronic LBP patients
compared to healthy controls. Popliteal fossa tenderness
correlates with LBP severity — traditional "pressing the crease"
(按委中) diagnostic technique.

This sensitization is mechanistically significant: BL40 (S1–S2
cutaneous) is 4–8 segments from the lumbar pathology site
(T12–L5). The tenderness cannot be explained by local tissue
pathology (no knee problem) or simple dermatomal convergence
(different dermatomes). Possible mechanisms:

1. **Central sensitization at lumbar enlargement** — chronic LBP
   activates wide dynamic range (WDR) neurons at L4–S2 cord
   segments → lowers thresholds for ALL afferent inputs to those
   segments, including tibial nerve inputs from BL40
2. **SBL myofascial chain tension** — lumbar pathology → hamstring
   tightness → popliteal fascial tension → mechanical sensitivity
   at BL40
3. **BL channel neural network** — lumbar dorsal rami + tibial
   nerve share the lumbar enlargement (L4–S2), creating a
   functional neural "channel" along the posterior body

This is one of the strongest dynamic acupoint examples in the
posterior limb, comparable to GB30's sciatic sensitization
(AP-11).
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint)

#### Cơ chế giải thích (Proposed Mechanistic Explanations)

**1. Spinal convergence at lumbar enlargement (PRIMARY)**

The most plausible mechanism for BL40's lumbar/back effect. The
tibial nerve (activated by needling BL40) projects to spinal cord
segments L4–S3 at the lumbar enlargement. The dorsal rami from
lumbar vertebrae (carrying back pain signals) ALSO project to
L1–S2 segments. The lumbar enlargement is where these two
afferent streams CONVERGE on the same dorsal horn neuron
population.

This is NOT classical dermatomal segmental correspondence (BL40
dermatome S1–S2 ≠ lumbar back T12–L5). It is CONVERGENCE at the
SPINAL CORD level: the tibial nerve projects across a wider cord
range (L4–S3) than its cutaneous dermatome (S1–S2), because it
is a large mixed nerve collecting from multiple segments. The
lumbar enlargement (L1–S2) processes BOTH lower limb afferents
AND lumbar paravertebral afferents. A strong tibial nerve input
(BL40 needle) modulates the same dorsal horn segments processing
back pain → gate control at the spinal cord level.

Comparison with other Command Points:
- LI4 (C6–C7 hand) → face/mouth (CN V/VII): NO spinal
  convergence possible → must use supraspinal pathway (PAG,
  confirmed by fMRI)
- ST36 (L4–L5 leg) → abdomen (T6–T12): NO spinal convergence
  → supraspinal + vagal pathway
- LU7 (C6–C7 wrist) → head/neck (C1–C3): PARTIAL convergence
  at cervical enlargement
- **BL40 (L4–S3 knee) → lumbar (L1–L5): DIRECT convergence at
  lumbar enlargement** — tibial nerve input zone (L4–S3) overlaps
  extensively with lumbar dorsal rami input zone (L1–S2)

BL40 may be the MOST mechanistically transparent of the Four
Command Points — the only one with a plausible spinal-level (not
supraspinal) mechanism. 🟡 Plausible.

**2. SBL myofascial pathway (CONTRIBUTORY)**

The Superficial Back Line provides continuous mechanical
connection from popliteal fossa to lumbar spine: gastrocnemius
← BL40 → hamstrings → sacrotuberous ligament → erector spinae →
thoracolumbar fascia. Needle stimulation at BL40 → connective
tissue deformation → propagates via SBL to the lumbar region.
The thoracolumbar fascia is innervated by lumbar dorsal rami —
mechanical stimulation from BL40 could activate fascial
mechanoreceptors in the lumbar region.

Limitation: the SBL mechanism explains BL40→lumbar specifically
(continuous fascial chain) but cannot generalize to all Command
Points (no SBL connection from hand LI4 to face). 🟡 Plausible.

**3. Supraspinal descending modulation (BACKGROUND)**

All needle stimulation at De Qi intensity activates Aδ fibers →
ascending signal → PAG → descending inhibition of dorsal horn
pain processing (DNIC). This mechanism is point-independent and
explains general analgesic effects. 🟢 Confirmed for general
mechanism, not BL40-specific.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S3 (convergence-projection), A03 (fascial
transmission), A01 S4 (supraspinal modulation)

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích (Stimulation Techniques)

**Standard acupuncture:**
- Perpendicular insertion, 0.5–1.0 cun (1.2–2.5 cm). Avoid
  deeper insertion toward the popliteal vessels.
- Patient position: PRONE with knee slightly flexed (pillow
  under ankle).
- De Qi: distending, aching sensation at the popliteal fossa,
  often radiating down the posterior calf (tibial nerve) or up
  the posterior thigh (hamstring territory).
- Moderate lifting-thrusting and rotation — excessive stimulation
  risks vascular disturbance.

**Bloodletting (络刺):**
- Three-edged needle (三棱针) pricking at visible superficial
  veins at the popliteal crease. Release a few drops of blood.
- Indicated for acute Heat conditions only — not for chronic
  deficiency or blood deficiency patients.
- Paired with PC3 for systemic bloodletting effect.
- Modern aseptic technique essential: sterile three-edged needle,
  skin antisepsis, sterile gauze.

**Wet cupping (hijama):**
- Cup applied over BL40 after superficial scarification. Combines
  negative pressure with bloodletting.
- Common in Middle Eastern and Chinese traditional medicine for
  chronic back pain.

**Electroacupuncture:**
- BL23–BL40 EA pair (electrode at BL23 + electrode at BL40) at
  2–15 Hz is a frequent research paradigm for LBP.
- EA at BL40 targets tibial nerve activation more consistently
  than manual stimulation.

**Moxibustion:**
- Not the primary technique at BL40. Moxa is more commonly used
  at BL23 (tonification) while BL40 is associated with draining
  techniques (He-Sea = draining, bloodletting = draining).

#### Tương tác & phối hợp (Interactions & Combinations)

**1. BL23 + BL40** — local + distal for lumbar/back pain. THE
most prescribed BL-channel pair. BL23 provides local
paravertebral stimulation (L2 dorsal ramus); BL40 provides
distal tibial nerve input. Together: spinal convergence at lumbar
enlargement from two directions (dorsal ramus from above + tibial
nerve from below).

**2. GB30 + BL40 + GB34** — sciatica triad. Three neural access
levels: sciatic trunk at gluteal (GB30), tibial nerve at
popliteal (BL40), common peroneal at fibular head (GB34). This
protocol accesses the sciatic system at progressively more distal
bifurcation points.
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB30

**3. PC3 + BL40** — bloodletting pair. Both He-Sea points at
major joint creases (cubital fossa and popliteal fossa) with
accessible superficial veins. For acute Heat in Ying/Blood level.
→ Cross-ref: AP-09 PC3

**4. BL23 + BL40 + BL60** — three-level BL-channel protocol
(lumbar-local + popliteal-distal + ankle-distal). Accesses the
BL channel at progressively more distal levels.

**5. Four Command Points set:** LI4 + LU7 + ST36 + BL40. Each
point used alone for its respective body region; rarely used as
a complete four-point set. The set represents the foundational
principle of distal treatment.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI4, AP-01 LU7, AP-03 ST36

**Lower He-Sea of BL vs Back-Shu of BL:** BL40 (Lower He-Sea at
popliteal, non-segmental) and BL28 (Back-Shu at S2, segmental
parasympathetic) both treat Bladder Fu organ conditions. In
modern practice, BL28 dominates for urinary indications (direct
S2 pelvic splanchnic access); BL40 dominates for musculoskeletal
(lumbar/back pain via tibial nerve). The Lower He-Sea designation
is historically important but functionally secondary to BL40's
Command Point role.

#### An toàn (Safety)

**Vascular risk — HIGH:**
- **Popliteal artery** lies 1.5–3 cm deep at the center of the
  popliteal fossa. Deep needling or inappropriate angle →
  popliteal hematoma, pseudoaneurysm. One of the most
  vascular-risk-significant points on the lower limb.
- **Popliteal vein** and small saphenous vein — more superficial.
  Venipuncture produces local bruising but is less dangerous
  than arterial puncture.
- **Mitigation:** perpendicular insertion to standard depth
  (0.5–1.0 cun). Palpate for popliteal pulse before needling.
  Patients on anticoagulants: extra caution, consider shorter
  depth.
- **Bloodletting safety:** prick SUPERFICIAL veins only (visible,
  engorged). NEVER prick deep enough to approach the popliteal
  artery.

**Nerve risk:**
- Tibial nerve is deep (below the vessels). Risk of direct nerve
  injury is LOW at standard depth. Strong De Qi sensation
