  (radiating to calf/foot) indicates tibial nerve proximity —
  do not stimulate aggressively.

**Pregnancy:** No specific contraindication. BL40 is NOT on
classical pregnancy contraindication lists (unlike BL60, BL67,
LI4, SP6, GB21).

**Bloodletting contraindications:**
- Blood deficiency (anemia) or Yin deficiency
- Patients on anticoagulants
- Hemophilia or bleeding disorders
- Pregnancy
- Cold/deficiency patterns (bloodletting is a clearing/draining
  technique for excess Heat only)

---

### Batch 8 Observations

**Observation 21 — SCIATIC NERVE TRAJECTORY: BL36→BL40.**
The 5 points in this batch track the sciatic nerve from gluteal
fold to its bifurcation at the popliteal fossa:

| Point | Location | Primary nerve | Segment |
|-------|----------|---------------|---------|
| BL36 | Gluteal fold | Sciatic (most superficial) | L4–S3 |
| BL37 | Mid-posterior thigh | Sciatic (deep, hamstrings) | L4–S3 |
| BL38 | Above popliteal | Common peroneal (pre-bif.) | L4–S2 |
| BL39 | Lateral popliteal | Common peroneal (post-bif.) | L4–S2 |
| BL40 | Center popliteal | Tibial (post-bifurcation) | L4–S3 |

The BL channel trajectory follows the sciatic nerve with high
fidelity. At the bifurcation, BL39 follows the common peroneal
(lateral) and BL40 follows the tibial (central) — one point per
branch. The main channel continues distally with the tibial nerve
(BL40→BL55→BL57→BL60), not the common peroneal. BL39 sits on the
"divergent" branch — its assignment as Lower He-Sea of the Triple Burner (an
arm channel organ) may reflect its position on the secondary
nerve branch rather than the main tibial trunk.

**Observation 22 — LOWER HE-SEA SYSTEM COMPLETE (6/6).**
With BL39 (Triple Burner) and BL40 (BL), all 6 Lower He-Sea points are
documented. Completed inventory:

| # | Lower He-Sea | Fu organ | Host channel | Same? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ST36 | Stomach | ST (leg) | Yes |
| 2 | ST37 | Large Intestine | ST (leg) | No — LI is arm |
| 3 | ST39 | Small Intestine | ST (leg) | No — SI is arm |
| 4 | GB34 | Gallbladder | GB (leg) | Yes |
| 5 | **BL39** | **Triple Burner** | **BL (leg)** | **No — TE is arm** |
| 6 | **BL40** | **Bladder** | **BL (leg)** | **Yes** |

Distribution: ST hosts 3 (1 own + 2 arm channels), BL hosts 2
(1 own + 1 arm channel), GB hosts 1 (own only). ST dominates —
consistent with its "Sea of Food and Drink" role absorbing GI
tract Fu organ treatment.

Pattern: all 3 arm Yang channels' Lower He-Sea migrate to leg
channels: LI→ST, SI→ST, TE→BL. This does NOT follow same-name
pairing: SI (Taiyang arm) → ST (Yangming leg, not BL Taiyang
leg); TE (Shaoyang arm) → BL (Taiyang leg, not GB Shaoyang
leg). Only LI (Yangming arm) → ST (Yangming leg) follows
same-name. 1/3 same-name correspondence.

Segmental note: all 6 Lower He-Sea share L4–S3 spinal cord
territory at the lumbar enlargement. The Fu organs they treat
span T6–S4 (Stomach through Bladder). This confirms the Lower
He-Sea mechanism is NOT segmental — it requires supraspinal or
spinal convergence pathways.

**Observation 23 — BL40: MOST MECHANISTICALLY TRANSPARENT
COMMAND POINT.**
The Four Command Points all use DISTAL points for PROXIMAL body
regions. Their mechanisms differ:

| Command Pt | Body region | Segment gap | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| LI4 (hand) | Face/mouth | >10 (C6→CN V) | Supraspinal only |
| ST36 (leg) | Abdomen | 6-10 (L4→T6) | Supraspinal + vagal |
| LU7 (wrist) | Head/neck | 3-6 (C6→C1) | Partial cervical conv. |
| **BL40 (knee)** | **Back/lumbar** | **4-8 (S1→T12)** | **Lumbar enlargement** |

BL40 has the SMALLEST segment gap that STILL permits spinal-level
convergence. The tibial nerve (L4–S3) and lumbar dorsal rami
(L1–S2) share the lumbar enlargement — no supraspinal pathway
required. In contrast, LI4's face/mouth treatment has NO possible
spinal convergence between median nerve (C6–T1) and trigeminal
(CN V) — its mechanism MUST be supraspinal (PAG, confirmed by
fMRI).

Mechanistic transparency hierarchy:
1. **BL40** — spinal convergence at lumbar enlargement (fewest
   assumptions, most direct pathway)
2. **LU7** — partial convergence at cervical enlargement (C5–T1
   overlaps C1–C3)
3. **ST36** — supraspinal required (vagal pathway proposed)
4. **LI4** — supraspinal required (PAG confirmed, most studied
   but most distant)

The Four Command Points may use FUNDAMENTALLY DIFFERENT mechanisms
to achieve the same clinical goal. The classification groups them
by treated body region, not by mechanism — it is a clinical tool,
not a mechanistic model.

---

## Outer Back Line — Upper / Mid-Thoracic (BL41–BL47)

The BL OUTER BACK LINE (第二側線, "second lateral line") runs 3 cun
lateral to the posterior midline, from T2 (BL41) to S4 (BL54) — 14
points parallel to the medial back line (1.5 cun lateral). This batch
covers the upper 7 points (T2–T9).

KEY ARCHITECTURAL FEATURE: every outer-line point sits at the SAME
spinal level as its corresponding medial-line counterpart, but 1.5
cun more lateral. The 1.5 cun difference shifts the needle from the
medial branch of the dorsal ramus (targeting multifidus, facet joints,
closest to the sympathetic chain) to the lateral branch (targeting
longissimus, iliocostalis, broader fascial territory). Same spinal
segment, different neural branch, different target tissue.

FIVE-SPIRIT NAMING: 3 of the 5 five-spirit (五神) points occur in
this batch — BL42/Po (Lung), BL44/Shen (Heart), BL47/Hun (Liver) —
each at the EXACT spinal level of the corresponding Zang organ's
Back-Shu point. This naming convention links each organ's
psychospiritual aspect to its medial counterpart's segmental level.

CLINICAL USAGE: outer-line points are used significantly LESS
frequently than medial Back-Shu points. Exception: BL43
(Gaohuangshu) is a major clinical point for chronic disease —
the only outer-line point regularly used as a primary prescription.

---

### BL41 — Phụ Phần (附分, Fùfēn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 附分 (Fùfēn) = "Attached Part" or "Appended Branch" — possibly
referring to the BL channel's lateral "attachment" as it branches
from the medial line. BL41 is the FIRST point on the outer back
line (3 cun lateral), at T2. Its medial-line counterpart is BL12
(Fengmen, Wind Gate, 1.5 cun lateral at T2). BL41 marks where the
BL channel's second lateral branch begins its descent — SI14
(Jianwaishu, 肩外俞) occupies the 3-cun lateral position at T1
(the level just above), belonging to the SI channel rather than BL.
The outer BL back line therefore begins one segment below SI14.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI14

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T2.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique toward spine or oblique
inferiorly. The scapular medial border overlies or is immediately
lateral to this point. Pneumothorax risk present at T2.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T2 spinal nerve** — the
  primary neural substrate at 3 cun lateral. Innervates
  iliocostalis, longissimus, and cutaneous territory of the
  upper back.
- Spinal segment: T2
- Compare: BL12 (medial pair) is innervated by the MEDIAL
  branch of the same T2 dorsal ramus → multifidus, facet joints.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At 3 cun lateral, the needle
passes through trapezius (upper fibers), rhomboid minor, and into
the lateral tract of erector spinae (iliocostalis). Deeper: serratus
posterior superior and rib periosteum.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 2nd posterior intercostal artery, lateral
distribution.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → trapezius → rhomboid
minor → serratus posterior superior → erector spinae (iliocostalis,
longissimus) → 2nd rib / transverse process of T2.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line points are NOT organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Special: **Meeting of BL + SI** (Jiayi Jing). BL41 is a crossing
  point of Bladder and Small Intestine channels — the two Taiyang
  channels (Hand and Foot) share territory here. SI14 at T1 (3 cun)
  sits one segment above; the Taiyang transition zone spans T1–T2.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL41 (Fufen 附分). Traditional functions: expel Wind,
  activate the channel, relieve pain. Shoulder stiffness, upper back
  pain, neck pain. A secondary Wind point — BL12 (medial counterpart)
  is the primary Wind Gate.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Paravertebral trigger points in
  erector spinae at T2, extending laterally into rhomboid minor
  territory. Referred pain: interscapular, posterior shoulder.

Ashi correspondence: upper interscapular tenderness, especially
  with postural strain. 🟡

Marma point: At the superior border of Bruhati Marma territory
  (T3–T6). BL41 at T2 is slightly above the core zone.

Head zone: T2 → cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5). 🟡 Partial — T2
  matches cardiac more than respiratory innervation.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: T2 is within Kim 2017 cardiac neurogenic spot
  range (C8–T2).

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable. Not a primary
  cupping site (BL12 preferred for Wind).

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains,
neurogenic spot, cupping). Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡, Head zone 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: shoulder and back pain, neck stiffness, numbness
  of the elbow
- Dacheng: Wind-Cold invasion, upper back stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Shoulder and upper back pain, neck stiffness
- Numbness of the elbow and arm

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL41 has no individual
evidence base. Musculoskeletal indications consistent with local
T2 paravertebral stimulation but no specific study.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T2 thoracic level. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.3–0.5 cun. The scapula provides some protection at 3 cun
  lateral but does not eliminate risk.

Vascular risk: Minimal at standard depth.
Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL42 — Phách Hộ (魄户, Pòhù)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Five-Spirit: Po (魄,
Lung)**

Name 魄户 (Pòhù) = "Door of Po" (Door of the Corporeal Soul). The
Po (魄) is one of the Five Spirits (五神), associated with the Lung.
In TCM psychospiritual theory, Po governs the corporeal/physical
soul — the somatic, sensory, and instinctive aspects of
consciousness. Po arrives at birth with the first breath (Lung) and
departs at death with the last breath. It governs: skin sensation,
bodily awareness, grief/letting go, rhythmic physiological functions
(breathing, peristalsis).

BL42 is at T3, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL13
(Feishu, Lung Back-Shu, T3, 1.5 cun). This is the first
medial-outer spirit pair: BL13 treats the Lung physically → BL42
accesses the Lung's psychospiritual aspect.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T3.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique. Pneumothorax risk at T3.
Scapula partially overlies this point.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T3 spinal nerve**
- T3 = lung viscerotome (T3–T5)
- Spinal segment: T3
- Compare: BL13 (medial pair) uses MEDIAL branch of same T3
  dorsal ramus
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia → trapezius → rhomboid major
→ erector spinae.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 3rd posterior intercostal artery, lateral
distribution.

Muscle layers: skin → trapezius (middle fibers) → rhomboid major
(T3–T5) → serratus posterior superior → erector spinae
(iliocostalis, longissimus) → 3rd rib.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Five-Spirit point: Po (魄) — Lung's spirit (1st of 5)**

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL42 (Pohu 魄户). Traditional functions: spread Lung
  Qi, relieve cough and asthma, regulate the Po. Indications: cough,
  asthma, shoulder/back stiffness, grief/sadness (psychospiritual).

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T3 paravertebral TrPs in erector
  spinae / rhomboid. Referred pain to interscapular region, similar
  to BL13.

Ashi correspondence: interscapular tenderness, especially in
  respiratory patients. Same sensitization zone as BL13.

Marma point: **Possible.** Within Bruhati Marma territory (T3–T6).

Head zone: T3 → lung viscerotome (T3–T5). **✅ MATCH** — same
  segmental match as BL13.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: predicted at T3 during pulmonary disease (by
  extension from Kim 2017 cardiac data at C8–T2).

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable. BL13 (medial) is
  the primary cupping point for respiratory conditions.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: cough, dyspnea, shoulder/back pain
- Dacheng: Lung deficiency with chronic cough, sadness and grief,
  shoulder/neck stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Cough, asthma, neck/shoulder/back stiffness

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL42-specific evidence.
Respiratory indications overlap with BL13 (same T3 segment) which
has far stronger evidence.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T3 thoracic level. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.3–0.5 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL43 — Cao Hoang Du (膏肓俞, Gāohuāng Shū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Most Important Outer-Line
Point**

Name 膏肓俞 (Gāohuāng Shū) = "Vital Region Transporting Point."
The name references the classical idiom 病入膏肓 (bìng rù
gāohuāng) — "disease has entered the gaohuang," meaning the
illness has become incurable. 膏 (gāo) = the fatty tissue below
the heart; 肓 (huāng) = the membrane above the diaphragm. Together,
膏肓 designates the mediastinal space between heart and diaphragm —
a region traditionally considered inaccessible to needle or medicine.
The paradox: BL43 is named after this "unreachable" region, yet it
IS needled to treat conditions that have penetrated deeply into the
body.

BL43 is at T4, 3 cun lateral. Its medial counterpart is BL14
(Jueyinshu, Back-Shu of Pericardium, T4, 1.5 cun). BL43 is the
MOST clinically important outer-line point — the only one regularly
used as a primary prescription point. Its traditional role in
chronic disease, consumptive illness, and immune tonification gives
it unique standing among the 14 outer-line points.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T4.

Anatomical landmarks: T4 is at the level of the spine of the
scapula (approximately). BL43 lies under or at the medial border
of the scapula. Some practitioners ask the patient to wrap their
arms around their chest (抱臂) to abduct the scapulae, exposing
the point more clearly.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique toward spine or oblique
inferiorly. Pneumothorax risk at T4. Oblique insertion mandatory.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T4 spinal nerve**
- T4 is within the cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5) and at the lower
  boundary of the lung viscerotome (T3–T5). T4 is also the level
  of BL14 (PC Shu), which accesses the same cardiac sympathetic
  territory as BL15 (T5, Heart Shu).
- Spinal segment: T4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T4 and 3 cun lateral, the
needle traverses trapezius, rhomboid major, and enters the lateral
tract of erector spinae. Serratus posterior superior attachment
extends to approximately T4–T5. Deep: 4th rib periosteum.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 4th posterior intercostal artery.
→ Cross-ref: A04

Muscle layers (superficial → deep): skin → trapezius (middle
fibers) → rhomboid major → serratus posterior superior → erector
spinae (iliocostalis, longissimus) → 4th rib / transverse process
of T4.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu. BL14
  (medial) is the PC Back-Shu at this level.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Special: **Gaohuangshu** — named as a specific treatment point
  for chronic, deep-seated disease. Not a standard category but
  a clinically significant individual point identification in
  classical texts (Qianjin Yaofang, Zhenjiu Dacheng).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL43 (Gaohuangshu 膏肓俞). Traditional functions:
  tonify deficiency (補虛), nourish Lung and Kidney, strengthen
  constitution (扶正), benefit chronic exhaustion. Primary
  indications: chronic cough, consumptive disease (虛勞), night
  sweats, spermatorrhea, poor memory, general debility. BL43 is
  particularly important in the context of moxa — classical texts
  consider moxibustion at Gaohuang a life-extending treatment.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T4 paravertebral trigger points
  in rhomboid major and erector spinae. Interscapular pain referral.
  The interscapular region (T3–T6) is one of the most common TrP
  zones.

Ashi correspondence: interscapular tenderness. BL43 tenderness in
  chronic disease reflects generalized viscerosomatic reflex from
  cardiopulmonary territory (T3–T5).

Marma point: **Possible.** Within Bruhati Marma (T3–T6). T4 is
  well within this territory.

Head zone: T4 → cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5) and lower lung
  viscerotome (T3–T5). **✅ MATCH** — T4 overlaps with both cardiac
  and pulmonary viscerosomatic reflex zones.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae. At T4, the SBL
  (erector spinae fascial chain) is the dominant myofascial
  substrate.

Neurogenic spot: T4 is adjacent to the cardiac neurogenic spot
  zone (C8–T2, Kim 2017). Predicted neurogenic activation at T3–T5
  during cardiopulmonary disease.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable at BL43. Some
  traditions apply moxa followed by cupping at Gaohuangshu for
  chronic disease.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Qianjin Yaofang (Sun Simiao, 652 CE): "After moxibustion at
  Gaohuangshu, there is no disease that cannot be treated"
  (灸此穴，无病不疗). Sun Simiao specifically highlighted BL43
  as one of the most important points for chronic disease.
- Jiayi Jing: chronic cough, night sweats, spermatorrhea, all
  consumptive diseases (虛勞)
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: "For all types of deficiency taxation (虛勞),
  moxa at Gaohuangshu is essential"
- Historical practice: extensive moxa protocols (often 100+ cones)
  at BL43 for serious chronic conditions

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Chronic cough, asthma, pulmonary tuberculosis (consumptive
  lung disease)
- Night sweats, spontaneous sweating
- Spermatorrhea, poor memory, exhaustion
- Immune tonification (modern interpretation of 扶正)

Clinical pattern: BL43's indications converge on CHRONIC DEFICIENCY
— conditions characterized by depleted reserves (Lung Qi deficiency,
Kidney essence deficiency, general debility). Distinctly different
from its medial counterpart BL14 (PC Shu), which focuses on
acute/sub-acute cardiac symptoms.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Chronic respiratory disease — 🔴 Unvalidated**

BL43 is frequently included in acupuncture protocols for COPD and
chronic asthma. SRs for acupuncture in COPD show moderate evidence
for quality-of-life improvement, but no trial isolates BL43's
individual contribution. Typically used alongside BL13, LU7, ST36
in multi-point protocols.

**2. Immune function / chronic fatigue — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional "tonification" indications have been interpreted as
immune modulation. Some preclinical studies show acupuncture at the
BL43 region affecting T-cell counts and NK cell activity, but no
controlled human trials isolate BL43. The "immune tonification"
claim remains at the hypothesis level.

**3. Moxibustion for chronic disease — 🔴 Unvalidated**

The historical emphasis on MOXA (not needling) at BL43 is notable.
Large-cone moxibustion at BL43 was considered more important than
needle stimulation. Modern research limited to case series and
observational studies for chronic fatigue and immune-compromised
states. No RCTs.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

No specific dynamic acupoint data for BL43. Expected sensitization
pattern: tenderness at T4 paravertebral region in chronic
cardiopulmonary conditions, similar to the BL14–BL15 zone. The
viscerosomatic reflex from T3–T5 territory should produce similar
sensitization at BL43 (lateral branch, same segment) as at BL14
(medial branch, same segment).
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (Proposed Mechanistic Explanations)

**1. Segmental somatovisceral reflex (T4)**

BL43 at T4 accesses the cardiac/pulmonary viscerotome through the
lateral branch of the T4 dorsal ramus:

```
BL43 at T4 → lateral branch T4 dorsal ramus → T4 dorsal horn
→ T4 preganglionic sympathetic → cardiac / pulmonary plexus
```

This is the SAME segmental pathway as BL14 (medial pair, T4, via
medial branch). Both converge on T4 dorsal horn neurons. The
anatomical difference: BL43 activates Aδ/C fibers from the lateral
erector spinae (iliocostalis), while BL14 activates fibers from
the medial erector spinae (multifidus). Both reach the same
central target.

**2. Moxa-specific thermal pathway**

The historical emphasis on moxibustion (not needling) raises a
mechanism question: does thermal stimulus access different receptors
than mechanical (needle) stimulation? TRPV1 (capsaicin receptor)
activation by moxa heat → C-fiber activation → potentially different
downstream effects than Aδ activation by needle. Large-cone moxa
protocols (100+ cones, high heat load) may produce systemic effects
via heat-shock protein induction and/or mast cell degranulation —
mechanisms not available to needle stimulation.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3

**3. "Chronic disease" specificity problem**

BSA cannot easily explain why BL43 specifically treats "chronic"
disease while BL14 (same segment, same pathway) treats "acute"
symptoms. The segmental model predicts both access the same visceral
territory. Possible explanations:
- **Technique difference** (moxa vs needle) → different receptor
  populations → different downstream effects
- **Lateral vs medial branch** → different muscle fiber types
  (postural iliocostalis vs segmental multifidus) → different
  autonomic reflex patterns
- **Classical observation bias** — BL43's reputation as a chronic
  disease point may reflect moxa technique's chronic-treatment
  suitability (repeated sessions, warming) rather than the point's
  unique anatomy

Gap: no clear mechanism explaining why the same spinal segment
accessed 1.5 cun more laterally would specifically treat chronic
rather than acute conditions.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01):
  Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique. Patient positioning: sitting
  with arms crossed to abduct scapulae (抱臂), or prone. De Qi:
  distending sensation in interscapular region. EA: rarely used;
  manual needling or moxa are traditional.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Cứu (B08):
  **MOXA IS THE PRIMARY TECHNIQUE at BL43.** One of the few points
  where moxibustion is traditionally considered more important than
  needling. Historical protocols: direct moxa (large cones, 7–14–21
  or even 100+ cones), indirect moxa (ginger or salt interposed),
  or moxa stick. Modern: indirect moxa or moxa box over BL43
  bilaterally. Combined with BL13 for chronic respiratory
  tonification.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S3.2

Bấm huyệt (B02):
  Deep pressure at BL43 is difficult due to scapular overlay.
  Thumb pressure along the medial scapular border with patient's
  arm across their chest. Used for self-care in chronic fatigue.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T4 thoracic level. Oblique insertion
  mandatory, max 0.3–0.5 cun. The scapula provides partial
  protection but does NOT eliminate risk — needle can slip under
  the scapula's medial border.

Moxa safety: large-cone direct moxibustion carries burn and
  scarring risk. Indirect moxa or moxa stick techniques are safer.
  Caution in patients with sensory deficits (neuropathy, diabetes).

Vascular risk: Minimal at standard depth.
Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL44 — Thần Đường (神堂, Shéntáng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Five-Spirit: Shen (神,
Heart)**

Name 神堂 (Shéntáng) = "Spirit Hall." 神 (Shén) is the central
spirit/consciousness, associated with the Heart in Five-Spirit
theory. Shen is the most encompassing of the five spirits — it
governs awareness, cognition, emotional regulation, sleep-wake
cycles, and the capacity for meaningful connection. Shen is both
the Heart's specific spirit and a general term for consciousness
itself.

BL44 is at T5, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL15
(Xinshu, Heart Back-Shu, T5, 1.5 cun). This is the second
medial-outer spirit pair: BL15 treats the Heart physically → BL44
accesses the Heart's spirit.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T5.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique. Pneumothorax risk at T5.
Under the medial border of the scapula.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T5 spinal nerve**
- T5 = cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5, center of range)
- Spinal segment: T5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia → rhomboid major → erector
spinae.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 5th posterior intercostal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → trapezius (lower fibers) → rhomboid major
→ serratus posterior superior → erector spinae (iliocostalis,
longissimus) → 5th rib.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point. BL15 (medial pair) is the
  Heart Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Five-Spirit point: Shen (神) — Heart's spirit (2nd of 5)**

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL44 (Shentang 神堂). Traditional functions: calm
  the Shen, regulate Heart Qi, relax the chest. Indications:
  palpitations, chest tightness, back stiffness, anxiety, insomnia
  (Shen disturbance).

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T5 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae / rhomboid major. Interscapular pain.

Ashi correspondence: interscapular tenderness, particularly in
  cardiac patients (viscerosomatic reflex at T5).

Marma point: **Possible.** Within Bruhati Marma (T3–T6).

Head zone: T5 → cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5). **✅ MATCH** — T5 is
  the lower boundary of the cardiac viscerosomatic zone.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: T5 is adjacent to the cardiac neurogenic zone
  (C8–T2, Kim 2017). Not within core zone but predicted overlap
  in cardiac disease.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: chest fullness, dyspnea, back pain
- Dacheng: Heart palpitations, anxiety, inability to settle the
  mind

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Chest pain, cardiac oppression
- Cough, asthma, back stiffness

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No specific evidence for
BL44. Cardiac/anxiety indications overlap with BL15 (same T5
segment) and HT7/PC6 (distal points with far stronger evidence).

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T5 thoracic level. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.3–0.5 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL45 — Ý Hỷ (噫嘻, Yìxī)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 噫嘻 (Yìxī) = an onomatopoeia representing a sighing or
belching sound. Unlike the five-spirit points, BL45's name
describes a SOUND, not a psychospiritual concept. The
sighing/belching reflects diaphragmatic and respiratory indications.
BL45 is at T6, 3 cun lateral — its medial counterpart is BL16
(Dushu, T6, 1.5 cun). BL16 is also a non-organ, non-spirit point
— the T6 "gap" between the cardiopulmonary cluster (T3–T5) and
the hepatobiliary cluster (T9–T10).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T6.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique. Pneumothorax risk at T6.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T6 spinal nerve**
- T6 = diaphragm transition zone (lower edge of cardiac
  viscerotome, upper edge of gastric)
- Spinal segment: T6
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T6, the superficial muscle
layer transitions from trapezius to latissimus dorsi — same
transition zone noted at BL16 on the medial line.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 6th posterior intercostal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → trapezius (lower fibers) / latissimus dorsi
(upper fibers) → rhomboid major (lowest fibers) → serratus
posterior superior → erector spinae (iliocostalis, longissimus)
→ 6th rib.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No. Not a five-spirit point.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL45 (Yixi 噫嘻). Traditional functions: relax the
  chest, regulate Qi, relieve cough. Minor point. Indications:
  shoulder/back pain, cough, asthma, sighing.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T6 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae. Mid-thoracic referred pain.

Ashi correspondence: mid-thoracic tenderness. Nonspecific. 🟡

Marma point: **Possible.** At the lower border of Bruhati Marma
  (T3–T6).

Head zone: T6 → transition zone. Peripheral diaphragm innervation
  territory (lower cardiac, upper gastric). 🟡 Partial.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data at T6.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains,
cupping). Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡, Head zone 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: shoulder/back pain, cough, dyspnea
- Dacheng: sighing, belching, shoulder stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Shoulder and back pain, cough, asthma

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL45 is a minor point with
no individual evidence. Functionally similar to BL16 (medial
counterpart, also a minor T6 point).

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T6 thoracic level. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.3–0.5 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL46 — Cách Quan (膈关, Géguan)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 膈关 (Géguan) = "Diaphragm Gate." The name mirrors its medial
counterpart: BL17 (膈俞, Geshu, "Diaphragm Shu") at the same T7
level. Both reference the diaphragm — BL17 is the Hui-Meeting of
Blood (one of the 8 Hui-Meeting points), while BL46 is a minor
point with no special category designation. T7 is the diaphragm
level — the anatomical boundary between thoracic and abdominal
viscera. The Hui-Blood designation does NOT transfer from BL17 to
its outer-line counterpart.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T7.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique. Pneumothorax risk at T7
(still within rib cage, approaching lower thorax).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T7 spinal nerve**
- T7 = diaphragm innervation level (motor via phrenic C3–C5;
  sensory from peripheral diaphragm via intercostal T5–T11, with
  T7 central). T7 marks the thoracic-abdominal visceral transition.
- Spinal segment: T7
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T7, latissimus dorsi is
the dominant superficial muscle layer.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 7th posterior intercostal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → latissimus dorsi → erector spinae
(iliocostalis, longissimus) → 7th rib.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No**. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: **No** — BL17 (medial pair) is Hui-Meeting of Blood;
  this designation does NOT transfer to outer-line counterpart.
Not a five-spirit point.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL46 (Geguan 膈关). Traditional functions: relax
  the diaphragm, harmonize the Stomach, descend rebellious Qi.
  Indications: vomiting, hiccup, intercostal pain, spinal
  stiffness.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T7 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae. Mid-lower thoracic referred pain.

Ashi correspondence: mid-thoracic tenderness. 🟡 Nonspecific.

Marma point: No clear Marma correspondence at T7 (below Bruhati
  T3–T6 territory).

Head zone: T7 → peripheral diaphragm innervation. At the boundary
  between gastric viscerotome (T6–T10) and hepatic viscerotome
  (T7–T9). 🟡 Partial.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data at T7.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Anatomy Trains,
cupping). Ashi 🟡, Head zone 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: vomiting, difficulty swallowing, spinal stiffness
- Dacheng: hiccup, intercostal pain, vomiting

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Vomiting, hiccup, belching
- Intercostal pain, spinal stiffness

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** BL46 has no individual
evidence. Diaphragmatic indications (hiccup, vomiting) overlap
with BL17 (same T7, medial) which has stronger clinical foundation
as Hui-Meeting of Blood.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T7, within rib cage. Oblique
  insertion, max 0.3–0.5 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL47 — Hồn Môn (魂门, Húnmén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Five-Spirit: Hun (魂,
Liver)**

Name 魂门 (Húnmén) = "Gate of Hun" (Gate of the Ethereal Soul).
Hun (魂) is the Liver's spirit in Five-Spirit theory. Unlike Po
(Lung's corporeal/physical soul), Hun governs the ethereal,
non-physical aspects of consciousness: dreaming, imagination,
vision for the future, planning, the capacity for aspiration, and
the smooth flow of emotional life. In TCM psychology, Hun
disturbance manifests as excessive anger, frustration, inability to
plan, depression (suppressed Liver Qi), vivid/disturbing dreams,
or aimlessness.

BL47 is at T9, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL18
(Ganshu, Liver Back-Shu, T9, 1.5 cun). This is the third
medial-outer spirit pair: BL18 treats the Liver physically → BL47
accesses the Liver's ethereal soul.

Note: between BL46 (T7) and BL47 (T9), there is NO outer-line
point at T8 — exactly mirroring the medial-line T8 gap
(BL17/T7 → BL18/T9).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T9.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique or perpendicular. T9 is
in the lower thorax — pneumothorax risk remains but decreasing
(thicker erector spinae mass, greater soft tissue depth).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T9 spinal nerve**
- T9 = hepatic viscerotome (liver T7–T9) and gastric viscerotome
  (stomach T6–T10, central range)
- Spinal segment: T9
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia. At T9, latissimus dorsi is
the superficial layer. Serratus posterior inferior begins at
approximately T9–T12.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 9th posterior intercostal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → latissimus dorsi → serratus posterior
inferior (upper attachment at T9) → erector spinae (iliocostalis,
longissimus) → 9th rib / transverse process of T9.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point. BL18 (medial pair) is the
  Liver Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Five-Spirit point: Hun (魂) — Liver's spirit (3rd of 5)**

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL47 (Hunmen 魂门). Traditional functions: smooth
  Liver Qi, regulate the Hun, harmonize Liver and Gallbladder.
  Indications: costal pain, chest/hypochondriac fullness, vomiting,
  jaundice, back pain, and (psychospiritual) emotional
  dysregulation, anger, frustration, disturbing dreams.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T9 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae / iliocostalis. Referred pain to lower thoracic
  region and costal margin.

Ashi correspondence: tenderness at T9 in hepatobiliary disease
  (viscerosomatic reflex). Similar sensitization as BL18.

Marma point: No clear Marma correspondence at T9.

Head zone: T9 → hepatic viscerotome (T7–T9) and gastric
  viscerotome (T6–T10). **✅ MATCH** for liver.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: No specific data at T9 for hepatic conditions.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: costal fullness, vomiting, back pain
- Dacheng: Liver Qi stagnation, jaundice, anger, disturbing dreams

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Chest/costal fullness, jaundice, vomiting
- Back pain

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL47-specific evidence.
Hepatobiliary indications overlap with BL18 (same T9 segment) and
LR14/GB24 (Front-Mu points). The psychospiritual Hun indications
have no modern evidence framework.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **YES** — T9, though lower risk than upper
  thorax due to greater soft tissue depth. Oblique insertion,
  max 0.5–0.8 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### Batch 9 Observations

**Observation 24 — MEDIAL-OUTER PAIRING: DORSAL RAMUS BRANCH
DIFFERENTIATION.**

The 7 outer-line points (BL41–BL47) each pair with a medial-line
point at the SAME spinal level:

| Outer (3 cun) | Medial (1.5 cun) | Level | Medial designation |
|---|---|---|---|
| BL41 (Fufen) | BL12 (Fengmen) | T2 | GV meeting, Wind Gate |
| BL42 (Pohu) | BL13 (Feishu) | T3 | Lung Back-Shu |
| BL43 (Gaohuangshu) | BL14 (Jueyinshu) | T4 | PC Back-Shu |
| BL44 (Shentang) | BL15 (Xinshu) | T5 | Heart Back-Shu |
| BL45 (Yixi) | BL16 (Dushu) | T6 | (no designation) |
| BL46 (Geguan) | BL17 (Geshu) | T7 | Hui-Blood |
| BL47 (Hunmen) | BL18 (Ganshu) | T9 | Liver Back-Shu |

The neural substrate difference: medial branch of dorsal ramus
(1.5 cun → multifidus, facet joints, closest to sympathetic chain)
vs lateral branch (3 cun → longissimus, iliocostalis, broader
fascial territory). Both branches converge on the SAME spinal cord
segment. The consistent clinical pattern: medial points have
VISCERAL functions (organ Back-Shu), outer points have
MUSCULOSKELETAL + CONSTITUTIONAL functions (spirit residence,
general tonification). This functional divergence from a single
spinal level may reflect differential sympathetic chain access:
medial branch → more direct sympathetic proximity → stronger
viscerosomatic reflex; lateral branch → broader muscular/fascial
territory → more diffuse, constitutional effect.

**Observation 25 — FIVE-SPIRIT POINTS AND SEGMENTAL MIRRORING.**

Three of the five 五神 spirit points occur in this batch. EVERY
five-spirit point sits at the EXACT same spinal level as the
corresponding Zang organ's Back-Shu:

| Spirit | Organ | Outer (3 cun) | Medial Back-Shu (1.5 cun) | Level |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Po (魄) | Lung | BL42 | BL13 | T3 |
| Shen (神) | Heart | BL44 | BL15 | T5 |
| Hun (魂) | Liver | BL47 | BL18 | T9 |
| Yi (意) | Spleen | BL49 | BL20 | T11 |
| Zhi (志) | Kidney | BL52 | BL23 | L2 |

All 5 spirit points pair with the 5 Zang organ Back-Shu — and ONLY
with Zang organs (not Fu). No Fu organ Back-Shu has a corresponding
spirit point. This reflects the TCM principle that Zang organs store
精/essence and house 神/spirits, while Fu organs process and
transmit substances. The five-spirit naming is a NOMENCLATURE-LEVEL
encoding of Zang-organ assignment — the outer-line names TELL you
which medial-line organ Shu they correspond to, even without knowing
the spinal level.

From BSA perspective: the same spinal segment produces visceral
effects (medial branch → organ Shu) and potentially psychoemotional
effects (lateral branch → spirit point). Whether the lateral branch
of a dorsal ramus at T9 can produce different emotional outcomes
than the medial branch remains mechanistically unclear. The most
parsimonious explanation: both branches activate the same dorsal
horn → same autonomic output → the "spirit" designation reflects
TCM psychological theory superimposed on the same somatovisceral
pathway, not a distinct anatomical mechanism.

**Observation 26 — T8 GAP MIRRORED ON OUTER LINE.**

Between BL46 (T7) and BL47 (T9), there is NO outer-line point at
T8 — exactly mirroring the medial-line gap between BL17 (T7) and
BL18 (T9). The T8 gap is the ONLY single-level omission in the BL
back line system (T1–S4). It is maintained across BOTH lines:

- Medial: BL17/T7 (Geshu, Hui-Blood) → **[T8 gap]** → BL18/T9
  (Ganshu, Liver)
- Outer: BL46/T7 (Geguan, Diaphragm Gate) → **[T8 gap]** → BL47/T9
  (Hunmen, Liver Spirit)

T7 = diaphragm level (anatomical thoracic-abdominal boundary).
T9 = liver/stomach upper border. T8 = transition zone with NO named
organ in TCM.

The systematic maintenance of this gap on BOTH lines strongly
suggests it reflects an anatomical observation rather than a
transcription error. T8 may represent a genuine paucity of distinct
visceral targets at the diaphragmatic transition — the diaphragm
is innervated primarily via phrenic (C3–C5), not T8, and no major
organ has T8 as its primary segmental access. The BL back line
builders recognized this transitional gap and preserved it
identically on both medial and lateral trajectories.

---

## Outer Back Line — Lower Thoracic / Lumbar / Sacral (BL48–BL54)

The lower outer back line (第二側線, continued) extends from T10
to S4, completing the 14-point outer-line system that began at T2
(BL41). These 7 points continue the medial-outer pairing
established in Batch 9 (Observation 24): each outer-line point
uses the LATERAL branch of the dorsal ramus at the same spinal
level as a medial-line point. A significant structural difference
appears here: while the upper outer line (BL41–BL52) maintains
continuous coverage from T2 to L2 (mirroring medial BL12–BL23),
the lower outer line SKIPS 5 spinal levels (L3, L4, L5, S1, S3)
where the medial line has points (BL24–BL27, BL29). This
lumbosacral gap coincides with iliac crest obstruction and the
medial line's weakest organ assignments.

Two five-spirit points complete the 五神 system: BL49/Yi (意,
Spleen, T11) and BL52/Zhi (志, Kidney, L2) — the 4th and 5th
of 5 spirit-organ designations.

BL54 (Zhibian, S4) is the LOWEST outer-line point and the second
most clinically used outer-line point after BL43 (Gaohuangshu) —
primarily for sciatic pain via direct piriformis/sciatic nerve
access.

### BL48 — Dương Cương (阳纲, Yánggāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 阳纲 (Yánggāng) = "Yang's Key Link" (阳 = Yang, 纲 =
headrope of a net, governing cord). The name references Yang
governance — the Gallbladder is the Yang Fu organ paired with
Liver (Yin). BL48 is at T10, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal
level as BL19 (Danshu, Gallbladder Back-Shu, T10, 1.5 cun). The
medial-outer pairing continues the dorsal ramus branch
differentiation: BL19 (medial branch) → visceral GB function;
BL48 (lateral branch) → musculoskeletal/constitutional.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T10.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique medially. Below the
scapula (inferior angle ~T7). Pneumothorax risk diminishing at
T10 but still present.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T10 spinal nerve**
- T10 = GB viscerotome upper boundary (T6–T10)
- Spinal segment: T10
- Compare: BL19 (medial pair) uses MEDIAL branch of same T10
  dorsal ramus
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia → latissimus dorsi →
serratus posterior inferior → erector spinae (longissimus,
iliocostalis).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 10th posterior intercostal artery,
lateral distribution.

Muscle layers: skin → latissimus dorsi → serratus posterior
inferior (variable, begins ~T11) → erector spinae (longissimus
thoracis, iliocostalis thoracis) → 10th rib.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Five-Spirit: **No** — GB is a Fu organ (no spirit designation).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL48 (Yangang 阳纲). Traditional functions: clear
  damp-heat from Liver/GB, regulate bile. Indications: jaundice,
  borborygmus, abdominal pain, diarrhea.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T10 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae / serratus posterior inferior. Referred pain to
  lower thorax and upper abdomen.

Ashi correspondence: T10 paravertebral tenderness in
  hepatobiliary disease, similar sensitization zone as BL19.

Marma point: ✗ No clear Marma correspondence at T10 posterior.

Head zone: T10 → GB viscerotome (T6–T10). **✅ MATCH** — same
  segmental match as BL19.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae continuous fascial
  chain.

Neurogenic spot: T10 paravertebral — limited data, 🟡 by
  extension from visceral disease sensitization patterns.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable but BL19 (medial)
  is the primary GB-area treatment point.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: jaundice, borborygmus, abdominal pain
- Dacheng: diarrhea, intestinal sounds, abdominal distension

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Jaundice, borborygmus, abdominal pain, diarrhea

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL48-specific evidence.
Hepatobiliary indications overlap with BL19 (same T10 segment)
which itself has limited individual evidence.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **Diminishing** — T10 is the lowest thoracic
  level with significant pleural proximity. Costophrenic recess
  risk rather than direct lung parenchyma. Oblique insertion, max
  0.5–0.8 cun.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL49 — Ý Xá (意舍, Yìshè)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Five-Spirit: Yi (意,
Spleen)**

Name 意舍 (Yìshè) = "Abode of Yi" (Abode of Intellect/Intention).
Yi (意) is the 4th of the Five Spirits (五神), associated with
the Spleen. In TCM psychospiritual theory, Yi governs THOUGHT and
INTENTION — the capacity for concentrated thinking, analysis, and
recall:

- **Thinking (思, sī):** focused mental activity, reasoning,
  analysis
- **Intention (意, yì):** the directive aspect of mind — what Yi
  literally means
- **Rumination:** overthinking is Yi's pathological extreme — when
  Spleen Qi weakens, thinking loops without resolution
- **Applied memory:** working/practical memory (distinct from
  Zhi's constitutional/dispositional memory)
- **Concentration:** capacity for sustained study and focused
  attention

The Spleen-worry connection: TCM teaches that worry (忧) and
overthinking (思虑过度) damage the Spleen. Conversely, Spleen
deficiency produces excessive worry and rumination. This maps to
the gut-brain axis: chronic stress → altered gut motility →
digestive symptoms, and gut inflammation → vagal afferents →
altered mood/cognition.

BL49 is at T11, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL20
(Pishu, Spleen Back-Shu, T11, 1.5 cun). This is the 4th
medial-outer spirit pair: BL20 treats the Spleen physically →
BL49 accesses the Spleen's psychospiritual aspect (thought,
rumination, worry).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T11.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun oblique. Transition zone —
pneumothorax risk minimal, kidney risk emerging (upper pole
~T11–T12).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T11 spinal nerve**
- T11 = spleen/pancreas viscerotome (T9–T11)
- Spinal segment: T11
- Compare: BL20 (medial pair) uses MEDIAL branch of same T11
  dorsal ramus
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia → latissimus dorsi →
serratus posterior inferior → erector spinae.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 11th posterior intercostal artery,
lateral distribution.

Muscle layers: skin → latissimus dorsi → serratus posterior
inferior (T11–L3 origin) → erector spinae (longissimus,
iliocostalis) → 11th rib.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Five-Spirit point: Yi (意) — Spleen's spirit (4th of 5)**

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL49 (Yishe 意舍). Traditional functions: tonify
  Spleen, resolve damp, regulate Yi. Indications: abdominal
  distension, vomiting, diarrhea, poor appetite, excessive
  rumination/overthinking (psychospiritual).

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T11 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae / serratus posterior inferior. Referred pain to
  lower thorax and flank.

Ashi correspondence: T11 paravertebral tenderness in digestive
  disease. Same sensitization zone as BL20.

Marma point: ✗ No clear Marma correspondence at T11 posterior.

Head zone: T11 → spleen/pancreas viscerotome (T9–T11).
  **✅ MATCH** — same segmental match as BL20.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: T11 paravertebral — limited data, 🟡 by
  extension from visceral disease sensitization.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable but BL20 (medial)
  is primary for Spleen treatment.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: abdominal distension, vomiting, diarrhea
- Dacheng: Spleen deficiency with excessive thinking, poor
  appetite, loose stools

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Abdominal distension, nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, borborygmus

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL49-specific evidence.
Digestive indications overlap with BL20 (same T11 segment) which
has established evidence base.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **Minimal** — T11 is below the typical lung
  extent. Costophrenic recess possible in deep inspiration.

Kidney risk: T11 approaches kidney upper pole territory. At 3 cun
  lateral, the needle trajectory is lateral to the kidney, but
  deep perpendicular insertion in thin patients warrants caution.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.
