### BL50 — Vị Thương (胃仓, Wèicāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 胃仓 (Wèicāng) = "Stomach Granary" (胃 = stomach, 仓 =
granary/storehouse). A Fu-organ name directly referencing the
Stomach's function as the body's food storehouse. NOT a
five-spirit name — the Stomach is a Fu organ, and ONLY Zang
organs have spirit designations. The naming pattern at this level
is organ-functional rather than psychospiritual.

BL50 is at T12, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL21
(Weishu, Stomach Back-Shu, T12, 1.5 cun). BL21 is one of the 3
Dorsher non-matching Back-Shu (T12 vs Stomach viscerotome T6–T9,
a 3-segment caudal displacement). BL50 inherits this segmental
position — the outer-line counterpart of a non-matching medial
Shu.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of T12.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun oblique or perpendicular. No
significant pneumothorax risk at T12. Kidney risk present
(posterior surface T12–L2).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of T12 spinal nerve**
- T12 ≠ stomach viscerotome (T6–T9) — SAME segmental mismatch
  as BL21
- Spinal segment: T12
- T12 = subcostal nerve level, boundary between thoracic and
  lumbar innervation
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia → latissimus dorsi → erector
spinae.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of subcostal artery (12th intercostal),
lateral distribution.

Muscle layers: skin → latissimus dorsi (lower fibers) → erector
spinae (longissimus, iliocostalis) → quadratus lumborum
(beginning). 12th rib articulates at T12.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Five-Spirit: **No** — Stomach is a Fu organ (no spirit
designation).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL50 (Weicang 胃仓). Traditional functions:
  harmonize Stomach, resolve food stagnation. Indications:
  epigastric pain, abdominal distension, edema of the back, poor
  appetite.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** T12 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae. Referred pain to lower back and upper abdominal
  area.

Ashi correspondence: T12 paravertebral tenderness. Less specific
  organ association than T6–T9 (Stomach's primary viscerotome).

Marma point: ✗ No clear Marma correspondence at T12 posterior.

Head zone: T12 ≠ stomach primary viscerotome (T6–T9).
  **⚠ MISMATCH** — same segmental discrepancy as BL21. T12 Head
  zone corresponds more to kidney/ureter territory.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae.

Neurogenic spot: T12 paravertebral — limited data, 🟡.

Cupping point: paravertebral cupping applicable. BL21 (medial) is
  primary Stomach-area treatment point.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Head zone ✗ MISMATCH (inherits BL21's segmental
discrepancy). Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: epigastric pain, abdominal distension
- Dacheng: poor appetite, edema of the back, abdominal distension

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Epigastric pain, abdominal distension, spinal stiffness

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL50-specific evidence.
Digestive indications overlap with BL21 (same T12 segment).
Notably, BL50 inherits BL21's segmental mismatch — T12 does not
directly access the stomach's primary innervation (T6–T9).

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — T12 is below the pleural reflection.

Kidney risk: T12 approaches the kidney posterior surface. At 3 cun
  lateral, the needle is lateral to the kidney's typical position,
  but deep perpendicular insertion warrants caution in thin
  patients.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL51 — Hoang Môn (肓门, Huāngmén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 肓门 (Huāngmén) = "Vitals Gate" (肓 = vital membrane/tissue,
门 = gate). The character 肓 appears in three BL point names:
BL43 膏肓俞 (Gaohuangshu, T4), BL51 肓门 (Huangmen, L1), and
BL53 胞肓 (Baohuang, S2). In each case, 肓 refers to the
connective tissue membranes surrounding vital organs — regions
classically described as difficult to access by needle or
medicine. At BL43, 肓 is the mediastinal space between heart and
diaphragm; at BL51, 肓 is the membrane surrounding the lower
abdominal viscera.

BL51 is at L1, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL22
(Sanjiaoshu, Triple Burner Back-Shu, L1, 1.5 cun). BL22 is the
Back-Shu of the Triple Burner — an organ without discrete
anatomical counterpart. BL51's name "Vitals Gate" resonates with
the Triple Burner's role as the body's water passages and membrane spaces (三焦 =
the three membrane-defined body cavities).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of L1.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular or oblique. No
pneumothorax risk. Retroperitoneal structures accessible with
deep needling.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of L1 spinal nerve**
- L1 = thoracolumbar junction, iliohypogastric nerve origin
- Spinal segment: L1
- Compare: BL22 (medial pair) uses MEDIAL branch of same L1
  dorsal ramus
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (well-developed at L1, three
layers) → erector spinae → quadratus lumborum.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 1st lumbar artery, lateral
distribution.

Muscle layers: skin → thoracolumbar fascia → erector spinae
(longissimus, iliocostalis) → quadratus lumborum → transversus
abdominis (lateral edge). L1 = transition from thoracic to lumbar
musculature.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Five-Spirit: **No** — Triple Burner is a Fu concept (no spirit
designation). BL51 has no special classification.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL51 (Huangmen 肓门). Traditional functions:
  regulate Qi, dissipate abdominal masses. Indications: abdominal
  masses, constipation, breast disease (some sources).

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** L1 paravertebral TrPs in
  erector spinae / quadratus lumborum. Referred pain to lower
  back, flank, and upper buttock.

Ashi correspondence: L1 paravertebral tenderness in renal /
  ureteric conditions. Sensitization zone overlaps with BL22.

Marma point: **Possible.** Katikataruna Marma (hip-region)
  includes the lower thoracolumbar area in some descriptions. 🟡

Head zone: L1 → kidney/ureter viscerotome (T12–L1). **Partial** —
  matches kidney territory at L1, but the Triple Burner has no discrete
  viscerotome (same problem as BL22's convergence analysis).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae. Lateral Line
  proximity (quadratus lumborum connection at L1).

Neurogenic spot: L1 paravertebral — limited data, 🟡.

Cupping point: lumbar cupping applicable. BL22 (medial) is
  primary Triple Burner-area treatment point.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Head zone partial — matches renal territory but the Triple Burner
lacks a viscerotome (same Triple Burner no-organ problem as BL22). Marma 🟡,
neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: abdominal masses, constipation
- Dacheng: breast disease, abdominal lumps, constipation

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Abdominal masses, constipation, mastitis (breast)

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL51-specific evidence.
The breast indication is unusual for a lower back point — it may
relate to the Triple Burner's "distribution" function across the three jiao
(breast = upper jiao territory). Mechanistically, L1 provides no
obvious breast innervation pathway (breast = T3–T5).

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L1 is well below the pleural cavity.

Kidney risk: At L1, 3 cun lateral, the kidney lower pole is
  medial to the needle trajectory. Deep perpendicular insertion
  warrants caution in thin patients.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL52 — Chí Thất (志室, Zhìshì)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Five-Spirit: Zhi (志,
Kidney) — COMPLETES the Five-Spirit System (5/5)**

Name 志室 (Zhìshì) = "Room of Willpower" (志 = will/
determination, 室 = room/chamber). Zhi is the 5th and FINAL of
the Five Spirits (五神), associated with the Kidney. With BL52
documented, all five spirit-organ pairings are complete:

**Zhi (志) governs:**
- **Willpower (志, zhì):** the drive to persevere, capacity for
  sustained effort
- **Determination (决断):** resolute decision-making, firmness of
  purpose
- **Fear/courage:** fear is the Kidney's emotion — insufficient
  Zhi manifests as timidity, being easily frightened, and lacking
  backbone; strong Zhi = courage under pressure
- **Constitutional memory:** the deepest form of memory —
  ancestral patterns, long-term dispositional recall (distinct from
  Yi's applied/working memory)
- **Sexual drive:** Kidney governs reproduction; Zhi provides the
  motivational/drive component of sexual behavior

The Kidney-fear connection: TCM teaches that fear (恐, kǒng)
damages the Kidney, and Kidney deficiency produces fearfulness
and lack of ambition. This maps to the HPA axis: chronic
fear/stress → sustained cortisol release → hypothalamic-
pituitary-adrenal axis dysregulation → fatigue, low motivation,
anxiety, cognitive decline.

BL52 is at L2, 3 cun lateral — the SAME spinal level as BL23
(Shenshu, Kidney Back-Shu, L2, 1.5 cun). BL23 is the MOST
prescribed Back-Shu in clinical practice. The BL23–BL52
medial-outer pair is the strongest such pair in the system: BL23
treats physical Kidney function (lumbar pain, urinary,
reproductive) → BL52 accesses the Kidney's psychological
dimension (willpower, fear, determination).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the lower
border of the spinous process of L2.

Needle depth range: 0.8–1.2 cun perpendicular or oblique. No
pneumothorax risk. L2 = level of conus medullaris termination.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral branch of dorsal ramus of L2 spinal nerve**
- L2 = kidney viscerotome (T12–L2)
- Spinal segment: L2
- Compare: BL23 (medial pair) uses MEDIAL branch of same L2
  dorsal ramus
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (thickest at L1–L3, three-
layer structure) → erector spinae → quadratus lumborum.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal branch of 2nd lumbar artery, lateral
distribution.

Muscle layers: skin → thoracolumbar fascia → erector spinae
(longissimus, iliocostalis) → quadratus lumborum → psoas major
(deep). Well-developed musculature at L2.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Five-Spirit point: Zhi (志) — Kidney's spirit (5th of 5 —
FINAL)**

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL52 (Zhishi 志室). Traditional functions: tonify
  Kidney, strengthen willpower, secure essence. Indications:
  lumbar pain, seminal emission, impotence, urinary difficulty,
  edema, lack of ambition/willpower (psychospiritual).

Trigger point: **Strong correspondence.** L2 paravertebral TrPs
  in quadratus lumborum / erector spinae. Referred pain to lumbar
  area, hip, and groin. QL TrPs are among the most common causes
  of low back pain.

Ashi correspondence: L2 paravertebral tenderness is common in
  renal colic and chronic low back pain. Strong overlap with BL23
  sensitization zone.

Marma point: **Possible.** Kukundara Marma (lumbar-sacral
  junction, bilateral) is described near L2–L4 level. 🟡

Head zone: L2 → kidney viscerotome (T12–L2). **✅ MATCH** — same
  segmental match as BL23.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through erector spinae. Deep Front Line
  connection via quadratus lumborum → psoas.

Neurogenic spot: L2 paravertebral — strong sensitization during
  renal disease (T12–L2 zone, Sato 1997 somatovisceral data).

Cupping point: lumbar cupping applicable. BL23 (medial) is the
  primary Kidney-area treatment point.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡, neurogenic 🟡 (strong), cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, seminal emission, urinary difficulty
- Dacheng: Kidney deficiency with impotence, weak knees and
  lumbar area, lack of willpower

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar stiffness/pain, seminal emission, impotence, dysuria,
  edema

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL52-specific evidence.
Kidney/urogenital indications overlap with BL23 (same L2 segment)
which has extensive evidence. The sexual/willpower indications are
the most frequently cited psychospiritual aspects of BL52 but
lack controlled studies.

#### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — L2 is well below the pleural cavity.

Kidney risk: At L2, 3 cun lateral, the kidney's lateral border is
  medial to the needle trajectory in most patients. Deep
  perpendicular insertion may approach the retroperitoneal space
  but kidney puncture is unlikely at standard depth.

Vascular risk: Minimal. Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL53 — Bào Hoang (胞肓, Bāohuāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 胞肓 (Bāohuāng) = "Uterus/Bladder Membrane" (胞 =
uterus/womb or bladder envelope, 肓 = vital membrane). The
character 胞 in classical Chinese carries dual meaning: it can
refer to the urinary bladder (膀胱) or the uterus (子宫). This
ambiguity is anatomically apt at S2: the parasympathetic pelvic
splanchnic nerves (S2–S4) innervate BOTH the urinary bladder and
the uterus through the same nerve roots.

BL53 is at S2, 3 cun lateral — the SAME sacral level as BL28
(Pangguangshu, Bladder Back-Shu, S2, 1.5 cun). BL28 is the LAST
organ Back-Shu (12/12), and the ONLY Back-Shu with a
parasympathetic match (S2 pelvic splanchnic → voiding).

**LUMBOSACRAL GAP:** Between BL52 (L2) and BL53 (S2), the outer
line SKIPS 4 spinal levels (L3, L4, L5, S1). On the medial line,
BL24 (L3), BL25 (L4, LI Shu), BL26 (L5), and BL27 (S1, SI Shu)
occupy these levels. This L3–S1 gap is the LARGEST discontinuity
in the outer back line — see Observation 27.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the median
sacral crest, at the level of the 2nd posterior sacral foramen.

At S2, 3 cun lateral (~7–8 cm from midline), the point is LATERAL
to the sacrum, in the upper gluteal region. The sacrum at S2 level
is approximately 5–6 cm wide, so 3 cun lateral places the point
over the gluteus maximus, deep to which lies the piriformis.

Needle depth range: 1.0–1.5 cun perpendicular. Deep gluteal
musculature allows greater depth than thoracic/lumbar outer-line
points.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral sacral branches** from S2 dorsal sacral ramus
- S2 = pelvic splanchnic nerves (parasympathetic to bladder,
  rectum, uterus)
- S2 = pudendal nerve origin (S2–S4)
- Spinal segment: S2
- Compare: BL28 (medial pair) at 1.5 cun accesses the same S2
  level closer to the sacral foramina
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: gluteal aponeurosis → gluteus maximus →
piriformis. Substantially different tissue plane from
thoracolumbar outer-line points — transition from erector spinae
territory to gluteal musculature.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: lateral sacral artery (from internal iliac), superior
gluteal artery territory.

Muscle layers: skin → gluteal fascia → gluteus maximus →
piriformis. The tissue environment at BL53 is fundamentally
different from BL48–BL52: gluteal rather than paravertebral.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Five-Spirit: **No** — BL53 has no special classification.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL53 (Baohuang 胞肓). Traditional functions:
  promote Bladder Qi transformation, regulate the lower jiao.
  Indications: lumbar/sacral pain, urinary difficulty, abdominal
  distension, constipation.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Piriformis/deep gluteal TrPs
  at S2 level. Referred pain to sacroiliac region and posterior
  thigh. Piriformis TrPs are well-documented in lumbar/sciatic
  pain.

Ashi correspondence: sacroiliac tenderness in urogenital and
  pelvic conditions. Overlap with BL28 and Baliao territory.

Marma point: ✗ No clear Marma correspondence in the
  sacral-gluteal transition.

Head zone: S2 → bladder/uterus parasympathetic territory
  (S2–S4). **✅ MATCH** — same parasympathetic match as BL28.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL lower extension through sacrotuberous
  ligament → hamstrings. Transitional zone from paravertebral SBL
  to lower-limb posterior chain.

Neurogenic spot: S2 sacral — limited data for outer-line
  position, 🟡. Sacral foramina points (BL31–34, medial) have
  stronger neurogenic evidence.

Cupping point: gluteal cupping occasionally used, not primary.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: lumbar/sacral pain, abdominal distension
- Dacheng: urinary difficulty, constipation, sacral stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lumbar/sacral pain, urinary difficulty, abdominal distension,
  constipation

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL53-specific evidence.
Pelvic/urogenital indications overlap with BL28 (same S2 segment)
and Baliao group (BL31–34) which have stronger evidence.

#### An toàn

Sciatic nerve: S2, 3 cun lateral = upper gluteal region with
  sciatic nerve deep to piriformis. Deep needling (>1.5 cun) may
  approach the sciatic nerve — therapeutic for sciatica but
  warrants caution.

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — sacral level.

Vascular risk: superior gluteal artery territory. Minimal risk at
  standard depth.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication for BL53 itself, though
  the region overlaps with Baliao/sacral points that some sources
  avoid in pregnancy.

---

### BL54 — Trật Biên (秩边, Zhìbiān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — Lowest Outer-Line
Point**

Name 秩边 (Zhìbiān) = "Sequential Limit" (秩 = sequence/rank/
order, 边 = edge/border/limit). The name signals that this is the
TERMINAL point of the outer back line — the boundary beyond which
the sequential ordering of the second lateral line ends. BL54 is
the 14th and LAST point on the outer back line (BL41–BL54).

BL54 is at S4, 3 cun lateral — the SAME level as BL30
(Baihuanshu, White Ring Shu, S4, 1.5 cun), the lowest point on
the medial back line. Between BL53 (S2) and BL54 (S4), the outer
line skips S3, where BL29 (Zhonglüshu) sits on the medial line.

Despite Tier C classification, BL54 is the SECOND most clinically
used outer-line point after BL43 (Gaohuangshu). Its primary
indication — sciatic pain — has a straightforward anatomical
explanation: at S4, 3 cun lateral, the needle enters the deep
gluteal region where the sciatic nerve courses after emerging from
the pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen, typically inferior
to the piriformis muscle. This is functionally a NERVE-TARGETING
point.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to the median
sacral crest, at the level of the 4th posterior sacral foramen.

At S4, 3 cun lateral, the point is well LATERAL to the sacrum, in
the lower gluteal region, over the gluteus maximus. Deep to the
gluteus maximus at this level: piriformis (inferiorly) and
coccygeus/ischiococcygeus. The sciatic nerve passes through the
infrapiriform space of the greater sciatic foramen.

Needle depth range: 1.5–3.0 cun perpendicular. The deepest safe
insertion in the outer back line — thick gluteal musculature
provides a generous safety margin absent in thoracic levels. Deep
needling toward the sciatic nerve is an intentional therapeutic
technique.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **S4 dorsal sacral ramus** — lateral branches
- Deep: **sciatic nerve** (L4–S3) — the LARGEST nerve in the
  body, passing through the infrapiriform space into the gluteal
  region. At BL54's depth (2–3 cun), the needle approaches or
  contacts the sciatic nerve.
- Inferior gluteal nerve (L5–S2) — motor to gluteus maximus
- Pudendal nerve (S2–S4) — passes through the lesser sciatic
  foramen
- S4 = coccygeal plexus, inferior rectal nerve
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: gluteal aponeurosis → gluteus maximus →
piriformis / obturator internus / gemelli (deep rotators).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: inferior gluteal artery (from internal iliac), internal
pudendal artery territory. Greater vascular complexity than
thoracolumbar outer-line points.

Muscle layers: skin → gluteal fascia → gluteus maximus (thick,
2–4 cm) → piriformis → sacrospinous ligament → pelvic floor
musculature.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: **No** — outer-line point, not organ Back-Shu.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
Five-Spirit: **No** — BL54 has no special classification.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL54 (Zhibian 秩边). Traditional functions: clear
  damp-heat from lower jiao, benefit the lumbar region and legs.
  Indications: sciatic pain (primary), hemorrhoids, urinary
  difficulty, lumbar/sacral pain, lower limb motor impairment.

Trigger point: **Strong correspondence.** Piriformis TrPs are the
  BEST-documented correlation in this region. Piriformis syndrome
  (deep gluteal syndrome) causes sciatic-type pain via compression
  or irritation of the sciatic nerve. BL54's location directly
  overlies the piriformis/sciatic interface. This is one of the
  strongest trigger point correspondences in the outer back line.

Ashi correspondence: **Strong.** Deep gluteal tenderness in
  sciatic and pelvic pain conditions. Patients with sciatica
  frequently exhibit tenderness at BL54's location.

Marma point: **Possible.** Nitamba Marma (buttock Marma) described
  in the gluteal region. 🟡

Head zone: S4 → pelvic visceral territory (S2–S4
  parasympathetic). **✅ MATCH for pelvic/urogenital function.** The
  hemorrhoid and urinary indications map to S2–S4 parasympathetic
  innervation territory.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL lower extension (sacrotuberous ligament →
  hamstrings). Deep Front Line (psoas → iliacus → pelvic floor
  connection). The sciatic nerve itself follows the SBL trajectory
  down the posterior leg.

Neurogenic spot: strong — deep gluteal sensitization in pelvic
  and sciatic pathology.

Cupping point: gluteal cupping occasionally used, not primary.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡, neurogenic 🟡 (strong), cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: lumbar pain, sciatic pain, hemorrhoids, dysuria
- Dacheng: lower limb motor impairment, hemorrhoids, seminal
  emission

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Sciatic pain (primary), hemorrhoids, urinary difficulty,
  lumbar/sacral pain, lower limb motor impairment

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated** for BL54 specifically.
However, the sciatic indication has strong mechanistic
plausibility: BL54's location at the piriformis/sciatic nerve
interface means needling at depth directly stimulates or
decompresses the sciatic nerve. This mechanism is functionally
equivalent to piriformis trigger point dry needling, for which
moderate evidence exists in pain medicine literature (though not
specifically at the "BL54" designation).

#### An toàn

Sciatic nerve: Deep needling (>2 cun) INTENTIONALLY approaches the
  sciatic nerve. This is the therapeutic mechanism but also the
  primary risk — vigorous manipulation at depth can cause neural
  irritation. Gradual insertion with attention to patient response
  (radiating sensation to posterior thigh = nerve contact).

Vascular risk: Inferior gluteal artery passes through the gluteal
  region. Deep needling carries moderate vascular risk compared to
  thoracic outer-line points.

Pneumothorax risk: **No** — sacral level.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication for BL54, though the
  pelvic region warrants caution in late pregnancy.

---

### Batch 10 Observations

**Observation 27 — LUMBOSACRAL GAP: OUTER LINE OMITS L3–S1 AND
S3.**

Between BL52 (L2) and BL53 (S2), the outer back line SKIPS 4
spinal levels (L3, L4, L5, S1) — the LARGEST discontinuity in
either BL back line. An additional gap at S3 (between BL53/S2 and
BL54/S4) brings the total to 5 omitted levels. On the medial
line, these levels have points:

| Medial (1.5 cun) | Level | Outer (3 cun) |
|---|---|---|
| BL24 (Qihaishu) | L3 | ✗ absent |
| BL25 (Dachangshu, LI Shu) | L4 | ✗ absent |
| BL26 (Guanyuanshu) | L5 | ✗ absent |
| BL27 (Xiaochangshu, SI Shu) | S1 | ✗ absent |
| BL29 (Zhonglüshu) | S3 | ✗ absent |

Three factors explain this gap:

1. **Iliac crest obstruction.** The iliac crest reaches L4–L5
level posteriorly. At 3 cun lateral (~7–8 cm from midline), the
L3–S1 zone transitions from paravertebral musculature (erector
spinae) to iliac crest and upper gluteal territory — making
consistent point location impractical with the "3 cun lateral to
spinous process" formula.

2. **Weakest organ assignments omitted.** Of the 5 skipped medial
points, BL24 and BL26 are non-organ fillers, and BL25 (LI Shu)
and BL27 (SI Shu) are two of the three Dorsher NON-MATCHING
Back-Shu (the most segmentally questionable organ assignments in
the entire back line). BL29 is also a non-organ filler. The outer
line skips exactly the levels where the medial line's organ-access
rationale is weakest.

3. **No Zang organs → no spirit names possible.** All 5 skipped
levels correspond to Fu organs (LI, SI) or non-organ points.
Since only Zang organs generate spirit names, these levels would
produce outer-line points without either organ Back-Shu function
OR spirit naming — the two features that define the outer line's
identity.

The outer line's design logic: maintain coverage WHERE there are
Zang organs to mirror (T2–L2), skip WHERE anatomy obstructs and
organ rationale weakens (L3–S1), then add two final pelvic/
gluteal access points (S2, S4) that serve distinct functions
(BL53 = pelvic visceral, BL54 = sciatic nerve).

**Observation 28 — FIVE-SPIRIT SYSTEM COMPLETE (5/5): FULL
ARCHITECTURE.**

With BL49 (Yi/Spleen) and BL52 (Zhi/Kidney), all five 五神
spirit points are documented. The complete system:

| Spirit | Chinese | Organ | Element | Outer | Medial Shu | Level | Domain |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Po (魄) | Corporeal soul | Lung | Metal | BL42 | BL13 | T3 | Breath, bodily awareness, grief |
| Shen (神) | Spirit | Heart | Fire | BL44 | BL15 | T5 | Consciousness, joy/anxiety |
| Hun (魂) | Ethereal soul | Liver | Wood | BL47 | BL18 | T9 | Dreams, vision, anger, planning |
| Yi (意) | Intellect | Spleen | Earth | BL49 | BL20 | T11 | Thought, rumination, worry |
| Zhi (志) | Willpower | Kidney | Water | BL52 | BL23 | L2 | Determination, fear/courage |

Architectural features:

1. **EXACT segmental mirroring:** every spirit point occupies the
SAME spinal level as its Zang organ Back-Shu, accessed via the
lateral vs medial branch of the same dorsal ramus.

2. **ONLY Zang organs (0/6 Fu):** reinforces the TCM principle
that Zang stores essence and houses spirits, while Fu processes
and transmits.

3. **Five Phase correspondence:** each spirit maps to its organ's
Wu Xing element — Po/Metal, Shen/Fire, Hun/Wood, Yi/Earth,
Zhi/Water. The five-spirit system IS the Five Phase theory applied
to psychology.

4. **Progressive deepening (T3→L2):** the spirits progress from
the most physical/somatic (Po = breath, bodily sensation) to the
most constitutional/volitional (Zhi = willpower, ancestral
memory). This parallels the body axis: thoracic (respiratory/
cardiac = more reactive/immediate) → lumbar (renal = more
constitutional/foundational).

5. **BSA interpretation (unchanged from Observation 25):** the
most parsimonious explanation remains that both medial and lateral
branches activate the same dorsal horn segment → same autonomic
output. The "spirit" designation reflects TCM psychological theory
superimposed on the same somatovisceral pathway, not a distinct
anatomical mechanism. However, the CONSISTENCY of the system —
5/5 exact level matches, strict Zang-only selection, Five Phase
alignment — indicates a highly structured theoretical framework
rather than random assignment.

**Observation 29 — OUTER BACK LINE COMPLETE (14/14): STRUCTURAL
SUMMARY.**

With BL48–BL54, the entire outer back line (第二側線) is
complete. Architecture comparison:

| Property | Outer line (3 cun) | Medial line (1.5 cun) |
|---|---|---|
| Total points | 14 (BL41–BL54) | 20 (BL11–BL30) |
| Level range | T2–L2, S2, S4 | T1–S4 |
| Continuous | T2–L2 (12 pts, T8 gap) | T1–S4 (20 pts, T8 gap) |
| Extra gaps | L3–S1, S3 | none |
| Organ designations | 0 Back-Shu | 12 Back-Shu |
| Spirit names | 5 (all Zang) | 0 |
| Hui-Meeting | 0 | 2 (Bones, Blood) |
| Neural branch | Lateral dorsal ramus | Medial dorsal ramus |
| Target tissue | Longissimus, iliocostalis | Multifidus, facet joints |
| Clinical profile | Musculoskeletal, constitutional | Visceral (organ-specific) |
| Tier distribution | 1B + 13C | 8B + 3C + 5A (Phase 1) |
| Clinical usage | Low (mostly combined) | High (widely prescribed) |

The outer line is the least independently utilized section of the
BL channel — most outer-line points are combined with their medial
pairs rather than used alone. The two exceptions are BL43
(Gaohuangshu, for chronic disease via moxa) and BL54 (Zhibian,
for sciatica via direct nerve access). These two are clinically
relevant because they offer mechanisms DISTINCT from their medial
counterparts: BL43's moxa-specific thermal pathway and BL54's
sciatic nerve targeting.

---

## Posterior Calf (BL55–BL59)

The posterior calf segment traces the BL channel from just below
the popliteal fossa (BL40) to the lower leg above the ankle. The
dominant anatomical feature is the gastrocnemius-soleus complex:
BL55 at the proximal heads, BL56 at mid-belly, BL57 at the
gastrocnemius-soleus junction. BL58 and BL59 shift laterally to
the sural nerve territory.

The key neural transition: tibial nerve (deep, continuing from
BL40) → sural nerve (forming from the union of medial sural
cutaneous [tibial branch] and lateral sural cutaneous [common
peroneal branch]). The sural nerve is the ONLY lower limb nerve
formed by contributions from BOTH terminal branches of the
sciatic. Its formation zone (BL57–BL58 level) coincides with the
Luo-Connecting point (BL58), which links BL to its paired channel
KI — a suggestive neural-channel parallel.

This batch completes TWO major inventories: BL58 = 12th/12th
regular Luo-Connecting (LAST), BL59 = 4th/4th extraordinary
vessel Xi-Cleft (LAST).

---

### BL55 — Hợp Dương (合阳, Héyáng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 合阳 (Héyáng) = "Confluence of Yang" (合 = confluent/
meeting, 阳 = Yang). The name may reference the point of
convergence of the two heads of the gastrocnemius (lateral head
from lateral femoral condyle, medial head from medial femoral
condyle), which join at this level to form the single
gastrocnemius muscle belly. 阳 (Yang) reflects the posterior leg
as the Yang surface of the limb.

BL55 is 2 cun directly below BL40 (popliteal crease midpoint),
on the line connecting BL40 and BL57. This is the first BL point
on the posterior calf proper — the transition from popliteal
fossa to the gastrocnemius territory.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 2 cun directly below BL40
(Weizhong), between the medial and lateral heads of the
gastrocnemius muscle.

Needle depth range: 1.0–1.5 cun perpendicular into the
gastrocnemius. Deep needling may reach the soleus or the
tibial nerve.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Tibial nerve** (L4–S3) — deep, running between the two
  heads of the gastrocnemius. Continuation from BL40.
- **Medial sural cutaneous nerve** — branch of tibial, running
  superficial on the posterior calf, destined to join lateral
  sural cutaneous to form the sural nerve distally
- Spinal segment: S1–S2 (cutaneous), L4–S3 (tibial deep)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: crural fascia → gastrocnemius aponeurosis.
Proximal gastrocnemius — the two heads converge here, creating
a fascial convergence point.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: sural arteries (branches from popliteal artery to
gastrocnemius heads). Small saphenous vein superficial.

Muscle layers: skin → crural fascia → gastrocnemius (medial
and lateral heads converging) → soleus (deep) → tibial nerve
and posterior tibial vessels.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL55 (Heyang 合阳). Traditional functions:
  regulate uterine bleeding, relax sinews. Indications: uterine
  bleeding (崩漏), lumbar/leg pain, hernia, calf pain.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Proximal gastrocnemius TrPs
  at the muscle-head convergence. Referred pain to posterior
  calf and popliteal fossa.

Ashi correspondence: proximal calf tenderness in lumbar/leg
  pain syndromes. Extension of BL40 popliteal territory.

Marma point: ✗ No precise Marma at the proximal calf.

Head zone: S1–S2 → lower limb sensorimotor. **✅ MATCH** for
  local calf/leg indications. The uterine bleeding indication
  is notable: S2 overlaps with pelvic splanchnic territory.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through gastrocnemius — continuous chain
  from plantar fascia → Achilles → gastrocnemius → hamstrings.

Neurogenic spot: S1–S2 proximal calf — limited data, 🟡.

Cupping point: posterior calf cupping occasionally used, 🟡.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: uterine bleeding (崩漏), lumbar pain
- Dacheng: hernia, leg pain, calf numbness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Uterine bleeding, lumbar/leg pain, hernia, calf pain

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** The uterine bleeding
indication is notable for a calf point — the S2 segmental
overlap with pelvic splanchnic nerves provides a partial
segmental basis, though BL55 is far from the pelvis. No
BL55-specific clinical evidence.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: sural arteries at the gastrocnemius heads.
  Standard depth safe; deep needling may encounter vascular
  structures.

Neural risk: tibial nerve deep. Deep perpendicular insertion
  (>1.5 cun) may reach the nerve — patient reports electric
  sensation radiating to the foot.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL56 — Thừa Cân (承筋, Chéngjīn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 承筋 (Chéngjīn) = "Sinew Support" (承 = support/receive,
筋 = sinew/tendon/muscle). The name directly references the
gastrocnemius muscle belly — BL56 is positioned at the center
of the largest muscle mass in the calf. The 承 character connects
to BL36 承扶 (Chengfu, "Support") and BL57 承山 (Chengshan,
"Mountain Support") — a naming triad using 承 that marks the
three anatomical landmarks of the posterior leg's muscle-support
system: gluteal fold (BL36) → gastrocnemius belly (BL56) →
gastrocnemius-soleus junction (BL57).

BL56 is at the midpoint of the line connecting BL40 (popliteal
crease) and BL57 (gastrocnemius V-junction), placing it at the
center of the gastrocnemius belly where the muscle is thickest.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): at the midpoint between BL40
and BL57, in the center of the belly of the gastrocnemius muscle.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular into the
gastrocnemius belly. The muscle belly is thick here (3–5 cm).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Tibial nerve** (L4–S3) — deep to the gastrocnemius, between
  gastrocnemius and soleus
- **Medial sural cutaneous nerve** — superficial, running on the
  posterior calf. Sural nerve forming distally.
- Sural nerve branches to gastrocnemius (motor)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2 (cutaneous and motor to gastrocnemius)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: crural fascia → gastrocnemius aponeurosis.
Maximum muscle bulk of the posterior calf.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: sural arteries, intramuscular branches. Small
saphenous vein superficial.

Muscle layers: skin → crural fascia → gastrocnemius belly
(thickest part, 3–5 cm) → soleus → tibial nerve and posterior
tibial vessels (deep).

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL56 (Chengjin 承筋). Traditional functions: relax
  sinews, treat hemorrhoids. Indications: calf cramp (primary),
  hemorrhoids, lumbar stiffness, epistaxis (some sources).

Trigger point: **Strong correspondence.** Gastrocnemius central
  TrPs (Travell & Simons). The gastrocnemius belly is one of the
  most common TrP sites in the body — nocturnal calf cramps
  originate here. Referred pain to posterior calf, popliteal
  fossa, and plantar foot.

Ashi correspondence: **Strong.** Mid-calf tenderness during calf
  cramps is the textbook Ashi example — patients instinctively
  grasp the mid-calf during a cramp, directly at BL56's location.

Marma point: **Possible.** Indrabasti Marma (Sushruta) is
  described at the center of the foreleg (jangha = calf), which
  corresponds closely to BL56's mid-gastrocnemius position. 🟡

Head zone: S1–S2 → lower limb sensorimotor. **✅ MATCH** for
  local calf/leg indications. S1–S2 motor innervation to
  gastrocnemius.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL through gastrocnemius belly — maximum muscle
  bulk on the Superficial Back Line.

Neurogenic spot: S1–S2 mid-calf sensitization during leg
  pathology, 🟡.

Cupping point: posterior calf cupping occasionally used for calf
  cramps, 🟡.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡 (Indrabasti), neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: calf cramp, lumbar stiffness
- Dacheng: hemorrhoids, epistaxis, calf numbness/pain

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Calf cramp (primary), hemorrhoids, lumbar stiffness

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL56-specific evidence.
Calf cramp indication has strong anatomical plausibility (direct
gastrocnemius belly access) but no controlled studies at BL56
specifically.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: intramuscular sural arteries. Minimal risk at
  standard depth.

Neural risk: tibial nerve deep. Standard depth (0.5–1.0 cun)
  stays within the gastrocnemius, well above the tibial nerve.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL57 — Thừa Sơn (承山, Chéng Shān)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Primary Hemorrhoid Point**

Name 承山 (Chéngshān) = "Mountain Support" (承 = support, 山 =
mountain). The two bellies of the gastrocnemius form a
"mountain"-shaped prominence on the posterior calf; BL57 is at
the base of this mountain, where the two bellies converge into
the inverted V-junction and transition to the Achilles tendon /
soleus complex. The 承 (support) character appears in all three
posterior leg BL points: BL36 承扶 (gluteal fold), BL56 承筋
(mid-calf), BL57 承山 (V-junction) — the longest
single-character naming chain in the BL channel.

BL57 is the MOST prescribed traditional acupoint for hemorrhoids
(痔疮) — more commonly used than BL30, GV20, GV1, or any other
hemorrhoid-indicated point. This is notable because the
posterior calf (S1–S2) is segmentally distant from the anorectal
region (S2–S4 pudendal/parasympathetic). The hemorrhoid
indication requires a non-standard mechanism explanation.

BL57 is also a major point for calf cramp/spasm, where the
mechanism is straightforward: direct muscle-junction access.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the posterior aspect of
the leg, on the line connecting BL40 and the calcaneal tendon
(Achilles), in the depression formed below the gastrocnemius
muscle bellies when the leg is stretched or the heel is lifted.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient prone and the foot
plantar-flexed, the inverted V-shaped depression at the lower
border of the gastrocnemius bellies is palpable. BL57 is at the
apex of this V. This is the gastrocnemius-soleus junction — the
transition from muscle belly to tendinous tissue.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.5 cun perpendicular. Needle enters the
gastrocnemius-soleus junction.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Tibial nerve** (L4–S3) — deep to the soleus at this level.
  The tibial nerve passes from the gastrocnemius-soleus
  intermuscular plane into the deep posterior compartment.
- **Sural nerve** — formed or forming at this level from the
  union of medial sural cutaneous (tibial) and lateral sural
  cutaneous (common peroneal). Runs superficial/lateral.
- Motor branches to gastrocnemius and soleus (from tibial)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: crural fascia → gastrocnemius aponeurosis
converging to Achilles tendon. The gastrocnemius-soleus junction
is a biomechanically critical transition: from contractile muscle
tissue to non-contractile tendon. Strain injuries commonly occur
at this junction.
→ Cross-ref: A03, A08

Vascular: posterior tibial artery (deep, between soleus and
tibialis posterior). Peroneal artery (deep, lateral). Small
saphenous vein (superficial, running posterior to lateral
malleolus).

Muscle layers: skin → crural fascia → gastrocnemius lower border
(tendinous) → soleus (muscular, continuing distally) → deep
posterior compartment (tibialis posterior, FDL, FHL).

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
BL57 has no special category designation — its importance derives
entirely from its clinical utility, particularly for hemorrhoids.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL57 (Chengshan 承山). Traditional functions:
  relax sinews, treat hemorrhoids, benefit the lumbar spine.
  Indications: hemorrhoids (primary), calf cramp/spasm, posterior
  leg pain, lumbar stiffness, prolapsed rectum, constipation.

Trigger point: **Strong correspondence.** Gastrocnemius-soleus
  junction TrPs (Travell & Simons). The musculotendinous junction
  is a common TrP site. Referred pain to posterior calf, Achilles
  tendon area, and plantar surface.

Ashi correspondence: **Strong.** The gastrocnemius V-junction
  becomes tender during calf strain, cramps, and Achilles
  tendinopathy. Palpation at BL57 during acute calf cramp
  reproduces the patient's pain.

Marma point: **Possible.** Indrabasti Marma territory extends
  from mid-calf toward the junction area. 🟡

Head zone: S1–S2 → lower limb sensorimotor. **✅ MATCH** for
  local calf indications. For hemorrhoids: S2 partially overlaps
  with the upper range of pudendal nerve territory, but S3–S4
  (primary hemorrhoidal innervation) are not directly accessed.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL — the gastrocnemius-soleus junction is a
  critical biomechanical node on the Superficial Back Line. The
  SBL transmits force from the plantar fascia → Achilles →
  gastrocnemius → hamstrings → sacrotuberous ligament. BL57
  marks the muscle-to-tendon transition within this chain.

Neurogenic spot: S1–S2 posterior calf sensitization in muscle
  pathology, 🟡.

Cupping point: posterior calf cupping used for muscle spasm.
  BL57 is an acceptable cupping site. 🟡

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Marma 🟡, neurogenic 🟡, cupping 🟡.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: hemorrhoids (痔疮), calf cramp (转筋), lumbar
  stiffness
- Dacheng: hemorrhoids (primary indication), prolapsed rectum
  (脱肛), constipation, posterior leg pain
- Bianque: hemorrhoids with bleeding, calf spasm in pregnancy

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Hemorrhoids (most-prescribed traditional point), calf cramp/
  spasm, posterior leg pain, lumbar stiffness, prolapsed rectum,
  constipation

**Hemorrhoids** — 🔴 Unvalidated for BL57 specifically. Chinese
clinical studies have examined acupuncture for hemorrhoids
(including BL57 in protocols), but point-specific evidence
isolating BL57 is absent. However, BL57 is the single most
frequently named point for hemorrhoids across classical sources.

**Calf cramp** — 🔴 Unvalidated. Anatomically plausible (direct
gastrocnemius-soleus junction access), but no controlled trials
at BL57.

**Prolapsed rectum** — 🔴 Unvalidated. Combined with GV20 (Baihui)
in classical protocols for rectal prolapse ("lifting" prescription).

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No point-specific clinical trials identified for BL57 in
isolation. Hemorrhoid studies in the acupuncture literature
typically use multi-point protocols (BL57 + BL30 + GV20 + GV1,
or BL57 + BL25 + SP6), making BL57-specific contribution
impossible to isolate. Some Chinese-language clinical reports
claim efficacy for hemorrhoid protocols including BL57 (Fu 2006,
Wang 2010), but these are low-quality (no control group,
inadequate blinding). The gastrocnemius cramp indication has no
controlled trials despite high anatomical plausibility.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal dynamic acupoint studies at BL57. However, clinical
observation suggests increased tenderness at BL57 in patients
with active hemorrhoids — consistent with a possible visceral
sensitization response (anorectal pathology → S2 segmental
overlap → posterior calf referred tenderness). This would parallel
the back-shu sensitization documented for thoracic points. Not
experimentally confirmed.

Calf cramp patients show obvious tenderness at BL57 during acute
episodes — this is Ashi-type sensitization (local muscle
pathology) rather than the distant visceral sensitization relevant
to the Dynamic acupoint concept.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Calf cramp mechanism (HIGH plausibility):** Direct mechanical.
Needling at the gastrocnemius-soleus junction stimulates the
muscle spindles and Golgi tendon organs at the musculotendinous
transition, inducing reflex muscle relaxation via Ib afferent →
spinal cord → alpha motor neuron inhibition. S1–S2 motor neuron
modulation. Equivalent to trigger point dry needling at the
same anatomical site.

**Hemorrhoid mechanism (MODERATE plausibility — three
hypotheses):** See Observation 31. The hemorrhoid indication is
segmentally imprecise (BL57 at S1–S2 vs hemorrhoidal plexus at
S2–S4), requiring a non-standard mechanism:

1. **Calf muscle pump hypothesis (MOST PLAUSIBLE):** The
   gastrocnemius-soleus complex acts as a venous pump —
   contraction drives blood from the deep veins of the calf
   toward the heart. Stimulating the muscle-pump junction (BL57)
   may improve venous return → reduce venous congestion in the
   pelvic/hemorrhoidal plexus → symptom relief. This is a
   HEMODYNAMIC mechanism, not a neural-segmental one.

2. **S2 segmental overlap:** BL57's S1–S2 innervation partially
   overlaps with the upper range of the pelvic floor innervation
   (S2). Pudendal nerve includes S2 fibers. Incomplete but
   adjacent overlap.

3. **Supraspinal modulation:** S1–S2 afferent input → ascending
   spinal pathways → PAG/NTS → descending modulation of pelvic
   autonomic output affecting anorectal vascular tone.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.5–1.5 cun perpendicular into the
gastrocnemius-soleus junction.

Moxa: commonly applied for hemorrhoids (indirect moxa). Combined
with GV20 moxa in the classical "lifting" protocol for rectal
prolapse.

Cupping: occasionally used at BL57 for calf cramp/pain.

Bloodletting: rarely. BL40 is the primary posterior leg
bloodletting point.

Electro-acupuncture: applicable for calf cramp. 2–4 Hz stimulation
for muscle relaxation.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: posterior tibial artery and peroneal artery are
  deep to the soleus — unlikely to be reached at standard depth.
  Small saphenous vein superficial (minor risk).

Neural risk: tibial nerve deep to soleus. Standard needling
  depth stays within the gastrocnemius-soleus junction, above
  the nerve.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication at BL57 itself, but some
  classical sources mention calf cramp treatment with BL57 during
  pregnancy as safe.

---

### BL58 — Phi Dương (飞扬, Fēi Yáng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Luo-Connecting of BL
(12th/12th = LAST Regular Luo)**

Name 飞扬 (Fēiyáng) = "Taking Flight" (飞 = fly, 扬 = soar/
raise). The Luo vessel "takes flight" from the BL channel to
connect with the paired KI channel — a poetic description of the
transverse vessel linking Exterior (BL) to Interior (KI). BL58
is the 12th and FINAL regular Luo-Connecting point in the Atlas,
completing the inventory of all 12 Interior-Exterior paired Luo
channels. With BL58 documented, every regular channel's Luo
point is recorded.

Luo-Connecting points serve a specific architectural function:
they send a branch (络脉, luòmài) from their own channel to the
paired Interior-Exterior channel, creating a direct link between
the paired pair. BL58 connects BL → KI. The paired Luo is KI4
(Dazhong 大钟, documented in AP-08), which connects KI → BL.
Together, BL58 and KI4 form the bidirectional BL↔KI Luo bridge.

**Host-Guest (主客, zhǔ-kè) pairing:**
- Host treatment (treating the paired channel): KI3 (Yuan-Source
  of KI) + BL58 (Luo of BL) — strengthens KI via BL's Luo
- Guest treatment (treating BL itself): BL64 (Yuan-Source of BL)
  + KI4 (Luo of KI) — strengthens BL via KI's Luo
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI4

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 7 cun proximal to the
prominence of the lateral malleolus, on the posterior border of
the fibula, on the lateral margin of the gastrocnemius muscle,
1 cun inferior and lateral to BL57.

Anatomical landmarks: on the lateral aspect of the posterior
calf, at the transition from the gastrocnemius (medial) to the
peroneal territory (lateral). The point is at the lateral border
of the gastrocnemius, where it overlies the soleus and
approaches the peroneal muscles.

Needle depth range: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular or oblique.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Sural nerve** — formed or forming at this level from the
  union of medial sural cutaneous nerve (from tibial = BL
  channel's main nerve) and communicating branch of lateral
  sural cutaneous nerve (from common peroneal = GB channel's
  nerve). The sural nerve at BL58 represents the CONVERGENCE
  of contributions from both terminal branches of the sciatic.
- Lateral sural cutaneous nerve (from common peroneal)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: crural fascia → lateral gastrocnemius border →
peroneal compartment transition.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: sural arteries (lateral distribution). Small saphenous
vein.

Muscle layers: skin → crural fascia → lateral gastrocnemius
border → soleus (deep) → peroneus longus (lateral). BL58 sits
at the interface between the superficial posterior compartment
(gastrocnemius/soleus) and the lateral compartment (peroneals).

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Xi-Cleft: No. Back-Shu: No.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Luo-Connecting: YES — 12th/12th regular Luo (LAST).** BL→KI.
Paired with KI4 (Dazhong, KI→BL). Host-Guest: KI3+BL58 vs
BL64+KI4.

**Luo-Connecting Inventory COMPLETE (12/12):**

| # | Luo | Channel | Hán-Việt | Links to | Location | AP |
|---|-----|---------|----------|----------|----------|-----|
| 1 | LU7 | Lung | Liệt Khuyết | → LI | 1.5 cun above wrist | AP-01 |
| 2 | LI6 | L. Intestine | Thiên Lịch | → LU | 3 cun above wrist | AP-02 |
| 3 | ST40 | Stomach | Phong Long | → SP | Mid-leg lateral | AP-03 |
| 4 | SP4 | Spleen | Công Tôn | → ST | 1st metatarsal | AP-04 |
| 5 | HT5 | Heart | Thông Lý | → SI | 1 cun above wrist | AP-05 |
| 6 | SI7 | S. Intestine | Chi Chánh | → HT | 5 cun above wrist | AP-06 |
| 7 | **BL58** | **Bladder** | **Phi Dương** | **→ KI** | **7 cun above ankle** | **AP-07** |
| 8 | KI4 | Kidney | Đại Chung | → BL | Below med. malleolus | AP-08 |
| 9 | PC6 | Pericardium | Nội Quan | → TE | 2 cun above wrist | AP-09 |
| 10 | TE5 | Triple Burner | Ngoại Quan | → PC | 2 cun above wrist | AP-10 |
| 11 | GB37 | Gallbladder | Quang Minh | → LR | 5 cun above ankle | AP-11 |
| 12 | LR5 | Liver | Lệ Câu | → GB | 5 cun above ankle | AP-12 |

**Inventory patterns:**
- ALL 12 Luo points are on LIMBS (6 forearm, 6 lower leg) — none
  on trunk or head
- Paired symmetry: in all 6 Interior-Exterior pairs, each
  channel contributes one Luo
- Position cluster: Luo points concentrate in the distal half of
  each limb segment (between wrist/ankle and elbow/knee)
- Two Luo points (PC6 and TE5) are ALSO Eight Confluent —
  dual-category. No other Luo holds a second major designation
- BL58 is the most PROXIMAL lower-leg Luo (7 cun above ankle vs
  SP4 at 1st metatarsal, KI4 below malleolus, GB37 and LR5 at
  5 cun above ankle)

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL58 (Feiyang 飞扬). Traditional functions:
  disperse Wind, benefit the head, strengthen the lumbar spine.
  Indications: headache, nasal congestion, dizziness, lumbar/leg
  pain, hemorrhoids, weakness of the legs.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Lateral gastrocnemius / soleus
  TrPs. Referred pain to posterior and lateral calf, ankle.

Ashi correspondence: lateral calf tenderness in ankle sprains
  (sural nerve territory sensitization) and chronic leg pain.

Marma point: ✗ No precise Marma at the lateral mid-calf.

Head zone: S1–S2 → lower limb. **✅ MATCH** for local leg/lumbar
  indications. Distant indications (headache, nasal congestion)
  require supraspinal pathways — expected for Luo points, which
  classically treat symptoms of the paired channel (KI governs
  head/sensory orifices via Kidney-Water ascending).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL lateral extension. Transition zone between
  SBL (gastrocnemius) and Lateral Line (peroneal compartment).

Neurogenic spot: S1–S2 lateral calf — limited data, 🟡.

Cupping point: lateral calf cupping uncommon. ✗

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Head zone ✅ for local but requires supraspinal for
distant. Counting as partial → 4/8. Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡,
cupping ✗.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache, dizziness, nasal congestion, lumbar pain
- Dacheng: hemorrhoids, weakness of the legs, cold sensation in
  the lower limbs

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache, dizziness, nasal congestion/epistaxis, lumbar/leg
  pain, hemorrhoids, leg weakness

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL58-specific evidence.
Distant indications (headache, nasal congestion) are attributed
to BL58's Luo function — connecting to KI, which governs the
head and sensory orifices. No controlled evidence for the
Luo-specific mechanism.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL58-specific clinical trials identified. The Luo-Connecting
mechanism (transverse vessel linking BL↔KI) has no anatomical
substrate identified in modern research. The sural nerve at BL58
does not selectively connect to any KI channel territory. However,
the sural nerve's dual-origin (from both tibial and common
peroneal branches of the sciatic) is an anatomical convergence
point that, while not BL→KI specific, represents a genuine neural
convergence at BL58's level.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal Dynamic acupoint studies at BL58. The Luo-Connecting
concept predicts that BL58 should become sensitized when the
paired channel (KI) is pathological — e.g., Kidney deficiency
patients showing tenderness at BL58. This prediction is testable
but has not been systematically investigated.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Luo-Connecting mechanism (LOW clarity):** The classical Luo
vessel (络脉) connecting BL58 to KI has no identified anatomical
counterpart. No nerve, vessel, or fascial structure selectively
connects BL58's territory (lateral calf, S1–S2) to KI channel
territory (medial ankle/leg, L4–S2). The Luo-Yuan (Host-Guest)
pairing system is a therapeutic protocol, not an anatomical
description.

**Local musculoskeletal mechanism (HIGH clarity):** Sural nerve
stimulation at BL58 → S1–S2 spinal segment → motor and sensory
modulation of the posterior/lateral calf. Straightforward for
leg pain indications.

**Distant (headache, nasal) mechanism (LOW clarity):** Requires
supraspinal ascending pathways (S1–S2 afferent → brainstem →
descending modulation to trigeminal or upper cervical segments).
Mechanism similar to other distal points (LI4, ST36) but
less studied.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.5–1.0 cun perpendicular or oblique medially.

Moxa: applicable. Used in deficiency patterns (KI deficiency
via Luo treatment protocol).

Cupping: not typically applied at BL58.

Luo-Yuan protocol: BL58 paired with KI3 (Yuan-Source of KI)
for Host treatment of KI deficiency patterns. Conversely, BL64
(Yuan-Source of BL) paired with KI4 (Luo of KI) for Guest
treatment of BL channel patterns.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: small saphenous vein superficial. Minimal risk.

Neural risk: sural nerve superficial at this level — direct
  stimulation possible but not dangerous.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL59 — Phụ Dương (跗阳, Fūyáng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Xi-Cleft of Yang Qiao Mai
(4th/4th = LAST Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Cleft)**

Name 跗阳 (Fūyáng) = "Instep Yang" (跗 = instep/tarsal region,
阳 = Yang). The 跗 character references the dorsal/tarsal area
of the foot, and 阳 marks this as a Yang-side point (lateral).
BL59 is positioned 3 cun above the lateral malleolus — the
Yang (lateral) aspect of the lower leg at the tarsal/ankle
transition zone.

BL59 is the Xi-Cleft (郄穴) of Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉, Yang
Heel/Motility Vessel). Xi-Cleft points are where Qi and Blood
accumulate deeply along a channel — used for ACUTE conditions
(B01 §3.1). For extraordinary vessels, the Xi-Cleft treats acute
pathology of that vessel's domain. Yang Qiao Mai governs:
- **Yang-side muscle tone** (lateral body musculature)
- **Eye opening** (Yang Qiao reaches BL1 at the inner canthus)
- **Wakefulness** (Yang Qiao = daytime vigor; counterpart Yin
  Qiao = sleep)
- **Gait and stepping** (跷 = stepping/walking — the vessel
  governs dynamic leg movement)

BL59 is the 4th and FINAL extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft in the
Atlas. With BL59 documented, all 4 standardized extraordinary
vessel Xi-Clefts are complete.

**Relationship to BL62 (Eight Confluent of Yang Qiao Mai):**
BL62 = the OPENING point for Yang Qiao Mai (regulates the vessel
generally). BL59 = the ACUTE ACCESS point (treats urgent vessel
conditions). Complementary: BL62 for chronic regulation, BL59
for acute pathology. Same pattern as KI6 (Opening) / KI8
(Xi-Cleft) for Yin Qiao Mai.
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI8

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun directly above the
prominence of the lateral malleolus, on the posterior border of
the fibula, between the peroneus brevis and the Achilles tendon.

Anatomical landmarks: posterior to the fibula, in the groove
between the peroneal tendons (anterolateral) and the Achilles
tendon (posteromedial). This is the distal posterior leg where
the musculature thins and tendinous structures dominate.

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun perpendicular. Fibula provides
a bony backstop laterally.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Sural nerve** — fully formed at this level. Runs posterior
  to the lateral malleolus, providing cutaneous innervation to
  the lateral ankle and lateral foot (5th toe territory).
- **Lateral sural cutaneous nerve** (from common peroneal)
- Spinal segment: S1 (primarily)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: crural fascia thickened. Retinacula beginning to
form at this level (peroneal retinacula, flexor retinacula
below). Transition from muscular to tendinous tissue.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: peroneal artery (deep, medial to fibula). Small
saphenous vein (posterior to lateral malleolus). Lateral
calcaneal arteries emerging below.

Muscle layers: skin → crural fascia → peroneus brevis tendon
(anterolateral) → posterior tibial compartment tendons → fibula
(deep lateral). Minimal muscular bulk — primarily tendinous.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Back-Shu: No.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
**Xi-Cleft: YES — Xi-Cleft of Yang Qiao Mai (4th/4th = LAST
extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft).**

**Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Cleft Inventory COMPLETE (4/4):**

| Extraordinary vessel | Xi-Cleft | Channel | Hán-Việt | Location | AP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yin Qiao Mai | KI8 | Kidney | Giao Tín | 2 cun above med. malleolus | AP-08 |
| Yin Wei Mai | KI9 | Kidney | Trúc Tân | 5 cun above med. malleolus | AP-08 |
| Yang Wei Mai | GB35 | Gallbladder | Dương Giao | 7 cun above lat. malleolus | AP-11 |
| **Yang Qiao Mai** | **BL59** | **Bladder** | **Phụ Dương** | **3 cun above lat. malleolus** | **AP-07** |

**Inventory patterns:**
- ALL 4 Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Clefts are on the LOWER LEG between knee and ankle
  — no Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Cleft on the arm or trunk
- Yin-Yang distribution: Yin vessels' Xi-Clefts on Yin channels
  (KI8, KI9 on KI), Yang vessels' Xi-Clefts on Yang channels
  (GB35 on GB, BL59 on BL). Perfect Yin-Yang channel matching.
- KI hosts TWO Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Clefts (KI8 + KI9) — the only channel
  hosting more than one
- Qiao-Wei pairing: Yin Qiao (KI8) + Yang Qiao (BL59) = the
  Qiao Mai pair. Yin Wei (KI9) + Yang Wei (GB35) = the Wei Mai
  pair. Each paired vessel has its Xi-Cleft on a Yin and Yang
  channel respectively.
- The 4 remaining extraordinary vessels (Du Mai, Ren Mai, Chong
  Mai, Dai Mai) do NOT have standardized Xi-Cleft points

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL59 (Fuyang 跗阳). Traditional functions: benefit
  the sinews, treat Yang Qiao Mai pathology. Indications:
  headache, lumbar/leg pain, facial paralysis, lateral ankle
  pain/weakness, heavy sensation in the head.

Trigger point: **Correspondence.** Peroneal/sural territory TrPs
  in the distal posterior leg. Referred pain to lateral ankle and
  foot.

Ashi correspondence: lateral lower-leg tenderness in ankle
  sprains (lateral ligament injuries sensitize the sural nerve
  territory), chronic ankle instability.

Marma point: ✗ No precise Marma at the distal posterior fibular
  area. Gulpha Marma is at the ankle joint itself, not 3 cun
  above.

Head zone: S1 → lateral foot/ankle. **✅ MATCH** for local
  ankle/leg indications. Distant indications (headache, facial
  paralysis) are attributed to Yang Qiao Mai pathway — the
  vessel ascends to the head and eye, reaching BL1.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL lateral extension / Lateral Line proximity.
  Peroneal tendons connect to the Lateral Line (Myers).

Neurogenic spot: sural nerve territory sensitization in ankle
  pathology, 🟡.

Cupping point: ✗ Not a cupping site (too distal, tendinous).

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Head zone ✅ for local only. Marma ✗, neurogenic 🟡,
cupping ✗.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache, lumbar pain, lateral ankle weakness
- Dacheng: facial paralysis, heavy head sensation, leg pain,
  Yang Qiao Mai pathology

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache, lumbar/leg pain, facial paralysis, lateral ankle
  pain, external eye disorders (Yang Qiao → BL1)

**All indications — 🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL59-specific evidence.
Facial paralysis and external eye disorder indications derive
from Yang Qiao Mai pathway theory — the vessel ascends from the
lateral ankle to BL1 at the inner canthus, passing through the
face. No controlled evidence for this pathway mechanism at BL59.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL59-specific clinical trials identified. Yang Qiao Mai as a
clinical entity has minimal modern research presence. The vessel's
traditional domain (eye opening, wakefulness, lateral body tone)
has not been investigated through BL59 stimulation. Yang Qiao
Mai's opening point BL62 has received more attention (insomnia
treatment, Ghost Point protocols).

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal Dynamic acupoint studies at BL59. As a Xi-Cleft of Yang
Qiao Mai, BL59 should theoretically sensitize during acute Yang
Qiao pathology — acute lateral ankle sprains, sudden-onset facial
paralysis (Bell's palsy), or acute insomnia. The prediction is
clinically reasonable (lateral ankle Xi-Cleft sensitizing during
lateral ankle trauma) but not formally studied.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local ankle/leg mechanism (HIGH clarity):** Sural nerve
stimulation at S1 level → local sensorimotor modulation of the
lateral ankle and foot. Straightforward for ankle pain, lateral
leg weakness.

**Yang Qiao Mai mechanism (LOW clarity):** The Yang Qiao Mai
pathway (ascending from lateral ankle to BL1 at the inner
canthus) has no identified continuous anatomical substrate. The
vessel theory requires BL59 stimulation at S1 to influence CN VII
(facial paralysis) and V1 (eye function) — a span of the entire
neuroaxis. No specific ascending pathway connects the sural nerve
at BL59 to facial nerve function. The most plausible route would
be supraspinal: S1 afferent → ascending dorsal column → brainstem
→ facial nucleus modulation, but this is non-specific and would
apply to any S1 stimulation point.

**Xi-Cleft acute function (MODERATE clarity):** Xi-Cleft points
treat acute conditions of their vessel. For Yang Qiao Mai, acute
pathology includes: sudden lateral ankle weakness/sprain (local),
acute onset Bell's palsy (distant), acute insomnia (distant).
Only the local indication has a plausible mechanism at BL59.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.5–0.8 cun perpendicular. Fibula provides
lateral bony limit.

Moxa: applicable. Indirect moxa for chronic Yang Qiao Mai
deficiency patterns.

Electro-acupuncture: BL59-BL62 pair for Yang Qiao Mai
stimulation (both are on the same vessel).

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: peroneal artery deep, medial to fibula. Small
  saphenous vein superficial. Low risk at standard depth.

Neural risk: sural nerve superficial — stimulation expected
  (tingling to lateral foot). Not dangerous.

Fibula: bony backstop limits depth laterally.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### Batch 11 Observations

**Observation 30 — TIBIAL-TO-SURAL NERVE TRANSITION IN
POSTERIOR CALF.**

The posterior calf trajectory (BL55–BL59) tracks a neural
transition unique in the BL channel:

| Point | Location | Superficial nerve | Deep nerve |
|---|---|---|---|
| BL55 | 2 cun below BL40 | Med. sural cutaneous | Tibial |
| BL56 | Mid-gastrocnemius | Med. sural cutaneous | Tibial |
| BL57 | Gastrocnemius V | Sural (forming) | Tibial |
| BL58 | Lateral gastroc. | Sural (formed) | Tibial (receding) |
| BL59 | 3 cun above ankle | Sural (established) | Peroneal territory |

The sural nerve is formed from the union of medial sural
cutaneous (from tibial nerve) and communicating branch of lateral
sural cutaneous (from common peroneal nerve). It is the ONLY
lower limb nerve formed by contributions from BOTH terminal
branches of the sciatic nerve. The formation zone (BL57–BL58
level) coincides with BL58 (the Luo-Connecting point linking BL
to KI). While this neural convergence has no BL→KI specificity,
the coincidence of a neural convergence point with a channel
convergence point is architecturally noteworthy.

**Observation 31 — BL57 HEMORRHOID PARADOX: VENOUS PUMP
HYPOTHESIS.**

BL57 (Chengshan) is the most-prescribed traditional point for
hemorrhoids, but its mechanism doesn't fit the segmental model.
The hemorrhoidal plexus is innervated by S2–S4 (pudendal /
inferior rectal nerve), while BL57 at the gastrocnemius V-junction
is innervated by S1–S2. Segmental overlap is partial at best (S2
shared, S3–S4 not accessed by BL57).

The most plausible alternative is hemodynamic rather than neural:
the gastrocnemius-soleus complex functions as a "peripheral heart"
— rhythmic contraction drives venous blood from the deep leg veins
toward the heart. Poor calf muscle function (sedentary lifestyle,
prolonged standing, pregnancy) contributes to venous
insufficiency, which exacerbates hemorrhoids via increased pelvic
venous pressure. BL57, at the gastrocnemius-soleus junction (the
critical transition point of the muscle pump), may work by
improving calf muscle tone → enhanced venous return → reduced
pelvic congestion.

This would represent a HEMODYNAMIC mechanism distinct from the
neural-segmental reflex model that explains most Back-Shu and
distal point actions. If confirmed, BL57 would join BL40
(bloodletting/phlebotomy) as a point where the mechanism is
vascular rather than neural — an important category for BSA
mechanism classification.

**Observation 32 — DUAL INVENTORY COMPLETION: LUO (12/12) +
EXTRAORDINARY VESSEL XI-CLEFT (4/4).**

Batch 11 completes two major category inventories simultaneously:

BL58 = 12th/12th regular Luo-Connecting. Inventory features:
all 12 on limbs (none trunk/head), paired symmetry (each
Interior-Exterior pair contributes one per channel), distal
limb clustering. PC6 and TE5 are dual Luo + Eight Confluent.

BL59 = 4th/4th extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft. Inventory features:
all 4 on lower leg (none on arm), perfect Yin-Yang channel
matching (Yin vessel Xi-Cleft on Yin channel, Yang on Yang),
KI hosts 2/4 (only multi-hosting channel), Qiao-Wei paired
vessel distribution.

BL channel's inventory completion tally now stands at:
- ✅ 12/12 Back-Shu (completed at BL28, Batch 6)
- ✅ 6/6 Lower He-Sea (completed at BL40, Batch 8)
- ✅ 4/4 Four Command Points (completed at BL40, Batch 8)
- ✅ 12/12 Luo-Connecting (completed at BL58, this batch)
- ✅ 4/4 Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Cleft (completed at BL59, this batch)
- ⬜ 12/12 Yuan-Source (BL64, Batch 12)
- ⬜ 12/12 regular Xi-Cleft (BL63, Batch 12)
- ⬜ 8/8 Eight Confluent (BL62, Batch 12)
- ⬜ 12/12 Five Shu sets (BL, Batch 12)

Five of 9 tracked inventories are now complete. Batch 12 will
complete the remaining four — making BL the single most
inventory-completing meridian in the Atlas.
