## Lateral Ankle / Dorsal Foot (BL60–BL66)

The final segment of the Bladder channel traverses the lateral
ankle and dorsal/lateral 5th metatarsal, terminating at the
lateral nail of the 5th toe (BL67, Phase 1). Seven points remain:
BL60–BL66. The territory transitions from sural nerve (S1–S2) at
the ankle to lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve and 4th common digital
nerve at the 5th toe.

The lateral malleolus area contains the densest cluster of BL
points outside the back: BL60, BL61, BL62, and BL63 are all
within ~3 cm of the lateral malleolus tip. This concentration
reflects anatomical convergence: peroneal tendons, Achilles
tendon, sural nerve and its terminal branches, and the ankle
joint complex (tibiotalar, subtalar, calcaneofibular ligament)
all converge in this narrow zone.

This batch is the most inventory-completing in the entire Atlas,
finalizing FOUR major category inventories simultaneously:
- Eight Confluent: 8/8 (BL62 = LAST)
- Regular Xi-Cleft: 12/12 (BL63 = LAST)
- Yuan-Source: 12/12 (BL64 = LAST)
- Five Shu sets: 12/12 (BL60/BL65/BL66 complete the BL set =
  12th/12th channel)

Additionally, BL60 carries a classical pregnancy contraindication
(joining LI4, SP6, GB21, BL67), and BL62 is Ghost Point #5 in
Sun Si-miao's 13 Ghost Points.

---

### BL60 — Côn Lôn (昆仑, Kūn Lún)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Five Shu: River/Fire, Ke
Subject. Pregnancy CONTRAINDICATED.**

Name 昆仑 (Kūnlún) = "Kunlun Mountains" — one of the longest
mountain ranges in Asia, mythologically the dwelling of the
Queen Mother of the West (西王母) and the axis mundi of Chinese
cosmology. The lateral malleolus prominence resembles a mountain
peak, and BL60 sits in the depression behind this "mountain."
The Kunlun reference conveys the point's importance: the ankle
as a structural pillar supporting the body, echoing the
mountain's cosmological role as a world-pillar.

BL60 is the River (經, Jīng) point of the BL channel, carrying
the Fire element. On a Water channel, Fire = Ke Subject (被克,
bèi kè): Water controls Fire in the Five Element cycle, making
BL60 the point where the channel's controlling relationship is
expressed. Nan Jing Ch.68: River points treat "cough with
alternating chills and fever" (喘咳寒熱) — reflecting the deeper
longitudinal flow stage of channel Qi.

BL60 is classically listed among the FORBIDDEN POINTS during
pregnancy (妊娠禁穴) alongside LI4, SP6, GB21, and BL67. It is
simultaneously indicated for "difficult labor" (難產) — a paradox
shared with the other pregnancy-forbidden points:
contraindicated during pregnancy to prevent premature labor,
but used therapeutically during active labor to promote delivery.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the depression between the
tip of the lateral malleolus and the Achilles tendon.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient's ankle in neutral
position, the point is at the midpoint of the line connecting
the highest point of the lateral malleolus and the posterior
border of the Achilles tendon. The depression is palpable when
the foot is slightly dorsiflexed.

Spatial relationships:
- BL59 (Fuyang) is 3 cun proximal — on the posterior border of
  the fibula
- BL61 (Pucan) is inferior — below the lateral malleolus on the
  lateral calcaneus
- KI3 (Taixi) is on the OPPOSITE side of the ankle — between
  medial malleolus and Achilles tendon. BL60 and KI3 are the
  BL-KI paired mirror points at the ankle, each occupying the
  same topographic position (malleolus-Achilles depression) on
  opposite sides
- GB40 (Qiuxu) is anterior-inferior — anterior to the lateral
  malleolus

Needle depth range: 0.5–0.8 cun perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Sural nerve** (S1–S2) — the primary superficial nerve at this
  level. Courses posterior to the lateral malleolus, closely
  associated with the small saphenous vein. Direct sural nerve
  stimulation is highly likely at BL60.
- **Lateral calcaneal nerve** — branch of the sural nerve,
  sensory innervation to the lateral heel
- Deep: tibial nerve passes medially behind the medial malleolus
  (not directly accessed from the lateral side)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: crural fascia → paratenon of Achilles tendon →
retromalleolar fat pad. The space between the lateral malleolus
and Achilles tendon contains loose areolar tissue, the small
saphenous vein, sural nerve, and peroneal tendon sheaths.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: **small saphenous vein** — courses posterior to the
lateral malleolus alongside the sural nerve. Superficially
accessible vein. Peroneal artery branches (deep, behind the
fibula). Lateral malleolar artery (branch of anterior tibial).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue (thin) → peroneal
tendon sheaths (peroneus longus and brevis, coursing behind the
lateral malleolus in the retromalleolar groove) → ankle joint
capsule / posterior talofibular ligament. No significant muscle
bulk — BL60 is in a tendinous/ligamentous environment.

#### Phân loại huyệt

**Five Shu: River (經, Jīng) — Fire element.**

The River (經) point is the 4th of 5 Five Shu Transport points,
where channel Qi flows through deeper "rivers" before the He-Sea.
On the Yang Water (BL) channel:
- Five Element role: **Ke Subject (被克)** — Fire is controlled
  by Water (水克火). The channel's controlling relationship is
  expressed at this point.
- Treatment applications (Nan Jing Ch.68): River points for
  cough with alternating chills and fever, and conditions
  involving the voice and respiratory function.

BL Five Shu set (3/5 in this batch, 5/5 total):

| Five Shu | Element | Point | Role | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Well | Metal | BL67 | Tonification | Phase 1 ✓ |
| Spring | Water | BL66 | Horary | This batch |
| Stream | Wood | BL65 | Sedation | This batch |
| **River** | **Fire** | **BL60** | **Ke Subject** | **This entry** |
| He-Sea | Earth | BL40 | Ke Controller | Batch 8 ✓ |

Yuan-Source: No (BL64). Luo-Connecting: No (BL58). Xi-Cleft: No
(BL63). Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

**Pregnancy CONTRAINDICATED (妊娠禁穴).**

Pregnancy-contraindicated points documented in this Atlas:

| Point | Channel | Mechanism basis | AP |
|---|---|---|---|
| LI4 | L. Intestine | Qi-descending (supraspinal) | AP-02 |
| SP6 | Spleen | 3 Yin crossing, uterine S2-S4 | AP-04 |
| GB21 | Gallbladder | Shoulder/trapezius, bearing-down | AP-11 |
| **BL60** | **Bladder** | **S1-S2, labor facilitation** | **AP-07** |
| BL67 | Bladder | S1-S2, breech (moxa therapeutic) | AP-07 |

BL60 and BL67 share the same spinal segment (S1-S2) and both
have "difficult labor" indications. However, BL67 moxa for
breech presentation is paradoxically used DURING pregnancy
(after week 33) with the strongest non-pain acupuncture evidence
in the Atlas. BL60's contraindication is more absolute in
classical texts — needle stimulation at BL60 during pregnancy is
traditionally avoided regardless of trimester.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL60 (Kunlun 昆仑). River/Fire, Ke Subject.
  Expels Wind, clears Heat, relaxes sinews, strengthens lumbar.
  Headache (occipital), neck stiffness, lumbar pain, sciatica,
  ankle swelling/pain, heel pain, difficult labor. Combined with
  BL40 for lumbar pain (distal-proximal BL pair). Combined with
  BL67 for labor facilitation.

Trigger point: **Partial.** Peroneus longus/brevis tendons at the
  retromalleolar groove refer pain to lateral ankle and
  dorsolateral foot. However, the peroneal tendons at BL60 are
  more tendinous than muscular, limiting TrP formation. 🟡

Ashi correspondence: **Strong.** Lateral ankle tenderness in the
  Achilles-malleolus depression is a reliable clinical sign for:
  lateral ankle sprain (anterior talofibular / calcaneofibular
  ligament injury), peroneal tendon dysfunction, Achilles
  tendinopathy (lateral aspect), retromalleolar bursitis. The
  BL60 depression is routinely palpated in orthopedic ankle
  examination.

Marma point: **Gulpha (गुल्फ, "Ankle")** — the lateral aspect.
  Sandhi (joint) Marma encompassing the ankle joint complex.
  BL60 and KI3 (medial side) represent the BL-KI paired aspects
  of the same Gulpha Marma. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI3

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. **✅ MATCH** for local ankle/leg
  indications. Distant indications (occipital headache, neck
  stiffness) require supraspinal pathways — S1-S2 afferent →
  ascending to upper cervical/brainstem.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: **SBL — Achilles tendon transition.** BL60 sits
  where the SBL crosses from Achilles tendon (connective tissue)
  to calcaneus (bone). The SBL connects plantar fascia →
  calcaneus → Achilles → gastrocnemius upward. BL60 marks the
  lateral edge of this transition.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S4.2

Neurogenic spot: S1-S2 lateral ankle — limited data. 🟡

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (bony/tendinous terrain).

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, trigger point 🟡, Ashi,
Marma Gulpha 🟡, Anatomy Trains SBL). Head zone ✅ for local
only. Neurogenic 🟡, cupping ✗.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Lingshu Ch.2 (Ben Shu): BL60 as the River (經) point
- Nan Jing Ch.68: River points for "cough with alternating chills
  and fever" (喘咳寒熱)
- Jiayi Jing: headache (occipital), neck stiffness, lumbar pain,
  sciatica, difficult labor, heel pain, nosebleed
- Dacheng: pregnancy contraindication (妊婦禁針); "difficult
  labor — needle BL60 and BL67"

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache (occipital), neck stiffness, dizziness
- Lumbar pain, sciatica, ankle pain/swelling
- Difficult labor (during active labor only)
- Heel pain, Achilles area pain
- Nosebleed (epistaxis)

**Headache/neck stiffness** — 🔴 Unvalidated for BL60
specifically. Common Taiyang channel indication. Mechanism
requires supraspinal pathway (S1-S2 → upper cervical/brainstem).

**Lumbar pain / sciatica** — 🔴 Unvalidated. Anatomically
plausible through sural nerve → S1-S2 spinal cord → lumbar
enlargement convergence. Same mechanism as BL40 but at a more
distal access point.

**Ankle pain** — 🔴 Unvalidated. HIGH anatomical plausibility:
direct sural nerve stimulation at the ankle joint → local pain
modulation.

**Difficult labor** — 🔴 Unvalidated. No modern trials of BL60
alone for labor augmentation. Combined protocols (SP6 + LI4 +
BL60 + BL67) studied but BL60 not isolated.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL60-specific clinical trials identified. BL60 appears in
combined protocols for labor pain (with SP6, LI4, BL67) and
lumbar pain (with BL40, BL23), but point-specific evidence is
absent. The pregnancy contraindication is based entirely on
classical authority — no studies have examined whether BL60
needling actually increases uterine contractility or causes
adverse pregnancy outcomes.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal Dynamic acupoint studies at BL60. The Achilles-
malleolus depression becomes tender in acute ankle sprains,
Achilles tendinopathy, and chronic lateral ankle instability —
Ashi-type local sensitization. Some classical texts note BL60
tenderness in chronic lumbar pain — if confirmed, this would
suggest S1-S2 segmental sensitization from lumbar pathology.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local ankle mechanism (HIGH clarity):** Sural nerve stimulation
at the ankle joint → segmental (S1-S2) pain modulation. Direct
access to the retromalleolar space containing sural nerve,
peroneal tendon sheaths, and ankle joint capsule.

**Lumbar/sciatic mechanism (MODERATE clarity):** S1-S2 afferent
input converges at the lumbar enlargement (L4-S3 cord segments)
with afferents from the lower back and sciatic distribution. Same
mechanism proposed for BL40, from a more peripheral access point.

**Occipital headache mechanism (LOW clarity):** Requires
supraspinal ascending pathways: S1-S2 afferent → spinal cord →
brainstem → descending modulation to upper cervical segments
(C1-C3 greater/lesser occipital nerve territory).

**Pregnancy mechanism (SPECULATIVE):** S1-S2 includes sacral
parasympathetic outflow territory (S2-S4 pelvic splanchnic
nerves). Stimulation might theoretically modulate sacral autonomic
output to the uterus. However, the sural nerve at BL60 is purely
sensory — the pathway from sural afferent → motor autonomic
output to the uterus is indirect. No direct evidence.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.5–0.8 cun perpendicular into the Achilles-
malleolus depression.

Moxa: applicable. Used for Cold-Bi syndrome of the ankle, chronic
heel pain. Preferred over needling in elderly patients with
Achilles tendon thinning.

Cupping: not applicable (bony terrain).

**Pregnancy caution:** AVOID needling BL60 during pregnancy.
During active labor, BL60 may be used therapeutically —
typically combined with BL67, SP6, LI4 for labor promotion
protocols.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: **small saphenous vein** runs alongside the sural
  nerve posterior to the lateral malleolus. Apply pressure after
  withdrawal. Risk: minor local hematoma.

Neural risk: **sural nerve** is superficial — direct stimulation
  intended. Not dangerous but may cause sharp electric-type
  sensation along the lateral foot.

Pregnancy: **CONTRAINDICATED.** See Phân loại huyệt section.

---

### BL61 — Bộc Tham (仆参, Pú Cān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

Name 仆参 (Púcān) = "Servant's Respect" (仆 = servant, 参 = to
call upon/pay respects). The character 参 also carries a meaning
relating to the heel in some readings (参拜, to bow/prostrate —
touching the heel to the ground). The name evokes the servant's
posture of bowing or prostrating, emphasizing the heel — the
point's anatomical location.

BL61 is on the lateral calcaneus, directly below the lateral
malleolus. No special category designation. Primary indication
is heel pain.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): posterior and inferior to the
lateral malleolus, in the depression at the junction of the red
and white skin of the heel.

Anatomical landmarks: with the foot in neutral position, BL61 is
at the lateral calcaneal surface, at the junction of thick
plantar skin (red/weight-bearing) and thinner dorsal skin
(white/non-weight-bearing). Inferior and posterior to BL60.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral calcaneal nerve** — terminal branch of the sural
  nerve. Sensory innervation to the lateral heel.
- Calcaneal branches of the tibial nerve may extend laterally
  in some individuals
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thick plantar skin → subcutaneous heel fat pad →
calcaneal periosteum. The heel fat pad is a specialized
shock-absorbing structure with fibrous septa enclosing fat
lobules. Periosteal territory — no muscle.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: lateral calcaneal artery (branch of peroneal artery).
Minimal vascular risk.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No. Luo-Connecting: No. Xi-Cleft: No.
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.
No special category designation.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL61 (Pucan 仆参). Strengthens the heel, relaxes
  sinews, calms the spirit. Heel pain, foot weakness/heaviness,
  epilepsy, mania.

Trigger point: ✗ No correspondence. Lateral calcaneus has no
  muscle belly — periosteal territory (similar to KI5).

Ashi correspondence: lateral heel tenderness in calcaneal stress
  fractures, lateral plantar fasciitis, peroneal tendon insertion
  pain. 🟡

Marma point: peripheral Gulpha territory. ✗ No specific Marma.

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. ✅ MATCH for local heel
  indications. ✗ for epilepsy (requires supraspinal).

Anatomy Trains: SBL endpoint — the calcaneus is the bony anchor
  (Achilles → calcaneus → plantar fascia). BL61 on the lateral
  calcaneal surface is adjacent but lateral to the main SBL force
  line. 🟡

Neurogenic spot: ✗ No data.

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (heel).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡).
Head zone ✅ for local only.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: heel pain (足跟痛), foot weakness, epilepsy (癫痫)
- Dacheng: heel pain, mania, leg heaviness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Heel pain, ankle pain, foot weakness/heaviness
- Epilepsy, mania (distant indications)

All indications — **🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL61-specific evidence.
The epilepsy indication is shared by multiple BL foot points
(BL62, BL63, BL66, BL67) — a Taiyang channel pattern requiring
supraspinal mechanism.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: minimal. Lateral calcaneal artery small.

Neural risk: lateral calcaneal nerve — direct stimulation
  possible. Heel fat pad protects deeper structures.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL62 — Thân Mạch (申脉, Shēn Mài)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Eight Confluent of Yang
Qiao Mai (8th/8th = LAST), Ghost Point #5**

Name 申脉 (Shēnmài) = "Extending Vessel" (申 = extend/stretch,
脉 = vessel/channel). The name references Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉,
Yang Heel/Motility Vessel), the extraordinary vessel that BL62
opens. "Extending" describes Yang Qiao Mai's function: extending
the body upward, maintaining upright posture, governing
Yang-side dynamic movement. The 跷 character in Yang Qiao
literally means "to step/stride" — the vessel governs stepping
and walking, and BL62 at the ankle is where this function is
accessed.

BL62 is the 8th and FINAL Eight Confluent point in the Atlas,
completing the inventory of all 8 regular channel points that
open access to the 8 extraordinary vessels. Paired with SI3
(Houxi 后溪, opening Du Mai) — the SI3+BL62 pairing is the MOST
cross-referenced point combination in the Atlas (~20 mentions
across AP-06, AP-08, AP-10).

BL62 is also Ghost Point #5 (鬼路, Guǐ Lù, "Ghost Path") in
Sun Si-miao's 13 Ghost Points — a system for treating acute
psychiatric/spiritual disturbances.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the depression directly
below the lateral malleolus.

Anatomical landmarks: with the foot in neutral position, BL62 is
in the depression on the inferior border of the lateral
malleolus, between the malleolus tip above and the calcaneus
below.

Spatial relationships:
- BL60 is posterior-superior — between malleolus and Achilles
- BL61 is inferior — on the lateral calcaneal surface
- BL63 is anterior-inferior — anterior to the malleolus, toward
  the 5th metatarsal
- BL59 (Xi-Cleft of Yang Qiao Mai) is 3 cun proximal — the
  Opening point (BL62) and Xi-Cleft (BL59) of the same
  extraordinary vessel are only 3 cun apart
- KI6 (Eight Confluent of Yin Qiao Mai) is on the OPPOSITE side
  of the ankle, below the medial malleolus. BL62 and KI6 are the
  Yang-Yin Qiao Mai mirror pair

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Sural nerve** (S1–S2) — terminal branches accessible at BL62
  as the sural nerve courses behind and below the lateral
  malleolus
- **Lateral calcaneal branches** — terminal sensory branches to
  the lateral heel and ankle
- Deep: peroneal tendon sheaths (slightly posterior)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thin subcutaneous tissue over the inferior border
of the lateral malleolus. Periosteal surface with minimal soft
tissue. Inferior peroneal retinaculum nearby, anchoring the
peroneal tendons.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: lateral malleolar artery (branch of anterior tibial).
Small saphenous vein (posterior). Minimal vascular risk at the
inframalleolar depression.

Muscle layers: skin → minimal subcutaneous tissue → periosteum of
the lateral malleolus / extensor digitorum brevis origin
(laterally). Primarily a bony/periosteal site — no muscle belly.

#### Phân loại huyệt

**Eight Confluent: YES — Opening point of Yang Qiao Mai
(阳跷脉, Yang Heel/Motility Vessel)**

Paired with SI3 (Houxi 后溪, Opening point of Du Mai). The
SI3+BL62 pairing opens access to Du Mai + Yang Qiao Mai,
covering the posterior midline (Du) and lateral body Yang
musculature (Yang Qiao).

**Eight Confluent Inventory COMPLETE (8/8):**

| # | Point | Channel | Extraordinary Vessel Opened | Paired With | Pair Extraordinary Vessel | AP |
|---|-------|---------|-----------|-------------|---------|-----|
| 1 | LU7 | Lung | Ren Mai | KI6 | Yin Qiao Mai | AP-01 |
| 2 | SI3 | S. Intestine | Du Mai | BL62 | Yang Qiao Mai | AP-06 |
| 3 | PC6 | Pericardium | Yin Wei Mai | SP4 | Chong Mai | AP-09 |
| 4 | TE5 | Triple Burner | Yang Wei Mai | GB41 | Dai Mai | AP-10 |
| 5 | SP4 | Spleen | Chong Mai | PC6 | Yin Wei Mai | AP-04 |
| 6 | KI6 | Kidney | Yin Qiao Mai | LU7 | Ren Mai | AP-08 |
| 7 | GB41 | Gallbladder | Dai Mai | TE5 | Yang Wei Mai | AP-11 |
| 8 | **BL62** | **Bladder** | **Yang Qiao Mai** | **SI3** | **Du Mai** | **AP-07** |

**Inventory architectural patterns:**
- **4 pairs, each = upper + lower limb.** ARM point + LEG point
  access 2 EVs together. No same-limb pairs.
- **Yang pairs = same meridian family:** SI3+BL62 (both Taiyang),
  TE5+GB41 (both Shaoyang). Two Yang families represented;
  Yangming absent.
- **Yin pairs = cross meridian family:** LU7 (Taiyin) + KI6
  (Shaoyin), PC6 (Jueyin) + SP4 (Taiyin). All three Yin families
  represented.
- **Yang-same / Yin-cross asymmetry:** confirmed across all 4
  pairs. Yang EVs accessed through same-name pairs; Yin EVs
  through cross-family pairs. No known anatomical explanation.
- **All 8 on LIMBS** (4 arm, 4 leg). No trunk or head points.
- **Dual-category overlap:** 4/8 Eight Confluent are also Luo
  (LU7, SP4, PC6, TE5). LU7 has TRIPLE designation (Luo + Eight
  Confluent + Command Point). BL62 has no other major category.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI3, AP-08 KI6, AP-10 TE5

**Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉) vessel profile:**
- Trajectory: BL62 area → lateral malleolus → lateral leg →
  lateral body → shoulder → neck → BL1 (inner canthus, Jingming
  睛明) where it meets Yin Qiao Mai
- Functions:
  1. **Eye opening:** Yang Qiao = eyes open (day, wakefulness).
     Yin Qiao = eyes close (night, sleep). At BL1, the two Qiao
     vessels meet and control the eyelid cycle.
  2. **Yang-side muscle tone:** lateral body musculature,
     maintaining upright posture and dynamic balance
  3. **Stepping/gait:** 跷 = stepping. Governs the stepping
     pattern in walking and running
  4. **Wakefulness regulation:** Yang Qiao excess = insomnia.
     Yang Qiao deficiency = somnolence, lateral muscle flaccidity
- Pathology: Ling Shu Ch.17: "阳气满则不得瞑" — when Yang Qi is
  full, one cannot close the eyes (insomnia from Yang Qiao
  excess). Complements KI6/Yin Qiao: "阴气尽而目不瞑" — when Yin
  Qi is exhausted, the eyes cannot close.
- Xi-Cleft: BL59 (Fuyang) — documented in Batch 11. Opening
  (BL62) and Xi-Cleft (BL59) of Yang Qiao Mai are only 3 cun
  apart — closest Opening-to-Xi-Cleft distance of any Extraordinary Vessel.

**BL62 + BL59 complementary roles:** BL62 (Opening) for chronic
Yang Qiao Mai regulation (sleep/wake, eye function, postural
tone). BL59 (Xi-Cleft) for ACUTE Yang Qiao pathology (sudden
Yang-side symptoms, acute lateral muscle spasm). Same vessel,
different temporal acuity.

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No (BL64). Luo-Connecting: No (BL58).
Xi-Cleft: No (BL63). Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

**Ghost Point #5 — Gui Lu (鬼路, "Ghost Path"):** fifth in Sun
Si-miao's 13 Ghost Points sequence (652 CE): GV26(#1) → LU11(#2)
→ SP1(#3) → PC7(#4) → **BL62(#5)** → GV16(#6) → ST6(#7) →
CV24(#8) → PC8(#9) → GV23(#10) → CV1(#11) → LI11(#12) →
EX-HN1(#13).

BL62 belongs to the **2nd trinity** (PC7 + BL62 + GV16):

| Ghost # | Point | Ghost Name | Location | Domain |
|---------|-------|------------|----------|--------|
| 4 | PC7 | Ghost Heart (鬼心) | Wrist | Spirit-calming |
| **5** | **BL62** | **Ghost Path (鬼路)** | **Ankle** | **Movement/stepping** |
| 6 | GV16 | Ghost Pillow (鬼枕) | Occiput | Wind Gate/brainstem |

The 2nd trinity spans wrist → ankle → occiput. "Ghost Path"
(鬼路) = the pathway by which pathogenic spirits travel. BL62's
Yang Qiao Mai function (wakefulness, stepping) links to
psychiatric disturbances involving motor/gait abnormalities and
sleep-wake disruption.
→ Cross-ref: AP-09 PC7 (#4), AP-03 ST6 (#7)

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL62 (Shenmai 申脉). Eight Confluent (Yang Qiao
  Mai) + Ghost Point #5. Calms the spirit, benefits the eyes,
  strengthens Yang, opens Yang Qiao Mai. Epilepsy (癫痫),
  insomnia, headache, dizziness, eye disorders, mania/psychosis,
  lumbar/leg pain. Combined with SI3 = standard pair for Du Mai +
  Yang Qiao Mai. Combined with KI6 for sleep-wake balance (BL62 =
  wakefulness, KI6 = sleep). Ghost Point: acute mania, psychotic
  episodes.

Trigger point: ✗ No correspondence. Inframalleolar depression =
  periosteal/tendinous zone.

Ashi correspondence: inframalleolar tenderness in lateral ankle
  instability, subtalar pathology, sinus tarsi syndrome. Limited
  significance compared to BL60 or GB40. 🟡

Marma point: **Gulpha (ankle)** territory — the inferior aspect.
  Same broader zone as BL60 and KI6. 🟡

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. ✅ MATCH for local ankle only.
  Epilepsy, insomnia, headache, and eye disorders ALL require
  supraspinal pathways. NO segmental basis for BL62's primary
  psychiatric/sleep indications.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: SBL lateral edge at calcaneus-malleolus
  transition. Minimal direct engagement. 🟡

Neurogenic spot: ✗ No data at inframalleolar site.

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (bony depression).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡). Notably
LOW for a point with such extensive traditional importance. The
3/8 reflects BL62's significance as an EXTRAORDINARY VESSEL
opening and Ghost Point — categories that are systemic and
non-anatomical, not reducible to local tissue convergence. The
8-system convergence score inherently undervalues systemic
regulatory functions.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch.17 (Maidou): Yang Qiao excess → insomnia
  ("阳气满则不得瞑")
- Nan Jing Ch.29: Eight Confluent pairing — SI3+BL62 for Du Mai
  + Yang Qiao Mai
- Jiayi Jing: epilepsy (癫痫), headache, dizziness, eye
  disorders, insomnia, mania, lumbar/leg pain
- Beiji Qianjin Yaofang (Sun Si-miao, 652 CE): Ghost Point #5
  (鬼路) for acute mania and spirit possession disorders
- Dacheng: combined SI3+BL62 for headache, epilepsy, back
  stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Epilepsy, mania, insomnia
- Headache (vertex, occipital), dizziness
- Eye disorders (redness, swelling, tearing)
- Lumbar/leg pain, ankle pain
- Facial paralysis

**Epilepsy/mania** — 🔴 Unvalidated for BL62 specifically.
Appears in multiple classical epilepsy protocols (often with
GV26, PC7, GV20). The Ghost Point tradition specifically targets
acute psychiatric episodes.

**Insomnia** — 🔴 Unvalidated. BL62 (Yang Qiao) + KI6 (Yin
Qiao) is the classical sleep-wake regulation pair. KI6 has 🟡
evidence for insomnia (AP-08, acupressure meta-analysis), but
BL62's contribution is undocumented.

**Headache/dizziness** — 🔴 Unvalidated. Common Taiyang
indication.

**Eye disorders** — 🔴 Unvalidated. Yang Qiao Mai reaches BL1
(inner canthus, "eye opening"), providing the classical basis.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL62-specific clinical trials identified. The SI3+BL62 pair is
widely referenced in textbooks but clinical evidence for the pair
as a unit is also absent. The Yang Qiao Mai concept (wakefulness,
eye opening, lateral muscle tone) has no identified physiological
substrate connecting BL62 (lateral ankle, S1-S2) to the eye (BL1,
cranial nerves) or to hypothalamic sleep-wake centers.

The Ghost Point system's claim that BL62 treats acute psychiatric
disorders has not been subjected to controlled investigation. The
13 Ghost Points represent one of the most culturally specific
treatment systems in Chinese medicine — deeply embedded in
cosmological concepts that resist biomedical translation.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal Dynamic acupoint studies. Yang Qiao Mai theory predicts
BL62 sensitization in patients with insomnia (Yang Qiao excess),
sleep-wake disturbances, or unilateral lateral body weakness.
Ghost Point theory predicts BL62 sensitivity in acute psychiatric
states. Neither prediction has been systematically investigated.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local ankle mechanism (HIGH clarity):** Sural nerve stimulation
at S1-S2 → segmental modulation for ankle/leg pain. Same as BL60
at a different approach angle.

**Epilepsy / psychiatric mechanism (LOW clarity):** S1-S2
afferent → ascending to brainstem/thalamus/cortex. No identified
pathway connects BL62's local neural substrate to seizure
threshold modulation or mood regulation. Ghost Point system
proposes a mechanism outside the BSA framework.

**Sleep-wake mechanism (LOW clarity):** Yang Qiao Mai posits BL62
regulates wakefulness through the extraordinary vessel network.
No anatomical substrate identified. Hypothalamic sleep-wake
centers (SCN, VLPO, orexin neurons) have no known afferent
connections from S1-S2 territory.

**Eye mechanism (LOW clarity):** Yang Qiao Mai "reaches BL1 to
open the eye" — but no continuous anatomical structure connects
BL62 (lateral ankle) to BL1 (inner canthus). Conceptual pathway,
not anatomical.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular into the
inframalleolar depression.

Moxa: applicable for Yang deficiency (somnolence, lateral
weakness).

Eight Confluent protocol: BL62 needled first (opening Yang Qiao
Mai), then SI3 (connecting Du Mai). In some protocols, sequence
reversed depending on the primary vessel. Combined BL62+SI3 =
standard treatment for occipital headache, back stiffness,
epilepsy.

Ghost Point protocol: in Sun Si-miao's sequence, BL62 is needled
5th (after GV26, LU11, SP1, PC7). The sequence alternates
left-right sides: #5 BL62 is LEFT for male, RIGHT for female
(Yin-Yang gender convention). Moderate stimulation for De Qi.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: lateral malleolar artery (small caliber). Minimal.

Neural risk: sural nerve branches nearby. Direct stimulation
  possible but not dangerous.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication at BL62 (unlike BL60).

### BL63 — Kim Môn (金门, Jīn Mén)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Xi-Cleft of BL (12th/12th
= LAST Regular Xi-Cleft)**

Name 金门 (Jīnmén) = "Golden Gate" (金 = gold/metal, 门 = gate).
The 金 (Metal) reference on a Water channel is noteworthy — Metal
generates Water in the Five Element cycle (母生子), suggesting the
gate through which generative Qi enters. Alternatively, 金门
evokes a fortified gate (gold = strength, gate = protection),
reflecting the Xi-Cleft's role as a site where Qi and Blood
accumulate in concentrated "reserves" for acute mobilization.

BL63 is the Xi-Cleft (郄穴) of the Bladder channel — the 12th
and FINAL regular Xi-Cleft in the Atlas. Xi-Cleft points are
where Qi and Blood accumulate deeply along a channel, used for
ACUTE conditions of that channel's domain (B01 §3.1). For Yang
channels, Xi-Cleft points treat acute PAIN; for Yin channels,
acute BLOOD disorders (documented at KI5, AP-08).

BL63's traditional indications emphasize epilepsy and infantile
convulsions — acute neurological events that align with the
Xi-Cleft's "acute condition" designation.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of the
foot, inferior to the anterior border of the lateral malleolus,
in the depression posterior to the tuberosity of the 5th
metatarsal bone.

Anatomical landmarks: anterior and inferior to BL62, at the
calcaneocuboid joint area. The depression is between the anterior
edge of the lateral malleolus and the styloid process (tuberosity)
of the 5th metatarsal.

Spatial relationships:
- BL62 is posterior-superior — directly below the malleolus
- BL64 is anterior — at the 5th metatarsal tuberosity
- GB40 (Qiuxu) is nearby — anterior to the lateral malleolus.
  BL63 and GB40 are adjacent points from different channels in
  the same anatomical neighborhood

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve** — terminal branch of the
  sural nerve. Provides sensory innervation to the dorsolateral
  foot, transitioning from sural to more distal branches.
- **Lateral plantar nerve** branches — deep, from the tibial
  nerve. Motor and sensory to the lateral foot.
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thin subcutaneous tissue → extensor digitorum
brevis muscle origin (most lateral fibers) → calcaneocuboid
joint capsule. The extensor digitorum brevis (EDB) is the only
intrinsic muscle on the DORSUM of the foot.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: lateral tarsal artery (branch of dorsalis pedis).
Minimal vascular risk.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → EDB (thin, lateral
fibers) → calcaneocuboid ligaments / cuboid periosteum.

#### Phân loại huyệt

**Xi-Cleft: YES — 12th/12th regular Xi-Cleft (LAST).**

**Regular Xi-Cleft Inventory COMPLETE (12/12):**

| # | Xi-Cleft | Channel | Hán-Việt | Yin/Yang | Location | AP |
|---|----------|---------|----------|----------|----------|-----|
| 1 | LU6 | Lung | Khổng Tối | Yin | 7 cun above wrist | AP-01 |
| 2 | LI7 | L. Intestine | Ôn Lưu | Yang | 5 cun above wrist | AP-02 |
| 3 | ST34 | Stomach | Lương Khâu | Yang | 2 cun above patella | AP-03 |
| 4 | SP8 | Spleen | Địa Cơ | Yin | 3 cun below knee | AP-04 |
| 5 | HT6 | Heart | Âm Khích | Yin | 0.5 cun above wrist | AP-05 |
| 6 | SI6 | S. Intestine | Dưỡng Lão | Yang | Radial head | AP-06 |
| 7 | **BL63** | **Bladder** | **Kim Môn** | **Yang** | **Lat. foot** | **AP-07** |
| 8 | KI5 | Kidney | Thủy Tuyền | Yin | Med. calcaneus | AP-08 |
| 9 | PC4 | Pericardium | Khích Môn | Yin | 5 cun above wrist | AP-09 |
| 10 | TE7 | Triple Burner | Hội Tông | Yang | 3 cun above wrist | AP-10 |
| 11 | GB36 | Gallbladder | Ngoại Khâu | Yang | 7 cun above ankle | AP-11 |
| 12 | LR6 | Liver | Trung Đô | Yin | 7 cun above ankle | AP-12 |

**Inventory patterns:**
- **Yin-Yang sub-rules confirmed (6+6):** ALL 6 Yin Xi-Clefts
  treat acute BLOOD disorders. ALL 6 Yang Xi-Clefts treat acute
  PAIN. BL63 = Yang → acute BL channel pain (lumbar, sciatic,
  ankle).
- **ALL on limbs** (6 arm, 6 leg) — same distribution as Luo.
- **Distal limb clustering:** most Xi-Clefts are between the
  elbow/knee and wrist/ankle. BL63 (lateral foot) is the MOST
  DISTAL Xi-Cleft of all 12.
- **Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Clefts (4/4)** already complete at BL59 (Batch 11).
  The 12 regular + 4 Extraordinary Vessel = 16 total Xi-Cleft points, all now
  documented.
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI5 (Yin sub-rule), AP-04 SP8

Five Shu: No. Yuan-Source: No (BL64). Luo-Connecting: No (BL58).
Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL63 (Jinmen 金门). Xi-Cleft of Bladder. Opens
  the channels, treats acute pain, calms the spirit, stops
  convulsions. Epilepsy (癫痫), infantile convulsions (小儿惊风),
  lumbar pain, ankle pain, mania.

Trigger point: ✗ No correspondence. Lateral foot at the
  calcaneocuboid area — minimal muscle for TrP formation. EDB
  TrPs refer to the dorsal foot, not the lateral border.

Ashi correspondence: tenderness at the calcaneocuboid joint line
  in midfoot sprains, cuboid syndrome, and lateral foot stress.
  Clinically meaningful for foot pathology. 🟡

Marma point: ✗ No specific Marma at the lateral midfoot.

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. ✅ MATCH for local foot/ankle
  indications. ✗ for epilepsy/convulsions (requires supraspinal).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Lateral Line (LL) territory at the lateral foot.
  The LL runs along the fibular/peroneal side: peroneal muscles →
  peroneal tendons → cuboid → 5th metatarsal. BL63 at the
  calcaneocuboid area is on this line. 🟡

Neurogenic spot: ✗ No data.

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (foot).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡).
Head zone ✅ for local only.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: epilepsy, infantile convulsions, mania, lumbar pain
- Dacheng: epilepsy, convulsions in children, ankle/foot pain,
  lumbar stiffness

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Epilepsy, infantile convulsions, mania
- Lumbar/leg pain, ankle/foot pain
- Headache

**Epilepsy/convulsions** — 🔴 Unvalidated. The Xi-Cleft
designation predicts use for ACUTE conditions — seizures and
convulsions are acute events that fit this pattern. However, no
clinical trials exist for BL63 in epilepsy management.

**Lumbar/ankle pain** — 🔴 Unvalidated. Anatomically plausible
via S1-S2 segmental convergence.

All indications 🔴.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL63-specific clinical trials identified. The Xi-Cleft point
concept (Qi/Blood accumulation site for acute conditions) is a
theoretical framework without controlled clinical validation for
any specific Xi-Cleft point. The Yang Xi-Cleft → acute pain
sub-rule is a consistent classical teaching but has not been
tested against sham-controlled designs.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal studies. The Xi-Cleft concept predicts BL63 should
become tender during acute BL channel pathology (acute lumbar
pain, acute ankle sprain, bladder infection). This is the most
directly testable Dynamic acupoint prediction for Xi-Cleft
points, but has not been systematically investigated at BL63.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local foot mechanism (HIGH clarity):** Lateral dorsal cutaneous
nerve stimulation → S1-S2 segmental modulation for lateral foot
and ankle pain. Direct access to calcaneocuboid joint territory.

**Lumbar mechanism (MODERATE clarity):** S1-S2 afferent →
lumbar enlargement convergence. Same mechanism as BL60 and BL40,
from the most distal access point.

**Epilepsy mechanism (LOW clarity):** No pathway identified from
S1-S2 peripheral input to cortical seizure threshold modulation.
The epilepsy/convulsion indication is shared across multiple BL
foot points (BL62, BL63, BL66, BL67) — possibly a Taiyang
channel pattern rather than a BL63-specific mechanism.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

Moxa: applicable. Less commonly used at BL63 than at BL60 or
BL67.

Xi-Cleft acute protocol: BL63 for acute lumbar pain onset, acute
ankle sprain, acute bladder inflammation. Needle with strong
stimulation for De Qi. May be combined with Back-Shu (BL28) for
acute bladder conditions: Xi-Cleft (distal acute) + Back-Shu
(local organ) = classical combination.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: lateral tarsal artery small. Minimal risk.

Neural risk: lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve — direct stimulation
  possible. Not dangerous.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL64 — Kinh Cốt (京骨, Jīng Gǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Yuan-Source of BL
(12th/12th = LAST Regular Yuan-Source)**

Name 京骨 (Jīnggǔ) = "Capital Bone" (京 = capital/great, 骨 =
bone). The "capital bone" is the classical Chinese anatomical name
for the tuberosity (styloid process) of the 5th metatarsal — the
bony prominence on the lateral midfoot where BL64 is located. The
naming convention is straightforward anatomical description: the
point is at the capital bone. The 京 character also carries
connotations of greatness/centrality, fitting the Yuan-Source
point's role as the "origin/center" of the channel's fundamental
Qi.

BL64 is the Yuan-Source (原穴) of the Bladder channel — the 12th
and FINAL regular Yuan-Source point in the Atlas. Yuan-Source
points reflect the state of the organ's fundamental Qi and serve
as the diagnostic and treatment "root" of each channel. BL64
confirms the universal Yin-Yang Yuan-Source rule: ALL 6 Yang
channels have Yuan-Source as a SEPARATE point from the Five Shu
(Yuan ≠ Five Shu), while ALL 6 Yin channels have Yuan-Source
overlapping with the Stream point (Yuan = Stream). This is now
the most consistently confirmed structural rule in the Atlas:
12/12 channels, 100% consistency.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of the
foot, inferior to the tuberosity of the 5th metatarsal bone, at
the junction of the red and white skin.

Anatomical landmarks: the tuberosity (styloid process) of the 5th
metatarsal is a prominent bony landmark on the lateral midfoot.
BL64 is in the depression inferior to this tuberosity, at the
border between the thick plantar skin (red, weight-bearing) and
the thinner dorsal skin (white, non-weight-bearing).

Spatial relationships:
- BL63 is proximal — at the calcaneocuboid area
- BL65 is distal — posterior to the 5th MTP joint
- GB40 (Qiuxu) is superior — anterior to the lateral malleolus.
  BL64 and GB40 are the two Yuan-Source points closest to each
  other from different channels

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve of the foot** — terminal
  branch of the sural nerve. Sensory to the lateral midfoot.
- **4th common digital nerve** territory approaches from medial
- Deep: lateral plantar nerve branches (motor to abductor digiti
  minimi)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: junction of plantar aponeurosis (lateral band) and
dorsal foot fascia. The red-white skin junction marks the
transition between thick plantar fascia and thin dorsal fascia —
a fascial boundary line.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: dorsal metatarsal arteries (small). 4th dorsal
metatarsal artery territory. Minimal vascular risk.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → abductor digiti
minimi (lateral) → 5th metatarsal periosteum. The abductor
digiti minimi is the primary muscle at this site — thin, running
along the lateral border of the foot.

#### Phân loại huyệt

**Yuan-Source: YES — 12th/12th regular Yuan-Source (LAST).**

Yuan-Source points reflect the organ's fundamental Qi. BL64
reflects Bladder organ Qi — used diagnostically (tenderness at
BL64 suggests Bladder dysfunction) and therapeutically (tonifying
BL64 strengthens Bladder Qi).

**Yuan-Source Inventory COMPLETE (12/12):**

| # | Yuan | Channel | Hán-Việt | Yin/Yang | Yuan = Five Shu? | AP |
|---|------|---------|----------|----------|------------------|-----|
| 1 | LU9 | Lung | Thái Uyên | Yin | = Stream/Earth | AP-01 |
| 2 | LI4 | L. Intestine | Hợp Cốc | Yang | ≠ (separate) | AP-02 |
| 3 | ST42 | Stomach | Xung Dương | Yang | ≠ (separate) | AP-03 |
| 4 | SP3 | Spleen | Thái Bạch | Yin | = Stream/Earth | AP-04 |
| 5 | HT7 | Heart | Thần Môn | Yin | = Stream/Earth | AP-05 |
| 6 | SI4 | S. Intestine | Uyển Cốt | Yang | ≠ (separate) | AP-06 |
| 7 | **BL64** | **Bladder** | **Kinh Cốt** | **Yang** | **≠ (separate)** | **AP-07** |
| 8 | KI3 | Kidney | Thái Khê | Yin | = Stream/Earth | AP-08 |
| 9 | PC7 | Pericardium | Đại Lăng | Yin | = Stream/Earth | AP-09 |
| 10 | TE4 | Triple Burner | Dương Trì | Yang | ≠ (separate) | AP-10 |
| 11 | GB40 | Gallbladder | Khâu Khư | Yang | ≠ (separate) | AP-11 |
| 12 | LR3 | Liver | Thái Xung | Yin | = Stream/Earth | AP-12 |

**Yin-Yang Yuan-Source Rule CONFIRMED (12/12, 100%):**
- **ALL 6 Yin channels:** Yuan = Stream (same point, dual role).
  Every Yin Yuan point is ALSO the Stream/Earth Five Shu point.
- **ALL 6 Yang channels:** Yuan ≠ Five Shu (separate point).
  BL64 is NOT a Five Shu point. Yang channels have 6 transport
  points total (5 Five Shu + 1 Yuan).
- Origin: Nan Jing Ch.62 — Yang channels receive extra Qi from
  San Jiao (三焦), granting an additional point between Stream
  and River.
- This is the MOST consistently confirmed structural rule in the
  Atlas: 12/12 channels, zero exceptions.
→ Cross-ref: AP-01 LU9 (first Yin), AP-02 LI4 (first Yang),
AP-11 GB40 (9/12 confirmation)

**Host-Guest (主客) Yuan-Luo pairing:**
- Guest treatment (BL as Host): **BL64 (Yuan) + KI4 (Luo)** —
  strengthens BL via KI's Luo pathway
- Host treatment (KI as Host): **KI3 (Yuan) + BL58 (Luo)** —
  strengthens KI via BL's Luo pathway
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI3 (Yuan), AP-08 KI4 (Luo), BL58 entry
(this file)

Five Shu: **No.** BL64 is NOT a Five Shu point. This confirms
the Yang channel pattern — Yuan is an extra, separate point.

Luo-Connecting: No (BL58). Xi-Cleft: No (BL63). Back-Shu: No.
Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No. Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL64 (Jinggu 京骨). Yuan-Source of Bladder.
  Clears Heat, calms the spirit, strengthens the lumbar spine.
  Headache, neck stiffness, lumbar pain, epilepsy, eye pain,
  nosebleed.

Trigger point: **Partial.** Abductor digiti minimi TrPs along the
  lateral foot border. Referred pain to the lateral 5th toe area.
  Moderate correspondence. 🟡

Ashi correspondence: lateral midfoot tenderness at the 5th
  metatarsal tuberosity in stress fractures (Jones fracture zone
  is slightly more distal), peroneus brevis tendon insertion
  strain, and lateral foot overuse. 🟡

Marma point: ✗ No specific Marma at the lateral midfoot.

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. ✅ MATCH for local foot
  indications. ✗ for headache/epilepsy.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Lateral Line at the 5th metatarsal — peroneus
  brevis inserts at the 5th metatarsal tuberosity, directly at
  BL64's location. This is an AT force-transmission node. 🟡

Neurogenic spot: ✗ No data.

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (foot).

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡,
Anatomy Trains 🟡). Head zone ✅ for local only.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Nan Jing Ch.62: Yuan-Source points — Yang channels receive
  extra Qi from San Jiao
- Jiayi Jing: headache, neck stiffness, lumbar pain, epilepsy,
  eye pain, nosebleed, leg/foot pain
- Dacheng: headache (occipital), neck rigidity, lumbar pain,
  mania, myopia

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache (occipital), neck stiffness
- Lumbar pain, leg pain
- Epilepsy, mania
- Eye pain, nosebleed
- Foot pain (local)

All indications — **🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL64-specific evidence.
The Yuan-Source designation has no demonstrated relationship to
diagnostic sensitivity or treatment specificity compared to
neighboring non-Yuan points.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL64-specific clinical trials identified. The Yuan-Source
concept (reflecting organ fundamental Qi, diagnostic point for
organ state) has not been validated: no controlled study has
demonstrated that Yuan-Source point tenderness reliably indicates
organ dysfunction, or that Yuan-Source point treatment differs
in efficacy from treatment at non-Yuan neighboring points.

The Yin-Yang Yuan-Source structural rule (Yuan=Stream for Yin,
Yuan≠Five Shu for Yang) is the most anatomically consistent
finding in the category analysis — but it describes a NAMING
convention, not a demonstrated physiological mechanism.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal studies. The Yuan-Source concept predicts BL64 should
become tender in patients with Bladder organ dysfunction
(urinary pathology) — the most specific Dynamic prediction for
Yuan points. This mirrors the back-shu sensitization documented
for BL28 (Back-Shu of Bladder, segmental). If BL64 (S1-S2)
shows tenderness in bladder dysfunction, the mechanism would need
to explain how pelvic visceral pathology (S2-S4) sensitizes a
distal foot point (S1-S2).

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local foot mechanism (HIGH clarity):** S1-S2 sensory
stimulation for lateral midfoot pain. Direct access to the 5th
metatarsal tuberosity area.

**Lumbar mechanism (MODERATE clarity):** S1-S2 convergence at
lumbar enlargement. Same mechanism as all distal BL points.

**Distant indications (LOW clarity):** Headache, epilepsy, eye
pain — all require supraspinal pathways with no demonstrated
connection from S1-S2 peripheral territory.

**Yuan-Source diagnostic mechanism (UNRESOLVED):** No identified
pathway explains how Bladder organ dysfunction would selectively
sensitize BL64 (S1-S2 at the foot) rather than other S1-S2
territory points. The Yuan-Source concept may describe a
classical observation pattern rather than a specific
physiological mechanism.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular into the depression
below the 5th metatarsal tuberosity.

Moxa: applicable. Used for BL Yang deficiency patterns (urinary
frequency, clear urine, cold sensation in the lower abdomen).

Yuan-Luo protocol: BL64 paired with KI4 (Luo of KI) for Guest
treatment of BL channel conditions. Conversely, KI3 (Yuan of KI)
+ BL58 (Luo of BL) for Host treatment of KI deficiency.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: minimal. Small dorsal metatarsal arteries.

Neural risk: lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve branches. Not
  dangerous.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### BL65 — Thúc Cốt (束骨, Shù Gǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Five Shu: Stream/Wood,
Sedation Point**

Name 束骨 (Shùgǔ) = "Binding Bone" (束 = bind/bundle, 骨 =
bone). The "bound bone" refers to the 5th metatarsophalangeal
(MTP) joint area where the metatarsal meets the phalanx — bones
"bound" together at the joint. BL65 is posterior to this joint
on the lateral 5th metatarsal.

BL65 is the Stream (輸, Shū) point of the BL channel, carrying
the Wood element. On a Water channel, Wood = Sedation (瀉法,
xiè fǎ): Water generates Wood (水生木), making Wood the "child"
of Water. The Sedation principle (子瀉母, "child drains parent"):
stimulating the child point draws excess Qi from the parent
(channel element), reducing excess. BL65 is therefore the point
for draining excess from the BL channel.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of the
foot, posterior to the 5th metatarsophalangeal joint, at the
junction of the red and white skin.

Anatomical landmarks: proximal and inferior to the head of the
5th metatarsal, at the red-white skin junction. The 5th MTP
joint is palpable; BL65 is proximal (toward the heel) from the
joint prominence.

Needle depth range: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **4th common digital nerve** (branch of lateral plantar nerve)
  — provides sensory innervation to the lateral 5th metatarsal
  and the webspace between 4th and 5th toes
- **Lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve** terminal branches (from
  sural nerve)
- Deep: lateral plantar nerve branches to abductor digiti minimi
  and flexor digiti minimi
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: red-white skin junction at the 5th MTP — same
fascial boundary as BL64. Plantar aponeurosis (lateral band)
terminates at the MTP joint level.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: 4th dorsal metatarsal artery (small). Plantar digital
arteries (deep). Minimal risk.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → abductor digiti
minimi / flexor digiti minimi brevis → 5th metatarsal
periosteum / MTP joint capsule.

#### Phân loại huyệt

**Five Shu: Stream (輸, Shū) — Wood element.**

The Stream (輸) point is the 3rd of 5 Five Shu, where channel Qi
"pours" from a smaller stream into deeper channels. On the Yang
Water (BL) channel:
- Five Element role: **Sedation (瀉)** — Wood is the "child" of
  Water (水生木). Stimulating the child drains excess from the
  parent element. BL65 is the primary point for reducing BL
  channel excess.
- Treatment applications (Nan Jing Ch.68): Stream points for
  "heaviness of the body and joint pain" (體重節痛).
- The Water→Wood generating relationship means BL65 addresses
  conditions where BL channel Qi is in excess: fullness,
  stagnation, acute pain from excess rather than deficiency.

Yuan-Source: No (BL64). Luo-Connecting: No (BL58). Xi-Cleft: No
(BL63). Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL65 (Shugu 束骨). Stream/Wood, Sedation point.
  Clears Heat, expels Wind, calms the spirit. Headache, neck
  stiffness, visual disorders, lumbar/leg pain, mania.

Trigger point: ✗ No meaningful correspondence. Abductor digiti
  minimi TrPs (lateral foot) are more proximal at BL64 territory.

Ashi correspondence: tenderness at the 5th MTP joint region in
  metatarsalgia, bunionette (tailor's bunion), and lateral foot
  stress. 🟡

Marma point: ✗ No specific Marma at the lateral 5th MTP.

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. ✅ MATCH for local foot
  indications. ✗ for headache/visual disorders.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: Lateral Line terminates at the 5th metatarsal
  base (BL64 territory). BL65 is distal to the LL endpoint —
  minimal AT engagement. ✗

Neurogenic spot: ✗ No data.

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (foot).

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡). The LOWEST
convergence score of all Tier B entries in this batch. Reflects
the extreme distal position where few cross-tradition systems
map specific therapeutic sites.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Lingshu Ch.2 (Ben Shu): BL65 as the Stream (輸) point
- Nan Jing Ch.68: Stream points for "heaviness of the body and
  joint pain"
- Jiayi Jing: headache, neck stiffness, blurred vision, lumbar
  pain, mania
- Dacheng: headache (occipital), neck rigidity, visual
  disturbances, leg pain

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache (occipital), neck stiffness
- Visual disorders, eye pain
- Lumbar/leg pain
- Mania, epilepsy

All indications — **🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL65-specific evidence.
The Sedation point function (draining BL channel excess) is a
theoretical treatment strategy without controlled validation.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL65-specific clinical trials identified. The Five Element
point selection system (Tonification-Sedation-Horary-Ke) has no
controlled evidence demonstrating that point selection based on
Five Element roles produces different outcomes than selection
based on other criteria (location, indication matching, etc.).

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal studies. The Sedation point concept predicts BL65
should become tender in BL channel EXCESS patterns (fullness,
acute inflammation, excess-type pain). Untested.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local foot mechanism (HIGH clarity):** 4th common digital nerve
and lateral dorsal cutaneous nerve stimulation → S1-S2 segmental
pain modulation for lateral foot.

**Distant indications (LOW clarity):** All distant indications
(headache, neck, visual) require supraspinal pathways. Same
mechanism challenge as all distal BL foot points.

**Sedation mechanism (UNRESOLVED):** No physiological
correlate identified for the Sedation function (draining channel
excess via the child element). The Five Element treatment
strategy operates within a conceptual framework that has no
demonstrated mapping to neurophysiological processes.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.3–0.5 cun perpendicular.

Moxa: generally NOT applied at Sedation points in Five Element
acupuncture — moxa is considered tonifying, which contradicts the
Sedation role. Needling with reducing (sedation) technique is
preferred: rapid insertion, slow withdrawal, wide manipulation.

Five Element protocol: BL65 (Sedation) for BL excess; contrast
with BL67 (Tonification, Well/Metal) for BL deficiency. The
Sedation-Tonification pair (BL65+BL67) represents the primary
channel-balancing dyad for BL.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: minimal. Small digital arteries.

Neural risk: 4th common digital nerve — direct stimulation may
  cause sharp sensation to the 4th-5th toe webspace.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

---

### BL66 — Thông Cốc (通谷, Tōng Gǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry) — Five Shu: Spring/Water,
Horary Point**

Name 通谷 (Tōnggǔ) = "Connecting Valley" (通 = connect/
penetrate, 谷 = valley). This name is SHARED with KI20 (腹通谷,
Fùtōnggǔ), which adds the prefix 腹 (abdomen) to disambiguate.
Both names use 通谷 but in entirely different contexts: BL66 at
the 5th toe "valley" (interdigital space) and KI20 at the
abdominal "valley" (epigastric depression). This naming collision
is rare in the acupoint system — most homophone pairs have at
least one character difference.
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI20

BL66 is the Spring (滎, Yíng) point of the BL channel, carrying
the Water element. On a Water channel, Water = Horary (本穴,
běnxué): the point's element matches the channel's element,
making BL66 the most "quintessential" expression of BL's Water
nature. Horary points are used at the channel's peak time
(15:00–17:00 for BL) for maximum treatment potency.

BL66 is the LAST individual acupoint entry in the BL channel's
Phase 2 sequence. BL67 (Well/Metal, Tonification) was Phase 1's
first entry — the final entry bookends the first.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of the
foot, in the depression anterior and inferior to the 5th
metatarsophalangeal joint, at the junction of the red and white
skin.

Anatomical landmarks: at the 5th MTP joint level, anterior and
inferior to BL65. The joint is palpable; BL66 is at the distal
border of the joint, at the red-white skin junction.

Spatial relationships:
- BL65 is proximal — posterior to the 5th MTP
- BL67 is distal — at the lateral nail of the 5th toe
- BL66 sits BETWEEN the Stream (BL65) and Well (BL67), following
  the Five Shu's distal-to-proximal convention: Well (toe) →
  Spring (MTP distal) → Stream (MTP proximal) → River (ankle)
  → He-Sea (knee)

Needle depth range: 0.1–0.3 cun perpendicular. Shallow due to
thin tissue over the MTP joint.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **4th proper plantar digital nerve** — terminal branch of the
  lateral plantar nerve. Sensory to the lateral aspect of the 5th
  toe and the 4th-5th interdigital space.
- **4th dorsal digital nerve** — from the lateral dorsal
  cutaneous nerve (sural origin)
- Spinal segment: S1–S2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: thin digital skin → minimal subcutaneous tissue →
MTP joint capsule / collateral ligaments → periosteum of the 5th
metatarsal head and proximal phalanx. Very thin tissue layer.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: proper plantar digital artery to the 5th toe. Dorsal
digital artery. Very small caliber vessels.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue (minimal) → no
significant muscle at the MTP joint level (tendons of extensor
and flexor digitorum pass superficially and deeply). Primarily
joint capsule and periosteum.

#### Phân loại huyệt

**Five Shu: Spring (滎, Yíng) — Water element.**

The Spring (滎) point is the 2nd of 5 Five Shu, where channel Qi
"springs" forth with increasing volume from the Well's initial
trickle. On the Yang Water (BL) channel:
- Five Element role: **Horary (本穴)** — Water element on a Water
  channel. The Spring point's element matches the channel's
  element, making BL66 the "essential" BL point.
- Treatment timing: most effective at BL's peak hour (15:00–
  17:00, 申時, Shen hour).
- Treatment applications (Nan Jing Ch.68): Spring points for
  "fever of the body" (身熱). Heat-clearing function.

BL Five Shu set COMPLETE (5/5):

| Five Shu | Element | Point | Role | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Well | Metal | BL67 | Tonification | Phase 1 ✓ |
| **Spring** | **Water** | **BL66** | **Horary** | **This entry** |
| Stream | Wood | BL65 | Sedation | This batch ✓ |
| River | Fire | BL60 | Ke Subject | This batch ✓ |
| He-Sea | Earth | BL40 | Ke Controller | Batch 8 ✓ |

This completes the BL Five Shu set — the **12th/12th channel
Five Shu set** in the Atlas. All 60 Five Shu points across all
12 regular channels are now documented.

Yuan-Source: No (BL64). Luo-Connecting: No (BL58). Xi-Cleft: No
(BL63). Back-Shu: No. Front-Mu: No. Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: BL66 (Tonggu 通谷). Spring/Water, Horary point.
  Clears Heat, benefits the head, calms the spirit. Headache,
  nasal congestion, neck stiffness, epistaxis, mania.

Trigger point: ✗ No correspondence. 5th MTP joint has no muscle
  belly for TrP formation.

Ashi correspondence: 5th MTP joint tenderness in metatarsalgia,
  gout (the 5th MTP is a less common but documented gout site
  after the 1st MTP). 🟡

Marma point: ✗ No specific Marma at the 5th MTP.

Head zone: S1-S2 → lower limb. ✅ MATCH for local foot. ✗ for
  headache/nasal congestion.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

Anatomy Trains: ✗ Minimal engagement at the distal 5th
  metatarsal. Beyond the LL terminal point.

Neurogenic spot: ✗ No data.

Cupping point: ✗ Not applicable (toe area).

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi 🟡). Same minimal score as
BL65. Expected at the extreme distal toe-MTP territory.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA)

Nguồn cổ:
- Lingshu Ch.2 (Ben Shu): BL66 as the Spring (滎) point
- Nan Jing Ch.68: Spring points for "fever of the body" (身熱)
- Jiayi Jing: headache, nasal congestion, neck stiffness,
  epistaxis, mania
- Dacheng: headache, nasal obstruction, neck rigidity, mania

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache, nasal congestion/epistaxis
- Neck stiffness
- Mania, epilepsy
- Abdominal distension

All indications — **🔴 Unvalidated.** No BL66-specific evidence.
The Horary function (peak-time treatment) has no controlled
evidence demonstrating time-dependent efficacy at BL66.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No BL66-specific clinical trials identified. The Horary point
concept (treating at the channel's peak time with the
element-matching point for maximum effect) is a classical
treatment strategy without any modern controlled evidence. No
study has compared BL66 treatment at 15:00-17:00 vs other times
to test the Horary timing hypothesis.

Evidence rating: **🔴 Unvalidated** — all indications.

#### Dynamic acupoint

No formal studies. The Horary concept does not predict specific
sensitization patterns — it is a treatment timing strategy, not
a diagnostic one.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local foot mechanism (HIGH clarity):** Digital nerve stimulation
at the 5th MTP joint → S1-S2 segmental modulation for local foot
pain.

**Distant indications (LOW clarity):** All distant indications
(headache, nasal, neck) require supraspinal pathways. Same
challenge as all distal BL points.

**Horary mechanism (UNRESOLVED):** The peak-time treatment
concept has no identified physiological basis. Circadian rhythms
in autonomic function, cortisol, and neurotransmitter levels are
well-documented, but no demonstrated pathway connects BL channel
peak time (15:00-17:00) to enhanced therapeutic response at BL66
specifically.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Standard needling: 0.1–0.3 cun perpendicular. Very shallow due
to thin tissue.

Moxa: applicable. Small moxa cones (米粒灸, rice-grain moxa) for
the Heat-clearing Spring point — paradoxically, moxa at Spring
points is used to "drain Heat via the child" in some Five Element
traditions.

Horary protocol: BL66 needled at 15:00–17:00 (BL peak time)
with the intention of maximally engaging the channel's Water
element. In Five Element acupuncture, this is the most
"harmonizing" treatment possible — matching element, matching
time.

#### An toàn

Vascular risk: minimal. Tiny digital arteries.

Neural risk: proper digital nerve — stimulation may cause sharp
  toe sensation. Not dangerous.

Pregnancy: No specific contraindication.

### Batch 12 Observations

**Observation 33 — LATERAL MALLEOLUS CLUSTER: DENSEST NON-BACK
BL CONCENTRATION.**

Four BL points (BL60, BL61, BL62, BL63) are located within ~3 cm
of the lateral malleolus tip — the densest cluster of BL points
outside the paravertebral back lines:

| Point | Position relative to lat. malleolus | Distance |
|---|---|---|
| BL60 | Posterior (Achilles depression) | ~1.5 cm |
| BL61 | Inferior (lateral calcaneus) | ~2 cm |
| BL62 | Directly below (inframalleolar) | ~1 cm |
| BL63 | Anterior-inferior (calcaneocuboid) | ~2.5 cm |

For comparison, the back line spacing: medial line points are
~1.5 cun (~3.75 cm) apart vertically; the malleolus cluster is
more compressed. This concentration reflects the anatomical
convergence at the lateral ankle: ankle joint, subtalar joint,
peroneal tendons, sural nerve terminal branches, and
calcaneocuboid articulation all converge in a small zone.

From BL63 onward, the points progress distally along the 5th
metatarsal to the 5th toe: BL64 (tuberosity) → BL65 (proximal
MTP) → BL66 (distal MTP) → BL67 (nail). This distal progression
follows the 4th dorsal digital nerve from the lateral dorsal
cutaneous nerve (sural origin), maintaining S1-S2 segmental
continuity throughout.

**Observation 34 — EIGHT CONFLUENT COMPLETE (8/8): FULL
ARCHITECTURAL ANALYSIS.**

With BL62, all 8 Eight Confluent points are documented. The
complete system reveals several confirmed structural patterns:

**1. Pairing architecture (4 pairs):**
Each pair = one UPPER limb point + one LOWER limb point, together
opening access to TWO extraordinary vessels. No same-limb pairs,
no trunk/head points.

**2. Yang-same / Yin-cross family asymmetry:**
- Yang pairs: SI3+BL62 (SAME family: both Taiyang), TE5+GB41
  (SAME family: both Shaoyang)
- Yin pairs: LU7+KI6 (CROSS: Taiyin+Shaoyin), PC6+SP4 (CROSS:
  Jueyin+Taiyin)
- Pattern: 100% consistent. Yang extraordinary vessels are
  accessed through same-name channel pairs; Yin through
  cross-family. No anatomical explanation identified.

**3. Dual-category overlap:**
4/8 Eight Confluent are also Luo-Connecting (LU7, SP4, PC6, TE5).
LU7 holds TRIPLE designation (Luo + Eight Confluent + Command
Point). The remaining 4 (SI3, KI6, GB41, BL62) have no Luo
overlap. Pattern: dual-category points are ALL on Yin or mixed
channels; the two strictly-Yang pairs (SI3, BL62) have no
additional major category.

**4. Absent family:** Yangming (LI/ST) has no Eight Confluent
point. The two represented Yang families are Taiyang (SI3+BL62)
and Shaoyang (TE5+GB41). All three Yin families are represented.

**Observation 35 — QUADRUPLE INVENTORY COMPLETION: ALL 9/9
TRACKED INVENTORIES COMPLETE.**

Batch 12 completes the remaining 4 tracked inventories:

| Inventory | Final Point | # | Batch |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ Back-Shu | BL28 | 12/12 | 6 |
| ✅ Lower He-Sea | BL40 | 6/6 | 8 |
| ✅ Four Command Points | BL40 | 4/4 | 8 |
| ✅ Luo-Connecting | BL58 | 12/12 | 11 |

| ✅ **Regular Xi-Cleft** | **BL63** | **12/12** | **12** |
| ✅ **Yuan-Source** | **BL64** | **12/12** | **12** |
| ✅ **Five Shu sets** | **BL66** | **12/12** | **12** |

**ALL 9 tracked inventories are now COMPLETE.** The BL channel
alone provided the LAST entry for 7 of 9 inventories (Back-Shu,
Lower He-Sea, Luo, Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, Xi-Cleft,
Yuan-Source). Only Four Command Points (LI4 was the first, BL40
the last) and Five Shu sets (BL is the 12th channel but any
channel could have been last) were not uniquely BL-dependent.

The Yuan-Source inventory confirms the MOST consistent structural
rule in the Atlas: **Yin-Yang Yuan-Source divergence (12/12,
100%)**. All 6 Yin channels: Yuan = Stream (same point). All 6
Yang channels: Yuan ≠ Five Shu (separate point). Zero exceptions
across 12 channels.

The regular Xi-Cleft inventory (12/12) confirms the **Yin-Yang
Xi-Cleft sub-rule**: all 6 Yin Xi-Clefts treat acute BLOOD
disorders; all 6 Yang Xi-Clefts treat acute PAIN. Combined with
the 4 Extraordinary Vessel Xi-Clefts, all 16 Xi-Cleft points in the system are
now documented.

With BL66, all **60 Five Shu points** (12 channels × 5 points)
are documented. The complete Five Shu system confirms:
- All Yang channels: Well/Metal, Spring/Water, Stream/Wood,
  River/Fire, He-Sea/Earth
- All Yin channels: Well/Wood, Spring/Fire, Stream/Earth,
  River/Metal, He-Sea/Water
- Yin and Yang sequences are REVERSED: Yang starts with Metal,
  Yin starts with Wood
- Five Element roles (Tonification/Sedation/Horary/Ke) are
  determined by the INTERACTION of Five Shu element with channel
  element — fully verified across all 12 channels
